### 01_doc_application_application_h

<a id="01-doc-application-application-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/01_doc_application_application_h/docu.output.md`



## Application

**Role:** `Application` is the abstract base class that every j9t/AIAssistant host application must implement. It defines the mandatory lifecycle contract — startup, per-frame update, event dispatch, and shutdown — that the engine's run-loop calls in sequence. Concrete subclasses supply the actual logic for each phase; the engine itself only holds and drives an `Application*` (or `shared_ptr<Application>`), knowing nothing about the specific integration being hosted.

**Public API:**

- `OnStart() → void` — Called once by the engine immediately after the application object is installed and before the first update tick. Subclasses perform one-time initialisation here (loading config, opening connections, initialising embedded subsystems). If initialisation cannot succeed, the subclass must populate `m_FatalStartupMessage` before returning so callers can surface the failure cleanly.

- `OnUpdate() → void` — Called repeatedly by the engine's main loop while `IsFinished()` returns `false`. Contains per-tick work: polling queues, dispatching pending tasks, refreshing state. Must not block indefinitely; the engine's responsiveness depends on this returning promptly.

- `OnEvent(std::shared_ptr<Event>&) → void` — Delivers a single engine event to the application. The `Event` is passed by reference-to-shared-ptr, allowing the callee to copy the pointer and extend lifetime if asynchronous handling is needed. Subclasses inspect the concrete `Event` subtype (via the interface declared in `event/event.h`) and react accordingly.

- `OnShutdown() → void` — Called once when the run-loop terminates (after `IsFinished()` returns `true` or the engine initiates teardown). Subclasses release resources, flush buffers, and close external connections here.

- `IsFinished() const → bool` — Queried by the engine's run-loop each iteration. When it returns `true` the engine stops calling `OnUpdate()` and proceeds to `OnShutdown()`. Subclasses own the logic that sets this condition (e.g., a stop signal received, a task queue drained, or a fatal error detected).

- `GetFatalStartupMessage() const → std::string const&` — Returns the error string written into `m_FatalStartupMessage` by the subclass. The engine checks this after `OnStart()` returns to decide whether to abort before entering the update loop. An empty string means no fatal error occurred.

**Collaborators:**

- `event/event.h` — Pulls in the `Event` base class (and presumably its type hierarchy) used by `OnEvent`. The concrete event types dispatched through this interface are defined elsewhere in the `event/` subsystem.
- Engine run-loop (not shown) — The code that instantiates a concrete `Application`, calls `OnStart`, loops on `OnUpdate`/`OnEvent`, and finally calls `OnShutdown`. That caller must also read `GetFatalStartupMessage()` after `OnStart()` to handle startup failures.

**Threading / lifetime:**

- The class carries a `virtual` destructor and is designed for heap allocation; the engine is expected to manage the concrete object through a pointer (likely `std::unique_ptr` or `std::shared_ptr`).
- No internal synchronisation is present. All four lifecycle methods (`OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, `OnEvent`, `OnShutdown`) are assumed to be called from the same thread — the engine's main/run-loop thread. Subclasses that spawn background threads must handle their own synchronisation before these methods return.
- `OnEvent` receives a `std::shared_ptr<Event>` by reference. Copying it inside the handler to keep the event alive across thread boundaries is safe, but the base class imposes no lifetime guarantee beyond the duration of the call.

**Notes:**

- `m_FatalStartupMessage` is the only formal error-reporting channel between `OnStart` and the engine. There is no return code or exception contract on `OnStart`; a subclass that throws or returns without setting this field leaves the engine with no structured error to report.
- The `IsFinished()` predicate is the sole mechanism for the application to request orderly termination. There is no engine-side forced-stop API visible in this header; if the engine needs to abort externally, it must either destroy the object or have its own complementary mechanism.
- Because `OnUpdate` is called in a tight loop, subclasses should avoid expensive blocking operations there or delegate them to background threads and consume results non-blockingly during the update tick.

### 02_doc_application_assistant_assistantController_h

<a id="02-doc-application-assistant-assistantcontroller-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/02_doc_application_assistant_assistantController_h/docu.output.md`



## AssistantController

**Role:** `AssistantController` owns the `/ws/assistant` WebSocket endpoint and implements the full conversational AI assistant feature. It accepts JSON-framed WebSocket messages from browser clients, routes them to either slash-command handlers or an asynchronous multi-step AI call pipeline, and delivers all responses back through a thread-safe pending-message queue that is drained into a single batch envelope on every `OnMessage` call. Within the AI call pipeline it orchestrates a tool-use loop (up to `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS = 10` rounds) that parses `<tool_call>` blocks from model output, optionally gates dangerous tools behind a blocking user-approval handshake, executes tools via `ToolRegistry`, accumulates results, and re-submits to the model until no further tool calls appear or the iteration cap is hit. Persistent state is maintained through `AssistantSession` (per-conversation JSONL files under `assistant/sessions/`), `MemoryStore` (a JSON file at `assistant/memory.json`), and `WorkspaceIndexer` (a file-summary index under `assistant/index`).

---

**Public API:**

- `AssistantController()` — Constructs the object. Initialises `MemoryStore` and `WorkspaceIndexer` with their absolute on-disk paths, wires `ToolRegistry` with pointers to both and with a `MakeToolAiCall` callback, then calls `m_WorkspaceIndexer.ScanWorkspace()` synchronously. Logs counts of loaded memories, indexed files, and cached summaries.

- `~AssistantController()` — Calls `Shutdown()`. Safe to call multiple times (idempotent via `m_ShuttingDown` exchange).

- `OnOpen(conn)` — Registers a new WebSocket connection in `m_Clients` and inserts a default `ClientState` entry. Called by the WebServer route handler on connection upgrade. Thread-safe via `m_ClientsMutex`.

- `OnClose(conn)` — Removes the connection and its `ClientState` from both maps. Thread-safe via `m_ClientsMutex`.

- `OnMessage(conn, data)` — Entry point for all inbound WebSocket frames. Parses the JSON `type` field with simdjson and dispatches to the appropriate private handler. Supported types: `ping`, `user_message`, `command`, `list_sessions`, `resume_session`, `new_session`, `approval_response`, `get_history`, `completion_request`. Always calls `DrainPendingMessages()` before returning, ensuring responses queued during handling are flushed in the same call-stack turn.

- `DrainPendingMessages()` — Swaps `m_PendingMessages` under `m_PendingMutex`, snapshot-copies `m_Clients` under `m_ClientsMutex`, packs all pending JSON strings into a single `{"type":"batch","messages":[…]}` envelope, and calls `send_text` on every connected client. Called both from `OnMessage` and can be called externally (the header comment says it follows the same pattern as `WebServer::DrainPendingBroadcasts`).

- `Shutdown()` — Idempotent (first call only acts). Sets `m_ShuttingDown`, notifies all `PendingApproval` condition variables so background threads unblock, closes all assistant WebSocket connections with `"server shutting down"`, then joins every thread in `m_BackgroundThreads`.

- `SetWorkflowRegistry(registry)` — Stores the pointer and forwards it to `m_ToolRegistry.SetWorkflowRegistry`. Safe to call before or after construction but should be called before any AI turn starts.

- `SetWorkflowRuntimeManager(runtimeManager)` — Same pattern as above; also forwarded to `m_ToolRegistry`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **`AssistantSession`** (`assistant/assistantSession.h`) — Per-session turn storage. `GetSession` lazily loads a session from a `.jsonl` file in `assistant/sessions/`; `CreateSession` generates a new one. Sessions are stored as `unique_ptr` values in `m_Sessions` and accessed directly by raw pointer within the scope of a lock.

- **`MemoryStore`** (`assistant/assistantMemory.h`) — Persistent key-value store injected into `ToolRegistry`. Also queried directly by `HandleMemoryCommand` (`/memory`, `/memory clear`) and by `RunAiCallAsync` for relevance-based recall via `GetRelevant`.

- **`WorkspaceIndexer`** (`assistant/workspaceIndexer.h`) — File summary index injected into `ToolRegistry`. Queried in `RunAiCallAsync` via `GetRelevantFiles` to inject file summaries into the AI context. Also exposed through `/index` and `/index rescan` slash commands.

- **`ToolRegistry`** (`assistant/assistantTools.h`) — Owns all registered tool definitions and executes `ToolCall` objects. `AssistantController` calls `BuildToolDescriptions()` for the system prompt, `ParseToolCalls()` (static) to extract calls from model output, `GetToolDefs()` to check `requiresApproval`, and `Execute()` to run a call. The controller feeds it `WorkflowRegistry*`, `WorkflowRuntimeManager*`, `MemoryStore*`, `WorkspaceIndexer*`, and a `SetAiCallFn` callback.

- **`ContextAssembler`** (`assistant/contextAssembler.h`) — Builds the `{stng, task, cntx, prob}` prompt struct from session turns, user message, and tool descriptions. Called once per AI turn; the `stng`/`task` fields are replaced on the final tool iteration to inhibit further tool calls.

- **`AiRequestPool`** (`workflow/aiRequestPool.h`) — The sole AI dispatch mechanism. `RunSingleAiCall` submits an `AiInvocation` envelope and blocks on a `std::future<AiReply>` with a 120-second timeout. Obtained via `JarvisAgent::GetAiRequestPool()`.

- **`AiInvocation` / `AiReply`** (`workflow/aiInvocation.h`, `workflow/aiReply.h`) — Data-transfer types wrapping the queue-file pipeline. The queue folder is written to disk under `<queueBase>/_assistant/call_<timestamp>_<seq>/` before submission for replay/debug purposes.

- **`WorkflowRegistry`** / **`WorkflowRuntimeManager`** (`workflow/workflowRegistry.h`, `workflow/workflowRuntimeManager.h`) — Optional; used by `/status`, `/runs`, and tab-completion of workflow IDs. Null-checked before every use.

- **`PythonEnginePool`** (`python/pythonEnginePool.h`) — Queried in `/status` for engine count; no AI calls go through it directly from this class.

- **`JarvisAgent`** / **`Core`** (`jarvisAgent.h`, `engine.h`) — Global singletons accessed as `App::g_App` and `Core::g_Core`. Used to obtain `AiRequestPool`, `PythonEnginePool`, `GetStartupTime()`, `GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()`, and the queue folder path from config.

- **Crow** (`crow.h`) — `crow::websocket::connection` is the raw WebSocket handle. Only `send_text` and `close` are called on it.

- **simdjson** — Used exclusively in `OnMessage` for parsing inbound JSON.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `AssistantController` is intended to live for the process lifetime, owned by the WebServer or application root.

- **`OnOpen` / `OnClose` / `OnMessage` / `DrainPendingMessages`** are called on the Crow I/O thread(s) and must not block. `OnMessage` is the only path that dispatches to background threads.

- **`RunAiCallAsync`** spawns a `std::thread` appended to `m_BackgroundThreads` (protected by `m_ThreadsMutex`). These threads call `QueueMessage` (locks `m_PendingMutex`) and `GetSession` (locks `m_SessionsMutex`). `JoinFinishedThreads` is called before each new spawn but is effectively a no-op in the current implementation (it cannot determine completion without blocking); authoritative cleanup happens in `Shutdown()`.

- **Approval blocking:** `RequestToolApproval` is called from a background AI thread. It publishes an `approval_request` message and then blocks on `PendingApproval::cv.wait_for` for up to `APPROVAL_TIMEOUT_S = 60` seconds. The Crow I/O thread delivers the `approval_response` message through `OnMessage → HandleApprovalResponse`, which locks the `PendingApproval::mutex` directly (not `m_ApprovalsMutex`) and calls `notify_one`. The `shared_ptr<PendingApproval>` is held by both the background thread and `m_PendingApprovals`, so the object outlives the map entry safely.

- **`m_Sessions`** values are raw pointers returned to callers; these are valid only while `m_SessionsMutex` is not held by a concurrent `CreateSession` that could reallocate the map. In practice all callers release the lock before using the returned pointer, relying on the `unique_ptr` remaining stable inside the map. Do not store the raw pointer across any call that may trigger session creation on another thread.

- **`m_ShuttingDown`** is `std::atomic<bool>`; background threads poll it at the start of each loop iteration and at the start of `MakeToolAiCall`. `QueueMessage` and session methods do not check it; messages queued after shutdown are silently dropped because `DrainPendingMessages` will find no clients.

---

**Notes:**

- **Queue-file pipeline side effect:** `RunSingleAiCall` writes four prompt files (`STNG_settings.txt`, `TASK_instructions.txt`, `CNTX_context.txt`, `prob.txt`) to `<queueBase>/_assistant/…` on every AI call, including tool-initiated summarisation calls. These directories are not automatically cleaned up; operators should monitor disk usage.

- **Tool loop termination:** On the iteration immediately before `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS - 1`, the PROB is rewritten with a `[SYSTEM: … Do NOT call any more tools.]` directive, and on the final iteration `stngNoTools` (built without tool descriptions) replaces the system prompt and the task is overridden to forbid further tool calls. This dual mechanism is intended to ensure a final textual answer.

- **Loop detection:** Per AI turn, `toolCallSignatureCounts` tracks `tool_name(sorted_key=value,…)` signatures. A signature called more than three times injects a synthetic `<tool_result status="error">` with a loop warning and skips execution; this also forces a final-answer PROB on the next iteration.

- **False-action detection:** When the first iteration produces no tool calls but the response text contains phrases like `"proceeding to execute"` or `"will run the command"`, a UTF-8 warning symbol and note are appended to the response before delivery.

- **Response relevance validation (`ValidateResponse`):** Applied only when no tools were used. It (1) tokenises user and response text into stopword-filtered keywords and appends a warning if fewer than 30% of user keywords appear in the response, and (2) extracts file paths with known extensions from the response and appends a `"not found in workspace"` note for each path that does not exist under `GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()`.

- **Tab completion (`completion_request`):** Supports three `kind` values — `slash` (matches against a hardcoded list of command strings), `workflow` (matches registered workflow IDs via `WorkflowRegistry::GetWorkflowIds`), and `history` (matches user turns from all in-memory sessions). An empty `kind` runs all three. Maximum 10 candidates are returned.

- **`get_history` deduplication:** Iterates sessions newest-first (session IDs are timestamp-based), then turns newest-first within each session, deduplicating on exact text equality. The result is a flat list of up to `maxEntries` unique user messages, not full turn pairs.

- **Batch delivery:** All messages to clients are packed into a single `{"type":"batch","messages":[…]}` JSON array per `DrainPendingMessages` call. Clients must unwrap the batch envelope; individual message types are never sent as top-level frames.

- **Windows compatibility:** `tail` log reading is wrapped through `bash -c "…"` on Windows (requires MSYS2/Git Bash). Platform shims for `gmtime_r`/`gmtime_s` and `popen`/`pclose` are applied inline.


---

### 03_doc_application_assistant_assistantMemory_h

<a id="03-doc-application-assistant-assistantmemory-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/03_doc_application_assistant_assistantMemory_h/docu.output.md`



## MemoryStore (`assistant/assistantMemory.h` / `assistantMemory.cpp`)

**Role:** `MemoryStore` is a persistent, thread-safe key-value memory store for the AI assistant subsystem. It maintains a flat list of `MemoryEntry` records in an in-process `std::vector<MemoryEntry>` and synchronises that list to a JSON file on disk after every mutating operation. Its primary job is to give the assistant a cross-session memory layer: facts, preferences, or context snippets are saved under short string keys, can be queried by keyword, and are ranked by relevance before being injected into prompts via `GetRelevant`.

---

**Public API:**

- `MemoryStore(std::filesystem::path storePath)` — Constructs the store and immediately calls `LoadFromDisk()`, deserialising the JSON array at `storePath` into `m_Entries`. If the file does not exist or is empty the store starts with zero entries; parse failures are logged and silently ignored rather than thrown. The caller owns the resulting object.

- `Save(key, value, tags, sessionId) → std::string` — Upserts a memory keyed on `key`. If an entry with the same key already exists its `value`, `tags`, `createdAt`, and optionally `sourceSessionId` are overwritten in-place; the existing `id` is preserved. If no match exists a new entry is appended with a freshly generated `id`. In both cases `SaveToDisk()` is called before returning. Returns the entry's `id` (format `mem_<16-hex-digits>`).

- `Delete(key) → bool` — Removes the first entry whose `key` matches, persists the updated list, and returns `true`. Returns `false` without touching disk if no match is found. Deletion is by key, not by id.

- `Recall(query) → std::vector<MemoryEntry>` — Full-text search across `key`, `value`, and `tags` of every stored entry. The query string is tokenised into lowercase words (alphanumeric, `_`, `-`; tokens shorter than two characters are discarded). Each entry is scored via `ScoreMatch` and only entries with score > 0 are returned, ordered descending by score. If `query` is empty or produces no tokens, all entries are returned unscored. Returned entries are copies.

- `ListAll() → std::vector<MemoryEntry>` — Returns a copy of the entire `m_Entries` vector with no filtering or scoring. Intended for administrative display; the comment says key + createdAt are the interesting fields for brief display, but the full `MemoryEntry` struct is returned.

- `GetRelevant(userMessage, maxResults = 5) → std::vector<MemoryEntry>` — Thin wrapper around `Recall`: calls `Recall(userMessage)` and truncates the result to `maxResults` entries. This is the primary entry point for prompt-injection use cases. Note that `GetRelevant` does not itself acquire the mutex; it delegates to `Recall`, which does — see threading notes.

- `ClearAll()` — Empties `m_Entries`, persists the resulting empty JSON array, and logs the event.

- `Size() → size_t` — Returns the number of stored entries under a lock. Accurate at the moment of the call only.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **`engine.h`** — Provides `LOG_APP_INFO` and `LOG_APP_WARN` macros used throughout for diagnostics. No other engine types are used in this file.
- **simdjson (`simdjson/simdjson.h`)** — Used exclusively in `LoadFromDisk()` via its `ondemand` API (`ondemand::parser`, `padded_string`, `ondemand::document`, `ondemand::array`, `ondemand::object`). simdjson is read-only here; serialisation is handled by a hand-rolled `ostringstream`-based writer with the local helper `JsonEscapeMem`.
- **`MemoryEntry`** (same header) — Plain aggregate struct; no virtual methods, no ownership semantics. All fields are `std::string` or `std::vector<std::string>`.
- **Callers** — Typically the assistant session logic, which calls `Save` when the model produces a memory-worthy fact and `GetRelevant` before constructing a prompt.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- All public methods acquire `m_Mutex` (declared `mutable`) via `std::lock_guard` before touching `m_Entries` or calling `SaveToDisk`.
- `GetRelevant` calls `Recall`, which acquires the mutex internally. Do not call `GetRelevant` while already holding `m_Mutex` from the same thread — that would deadlock. In practice this is not an issue because all locking is done inside the class, never exposed to callers.
- `SaveToDisk` is called synchronously on the calling thread inside the lock, so every mutating call blocks until the file write completes. There is no async flush or write queue.
- `GenerateId` uses a function-local `static std::mt19937_64` seeded once at first call from `steady_clock`. This static is not guarded by the class mutex; concurrent first-calls from multiple threads before C++11 would race, but under C++11 and later static-local initialisation is thread-safe by the standard.
- The `MemoryStore` object has no internal threads of its own. Lifetime is entirely caller-managed; no shared ownership or weak references are involved.

---

**Notes:**

- **Key uniqueness is the identity axis, not `id`.** `Save` deduplicates on `key`; `Delete` also operates on `key`. The `id` field is generated once at creation and never changes on update, but callers have no delete-by-id path.
- **`ScoreMatch` double-weights the key field** by concatenating `entry.key` to the haystack twice. A query word that appears in the key therefore contributes at least 2 points versus 1 for a match in `value` or tags.
- **Minimum query token length is 2 characters.** Single-character words are silently dropped during tokenisation in both `Recall` and, transitively, `GetRelevant`.
- **`LoadFromDisk` does not lock.** It is called only from the constructor before the object is published to any other thread, so this is safe, but it means the constructor must complete before the object is shared.
- **Serialisation is manual, not via a JSON library.** `SaveToDisk` builds JSON with `ostringstream` and `JsonEscapeMem`. `JsonEscapeMem` handles `"`, `\`, `\n`, `\r`, `\t` but does **not** encode non-ASCII bytes or control characters below 0x20 (other than the four listed). Entries containing such bytes will produce non-conformant JSON that simdjson may reject on the next load.
- **Write failures are non-fatal.** Both `LoadFromDisk` and `SaveToDisk` log a warning and return if file I/O fails; no exception is thrown and no error code is surfaced to the caller. A failed `Save` still returns the id and updates in-memory state even if the disk write silently failed.
- **No file locking.** If two processes (or two `MemoryStore` instances pointing at the same path) run concurrently, their writes will race at the OS level; there is no advisory lock or atomic rename.


---

### 04_doc_application_assistant_assistantSession_h

<a id="04-doc-application-assistant-assistantsession-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/04_doc_application_assistant_assistantSession_h/docu.output.md`



## AssistantSession

**Role:** `AssistantSession` manages a single AI conversation session within the j9t workflow engine. It owns an in-memory ordered list of conversation turns (`AssistantTurn` structs carrying role, text, and ISO 8601 timestamp) and mirrors every new turn to an append-only JSONL file stored under a caller-supplied `sessionsDir`. The class supports both creating brand-new sessions and resuming previously persisted sessions by session ID. It provides a token-budget-aware window into recent history for use when constructing LLM prompt context, and a static directory scanner so callers can enumerate all available sessions.

**Public API:**

- `AssistantSession(sessionsDir) → (constructor)` — Creates a fresh session. Generates a unique session ID of the form `sess_<epoch_ms>_<counter>`, records the creation time as `m_StartedAt`, and calls `std::filesystem::create_directories` on `sessionsDir` (ignoring errors via `error_code`). The backing `.jsonl` file is not created until the first turn is written.

- `AssistantSession(sessionsDir, sessionId) → (constructor)` — Resumes an existing session by reading `sessionsDir/<sessionId>.jsonl` line-by-line through `LoadFromFile`. Sets `m_StartedAt` from the timestamp of the first loaded turn, or to the current time if the file is empty or missing. Logs the turn count on success.

- `GetSessionId() const → std::string const&` — Returns the session ID string (e.g. `sess_1714000000000_0`).

- `GetStartedAt() const → std::string const&` — Returns the ISO 8601 UTC timestamp recorded when the session was created or resumed.

- `GetTurnCount() const → size_t` — Returns the number of turns currently in memory, under lock.

- `AddUserMessage(text)` — Constructs an `AssistantTurn` with `role="user"`, the supplied text, and the current UTC time, then delegates to `AppendTurn`. Acquires the mutex and appends both to `m_Turns` and to the JSONL file via `std::ios::app`. Logs a warning if the file cannot be opened; the turn is still recorded in memory.

- `AddAssistantMessage(text)` — Identical to `AddUserMessage` but sets `role="assistant"`.

- `GetRecentTurns(maxTokens) const → std::vector<AssistantTurn>` — Walks `m_Turns` in reverse (newest-first), accumulating turns whose estimated token cost (`text.size() / 4`) fits within `maxTokens`. Stops when the next turn would exceed the budget *and* at least one turn has already been collected. Returns results in chronological order (oldest first). Acquires the mutex for the full traversal.

- `GetAllTurns() const → std::vector<AssistantTurn>` — Returns a copy of the complete in-memory turn list under lock. Intended for session-summary or export use cases rather than prompt construction.

- `ListSessions(sessionsDir) → std::vector<std::string>` (static) — Iterates `sessionsDir` with `std::filesystem::directory_iterator`, collects the stem of every `.jsonl` file as a session ID, and sorts them in descending lexicographic order. Because session IDs embed millisecond timestamps, descending lexicographic order is equivalent to newest-first. Returns an empty vector if the directory does not exist.

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` — Provides `LOG_APP_INFO` and `LOG_APP_WARN` macros used for operational tracing. No other coupling to the broader engine is present in this file.
- `assistant/assistantSession.h` — The header that declares `AssistantTurn`, `AssistantSession`, and their members; this `.cpp` is its sole implementation unit.
- The anonymous-namespace helpers `JsonEscape` and `ExtractJsonString` are file-local. They form a deliberately minimal JSONL serializer/deserializer that handles the six standard JSON escape sequences plus `\uXXXX` for control characters below `0x20`. `ExtractJsonString` is not a general JSON parser — it locates only string values matching the pattern `"key":"value"` and will silently produce empty results for any other JSON structure.

**Threading / lifetime:**

- All public methods except the two constructors are guarded by `m_Mutex` (declared `mutable` to allow locking inside `const` methods).
- The constructors are **not** thread-safe relative to each other or to any other method; callers must ensure construction is complete before sharing the object across threads.
- `LoadFromFile` is called from the resuming constructor under the same mutex, so it is safe if the object is not yet shared, but calling it from any other context after construction would deadlock because `AppendTurn` and `GetRecentTurns` also acquire `m_Mutex` (non-recursively).
- `AssistantSession` is not copyable or movable by default (the `std::mutex` member suppresses those operations). Callers must manage it via pointer or reference, typically through `std::unique_ptr`.
- `ListSessions` is a static method with no shared state; it is safe to call concurrently from multiple threads.

**Notes:**

- The token estimate of `chars / 4` is a rough heuristic. For non-ASCII or code-heavy content it can significantly undercount tokens, potentially allowing more turns into the context window than the target LLM model expects.
- `GetRecentTurns` has an edge-case behaviour: if the very first turn evaluated (the newest) already exceeds `maxTokens`, it is still included, because the budget break condition requires `!result.empty()` to be true. This means the method always returns at least one turn regardless of budget.
- The JSONL format uses the key `ts` for timestamps on disk, but `AssistantTurn::timestamp` is the in-memory field name. `LoadFromFile` correctly maps `ts` → `timestamp` via `ExtractJsonString(line, "ts")`.
- Session ID uniqueness relies on combining millisecond wall-clock time with a process-wide `std::atomic<uint32_t>` counter. IDs are not globally unique across process restarts if two processes start within the same millisecond and both produce counter value `0`.
- File I/O failures during `AppendTurn` are non-fatal and only emit a warning log. The turn is retained in memory, so an in-process caller will see consistent state, but the JSONL file and the in-memory vector can silently diverge if writes fail repeatedly.
- `ExtractJsonString` does not handle `\uXXXX` escape sequences during *reading* (only during writing via `JsonEscape`). Any turn text that was serialised containing a control character below `0x20` will be read back with the literal text `uXXXX` rather than the original character.


---

### 05_doc_application_assistant_assistantTools_h

<a id="05-doc-application-assistant-assistanttools-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/05_doc_application_assistant_assistantTools_h/docu.output.md`



## ToolRegistry (`assistant/assistantTools.h` + `assistantTools.cpp`)

**Role:** `ToolRegistry` is the AI assistant's complete tool execution layer. It owns the canonical list of every tool the AI can call (grouped into three access levels: L1 read-only system queries, L2 read-only file/memory operations, and L3 mutating operations that require user approval), provides the text block injected into the system prompt, parses `<tool_call>` XML-wrapped JSON blocks out of raw AI response text, and dispatches parsed calls to the correct C++ implementation. It is the single choke-point between an AI-generated text response and any side effect in the j9t runtime — file writes, workflow starts/stops, shell execution, and JCWF development all flow through here.

---

**Public API:**

- `ToolRegistry()` — Constructor. Registers all tools by populating `m_ToolDefs` (ordered `vector<ToolDef>`) and `m_ToolFns` (dispatch map `string → ToolFn`). No external dependencies are needed at construction time; collaborators are injected separately via the setters below.

- `SetWorkflowRegistry(WorkflowRegistry*)` — Injects the registry used by `list_workflows`, `run_workflow`, `jcwf_*` tools. Caller retains ownership; pointer is stored as a raw non-owning pointer.

- `SetWorkflowRuntimeManager(WorkflowRuntimeManager*)` — Injects the runtime manager used by run-status queries and pause/resume/stop/clean control tools. Same raw-pointer ownership model.

- `SetMemoryStore(MemoryStore*)` — Injects persistent memory backing for `save_memory`, `recall_memory`, `list_memories`, `delete_memory`.

- `SetWorkspaceIndexer(WorkspaceIndexer*)` — Injects the workspace index used by `get_file_summary` and `get_folder_summary` to read/write cached file summaries.

- `SetAiCallFn(AiCallFn fn)` — Injects a blocking AI call callback (`bool(systemPrompt, userPrompt, &outResponse, &outError)`). Used by `get_file_summary` to generate summaries on cache miss and by `jcwf_generate` / `jcwf_fix_task` to invoke generative AI. The callback must be thread-safe if `Execute` is ever called from a non-UI thread.

- `GetToolDefs() const → vector<ToolDef> const&` — Returns the ordered list of tool definitions. Callers use this to know which tools exist; the ordering matches the system prompt output.

- `BuildToolDescriptions() const → string` — Produces the human-readable tool catalogue block that is embedded verbatim in the system prompt. Each entry includes the tool name, whether approval is auto-handled, its description, and its argument description.

- `Execute(ToolCall const& call) → ToolResult` — Dispatches a parsed tool call. Looks up `call.name` in `m_ToolFns`; returns a `ToolResult{name, false, "Unknown tool: ..."}` if not found. Catches any `std::exception` thrown by an implementation and converts it to a failed `ToolResult`. Does not itself enforce approval gating — that is the caller's responsibility before invoking `Execute`.

- `ParseToolCalls(string const& responseText, string& outCleanText) → vector<ToolCall>` *(static)* — Scans `responseText` for `<tool_call>…</tool_call>` delimiters and extracts JSON objects from each block. The response text with all recognized `<tool_call>` blocks removed is written to `outCleanText`. If a closing `</tool_call>` tag is absent, the parser falls back to brace-counting to extract a complete JSON object. Unparseable blocks are replaced with a `[Failed to parse tool call: ...]` inline note in `outCleanText`. Returns an empty vector if no tool calls are found.

---

**Individual Tool Implementations (private, dispatched via `Execute`):**

*L1 — Read-only system status:*
- `ExecGetSystemStatus` — Queries `App::g_App` (global `JarvisAgent*`) for version string, uptime, workflow count, active/completed/failed run counters, and Python engine pool size. Returns a plain-text status report.
- `ExecListWorkflows` — Calls `WorkflowRegistry::GetWorkflowIds()` and `GetWorkflow()` for each; returns IDs and labels.
- `ExecGetRunStatus(run_id)` — Calls `WorkflowRuntimeManager::TryGetRunById`; returns run state, timestamps, and per-task states including last error messages.
- `ExecListRecentRuns([count])` — Combines `GetActiveRunsSnapshot()` and `GetLastRunsSnapshot()` (one entry per workflow).
- `ExecGetTaskOutput(run_id, task_id)` — Extracts `m_CapturedStdout` and `m_CapturedStderr` from a `TaskInstanceState`; truncates each to `kMaxToolOutputSize` (4 KB).
- `ExecRunWorkflow(workflow_id)` — Validates the workflow exists then calls `WorkflowRuntimeManager::EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId`; returns the assigned run ID. Marked `requiresApproval = true`.

*L2 — Read-only file and memory operations:*
- `ExecReadFile(path, [start], [end])` — Reads a file relative to CWD, emitting line numbers. Hard limits: 100 KB file size, 200 lines per call (configurable via start/end), 8 KB output cap via `TruncateOutput`. Enforces deny-list via `IsPathDenied`, rejects absolute paths and `..` traversal.
- `ExecSearchFiles(query, [glob])` — Shells out via `popen` to `rg` with a `grep` fallback; excludes `node_modules`, `.git`, `bin-int`, `vendor`, `bin`. Output capped at `kMaxToolOutputSize`.
- `ExecListFiles([path], [depth])` — Shells out via `popen` to `find`; depth clamped to 1–5, results limited to 100 lines, same exclusion list as search.
- `ExecGetLogTail([lines])` — *(not registered as an AI tool — intentionally removed from `m_ToolDefs` to avoid AI noise; implementation exists for potential future reuse)* — Reads `log/log.txt` tail via `popen`/`tail`.
- `ExecSaveMemory(key, value, [tags])` — Calls `MemoryStore::Save`; tags are comma-split and whitespace-trimmed. Upserts on duplicate key.
- `ExecRecallMemory(query)` — Calls `MemoryStore::Recall(query)`; returns scored results.
- `ExecListMemories()` — Calls `MemoryStore::ListAll()`; returns keys and creation timestamps only.
- `ExecDeleteMemory(key)` — Calls `MemoryStore::Delete`; returns false result if key is absent.
- `ExecGetFileSummary(path)` — Checks `WorkspaceIndexer::GetFileSummary` first; on miss reads up to 32 768 bytes via `WorkspaceIndexer::ReadFileContent` and makes a blocking AI call via `m_AiCallFn`. Caches the result via `WorkspaceIndexer::SetFileSummary`. Output limit 4 KB.
- `ExecGetFolderSummary(path)` — Iterates `WorkspaceIndexer::GetAllEntries()` filtering by path prefix; returns only entries that already have cached summaries and counts how many are pending. Output limit 8 KB.

*L3 — Mutating tools (all `requiresApproval = true`):*
- `ExecRunShell(command, [cwd])` — **POSIX path:** `fork`/`exec` via `/bin/sh -c`; child placed in new process group via `setpgid(0,0)`; parent uses `poll` with 200 ms slices up to a 30-second wall-clock timeout; on timeout sends `SIGTERM` then `SIGKILL` to the entire process group. **Windows path:** `CreateProcess` routing to PowerShell or bash depending on `ShellTaskExecutor::GetWindowsShell()`; a reader `std::thread` drains the anonymous pipe; `WaitForSingleObject` enforces the 30-second timeout; `TerminateProcess` + 5-second grace follow on timeout. Output capped at 16 KB on both platforms. Blocklist in `IsCommandBlocked` rejects patterns like `rm -rf /`, `mkfs`, fork-bombs, `shutdown`, etc.
- `ExecWriteFile(path, content)` — Creates parent directories; backs up existing file to `.bak`; atomically writes via `.tmp` + `fs::rename`. Enforces deny-list, `..` traversal, and absolute-path checks.
- `ExecEditFile(path, old_text, new_text)` — Reads the entire file; requires `old_text` to match exactly once (returns error if zero or multiple matches); performs string replacement; backs up to `.bak`; atomically writes via `.tmp` + `fs::rename`. Reports replacement line number, old/new line counts, and old/new byte counts.
- `ExecWorkflowPause(run_id)` — Requires run to be in `Running` state; calls `WorkflowRuntimeManager::RequestPauseRun`.
- `ExecWorkflowResume(run_id)` — Requires run to be in `Paused` state; calls `RequestResumeRun`.
- `ExecWorkflowStop(run_id)` — Accepts `Running`, `Paused`, or `Pending` states; calls `RequestStopRun`.
- `ExecWorkflowClean(workflow_id)` — Calls `WorkflowRuntimeManager::CleanWorkflow`; the runtime manager enforces that no active run exists for the workflow.
- `ExecGetDashboardStatus()` — Aggregates version, uptime, all registered workflows, all active runs, run counters, last-run-per-workflow, and Python engine pool state. Not restricted to approval despite being in the L3 section; `requiresApproval = false`.
- `ExecJcwfRead(workflow_id)` — Resolves workflow path via `ResolveWorkflowPath`; reads root canvas JSON from the `.jcwf` zip container via `ReadJcwfContent` / `JcwfContainer::ReadFile`. Output limit 16 KB.
- `ExecJcwfExplain(workflow_id)` — Pulls the parsed `WorkflowDef` from `WorkflowRegistry::GetWorkflow` and formats tasks, triggers, dataflow edges, and controlflow edges as human-readable text. Output limit 8 KB.
- `ExecJcwfValidate(workflow_id)` — Reads canvas JSON then calls `AiJcwfService::ValidateJcwf`; passes `ScriptRegistry*` obtained from `App::g_App`. Reports valid/invalid and the full validation summary.
- `ExecJcwfReadPlan(workflow_id)` — Reads `workflows/<id>.plan.md`; returns a prompt to create one if absent.
- `ExecJcwfWritePlan(workflow_id, content)` — Atomically writes `workflows/<id>.plan.md` via `.tmp` + `fs::rename`.
- `ExecJcwfGenerate(workflow_id)` — Reads plan, makes a blocking AI call with a detailed system prompt that enforces the JCWF task-as-object (not array) rule and the ai_call output-slot convention; strips markdown fences from response; validates via `AiJcwfService::ValidateJcwf`; creates `workflows/<id>/global.json` + `workflows/<id>/<id>.json`; packs via `JcwfContainer::Pack`. Backs up existing `.jcwf` to `.bak`. Logs via `LOG_APP_INFO`.
- `ExecJcwfFixTask(workflow_id, task_id, instructions)` — Reads current canvas JSON, verifies `task_id` appears as a quoted string, makes a blocking AI call instructing it to return the complete updated JSON, strips markdown fences, validates, then writes back via `WriteJcwfContent` (which extracts the zip to a directory, replaces the canvas JSON, and repacks). Backs up to `.bak`.
- `ExecJcwfWriteScript(path, content, type)` — Validates `path` starts with `scripts/`; for `type="shell"` enforces shebang (`#!/`) presence and `set -euo pipefail`; atomically writes; calls `fs::permissions` to add execute bits on POSIX for shell scripts.

---

**Collaborators:**

| Collaborator | How Used |
|---|---|
| `WorkflowRegistry` | Workflow enumeration, path resolution, parsed `WorkflowDef` retrieval |
| `WorkflowRuntimeManager` | Run enqueueing, status queries, pause/resume/stop/clean, active/last-run snapshots and counters |
| `MemoryStore` | Persistent key-value memory: save, recall (scored), list, delete |
| `WorkspaceIndexer` | File summary cache read/write; `ReadFileContent` static helper |
| `AiJcwfService` | JCWF JSON validation (`ValidateJcwf`) |
| `JcwfContainer` | `.jcwf` zip container list-entries, read-file, extract, and pack |
| `JarvisAgent` (`App::g_App`) | Global application singleton; supplies `GetStartupTime()`, `GetPythonEnginePool()`, `GetScriptRegistry()` |
| `PythonEnginePool` | Engine count reporting in status tools |
| `ShellTaskExecutor` | Windows-only: `GetWindowsShell()` selects PowerShell vs bash for `run_shell` |
| `WorkflowValidator` / `workflowTypes.h` | `WorkflowRunState`, `TaskInstanceStateKind`, `TaskType`, `WorkflowTriggerType` enums used for display |
| `AiCallFn` callback | Blocking AI call for summary generation, JCWF generation, and task fixing |

---

**Threading / Lifetime:**

- `ToolRegistry` does not own any of its injected collaborators. All four collaborators (`WorkflowRegistry`, `WorkflowRuntimeManager`, `MemoryStore`, `WorkspaceIndexer`) are raw non-owning pointers. Callers must ensure all injected objects outlive the `ToolRegistry` instance.
- `ToolRegistry` itself has no internal synchronisation. `Execute` and `ParseToolCalls` must not be called concurrently from multiple threads without external locking, except for `ParseToolCalls` which is `static` and purely reads its input string — it is safe to call from any thread.
- The `AiCallFn` callback injected via `SetAiCallFn` is invoked synchronously inside `Execute`. If the AI backend does blocking HTTP, `Execute` will block the calling thread for the duration of that call. Callers in the UI layer must dispatch to a background thread before calling `Execute` on tools that trigger AI calls (`get_file_summary`, `jcwf_generate`, `jcwf_fix_task`).
- The `run_shell` POSIX implementation uses `fork`/`exec` and manages its own child lifecycle entirely within the `Execute` call. The Windows implementation spawns a `std::thread` reader that is `join`ed before `Execute` returns. Both variants are fully synchronous from the caller's perspective.
- `App::g_App` is accessed as a raw global pointer in several tool implementations. This is safe only if `Execute` is called while the application is fully initialised.

---

**Notes:**

- `get_log_tail` is intentionally **not registered** in `m_ToolDefs` or `m_ToolFns` despite having a full implementation. The constructor comment explains the reason: every AI call generates new log lines, so asking the AI to tail the log produces AI-internal noise rather than pre-existing content. The `/log [N]` slash command is the intended path.
- The `requiresApproval` flag on a `ToolDef` is metadata only. `ToolRegistry` does not enforce approval gating itself — it relies entirely on the caller to check `ToolDef::requiresApproval` before invoking `Execute`. No approval-bypassing is possible at this layer; it must be handled upstream.
- `Is

### 06_doc_application_assistant_contextAssembler_h

<a id="06-doc-application-assistant-contextassembler-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/06_doc_application_assistant_contextAssembler_h/docu.output.md`



## ContextAssembler (`assistant/contextAssembler.h` + `.cpp`)

**Role:** `ContextAssembler` is a stateless prompt-assembly utility within the `AIAssistant` namespace. Its sole job is to transform a slice of conversation history, an incoming user message, and an optional set of tool descriptions into a fully structured `AssembledPrompt` — the four-part payload (`stng`, `task`, `cntx`, `prob`) that maps directly to the queue folder files (`STNG_settings.txt`, `TASK_instructions.txt`, `CNTX_context.txt`, `PROB_*`) consumed by the workflow engine's `ai_call` task mechanism. It owns no state; every call is independent.

**Public API:**

- `static AssembledPrompt Assemble(vector<AssistantTurn> const& recentTurns, string const& userMessage, string const& toolDescriptions = {}) → AssembledPrompt` — the single entry point. Calls `BuildSystemPrompt()` to populate `prompt.stng`, then conditionally appends the full tool-calling protocol block (rules, multi-step guidance, CRITICAL RULES 1–4) plus the caller-supplied `toolDescriptions` string when `toolDescriptions` is non-empty. Sets `prompt.task` to a fixed one-line instruction string. Calls `BuildConversationContext(recentTurns)` for `prompt.cntx`. Assigns `userMessage` verbatim to `prompt.prob`. No I/O, no allocation beyond string construction, no exceptions thrown.

- `struct AssembledPrompt` — plain aggregate; four `std::string` members (`stng`, `task`, `cntx`, `prob`). Callers own the returned value by value; there is no heap handle to manage.

**Private helpers (no external callers):**

- `static string BuildSystemPrompt() → string` — returns a large raw-string literal defining the JarvisAgent AI persona, the full JCWF reference (format rules, task types, trigger types, dependency model, controlflow/filters, pitfalls), tool-use guidelines, shell/file-editing rules, compile/fix workflow, memory guidelines, and slash-command documentation. The content is entirely compile-time constant; nothing is read from disk or config at runtime.

- `static string BuildConversationContext(vector<AssistantTurn> const& turns) → string` — iterates `turns` and formats each entry as `"User: <text>\n\n"` or `"Assistant: <text>\n\n"`, prefixed with `"Previous conversation:\n\n"`. Returns an empty string when `turns` is empty. Only `role` values `"user"` and `"assistant"` are emitted; any other role value is silently dropped.

**Collaborators:**

- `AssistantTurn` (declared in `assistant/assistantSession.h`) — the turn struct must expose at minimum `role` (`std::string`) and `text` (`std::string`). `ContextAssembler` reads these fields directly; it does not own or manage `AssistantTurn` lifetime.
- The `ai_call` task runner in the workflow engine — the `AssembledPrompt` fields are written to the queue folder files that the engine reads. The field-to-file mapping is: `stng` → `STNG_*`, `task` → `TASK_*`, `cntx` → `CNTX_*`, `prob` → `PROB_*`.
- Tool dispatch layer — the caller is responsible for building `toolDescriptions` (the formatted list of available tools) and passing it in. `ContextAssembler` does not discover or register tools itself.

**Threading / lifetime:**

All methods are `static` with no shared mutable state. `ContextAssembler` cannot be instantiated and holds no member variables. Callers may invoke `Assemble` concurrently from any thread without synchronization. The returned `AssembledPrompt` is a value type; the caller owns it from the moment `Assemble` returns. `recentTurns` and `userMessage` must remain valid only for the duration of the call.

**Notes:**

- The `task` field is hardcoded to `"Respond to the user's message. Be concise, helpful, and direct."` regardless of context. It is never empty, unlike the comment in the header which states it is "empty for assistant" — that comment describes the queue-file convention for non-assistant workflows, not the actual runtime behaviour of this class.
- The tool-injection block inside `stng` is appended after the static system prompt, not replacing any part of it. The boundary markers `=== Tool System ===` / `=== End Tool System ===` are embedded in the assembled string.
- `BuildConversationContext` pre-reserves 4096 bytes but does not enforce any token or character budget. Trimming conversation history to a token limit is entirely the caller's responsibility — `recentTurns` should already be the pre-truncated slice before calling `Assemble`.
- CRITICAL RULE 4 in the injected tool block states that `[APPROVAL HANDLED BY SYSTEM]` tools must not prompt the user for confirmation. This is a prompt-level constraint enforced by instruction only; the C++ layer does not validate model compliance.
- The JCWF reference embedded in `BuildSystemPrompt` documents version `"1.0"` and `"1.1"` specifics inline. If the workflow format evolves, this string literal must be updated manually — there is no dynamic generation from a schema.
- Sensitive-file restrictions (config.json, keys.json, .env, .pem, .key) mentioned in the system prompt are enforced at the tool-execution layer, not here. `ContextAssembler` merely instructs the model; it performs no path validation.

### 07_doc_application_assistant_workspaceIndexer_h

<a id="07-doc-application-assistant-workspaceindexer-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/07_doc_application_assistant_workspaceIndexer_h/docu.output.md`



## WorkspaceIndexer (`assistant/workspaceIndexer.h` + `workspaceIndexer.cpp`)

**Role:** `WorkspaceIndexer` maintains a persistent, mtime-based index of every source file in the j9t workspace that an AI assistant may need to read or summarize. On the first run it scans five hard-coded workspace subtrees (`application`, `engine`, `workflow-editor/ui/src`, `scripts`, `workflows`) plus top-level indexable files, writes the results to `assistant/index/file_index.jsonl` in JSONL format, and records per-file AI-generated summaries alongside each entry. On subsequent runs it reloads the persisted JSONL, performs the same scan, and carries forward any cached summary whose `summaryMtimeNs` still matches the file's current `mtimeNs`, discarding stale ones automatically. The class also provides a lightweight keyword-relevance search over cached summaries so that the AI toolchain can retrieve the most pertinent files for a given query without reading every file in the workspace.

---

**Public API:**

- `WorkspaceIndexer(fs::path indexDir)` — Constructs the indexer. `indexDir` becomes `m_IndexDir`; the JSONL file path is derived as `indexDir / "file_index.jsonl"`. No I/O occurs at construction time.

- `void ScanWorkspace()` — Full scan entry point. Locks `m_Mutex`. Calls `LoadIndex()` to seed cached summaries from disk, then walks all entries in `kScanDirs` (max depths 5–10) and top-level files. Removes entries for files that no longer exist (they simply drop out of the new `m_Entries` vector). Preserves summaries from the old index wherever `oldEntry.summaryMtimeNs == newEntry.mtimeNs`. Writes the updated index back to disk via `SaveIndex()`, sets `m_LastScanTime`, and logs a summary line via `LOG_APP_INFO`.

- `std::string GetFileSummary(std::string const& relativePath) const` — Returns the cached AI summary for `relativePath`, or an empty string if the file is not indexed or if `entry.summaryMtimeNs != entry.mtimeNs` (staleness check). Acquires `m_Mutex`.

- `void SetFileSummary(std::string const& relativePath, std::string const& summary)` — Stores an AI-generated summary for an already-indexed file, stamps `summaryMtimeNs = entry.mtimeNs`, then immediately calls `SaveIndex()` to persist. Acquires `m_Mutex`. No-op if `relativePath` is not in the index.

- `static std::string ReadFileContent(std::string const& relativePath, size_t maxBytes = 32768)` — Reads up to `maxBytes` of a workspace file's raw bytes. **Does not acquire any lock** (static, no shared state). Returns empty string if the path contains `..`, starts with `/`, does not exist, or is not a regular file. Appends a truncation notice (`... [truncated at N bytes]`) when the actual file is larger than `maxBytes`.

- `std::vector<FileIndexEntry> GetRelevantFiles(std::string const& query, int maxResults = 5) const` — Tokenizes `query` into lowercase alphanumeric/underscore/hyphen/dot words of length ≥ 2, scores every indexed entry that has a non-empty summary by counting substring occurrences (file path counted twice for extra weight), sorts descending by score, and returns at most `maxResults` copies of the top-scoring `FileIndexEntry` structs. Returns empty vector if `query` is empty or produces no tokens. Acquires `m_Mutex`.

- `size_t FileCount() const` — Returns `m_Entries.size()` under lock.

- `size_t SummaryCount() const` — Returns the count of entries with a non-empty `summary` field under lock.

- `std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point LastScanTime() const` — Returns `m_LastScanTime` **without** acquiring `m_Mutex` (the time point is set atomically from the single `ScanWorkspace` call and only read here; callers should treat it as a best-effort value).

- `std::vector<FileIndexEntry> GetAllEntries() const` — Returns a by-value copy of the entire `m_Entries` vector under lock. Used by the `/index` command to enumerate all indexed files.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` — Provides `LOG_APP_INFO` and `LOG_APP_WARN` macros used for all diagnostic output. The indexer has no other coupling to the engine's workflow or request infrastructure.
- `simdjson` (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) — Used exclusively in `LoadIndex()` via `simdjson::ondemand::parser` to parse individual JSONL lines. Each line is wrapped in a `simdjson::padded_string` and iterated as an `ondemand::document`. Serialization in `SaveIndex()` is done manually with `JsonEscapeIdx`, not through simdjson.
- `std::filesystem` — All directory traversal, `file_size`, and `last_write_time` queries go through `std::filesystem` with `std::error_code` overloads (no exceptions thrown).
- `FileIndexEntry` (same header) — Plain data struct holding `relativePath`, `extension`, `sizeBytes`, `mtimeNs`, `summary`, and `summaryMtimeNs`; this is the unit of storage throughout the class.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

All public methods except `ReadFileContent` and `LastScanTime` acquire `m_Mutex` (declared `mutable`). `ReadFileContent` is a static utility with no shared state and is safe to call from any thread at any time. `LastScanTime` reads `m_LastScanTime` without a lock; because `std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point` is a trivially copyable type and only written once per `ScanWorkspace` call, the practical risk is a torn read returning a stale value rather than a crash, but callers that need precision should call it after `ScanWorkspace` completes. `WorkspaceIndexer` is not copyable or movable in any documented way; it should be owned by a single long-lived owner (e.g., the AI assistant subsystem) and shared via pointer or reference. There is no background thread inside the class; `ScanWorkspace` is a blocking synchronous call and should not be invoked on a latency-sensitive thread.

---

**Notes:**

- **Summary staleness is purely mtime-based.** If a file is modified and then restored to its exact previous byte content without changing the filesystem mtime (unlikely but possible), the old summary is incorrectly considered valid.
- **`ScanWorkspace` rebuilds `m_Entries` in full on every call.** It does not do an incremental update. All existing entries from the old scan are discarded from memory before the new scan populates `m_Entries`; the only continuity is through the `oldEntries`/`oldPathToIndex` copies taken at the start. After `ScanDirectory` populates `m_PathToIndex` during the new scan, the method rebuilds `m_PathToIndex` from scratch a second time to guarantee consistency — the first population from inside `ScanDirectory` is effectively thrown away.
- **Top-level file scanning happens after directory scanning** and deduplicates against `m_PathToIndex` already built by `ScanDirectory`, so files in the project root are indexed only once even if they happen to match a scanned directory entry.
- **`ReadFileContent` applies a path-traversal guard** by rejecting any path containing `..` or starting with `/`. This is a substring check, not a canonical-path comparison, so crafted relative paths using encoded sequences or alternate separators are not blocked — callers should not pass untrusted user input directly.
- **Scan directories and skip directories are compile-time constants** (`kScanDirs`, `kSkipDirNames`, `kIndexableExtensions`). There is no runtime configuration mechanism; changing the set of indexed paths or extensions requires a code change and recompilation.
- **`ScoreMatch` is additive and not normalized.** A long summary with many keyword repetitions will score higher than a short, highly relevant one. The file path is concatenated into the haystack twice before lowercasing, giving path matches roughly double the weight of summary matches.
- **`SaveIndex` truncates and rewrites the entire JSONL file** on every call to `SetFileSummary`, meaning that storing N summaries in sequence incurs N full-file rewrites. For large workspaces this can be slow; consider batching summary writes if this becomes a bottleneck.


---

### 08_doc_application_cloud_azureBlobCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="08-doc-application-cloud-azureblobcloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/08_doc_application_cloud_azureBlobCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## AzureBlobCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** Implements the `ICloudTaskExecutor` interface for Azure Blob Storage, handling exactly two JCWF task operations — `"upload"` and `"download"`. Upload reads a local file entirely into memory and issues a `PUT` request with `x-ms-blob-type: BlockBlob`. Download streams a blob directly to a local file via libcurl's write-to-`FILE*` path, capped at 256 MB. The executor resolves the blob endpoint URL via `AzureBlobConnector`, signs requests via `AzureSharedKeySigner` (for Shared Key auth) or injects a Bearer token (for OAuth2), then records a small JSON result into the task working directory and `TaskInstanceState`.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — The sole overridden method called by the engine dispatch loop. Reads `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` with simdjson to extract `operation`, `container` (falling back to `connection.m_Params["container"]`), `blob_name`, and `local_path`. Performs the upload or download, sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded` or `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed`, populates `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` on failure, writes a JSON result file via `WriteResponseJson`, and logs via `LOG_APP_INFO`. Returns `true` on success, `false` on any error. The `cancellationToken` parameter is accepted but not currently consulted inside this method.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class; provides `WriteResponseJson` and the constructor inherited via `using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor`.
- `AzureBlobConnector::BuildEndpointUrl(connection)` — constructs the storage account base URL from the `CloudConnection`; this determines the hostname used for all requests.
- `AzureSharedKeySigner::Sign(method, url, accessKeyId, secretKey, extraHeaders, contentLength)` — produces the canonicalised HMAC-SHA256 `Authorization` header and `x-ms-date`/`x-ms-version` headers when `credentials.m_AuthType == CloudAuthType::AzureSharedKey`.
- `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` — supplies the CA bundle path set up globally by the engine; applied to every `CURL*` handle. If the path is empty, libcurl's default CA store is used.
- `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory(workflowDefinition)` and `TaskPathResolver::ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath(base, workingDirectory)` — resolve where the JSON result file is written.
- `simdjson` (ondemand API) — used to parse `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`; the padded string and parser are stack-allocated per call.
- libcurl — all HTTP I/O; each request creates and destroys its own `CURL*` handle; no shared handle or connection pool is used.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`AzureBlobCloudTaskExecutor` carries no mutable member state of its own; all state for a single execution lives in stack-local variables or the `taskState` / `workflowRun` arguments passed by the engine. The class can therefore be called concurrently on different tasks as long as each call receives its own `taskState`. However, the two file operations (`std::ifstream` for upload, `std::fopen`/`fclose` for download) operate on paths supplied by the workflow definition and are not protected by any lock — concurrent tasks targeting the same `local_path` will race. libcurl is used in an init-per-request pattern (`curl_easy_init` / `curl_easy_cleanup`), which is safe provided `curl_global_init` has been called once at engine startup. The executor does not own the `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials`, `WorkflowDefinition`, or `WorkflowRun` objects; those are owned by the calling engine layer and must outlive the call.

---

**Notes:**

- **Upload is fully buffered in memory.** The entire file is read into a `std::string` before the `PUT` request. There is no size guard before loading; extremely large files will exhaust process memory. The 256 MB cap (`kMaxDownloadBytes`) applies only to downloads via `CURLOPT_MAXFILESIZE_LARGE`.
- **`cancellationToken` is ignored.** Cancellation signals from the engine will not interrupt an in-progress HTTP transfer; the 300-second `CURLOPT_TIMEOUT` is the only termination bound.
- **Container resolution order:** task param `"container"` takes precedence; if absent, `connection.m_Params["container"]` is used; if both are absent, the task fails immediately.
- **OAuth2 auth hardcodes API version `2024-11-04`** via the `x-ms-version` header. Shared Key auth delegates version selection to `AzureSharedKeySigner::Sign`.
- **`m_CapturedStdout` is capped at `kMaxCaptureChars` (1024 characters).** The result JSON itself is always a short fixed string, so this limit is never actually reached in practice, but the truncation is applied unconditionally via `substr`.
- **Download creates intermediate directories** with `std::filesystem::create_directories`; errors from directory creation are silently swallowed via `std::error_code ec` and execution continues, meaning the subsequent `fopen` will fail with a clearer message if the directory could not be created.
- **HTTP errors from the download path are detected after `fclose`.** A failed download that received an HTTP 4xx/5xx body will leave a partial or empty file at `local_path`; the file is not removed on failure.
- **Extra headers are appended twice during upload** — once through `SetAuthHeaders` (which forwards `extraHeaders` to the signer) and once by the explicit loop in `AzureBlobRequest`. This means `x-ms-blob-type` and `Content-Type` appear in the signed canonical string and are also physically added to the request header list, which is correct for Shared Key signing but callers should be aware of this dual-path pattern if modifying header handling.


---

### 09_doc_application_cloud_azureBlobConnector_h

<a id="09-doc-application-cloud-azureblobconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/09_doc_application_cloud_azureBlobConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## AzureBlobConnector

**Role:** `AzureBlobConnector` is the Azure Blob Storage implementation of the `ICloudConnector` interface within j9t's cloud abstraction layer. It is responsible for identifying itself as the `"azure_blob"` provider type, resolving credentials (either Azure Shared Key or Azure AD OAuth2) from the engine's `KeyManager` and `OAuthTokenManager`, constructing the correct Blob service endpoint URL, and verifying that a configured connection is reachable and authenticated by issuing a live HTTP probe against the target container. It does not perform actual blob I/O; it exists purely for connection configuration and validation.

---

**Public API:**

- `GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the fixed string `"azure_blob"`. Used by the cloud connector registry to match this implementation against connection configurations that declare their type as `azure_blob`.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Reads auth material from the engine's credential stores and populates a `CloudCredentials` output struct. When `connection.m_AuthType` is `CloudAuthType::OAuth2`, it calls `OAuthTokenManager::GetAccessToken(connection.m_KeyName, ...)` and stores the bearer token in `credentials.m_Token`. For `CloudAuthType::AzureSharedKey` (the default), it looks up the named provider via `KeyManager::GetProvider(connection.m_KeyName)` and extracts the Base64-encoded account key from either `provider->m_ApiKey` or, as a fallback, `provider->m_Password` (Basic Auth style). The account name is taken from `connection.m_Params["account_name"]`. Fails with a descriptive `errorMessage` if the key name is empty, the provider is not found, no key material is present, or `account_name` is missing.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Validates connectivity end-to-end. Requires both `account_name` and `container` in `connection.m_Params`. Calls `ResolveCredentials` internally, builds the container-level URL as `{endpoint}/{container}?restype=container`, then issues a raw `curl_easy_perform` GET. For Shared Key auth it delegates header generation to `AzureSharedKeySigner::Sign`. For OAuth2 it manually appends an `Authorization: Bearer` header and a hard-coded `x-ms-version: 2024-11-04` header. Returns `false` on any curl transport error or HTTP status ≥ 400; appends up to 500 bytes of response body to `errorMessage` when the server returns an error body. Timeout is fixed at 15 seconds.

- `BuildEndpointUrl(connection) → std::string` (static) — Returns the Blob service root URL. If `connection.m_Endpoint` is non-empty it strips a trailing slash and returns it verbatim (enabling Azurite or sovereign-cloud overrides). Otherwise it synthesises `https://{account_name}.blob.core.windows.net` from `connection.m_Params["account_name"]`, falling back to `"unknown"` if that key is absent.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — base interface; defines `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials`, `CloudAuthType`, and the virtual method signatures this class implements.
- `AzureSharedKeySigner` (`cloud/azureSharedKeySigner.h`) — called inside `TestConnection` to produce the `Authorization` and any required date/content headers for Shared Key requests. The `Sign("GET", url, accountKeyId, secretKey)` call returns a struct carrying a header map.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`. Provides `GetProvider(name)` returning a credential record whose `m_ApiKey` or `m_Password` field holds the Base64 account key.
- `OAuthTokenManager` (`keys/oauthTokenManager.h`) — accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetOAuthTokenManager()`. `GetAccessToken(keyName, errorMessage)` handles token caching and refresh for Azure AD flows.
- `Core` / `Engine` (`core.h`, `engine.h`) — global singleton `Core::g_Core` is the access point for both managers above.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — consulted for `GetCaBundlePath()` to set `CURLOPT_CAINFO`; if the path is empty, no custom CA bundle is set.
- `libcurl` — used directly (not through any wrapper) in `TestConnection`; `curl_easy_init`, `curl_easy_setopt`, `curl_easy_perform`, and `curl_slist_*` are called inline.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`AzureBlobConnector` is a stateless class — it holds no member data beyond what `ICloudConnector` may declare. It can be instantiated on the stack or as a singleton; callers own the object. All external state is accessed through `Core::g_Core`, so callers must ensure the engine core is fully initialised before invoking any method. `TestConnection` performs a synchronous blocking HTTP call (up to 15 s) on whatever thread calls it; do not invoke it on a latency-sensitive or UI thread. No internal locking is performed; thread safety of `KeyManager` and `OAuthTokenManager` is their own concern.

---

**Notes:**

- `BuildEndpointUrl` silently substitutes `"unknown"` as the account name if `account_name` is absent from `m_Params`; no error is surfaced. Callers that use the URL downstream without separately validating `account_name` will receive a malformed but syntactically legal URL.
- The `x-ms-version: 2024-11-04` header is hard-coded in `TestConnection` for OAuth2 paths only; Shared Key requests get their version header from `AzureSharedKeySigner::Sign`. If the API version needs updating, both sites must be changed independently.
- `TestConnection` allocates a raw `CURL*` handle and cleans it up in-function; it does not use j9t's `CurlWrapper` RAII facilities beyond the CA-bundle query. A thrown exception between `curl_easy_init` and `curl_easy_cleanup` would leak the handle, though no exceptions are expected in the current flow.
- The OAuth2 path in `ResolveCredentials` does not validate that the retrieved token is non-expired beyond what `OAuthTokenManager` guarantees internally.
- `ResolveCredentials` accepts `m_Password` as a fallback for the account key to support Basic Auth-style credential records, but this is purely a convenience; the account name must still come from `m_Params["account_name"]`, not from `m_Username`/`m_Password`.


---

### 100_doc_application_workflow_workflowRuntimeManager_h

<a id="100-doc-application-workflow-workflowruntimemanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/100_doc_application_workflow_workflowRuntimeManager_h/docu.output.md`



## WorkflowRuntimeManager

**Role:** `WorkflowRuntimeManager` is the central tick-driven execution engine for j9t workflows. It owns the full lifecycle of every workflow run: accepting enqueue requests, materialising `ActiveRun` instances from `WorkflowDefinition` records, driving a per-tick dispatch/harvest loop for task execution on a background `ThreadPool`, propagating AI request completions from `AiRequestPool`, evaluating per-item filter fan-outs, enforcing control-flow branching, managing retry back-off, cascade-cancelling in-flight HTTP requests when a run terminates, maintaining a 2-second minimum-visibility hold so fast runs appear in dashboard snapshots, and writing the final `WorkflowRun` state into a per-workflow last-run map. It is explicitly designed to be called from `JarvisAgent::OnUpdate()` so the main thread remains unblocked and filesystem events (required for `ai_call` completion delivery) can still arrive through `JarvisAgent::OnEvent()`.

---

**Public API:**

- `Start() → void` — Sets `m_IsRunning = true` under `m_Mutex`. Must be called before `Update()` will process anything.

- `Stop() → void` — Calls `SignalStop()` then `WaitStop()` in sequence; blocks until all in-flight task and filter-eval futures have drained (with a 500 ms per-future timeout). Safe to call from the main thread during shutdown.

- `SignalStop() → void` — Acquires `m_Mutex`, clears `m_IsRunning`, drains `m_PendingRuns`, and sets `m_CancelRequested = true` on every active run. Does not block on futures.

- `WaitStop() → void` — Collects all task and filter-eval futures, waits on each for up to 500 ms, then acquires `m_Mutex` and fails any lingering `WaitingExternal` tasks (calling `AiRequestPool::Forget` on their handles) before clearing `m_ActiveRuns` and `m_DeferredAiCompletions`.

- `EnqueueWorkflowRun(workflowId) → void` — Convenience wrapper; calls `EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId` and discards the run ID.

- `EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId(workflowId) → std::string` — Looks up the workflow in the registry, runs `CheckAiProviderPrerequisites`, generates a run ID via `GenerateRunId` (falling back to `workflowId + "_" + epochMs`), and pushes a `PendingRun` onto `m_PendingRuns` under `m_Mutex`. Returns the run ID, or an empty string if prerequisites fail or the workflow ID is empty.

- `EnqueueWorkflowRunWithContextAndGetRunId(workflowId, runId, context) → std::string` — Same as above but accepts a caller-supplied run ID (generated if empty) and an initial `ContextMap` that seeds `WorkflowRun::m_Context`. Used by REST integrations and `AdhocWorkflowManager`.

- `Update() → bool` — Must be called from the main thread on every frame/tick. Drains `m_PendingRuns` into new `ActiveRun` instances, calls `DrainAiRequestCompletions` and `PropagateSubWorkflowCompletions`, ticks every active run via `TickActiveRun`, applies the 2-second visibility hold, moves completed runs into `m_LastRuns`, fires `FireCompletionCallback` and the `RunTerminalObserver`, and increments `m_TotalCompletedRuns` / `m_TotalFailedRuns`. Returns `true` if any run changed state this tick.

- `SetRegistry(workflowRegistry) → void` — Stores a non-owning pointer to `WorkflowRegistry` under `m_Mutex`. Must be set before any run is enqueued.

- `TryGetLastRun(workflowId, outRun) → bool` — Thread-safe (acquires `m_Mutex`). Copies the last completed `WorkflowRun` for the given workflow ID into `outRun`. Returns false if no run has ever completed for that workflow.

- `TryGetActiveRun(runId, outRun) → bool` — Thread-safe. Searches `m_ActiveRuns` by run ID and copies the matching `WorkflowRun`. Does not search `m_LastRuns`.

- `RequestCancelRun(runId) → bool` — Sets `m_CancelRequested = true`, cancels the shared `TaskCancellationToken`, and recursively cancels child sub-workflow runs via `CancelChildSubWorkflowRuns`. Returns false if the run is not found in `m_ActiveRuns`. Cancellation is cooperative: new tasks stop being dispatched, and once all in-flight futures complete the run transitions to `WorkflowRunState::Cancelled`.

- `RequestPauseRun(runId) → bool` — Sets `m_PauseRequested = true` and `m_Run.m_State = Paused`. Rejected if `m_CancelRequested` or `m_StopRequested` is already set. In-flight tasks continue to completion; no new tasks are dispatched while paused.

- `RequestResumeRun(runId) → bool` — Clears `m_PauseRequested` and restores `m_Run.m_State = Running`. Returns false if the run is not currently paused.

- `RequestStopRun(runId) → bool` — Sets `m_StopRequested = true` and `m_Run.m_State = Stopping`, clears any pause. In-flight tasks are allowed to finish; no new tasks are dispatched. Once all futures drain, pending/ready tasks are skipped and the run completes as `Stopped` (or `Failed` if `m_HasFailed`).

- `TryGetRunById(runId, outRun) → bool` — Checks `m_ActiveRuns` first via `TryGetActiveRun`, then searches `m_LastRuns` by run ID. The only method that spans both collections.

- `GetActiveRunsSnapshot() → std::vector<WorkflowRun>` — Thread-safe copy of all active `WorkflowRun` objects (including runs in their 2-second visibility hold).

- `GetLastRunsSnapshot() → std::unordered_map<std::string, WorkflowRun>` — Thread-safe copy of the last completed run per workflow ID.

- `CleanWorkflow(workflowId, outErrorMessage) → bool` — Rejects if any active run exists for the workflow. Deletes: (1) `queue/<workflowId>/` recursively, (2) declared `file_outputs` per task (with simple `*`-prefix/suffix glob expansion), (3) task working directories recursively in deepest-first order, (4) the workflow output directory if it is then empty. Never touches input/source files. Returns false and populates `outErrorMessage` on partial failure.

- `GetRunCounters(outCompleted, outFailed) → void` — Thread-safe read of lifetime completed and failed run counters.

- `Heartbeat(taskInstanceId) → bool` — Acquires `m_WatchdogMutex`, looks up the `TaskWatchdog` for the given task instance ID, and calls `Kick()`. Used by REST endpoints to reset inactivity timers for long-running tasks.

- `RegisterSubWorkflowLink(childRunId, parentRunId, parentTaskInstanceId) → void` — Records a parent-child run relationship in `m_SubWorkflowLinks`. When the child run terminates, `PropagateSubWorkflowCompletions` will flip the parent task's state from `WaitingExternal` to `Succeeded` or `Failed`.

- `SetRunTerminalObserver(observer) → void` — Registers a `std::function<void(runId, finalState)>` invoked (on the main thread, synchronously inside `Update()`) each time a run reaches a terminal state. Used by `AdhocWorkflowManager` to clean up `on_completion` folders.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `WorkflowRegistry` — Non-owning pointer. Queried for `WorkflowDefinition` by workflow ID at enqueue time and when starting pending runs. Read-only access; not locked by `WorkflowRuntimeManager` directly (the registry owns its own synchronisation).
- `AiRequestPool` — Obtained each tick via `App::g_App->GetAiRequestPool()`. Completions are drained through `TryPopCompletion`; stale handles are released with `Forget`; in-flight HTTP requests are bulk-cancelled with `CancelRequestsForRun` when a run terminates.
- `TaskExecutorRegistry` — Singleton accessed via `TaskExecutorRegistry::Get()`. Dispatches the actual task logic (shell, Python, ai_call, sub_workflow, etc.) inside `ExecuteTaskOnWorker` on a worker thread.
- `DataflowResolver` — Instantiated per task execution inside `ExecuteTaskOnWorker`. Resolves template substitutions and dataflow wiring into `TaskResolvedInputs`.
- `FilterEngine` / `FilterManifestManager` — Used in background threads dispatched by `DispatchFilterEvaluation` to evaluate per-item filters and build/diff manifests for freshness decisions.
- `TaskPathResolver` — Used during dispatch to resolve absolute working directory and freshness paths.
- `TaskFreshnessChecker` — Consulted in the dispatch loop; if a task's outputs are newer than all inputs it is skipped with `TaskInstanceStateKind::Skipped`.
- `JcwfContainer` — Checked at run start; if the workflow was loaded from a `.jcwf` archive and the extracted tree is stale it is re-extracted before the first task runs.
- `Core::g_Core` — Provides the `ThreadPool` used for worker tasks and filter evaluations, `EngineConfig` (including `m_MaxAiCallsPerJcwf` and `m_QueueFolderFilepath`), and the `KeyManager` used for AI provider prerequisite checks.
- `JarvisAgent` / `App::g_App` — Source of `AiRequestPool*` on every tick.
- `simdjson` — Used in anonymous-namespace helpers (`FlattenJsonDom`, `InjectUpstreamOutputs`, `InjectUpstreamAiStructuredOutput`) to parse upstream task `response.json` and structured `ai_call` output files into flat dotted-path context keys for downstream template resolution.
- `libcurl` — Used in the detached `FireCompletionCallback` thread to POST run completion payloads to caller-supplied `callbackUrl` context values (15-second timeout, fire-and-forget).

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `WorkflowRuntimeManager` is not copyable. It must outlive all callers because worker-thread lambdas capture `workflowDefinition` (a reference into `ActiveRun::m_Definition`) and `this` (for `RegisterWatchdog`/`UnregisterWatchdog`).
- `m_ActiveRuns` holds `std::unique_ptr<ActiveRun>` so element addresses remain stable across `push_back` reallocations. This is load-bearing: worker lambdas hold a `const&` into `ActiveRun::m_Definition` for the duration of task execution.
- `m_Mutex` guards `m_IsRunning`, `m_PendingRuns`, `m_ActiveRuns`, `m_LastRuns`, `m_WorkflowRegistry`, `m_TotalCompletedRuns`, `m_TotalFailedRuns`, and `m_RunTerminalObserver`. **It must not be held while calling into executors or futures.**
- `m_WatchdogMutex` is a separate lock used exclusively for `m_ActiveWatchdogs`. It is never held simultaneously with `m_Mutex`.
- `Update()`, `TickActiveRun()`, `DrainAiRequestCompletions()`, `PropagateSubWorkflowCompletions()`, and all dispatch/harvest logic run on the **main thread** without holding `m_Mutex` (the mutex is only taken for the brief window of promoting pending runs, reading registry pointer, or writing to `m_LastRuns`). Worker-thread results are delivered back through `std::shared_future` polled non-blockingly (`wait_for(0ms)`).
- `m_ActiveRun::m_AiCallsDispatched` is incremented exclusively in the main-thread tick loop; no atomic is required.
