- `FireCompletionCallback` detaches a `std::thread` that performs a blocking `curl_easy_perform`. The thread captures copies of all data it needs; the caller does not wait for it.
- Sub-workflow links (`m_SubWorkflowLinks`) are accessed only from `Update()` on the main thread with no additional lock.

---

**Notes:**

- **2-second minimum-visibility hold**: Completed runs are kept in `m_ActiveRuns` until at least 2 seconds have elapsed since `m_StartedAtSteady`. Without this, sub-second adhoc runs would vanish from the active list before the dashboard's snapshot broadcast could deliver them. The hold is applied in `Update()` after `m_IsCompleted` is set; `TickActiveRun` detects and early-returns on re-entry during the hold to prevent repeated completion logging.
- **Fingerprint-based change detection**: `Update()` snapshots a rolling hash of `(m_IsCompleted, task states)` for each run **before** calling `DrainAiRequestCompletions` and `PropagateSubWorkflowCompletions`. This is intentional: both functions mutate task states (flipping `WaitingExternal → Succeeded`), so sampling after the drain would make those transitions invisible to the change detector, freezing the dashboard task counter mid-run.
- **Cascade cancellation**: When `m_HasFailed || m_CancelRequested || m_StopRequested` becomes true, `AiRequestPool::CancelRequestsForRun` is called exactly once (guarded by `m_CancelCascadeFired`) to abort any in-flight curl requests bound to the run, preventing continued token consumption after the workflow has given up.
- **AI call cap**: `EngineConfig::m_MaxAiCallsPerJcwf` (0 = disabled) is enforced per active run. Exceeding it fails the task immediately with an error message rather than enqueuing a new request.
- **WaitingExternal timeout**: The 5-minute default safety-net timeout applies to non-`ai_call` tasks in `WaitingExternal` state (e.g. sub-workflow tasks). `ai_call` tasks are explicitly excluded because their curl timeout (`CURLOPT_TIMEOUT_MS`) is managed by `AiRequestPool` and a 5-minute wall-clock kill would abort legitimately slow model responses.
- **Deferred AI completions**: If `TryApplyAiCompletion` cannot match a completion to an active run (race between completion arrival and run startup), it is held in `m_DeferredAiCompletions` (capped at 256 entries) and retried on every subsequent `DrainAiRequestCompletions` call. Completions for runs already in `m_LastRuns` are silently dropped.
- **Controlflow-gated deadlock pruning**: At the end of each `TickActiveRun`, if no work is in-flight and no `WaitingExternal` tasks remain, any `Pending`/`Ready` tasks that have incoming controlflow edges but were never activated are marked `Skipped` so the run can terminate rather than deadlock.
- **Upstream output injection**: `InjectUpstreamOutputs` merges `captured_stdout`, named output slots, and all flattened JSON fields from `response.json` (cloud tasks) and `<stem>.output.json` (ai_call structured output) into a downstream task's `InputValues`. For per-item child instances it selects `response_<N>.json` by parsing the `#N` suffix from the instance ID so each child reads its own index's file.
- **CleanWorkflow safety gate**: The clean operation is rejected while any active run exists for the workflow to avoid deleting files that a running task may be reading or writing. Source/input files are never removed; only declared `file_outputs`, task working directories, and the queue directory are touched.


---

### 101_doc_application_workflow_workflowTriggerBinder_h

<a id="101-doc-application-workflow-workflowtriggerbinder-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/101_doc_application_workflow_workflowTriggerBinder_h/docu.output.md`



## WorkflowTriggerBinder

**Role:** `WorkflowTriggerBinder` is a stateless binding layer that walks every top-level workflow stored in a `WorkflowRegistry`, parses each trigger's JSON parameter blob, and calls the appropriate registration method on a `TriggerEngine`. It is the single point of translation between the declarative workflow-definition model (`WorkflowDefinition` / `WorkflowTrigger`) and the runtime trigger subsystem. It handles all nine trigger kinds currently defined — Auto, Cron, FileWatch, Manual, Webhook, S3Watch, OneDriveWatch, EmailWatch, AzureBlobWatch, GcsWatch — plus a pass-through for the structural `Structure` type that intentionally registers nothing.

**Public API:**

- `RegisterAll(WorkflowRegistry const& workflowRegistry, TriggerEngine& triggerEngine, bool fireAutoTriggers = true) → void` — Iterates every workflow ID returned by `WorkflowRegistry::GetWorkflowIds()`, fetches the corresponding `WorkflowDefinition` via `WorkflowRegistry::GetWorkflow()`, and for each non-sub-workflow calls one of the `TriggerEngine::Add*Trigger` methods. If a workflow has no triggers at all, an `Auto` trigger is synthesised with the id `"auto"`. Per-trigger JSON params are parsed inline using three file-local helpers (`ParseCronParams`, `ParseFileWatchParams`, `ParseWebhookParams`) and inline simdjson blocks for the four cloud-storage and email types. The `fireAutoTriggers` flag is forwarded verbatim to `TriggerEngine::AddAutoTrigger` for every Auto-type registration, controlling whether the engine fires the trigger immediately upon registration. Workflows whose `m_IsSubWorkflow` flag is `true` are silently skipped — sub-workflows are never auto-triggered. Failures in JSON parsing are logged and cause the individual trigger to be skipped via `break`; they do not abort processing of other triggers or workflows.

**Collaborators:**

- `WorkflowRegistry` — read-only source of workflow IDs and definitions. Accessed via `GetWorkflowIds()` and `GetWorkflow(id)`. The registry is passed `const&`; no mutation occurs.
- `TriggerEngine` — mutable sink. `RegisterAll` calls `AddAutoTrigger`, `AddCronTrigger`, `AddFileWatchTrigger`, `AddManualTrigger`, `AddWebhookTrigger`, `AddS3WatchTrigger`, `AddOneDriveWatchTrigger`, `AddEmailWatchTrigger`, `AddAzureBlobWatchTrigger`, and `AddGcsWatchTrigger` depending on `WorkflowTriggerType`.
- `TaskPathResolver` — used only for `FileWatch` triggers. `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory(workflowDefinition)` converts the workflow's base directory to an absolute `std::filesystem::path` against which relative watch paths are resolved.
- `WorkflowDefinition` / `WorkflowTrigger` / `WorkflowTriggerType` — from `workflowTypes.h`. Key fields consumed: `m_IsSubWorkflow`, `m_Id`, `m_Triggers`, `m_WorkflowBaseDirectory`, `m_WorkflowBaseDirectoryAbsolute`, `m_ParamsJson`, `m_IsEnabled`, `m_Type`.
- `simdjson` (simdjson `ondemand` API) — all JSON parsing. Each trigger type creates its own `ondemand::parser` and `padded_string` locally; parsers are not shared across calls.
- `TriggerEngine::FileEventType` — enum with values `Created`, `Modified`, `Deleted`; string matching is case-insensitive via the file-local `ToLowerAscii` helper.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`WorkflowTriggerBinder` carries no state whatsoever (no member variables beyond the implicit `this`). It is safe to construct on the stack and discard immediately after `RegisterAll` returns. The class is neither copyable nor movable in any meaningful sense — default copy/move behave correctly since there is nothing to copy. `RegisterAll` must not be called concurrently with any mutation of `workflowRegistry` or with any other caller registering triggers on the same `triggerEngine` instance, as neither argument provides internal synchronisation guarantees visible from this layer. The call is synchronous; all `TriggerEngine::Add*` calls complete before `RegisterAll` returns.

**Notes:**

- **Sub-workflow exclusion is unconditional.** Any `WorkflowDefinition` with `m_IsSubWorkflow == true` is entirely skipped, even if it carries explicit trigger definitions. This is by design: sub-workflows are invoked by their parent workflow, never by the trigger engine directly.
- **Synthesised auto trigger.** A workflow with an empty `m_Triggers` vector is not an error; `RegisterAll` treats it as an implicit single Auto trigger with id `"auto"` and `m_IsEnabled = true`. This means any workflow that omits trigger configuration will run immediately on startup when `fireAutoTriggers` is `true`.
- **Webhook without params is an open webhook.** `ParseWebhookParams` returns `true` and leaves `outSecret` empty when `paramsJson` is empty or unparseable. This is deliberate — an absent or malformed params blob degrades gracefully to an unauthenticated webhook rather than blocking registration.
- **FileWatch path resolution rule.** Relative watch paths (those not starting with `/`) are resolved against `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory(workflowDefinition)`. Absolute paths are normalised with `lexically_normal()` but are not re-rooted. Two `[paths debug]` log lines are emitted at `INFO` level for every FileWatch binding showing both the raw and resolved paths — these are intentional diagnostic output, not temporary debugging.
- **`debounce_ms` clamping.** Values below zero are clamped to 0; values above `UINT32_MAX` are clamped to `UINT32_MAX`. Non-integer values produce a warning and default to 0.
- **Cloud-storage trigger params parse failures are soft.** If `connection` is absent or the JSON is malformed for S3Watch, OneDriveWatch, EmailWatch, AzureBlobWatch, or GcsWatch, the trigger is logged as an error and skipped; remaining triggers on the same workflow continue to be processed.
- **`Structure` triggers are explicitly ignored** at registration time with an `INFO` log. They exist solely to drive per-item expansion inside the execution engine and must not produce a runtime trigger entry.
- **`GetWorkflow` TOCTOU window.** Workflow IDs are snapshot via `GetWorkflowIds()` and then fetched one-by-one. If the registry is mutated between those two calls (e.g., a workflow is unloaded), `GetWorkflow` returns `std::nullopt` and the binder logs a warning and continues. Callers must ensure the registry is stable for the duration of `RegisterAll` to avoid partial registration.


---

### 102_doc_application_workflow_workflowTypes_h

<a id="102-doc-application-workflow-workflowtypes-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/102_doc_application_workflow_workflowTypes_h/docu.output.md`



## workflow\_types.h

**Role:** This header is the central type-definition layer for the j9t workflow engine. It declares every plain-data struct, enum, and inline utility that the rest of the engine uses to represent a workflow from static configuration (`WorkflowDefinition`, `TaskDef`) through runtime execution state (`WorkflowRun`, `TaskInstanceState`). It does not contain any business logic beyond `TruncateUtf8Safe` and the atomic `TaskWatchdog`; all orchestration, scheduling, and execution happens in other translation units that consume these types.

---

**Public API:**

- `TruncateUtf8Safe(s, maxBytes) → std::string` — Returns a copy of `s` shortened to at most `maxBytes` bytes, walking backward from the cut point to discard any trailing UTF-8 continuation bytes (0x80–0xBF) so the result is always a valid UTF-8 string. The primary motivation stated in the comment is preventing a truncated string from triggering a Python exception inside `PyUnicode_FromString`, which would then poison the next `PyObject_Call` with a misleading `dict.get` error. Callers that pass strings to the embedded Python interpreter or to any strict UTF-8 consumer should route through this helper rather than using `substr` directly.

- `TaskWatchdog::Kick()` — Records the current `steady_clock` time in milliseconds into `m_LastActivityMs` with `memory_order_release`. Must be called by executors on any observable progress event (stdout line, loop iteration, heartbeat REST call) to reset the inactivity counter.

- `TaskWatchdog::ElapsedSinceLastKickMs() → int64_t` — Returns milliseconds since the last `Kick()`, using `memory_order_acquire`. The watchdog monitor (in WRM and the REST heartbeat endpoint) polls this to decide whether a task has exceeded its inactivity budget.

All other members are pure data structs with no methods beyond `QueueFileRef`'s convenience constructors and implicit string-conversion operator.

`QueueFileRef::operator std::string const&()` — implicit conversion to the path string, allowing `QueueFileRef` to be passed where a string path is expected without explicit `.Path()` calls. `m_HasInlineContent` distinguishes between a filesystem reference and an inline content blob; callers must check this flag before attempting to read from disk.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` — included directly; this header therefore inherits whatever platform and base-type definitions live there.
- `cloud/taskCancellationToken.h` — `WorkflowRun::m_CancellationToken` is a `std::shared_ptr<TaskCancellationToken>`. The run creates one token and shares it across all tasks; `Cancel()` on the token propagates cooperative cancellation to every executor.
- `AiCallTaskExecutor` — writes PROV_* sidecar files described in `QueueBinding::m_ProbFiles` comment; referenced by name in `TaskDef::m_OutputSchemaJson` / `m_OutputSchemaMaxAttempts` for schema-validation retry logic.
- `WorkflowRunManager (WRM)` — polls `TaskWatchdog::ElapsedSinceLastKickMs()` and shares `TaskInstanceState::m_Watchdog` with the REST heartbeat endpoint.
- Python embedding layer — `TruncateUtf8Safe` exists specifically to protect `PyUnicode_FromString` calls in the Python executor.
- JCWF parser / loader — populates `WorkflowDefinition` fields including the six filesystem path variants (`m_WorkflowFilePath`, `m_WorkflowFileDirectory`, `m_WorkflowBaseDirectory`, and their `*Absolute` counterparts) and the computed `m_HasAiCallTasks` / `m_RequiredAiProviders` fields.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `TaskWatchdog` is explicitly designed for concurrent access: `m_LastActivityMs` is `std::atomic<int64_t>`. `Kick()` and `ElapsedSinceLastKickMs()` are safe to call from different threads simultaneously without external locking.
- `TaskInstanceState::m_Watchdog` is a `std::shared_ptr<TaskWatchdog>`, allowing the executor thread and the WRM monitor thread to hold independent references. The watchdog outlives both as long as at least one shared_ptr is alive.
- `WorkflowRun::m_CancellationToken` follows the same shared-ownership pattern. `WorkflowRun` is ephemeral — created per activation and destroyed when the run completes or is cancelled.
- All other structs (`WorkflowDefinition`, `TaskDef`, `WorkflowRun`, etc.) are plain data with no internal synchronisation. Callers are responsible for external locking when sharing these structs across threads. `WorkflowDefinition` is typically loaded once and treated as immutable after that point; `WorkflowRun` and `TaskInstanceState` are mutated by the orchestrator and must be protected by the caller.

---

**Notes:**

- `TaskInstanceStateKind::WaitingExternal` is a non-terminal state introduced specifically for asynchronous `ai_call` completion driven by filesystem events. `TaskInstanceState::m_WaitingExternalSince` records when this state was entered so the orchestrator can enforce a timeout independently of the normal `m_TimeoutMs` budget.
- `TaskInstanceState::m_CapturedStdout` / `m_CapturedStderr` are capped at 1024 characters for in-memory storage; full output is written to `stdout.txt` / `stderr.txt` in the task working directory. Code that needs the complete output must read those files rather than relying on the struct fields.
- `WorkflowDefinition` carries six path fields for the loaded `.jcwf` file: three relative (`m_WorkflowFilePath`, `m_WorkflowFileDirectory`, `m_WorkflowBaseDirectory`) and three canonical-absolute versions. Only the absolute variants are safe to use for filesystem operations after the working directory has changed.
- `TaskDef::m_OutputSchemaMaxAttempts` is a separate retry budget for schema-validation failures on `ai_call` tasks, independent of `RetryPolicy::m_MaxAttempts`, which governs general execution failures. Zero means fall back to the retry policy default.
- `FilterDef::m_MaxItems` defaults to 10 000; a value of 0 means unlimited. Callers generating per-item task instances must explicitly handle the unlimited case to avoid unbounded expansion.
- `QueueBinding::m_ProbFiles` comment notes that PROV_\* files are never forwarded to the AI model — they are a local sidecar written by `AiCallTaskExecutor` for replay tooling only. Do not add PROV paths to the AI request envelope.
- `WorkflowDefinition::m_RequiredAiProviders` is computed at load time and checked at enqueue time; an empty string in the vector means "system default provider", not a missing value.
- The `TaskDefinition` alias exists solely for backward compatibility with existing call sites; new code should use `TaskDef`.


---

### 103_doc_application_workflow_workflowValidator_h

<a id="103-doc-application-workflow-workflowvalidator-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/103_doc_application_workflow_workflowValidator_h/docu.output.md`



## WorkflowValidator

**Role:** `WorkflowValidator` is the central static validation service for JCWF (JC Workflow Format) workflow definitions in j9t. It performs multi-tier structural and semantic validation of a `WorkflowDefinition`, emitting a ranked list of `WorkflowValidationIssue` records that carry machine-readable error codes, JSON-path pointers, AI-directed fix suggestions, and severity/tier classifications. Validation is layered: Tier A covers hard structural invariants (duplicate IDs, DAG cycles, missing required fields, controlflow endpoint resolution); Tier B covers runtime policy enforcement (shell command path restrictions, Python module naming, script file existence, STNG content hygiene, shell script header conventions); Tier C covers feasibility warnings (file-input reachability, `cntx_files` path resolution, `ai_call` file-output hygiene, working-directory conventions); Tier D covers cross-task advisory checks (file-output path collisions between tasks). The class has no instance state and exposes only static methods.

---

**Public API:**

- `Validate(WorkflowDefinition const& workflow, std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>& issues) → void` — Baseline validation (Tier A + B baseline). Clears `issues` on entry, then checks: presence and character-set legality of `workflow.m_Version` and `workflow.m_Id`; non-empty task map; duplicate task IDs; task-ID collision with control-node IDs; per-task type presence and `ValidateTaskParams` dispatch; `depends_on` referencing unknown task IDs; controlflow edge endpoint resolution and kind validity; DAG cycle detection via DFS coloring over the combined task + controlflow adjacency graph; dataflow slot reference validity and duplicate `to_input` bindings; trigger type, ID, and type-specific params (`expression` for Cron, `path` for FileWatch, `secret` for Webhook); and duplicate `working_directory` across tasks. The `issues` vector is the only output; the function returns nothing. Callers must treat the cleared vector as the authoritative result after the call.

- `Validate(WorkflowDefinition const& workflow, ScriptRegistry const* scriptRegistry, std::vector<GeneratedScript> const* pendingScripts, std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>& issues, WorkflowFileIndex const* workflowFileIndex = nullptr) → void` — Full validation. Calls the two-argument overload first, then runs extended Tier B and Tier C/D passes: `ValidatePythonScriptRegistry` (module naming policy, disk/registry/pending-script existence, function presence, script header content via `ValidateScriptContentOnDisk`); `ValidateShellScriptContent` (bash shebang, `@jarvis-script` marker, `@short` metadata, `set -euo pipefail`); `ValidateAiCallStng` (STNG content must contain "No markdown fences"); `ValidateWorkingDirectoryConventions` (`ai_call` dirs must start with `../queue/`, Python task dirs must not); `ValidateAiCallFileOutputs` (output must stay inside launch CWD tree, must not write a requirement-triggering filename into the task's own queue folder); `ValidateFileInputReachability` (file exists on disk or is produced by an upstream `depends_on` task, with doubled-prefix detection and `WorkflowFileIndex` basename lookup for fix suggestions); `ValidateCntxFilesReachability` (same reachability logic for `cntx_files` string-path entries); `ValidateCrossTaskFileChains` (file-output path collision between any two tasks). `scriptRegistry` and `pendingScripts` may be null; `workflowFileIndex` is optional. Null pointers cause the corresponding sub-checks to skip gracefully.

- `HasErrors(std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue> const& issues) → bool` — Linear scan of the issue list; returns `true` if any entry has `m_Severity == WorkflowValidationSeverity::Error`. Does not distinguish tiers. Intended as a go/no-go gate before workflow execution.

- `ValidateSubWorkflowGraph(std::unordered_map<std::string, std::vector<std::string>> const& dependencyGraph, std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>& issues, uint32_t maxDepth = 10) → void` — Standalone cross-workflow cycle and depth checker operating on a caller-supplied adjacency map (workflow ID → list of workflow IDs it depends on). Uses its own DFS coloring pass independent of the per-workflow `DetectCycle` helper. Adds Tier A `Error` issues for any back-edge cycle (`sub_workflow_cycle`) and for any DFS path that exceeds `maxDepth` levels of nesting (`sub_workflow_depth_exceeded`). Does not clear `issues`; appends to whatever the caller passes in. The caller (typically the engine's workflow loader) is responsible for building the dependency graph from all loaded workflows.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `WorkflowDefinition`, `TaskDef`, `DataflowDef`, `WorkflowTrigger`, `ControlflowEdgeDef`, `ControlNodeDef` (from `workflowTypes.h`) — the primary data model structures that the validator reads. All accesses are read-only const references.
- `ScriptRegistry` / `ScriptRegistryEntry` (from `file/scriptRegistry.h`) — queried via `FindByModulePath(module)` to check whether a Python module is registered and to retrieve its `m_ExportedFunctions` list for function-existence validation.
- `WorkflowFileIndex` (from `workflow/workflowFileIndex.h`) — used in `ValidateFileInputReachability` to call `FindByBasename(basename)` and `GetRootDirectory()` when a file-input cannot be found at its declared path, enabling the validator to suggest the correct location.
- `TaskPathResolver` (from `workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — called as `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory(workflow)` to compute the filesystem anchor for relative path resolution in file-input, file-output, and cntx-file checks.
- `Core::g_Core` (from `core.h`) — accessed as a global singleton for `GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()`, which provides the absolute root for all disk-existence checks. Any sub-check that needs a disk path skips silently if `g_Core` is null.
- `simdjson` — used in `TryGetParamsString` to parse `m_ParamsJson` fields from task and trigger definitions on demand without pre-deserializing the entire params blob.
- `GeneratedScript` (defined in the header) — a plain struct (`path`, `content`, `executable`) used in `pendingScripts` to check whether a Python script about to be code-generated satisfies existence and function requirements before it lands on disk.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

All methods are static and `WorkflowValidator` has no instance data, so no constructor or destructor is involved. There is no internal locking. The class is safe to call from any single thread, but callers must not share the `issues` output vector across concurrent calls without external synchronisation. `Core::g_Core` is read as a raw global pointer without a lock; callers must ensure the `Core` object is fully initialised and not being destroyed on another thread during validation. `ScriptRegistry`, `WorkflowFileIndex`, and `WorkflowDefinition` are accessed read-only and must remain alive for the duration of the call — no ownership is transferred or retained. The `pendingScripts` vector is also read-only; the pointer lifetime obligation rests entirely with the caller.

---

**Notes:**

- The `DetectCycle` free function (used for intra-workflow DAG validation) and the DFS in `ValidateSubWorkflowGraph` are two separate implementations. `DetectCycle` records the cycle path by extracting it from the DFS stack using `stackIndex`; `ValidateSubWorkflowGraph`'s DFS reconstructs the cycle by scanning the path vector for the re-encountered gray node. Both report Tier A errors.
- Edge direction in the intra-workflow adjacency graph is `dep → task` (i.e. if task B depends on task A, the edge is A→B). This means a cycle in the graph correctly represents a dependency loop, not a reverse one.
- `ValidateTaskParams` for `Shell` tasks checks three independent conditions in order: params presence, `command` field presence, `scripts/` prefix policy, and `..` path traversal. The script-existence disk check is skipped if either the policy or traversal check fails, guarding against path-injection side-effects from `fs::exists`.
- The `shell_args_duplicate_file_io` warning fires only when a shell task's `args` array contains no `{{input…}}` or `{{output…}}` macros AND contains at least one string that literally matches a declared `file_inputs` or `file_outputs` path. The rationale is that the executor auto-injects file I/O as positional arguments when no macros are present, which doubles those arguments.
- Python module validation has a three-way existence check (registry → disk → pending scripts) and stops at the first hit. Function-existence is checked against `m_ExportedFunctions` in the registry entry first; if not found there but the script was found on disk or in pending scripts, `FunctionExistsInPendingScript` does a raw `def functionName(` substring scan of the source text.
- `ValidateAiCallFileOutputs` uses a local lambda `filenameFiresRequirement` that mirrors the file-categoriser's prefix rules (`STNG_`, `CNTX_`, `TASK_`, `PROV_` are safe; anything else including `PROB_` and plain names triggers a new AI query). The outer-tree check is an `Info` (not `Warning`) because writing outside the working tree is intentional in studio and external-project scenarios.
- Tier C `ValidateFileInputReachability` has a secondary existence check that resolves the file-input path against the workflow base directory directly (ignoring `working_directory`) and, if the file is found there, emits a `file_input_path_hint` warning with a computed relative fix rather than the generic unreachable error. This covers the common authoring mistake of writing an absolute-from-workflows-root path in a field that expects a path relative to the task's `working_directory`.
- `ValidateCntxFilesReachability` skips any `cntx_files` entry where `m_Content` is non-empty, because inline content has no filesystem path to validate.
- Webhook triggers require a non-empty `secret` field; this is an `Error` (not a warning) because the engine enforces HMAC-SHA256 verification and will reject webhook requests without it.
- `IsValidId` permits only `[a-zA-Z0-9_\-.]` — no spaces, slashes, or Unicode. This applies to workflow ID, task IDs, trigger IDs, and control-node IDs uniformly.
- The `DefaultTierForSeverity` mapping is: Error→B, Warning→C, Info→D. Tier A is only assigned explicitly at call sites for the most structurally critical errors (cycles, duplicate IDs, missing dependency references, controlflow endpoint faults).


---

### 104_doc_engine_auxiliary_file_h

<a id="104-doc-engine-auxiliary-file-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/104_doc_engine_auxiliary_file_h/docu.output.md`



## AIAssistant::EngineCore — File System Utilities (`auxiliary/file.h` / `file.cpp`)

**Role:** This file provides a thin, cross-platform file system utility layer used throughout the j9t workflow engine. It wraps `std::filesystem`, `std::ifstream`, and `std::ofstream` into a flat collection of free functions inside `AIAssistant::EngineCore`. The functions cover the common needs of other engine subsystems — checking existence, decomposing paths, querying and mutating the working directory, creating directory trees, copying files, measuring file sizes, and finding the newest modification timestamp across a set of paths. All platform divergence between POSIX and Windows (`_WIN32`, `_MSC_VER`) is handled inside the implementations, keeping callers portable.

---

**Public API:**

- `FileExists(const char*) → bool` — Opens an `std::ifstream` and returns `infile.good()`. Existence is inferred from successful stream open, not from `std::filesystem::exists`. Returns `false` for directories because opening a directory as an `ifstream` typically fails.

- `FileExists(const std::string&) → bool` — Same logic as the `const char*` overload, delegates to `.c_str()`.

- `FileExists(const std::filesystem::directory_entry&) → bool` — Delegates directly to `directory_entry::exists()`; this is the cheapest overload when you already have a cached `directory_entry`.

- `IsDirectory(const char*) → bool` — Constructs a `std::filesystem::path` and calls `std::filesystem::is_directory`. No exception handling; can propagate filesystem errors.

- `IsDirectory(const std::string&) → bool` — Same semantic as above but wraps `is_directory` in a `catch(...)` block and returns `false` on any exception, making it safe to call on arbitrary or inaccessible paths.

- `GetFilenameWithoutPath(const fs::path&) → std::string` — Returns `path.filename()` as a `std::string`. On Windows, goes through an intermediate `fs::path` to force `.string()` conversion.

- `GetPathWithoutFilename(const fs::path&) → std::string` — Returns `path.parent_path()` as a string. Always appends a trailing `/` if the result is non-empty, regardless of platform (note: does **not** use `\` on Windows here).

- `GetFilenameWithoutExtension(const fs::path&) → std::string` — Returns `path.stem()`.

- `GetFilenameWithoutPathAndExtension(const fs::path&) → std::string` — Composes the two helpers above: strips extension first, then strips path. Because `GetFilenameWithoutExtension` returns a plain `std::string` (not a `fs::path`), the result is fed back into `GetFilenameWithoutPath` which accepts a `fs::path` via implicit construction.

- `GetFileExtension(const fs::path&) → std::string` — Returns `path.extension()` including the leading dot, e.g. `".json"`.

- `GetCurrentWorkingDirectory() → std::string` — Returns `std::filesystem::current_path()`. Under MSVC the `.string()` call is explicit; on other compilers an implicit conversion is used.

- `SetCurrentWorkingDirectory(const fs::path&)` — Calls `std::filesystem::current_path(path)`, mutating the process-wide working directory. Throws `std::filesystem::filesystem_error` on failure.

- `CreateDirectory(const std::string&) → bool` — Calls `std::filesystem::create_directories`, which creates the full directory chain. Under MSVC the return value of `create_directories` is discarded and existence is re-verified with `IsDirectory`; on other compilers the return value of `create_directories` is returned directly.

- `CopyFile(const std::string& src, const std::string& dest) → bool` — Opens `src` in binary read mode and `dest` in binary write mode, then streams `rdbuf()` from source to destination. Returns `true` only if both streams are in a good state after the operation. Does **not** use `std::filesystem::copy_file`; there is no overwrite policy check or error message.

- `FileSize(const std::string&) → std::ifstream::pos_type` — Opens the file with `std::ifstream::ate | std::ifstream::binary` and returns `tellg()`. Returns `-1` (cast to `pos_type`) if the file cannot be opened.

- `AddSlash(std::string&) → std::string&` — Appends a platform-appropriate path separator (`\` on MSVC, `/` elsewhere) to the input string if it does not already end with one. Modifies the string in place and returns a reference to it. Uses `substr` for the trailing-character check rather than direct `back()` access.

- `GetNewestTimestamp(const std::vector<fs::path>&) → fs::file_time_type` — Iterates the supplied path list and returns the largest `fs::last_write_time` found. Paths that do not exist or throw are silently skipped. Returns `fs::file_time_type::min()` if the list is empty or all paths are inaccessible.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **File watcher subsystem** — `GetNewestTimestamp` is the primary consumer in a polling-based file-watch loop; callers compare its return value against a previously recorded timestamp to detect changes.
- **Python embedding / script loader** — `FileExists`, `GetFilenameWithoutPathAndExtension`, and `GetFileExtension` are expected consumers for locating and classifying Python source files before handing them to the embedded interpreter.
- **Secrets / config loader** — `FileExists` and `FileSize` guard reads of credential and configuration files before they are parsed.
- Standard library only: `<filesystem>`, `<fstream>`, `<iostream>`, `<vector>`. No third-party dependencies.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

All functions are stateless free functions with no shared mutable state of their own. However, `SetCurrentWorkingDirectory` and `GetCurrentWorkingDirectory` operate on the process-wide working directory, which is a single global OS resource. Calling `SetCurrentWorkingDirectory` concurrently from multiple threads, or calling it while another thread is resolving relative paths, is a data race on the OS level and must be serialised by the caller. All other functions are safe to call concurrently provided the underlying files are not being renamed or deleted mid-call.

---

**Notes:**

- `FileExists(const char*)` and `FileExists(const std::string&)` detect existence by attempting to open the file as an `ifstream`. This means they consume a file descriptor briefly, are subject to permission checks (a file may exist but be unreadable and return `false`), and will return `false` for directories on most platforms. Prefer the `directory_entry` overload or `std::filesystem::exists` directly when only existence — not readability — matters.
- `GetPathWithoutFilename` always appends `/` (not `\`) even on Windows, which may produce mixed-separator strings when combined with Windows-native paths.
- `CreateDirectory` has a subtle MSVC-specific divergence: it discards the `create_directories` return value and re-probes with `IsDirectory`. If the directory was created but immediately became inaccessible, the function returns `false` even though creation succeeded.
- `AddSlash` uses `substr(filename.size() - 1)` for the trailing character check. If `filename` is an empty string this is undefined behaviour (underflow of `size_t`). Callers must ensure the string is non-empty.
- `FileSize` returns `std::ifstream::pos_type(-1)` (typically `-1` as a signed 64-bit value) on failure; callers must check for this sentinel rather than assuming a non-negative result.
- `CopyFile` truncates `dest` unconditionally on open and provides no rollback if the stream fails partway through. It is not an atomic replace.


---

### 105_doc_engine_auxiliary_threadPool_h

<a id="105-doc-engine-auxiliary-threadpool-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/105_doc_engine_auxiliary_threadPool_h/docu.output.md`



## ThreadPool (`auxiliary/threadPool.h` / `threadPool.cpp`)

**Role:** `AIAssistant::ThreadPool` is a thin, shutdown-aware wrapper around `BS::thread_pool<>` (the Barak Shoshany thread-pool library). Its single additional responsibility beyond raw task submission is coordinated shutdown: it exposes an atomic stopped flag that the rest of the engine (notably curl/HTTP callback paths) can poll to abort in-flight work, and it gates `SubmitTask` so that tasks submitted after `Shutdown()` or `RequestStop()` are silently dropped rather than enqueued into a pool that may already be draining.

---

**Public API:**

- `ThreadPool()` — Default-constructs the underlying `BS::thread_pool<>` with its default thread count (hardware concurrency). No additional initialisation is performed.

- `SubmitTask(FunctionType&& task) → std::future<ReturnType>` — Submits a callable to the pool and returns a future for its result. Acquires `m_Mutex` before checking `m_Stopped`; if the pool has been stopped the task is never enqueued — a pre-satisfied future carrying a default-constructed `ReturnType{}` (or a void future) is returned immediately, and a warning is printed to `std::cerr`. When the pool is live the call forwards directly to `BS::thread_pool::submit_task`. The template deduces `ReturnType` via `std::invoke_result_t` on the decayed callable; the `[[nodiscard]]` attribute means callers must capture the returned future (or they lose the ability to synchronise with or observe exceptions from the task).

- `Wait()` — Blocks the calling thread until all currently queued and in-flight tasks have completed. Logs the thread count, queue depth, and each worker thread's `std::thread::id` before blocking. Does **not** set `m_Stopped`; the pool continues accepting new tasks afterwards.

- `RequestStop()` — Sets `m_Stopped = true` atomically and returns immediately without waiting. Intended as an early-abort signal for long-running tasks (e.g., curl streaming callbacks that check `IsStopped()`) so they can self-terminate before `Shutdown()` is called.

- `Shutdown()` — Sets `m_Stopped = true`, then calls `m_Pool.wait()` to drain all already-queued tasks to completion. After this returns, no new work can enter the pool. Logs the queue depth before and after the drain.

- `Reset(size_t numThreads)` — Delegates directly to `BS::thread_pool::reset(numThreads)`, resizing the worker thread count. Does **not** clear `m_Stopped`; callers should not call `Reset` after `Shutdown` without also clearing the stopped flag if they intend to resume work.

- `Size() const → size_t` — Returns `m_Pool.get_thread_count()`, the live worker thread count.

- `IsStopped() const → bool` — Returns the current value of `m_Stopped` via an atomic load. Intended for polling inside long-running tasks submitted to the pool.

- `GetThreadIDs() const → std::vector<std::thread::id>` — Returns the OS-level thread IDs of all current worker threads by delegating to `BS::thread_pool::get_thread_ids()`. Used internally by `Wait()` for diagnostic logging.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **`BS::thread_pool<>`** (`BS_thread_pool/BS_thread_pool.hpp`) — The underlying implementation that owns the worker threads and the task queue. All actual scheduling, queuing, and wait semantics are provided by this library. `ThreadPool` never reimplements them.
- **`engine.h`** — Included in the `.cpp` for access to `LOG_CORE_INFO`, the engine's structured logging macro. The class has no other coupling to the engine at the header level, keeping the header dependency-light.
- **Curl/HTTP callback paths** — Described in `RequestStop()`'s log message as the primary consumers of `IsStopped()`. Those callbacks are expected to poll `IsStopped()` and abort their streaming loops when true.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `m_Stopped` is `std::atomic<bool>`, so reads from any thread (e.g., worker callbacks calling `IsStopped()`) are safe without holding `m_Mutex`.
- `m_Mutex` protects only the `SubmitTask` path — specifically the check-then-enqueue sequence — to prevent a task sneaking into the pool between the stopped check and the `submit_task` call on a concurrent submission. `Wait`, `RequestStop`, `Shutdown`, and `Reset` do **not** acquire `m_Mutex`; they are not designed for concurrent call with each other.
- `ThreadPool` is intended to be owned by the engine as a long-lived singleton-like object. The destructor is implicitly defined; `BS::thread_pool`'s own destructor will join worker threads, but callers should call `Shutdown()` explicitly before destruction to ensure orderly draining with logging.
- `Reset` recreates the worker threads inside `BS::thread_pool`. Any futures already outstanding from `SubmitTask` before a `Reset` remain valid (the tasks will have completed or will complete during the reset's internal wait), but callers must not assume the thread IDs returned by `GetThreadIDs()` are stable across a `Reset`.

---

**Notes:**

- **Post-shutdown tasks silently succeed:** A caller that submits after `Shutdown()` gets back a valid, immediately-ready future with a default-constructed value and no exception. The only signal is a `std::cerr` warning. If the task's return value matters, the caller cannot distinguish this silent drop from a real result without consulting `IsStopped()` first.
- **`RequestStop` does not drain:** After `RequestStop()`, already-queued tasks continue running to completion unless the tasks themselves poll `IsStopped()`. The pool's threads are not interrupted; only cooperative cancellation is possible.
- **`Reset` does not clear `m_Stopped`:** Calling `Reset` after `Shutdown` (or `RequestStop`) leaves `m_Stopped == true`, so the pool will accept no new submissions even though it has fresh worker threads. This is almost certainly a usage bug; callers must manage the flag manually if a reset-and-resume pattern is needed.
- **`Wait` is not a stop:** The log prefix `[shutdown]` on `Wait()` is cosmetic; `Wait` is used during the shutdown sequence but does not itself prevent future task submission.


---

### 106_doc_engine_core_h

<a id="106-doc-engine-core-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/106_doc_engine_core_h/docu.output.md`



## Core (`core.h` / `core.cpp`)

**Role:** `Core` is the root singleton of the AIAssistant engine. It owns and sequences every major subsystem: the `ThreadPool`, `EventQueue`, `TerminalManager`, file-backed logger, `KeyboardInput`, `KeyManager`, `OAuthTokenManager`, `CloudConnectionManager`, `CloudConnectorRegistry`, and `CloudCircuitBreaker`. It installs the process-level `SIGINT` handler, redirects `std::cout` and `std::cerr` through `TerminalLogStreamBuf`, drives the main run loop (event dispatch → `Application::OnUpdate` → terminal render → sleep), and performs an ordered, timed teardown of all owned resources. There is exactly one `Core` instance per process; a raw pointer to it is published as the static `Core::g_Core` immediately in the constructor.

---

**Public API:**

- `Core()` — Captures the process launch directory into `m_LaunchCWDAbsolute`, sets `g_Core = this`, installs `SignalHandler` for `SIGINT`, calls `DisableCtrlCOutput()` to suppress `^C` echo on the terminal, creates `TerminalManager`, opens `log/log.txt` (truncating any existing file), redirects `std::cout` and `std::cerr` to the `TerminalLogStreamBuf`, and creates the global `g_Logger`. Must be called from the main thread before any other subsystem.

- `~Core()` — Restores the original `std::cout`/`std::cerr` stream buffers if `Shutdown()` was not called or did not complete that step. Does not perform a full subsystem shutdown; callers must call `Shutdown()` explicitly before destruction.

- `void Start(ConfigParser::EngineConfig const& engineConfig)` — Copies the supplied config into `m_EngineConfig`, sizes the `ThreadPool` to `m_MaxThreads + THREADS_REQUIRED_BY_APP` (2 extra for file watcher and keyboard input), calls `TerminalManager::Initialize()`, creates and starts `KeyboardInput`, and starts `OAuthTokenManager`. Must be called after construction and before `Run()`.

- `void Run(std::unique_ptr<AIAssistant::Application>&)` — Runs the blocking main loop on the calling thread (named `"main thread (run loop)"` in Tracy). Each iteration: polls `CheckSignalFlags()` for deferred `SIGINT` handling, drains `EventQueue::PopAll()` and dispatches events (engine handles `AppErrorEvent` at critical level; unhandled events are forwarded to `app->OnEvent()`), calls `app->OnUpdate()`, calls `TerminalManager::Render()`, then sleeps for `m_EngineConfig.m_SleepDuration` (asserted to be in the range `(0ms, 256ms]`). Exits when `app->IsFinished()` returns `true`.

- `void Shutdown()` — Performs ordered teardown with elapsed-millisecond logging to aid post-mortem analysis. Sequence: `OAuthTokenManager::Stop()` → `KeyboardInput::Stop()` → `ThreadPool::Shutdown()` → `CurlWrapper::GlobalCleanup()` → `TerminalManager::Shutdown()` → flush `cout`/`cerr` → restore original stream buffers → reset `TerminalLogStreamBuf` → close log file. Post-`TerminalManager` steps use `RAW_SHUTDOWN` (`::write` / `_write` to `stderr`) to bypass the logging stack and catch any post-shutdown hangs.

- `void SignalShutdown()` — Sets `m_ShuttingDown` to `true` and calls `ThreadPool::RequestStop()`. Intended to notify background threads to wind down cooperatively without fully tearing down resources. Distinct from `Shutdown()`.

- `void SignalShutdown()` vs `void RequestQuit()` — `SignalShutdown()` operates on the `ThreadPool` and the `m_ShuttingDown` flag. `RequestQuit()` sets `m_QuitRequested` atomically; both flags are readable via `IsShuttingDown()` / `IsQuitRequested()` and are used by different layers to coordinate graceful exit.

- `void PushEvent(EventQueue::EventPtr eventPtr)` — Thread-safe insertion of an event into `m_EventQueue`. The only legal way for non-main threads to communicate with the run loop. Used by the signal handler path (`CheckSignalFlags`) to inject an `EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown` event after `SIGINT`.

- `ConfigParser::EngineConfig const& GetConfig() const` — Returns the config snapshot copied during `Start()`.

- `ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType const& GetInterfaceType() const` — Returns the interface type for the currently selected API interface (`m_ApiInterfaces[m_ApiIndex].m_InterfaceType`).

- `std::filesystem::path const& GetLaunchCWDAbsolute() const` — Returns the working directory captured at construction time, before any `chdir`. Useful for resolving relative paths from config files regardless of later directory changes.

- `ThreadPool& GetThreadPool()` — Returns a mutable reference. Lifetime is tied to `Core`; callers must not hold references past `Shutdown()`.

- `TerminalManager* GetTerminalManager()` — Returns a raw pointer; may be `nullptr` if called after `Shutdown()`. Non-owning.

- `KeyManager& GetKeyManager()` / `OAuthTokenManager& GetOAuthTokenManager()` / `CloudConnectionManager& GetCloudConnectionManager()` / `CloudConnectorRegistry& GetCloudConnectorRegistry()` / `CloudCircuitBreaker& GetCloudCircuitBreaker()` — Direct references into value members owned by `Core`. Valid for the lifetime of the `Core` object; invalid after destruction.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `Application` — The engine owns a `unique_ptr<Application>` passed into `Run()`; the run loop calls `OnEvent()` and `OnUpdate()` on it each iteration.
- `EventQueue` — Single-producer/multi-producer queue; `PushEvent()` is the only public write path. `PopAll()` is called exclusively from the run loop on the main thread.
- `ThreadPool` — Sized at startup; provides worker threads for file watching, keyboard input, and application-level async tasks. `THREADS_REQUIRED_BY_APP = 2` is reserved above the user-configured maximum.
- `TerminalManager` / `TerminalLogStreamBuf` — `TerminalLogStreamBuf` replaces both `cout` and `cerr` rdbuf pointers; all `std::cout`/`LOG_*` output flows through it to the ncurses terminal window and `log/log.txt`.
- `KeyManager` — Value member; `OAuthTokenManager` holds a reference to it (`OAuthTokenManager{m_KeyManager}`), so member declaration order is significant.
- `OAuthTokenManager` — Constructed with a reference to `m_KeyManager`; started in `Start()`, stopped first in `Shutdown()`.
- `CurlWrapper` — `CurlWrapper::GlobalCleanup()` is called during `Shutdown()` after the thread pool has stopped, ensuring no thread holds a curl handle when global cleanup runs.
- `ConfigParser::EngineConfig` — Value-copied from the argument to `Start()`; not parsed inside `Core`.
- Tracy (`tracy/Tracy.hpp`) — Zone annotations on the run loop's event handling, `OnUpdate`, and sleep sections. Thread named via `tracy::SetThreadName`.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `Core` must be constructed, `Start()`ed, `Run()`ned, `Shutdown()`'d, and destroyed entirely on the **main thread**.
- `g_Core` is set in the constructor and is a raw non-owning pointer. Any code reading `g_Core` after `Core`'s destructor is use-after-free.
- `s_ShutdownRequested` and `s_ForceShutdownRequested` are `static atomic<bool>` written only from `SignalHandler` (async signal context) and read/cleared by `CheckSignalFlags()` on the main thread. `SignalHandler` is intentionally minimal: no heap allocation, no logging, no mutex acquisition.
- `PushEvent()` is safe to call from worker threads; `EventQueue::PopAll()` is only called from the run loop thread.
- `ThreadPool::Shutdown()` must complete before `CurlWrapper::GlobalCleanup()` is called, because pool tasks may hold curl handles and may still emit log output through the stream buffer.
- `TerminalLogStreamBuf` is reset only after `TerminalManager::Shutdown()` and after the stream buffers are restored, ensuring no logging occurs into a destroyed buffer.
- The raw pointer stored in `m_OriginalCoutBuffer` is captured once in the constructor; it is restored in both `Shutdown()` and `~Core()` (guarded by a `nullptr` check) to avoid a double-restore.

---

**Notes:**

- The first `SIGINT` sets `s_ShutdownRequested`, which `CheckSignalFlags()` translates into a `RequestQuit()` call and an `EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown` push on the next run-loop iteration. A **second** `SIGINT` while the first is still being processed sets `s_ForceShutdownRequested` and calls `_exit(EXIT_FAILURE)` immediately from the signal handler, bypassing all destructors and the shutdown sequence.
- `THREADS_REQUIRED_BY_APP = 2` is a compile-time constant comment stating that file watching and keyboard input each consume one thread from the pool; the web server (if active) manages its own thread independently.
- `m_SleepDuration` has a hard assert bounding it to `(0ms, 256ms]`. Violating this crashes in debug and is undefined in release.
- `JarvisRedirect` is a C-linkage shim exported for use by embedded Python or other C consumers. It writes to `std::cout`, which at runtime flows through the redirected `TerminalLogStreamBuf`. Calling it before `Core` construction or after `Shutdown()` restores the original rdbuf will write to the original terminal, not to the log window.
- `GetInterfaceType()` indexes into `m_ApiInterfaces` using `m_ApiIndex` without bounds checking visible in this file; callers must ensure the config is valid before calling it.
- `SetConfigFilePath()` / `GetConfigFilePath()` store a path that is not read by `Core` itself; this is purely a registry service for other subsystems to resolve relative config includes.
