### 107_doc_engine_curlWrapper_authSigner_h

<a id="107-doc-engine-curlwrapper-authsigner-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/107_doc_engine_curlWrapper_authSigner_h/docu.output.md`



## IAuthSigner / authSigner

**Role:** `IAuthSigner` is the polymorphic authentication-signing interface for all outbound HTTP requests in the `AIAssistant` workflow engine. It abstracts over every auth style the engine supports — Bearer token, Google API key (`x-goog-api-key`), Anthropic API key (`x-api-key` + mandatory version header), Azure API key (`api-key`), and AWS Signature Version 4 — behind a single `Apply` call that appends fully-formed header strings to a caller-supplied vector. Both `CurlWrapper::Query` and `CurlMultiDispatcher` can authenticate requests without knowing which provider they are talking to. The concrete signer instances are static singletons obtained through the factory method `Get`.

**Public API:**

- `Apply(QueryData const& queryData, std::vector<std::string>& outHeaders) → void` — Reads the request description in `queryData` (minimally `m_ApiKey`, and for `SigV4Signer` the full request data) and pushes one or more complete HTTP header strings in `"Name: value"` format onto `outHeaders`. Does not clear `outHeaders` first; it only appends. `AnthropicXApiKeySigner` always appends two headers: `x-api-key` and the hard-coded `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`. All other static signers append exactly one header. `SigV4Signer` (defined in `awsSigV4.h`/`.cpp`) may append several computed headers.

- `Get(CurlWrapper::AuthStyle style) → IAuthSigner&` — Factory that maps a `CurlWrapper::AuthStyle` enum value to the corresponding static singleton signer. Returns a reference to one of five function-local statics: `s_Bearer`, `s_XGoog`, `s_Anthropic`, `s_Azure`, or `s_SigV4`. Falls back to `s_Bearer` for any unrecognised `style` value (the switch has no `default`, so an out-of-range enum silently yields Bearer).

**Collaborators:**

- `CurlWrapper::QueryData` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — the input record read by every `Apply` implementation; carries at minimum `m_ApiKey` and whatever fields `SigV4Signer` needs (URL, method, body, region, service, credentials).
- `CurlWrapper::AuthStyle` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — the enum that drives the `Get` factory switch.
- `SigV4Signer` (`curlWrapper/awsSigV4.h`) — the heavyweight concrete implementation for AWS; included in the `.cpp` but not declared in this header, so callers only see it through `IAuthSigner&`.
- `engine.h` — included in the `.cpp`; exact dependency surface not visible here but likely provides engine-wide configuration or utilities used by `SigV4Signer`.

**Threading / lifetime:**

All five concrete signers (`s_Bearer`, `s_XGoog`, `s_Anthropic`, `s_Azure`, `s_SigV4`) are function-local statics initialised on first call to `Get`. Their lifetime is the process lifetime. `Get` returns non-const references to these statics, so callers must not delete or reassign the returned reference. The static signers for Bearer, XGoogApiKey, Anthropic, and Azure contain no mutable state, making concurrent calls to `Apply` on those instances safe. Whether `SigV4Signer::Apply` is re-entrant depends entirely on its own implementation in `awsSigV4.cpp` — callers should verify that before sharing one `SigV4Signer` across threads without external locking.

**Notes:**

- The fall-through default in `Get` is silent: an unknown `AuthStyle` value returns the Bearer signer without any assertion, log, or exception. Callers adding new `AuthStyle` enumerators must also add a corresponding `case` in `Get` or they will silently get Bearer auth.
- `Apply` appends to `outHeaders` rather than replacing it, so the caller is responsible for starting with a clean or intentionally pre-populated vector. Calling `Apply` twice on the same vector will duplicate headers.
- `AnthropicXApiKeySigner` hard-codes `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`. If Anthropic requires a newer version, this must be changed in the source; there is no runtime override path.
- Because `Get` returns references to statics, polymorphic deletion through `IAuthSigner*` obtained from `Get` would be undefined behaviour — the virtual destructor is present for subclasses owned by the caller, not for the singletons.


---

### 108_doc_engine_curlWrapper_awsSigV4_h

<a id="108-doc-engine-curlwrapper-awssigv4-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/108_doc_engine_curlWrapper_awsSigV4_h/docu.output.md`



## SigV4Signer (`curlWrapper/awsSigV4.h` / `awsSigV4.cpp`)

**Role:** Implements AWS Signature Version 4 request signing for the j9t engine's outbound HTTP calls, specifically targeting Amazon Bedrock (and any other AWS service reachable through the same `CurlWrapper` pipeline). The implementation is hand-rolled on top of OpenSSL's `HMAC` and `SHA256` primitives — no AWS SDK dependency. It derives a per-request four-stage signing key (`AWS4+secret → date → region → service → aws4_request`), constructs a canonical request and string-to-sign per the AWS SigV4 specification, and emits the five required HTTP headers (`Host`, `X-Amz-Date`, `X-Amz-Content-Sha256`, `Authorization`, and optionally `X-Amz-Security-Token`) into the outgoing header vector consumed by `CurlWrapper`.

---

**Public API:**

- `Apply(CurlWrapper::QueryData const& queryData, std::vector<std::string>& outHeaders) → void` — The `IAuthSigner` interface entry point called by `CurlWrapper` before each request is dispatched. Reads the target URL and body from `queryData.m_Url` / `queryData.m_Data`, the AWS access key ID from `queryData.m_ApiKey`, and the secret key, session token, region, and optional service name from `queryData.m_Params` (keys `"secret_access_key"`, `"session_token"`, `"region"`, `"service"`). If any of access key, secret key, or region are absent it logs an error via `LOG_CORE_ERROR` and pushes a sentinel `Authorization: AWS4-HMAC-SHA256 MISSING-CREDENTIALS` header so that the request fails visibly rather than silently. On success it appends five correctly formatted headers to `outHeaders`. The `m_AmzDate` field of `Inputs` is left empty here, so `Apply` always uses the live UTC wall clock via `FormatAmzDateNow()`.

- `static Sign(Inputs const& in) → SignedHeaders` — Pure, deterministic function that performs the full SigV4 signing computation given a fully populated `Inputs` struct. If `in.m_AmzDate` is non-empty it is used as-is (ISO 8601 basic format `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ`); if empty the current UTC time is captured. Produces a `SignedHeaders` struct containing `m_Host`, `m_AmzDate`, `m_ContentSha256`, `m_Authorization`, and `m_SecurityToken`. No side effects. The test seam: callers that inject a fixed `m_AmzDate` get reproducible output, which is how `RunSelfTest` verifies determinism and how unit tests can check against known-answer vectors.

- `static RunSelfTest() → bool` — Executes three inline checks: (1) SHA256 of the empty string against the universally known constant, (2) signing-key derivation against the AWS documentation's published example (secret `wJalrXUtnFEMI/K7MDENG+bPxRfiCYEXAMPLEKEY`, date `20120215`, region `us-east-1`, service `iam`, expected kSigning hex `f4780e2d9f65fa895f9c67b32ce1baf0b0d8a43505a000a1a9e090d414db404d`), and (3) a determinism check plus `Authorization` header prefix structure verification on a realistic Bedrock URL. Returns `true` if all three pass. Logs per-failure details and a summary via `LOG_CORE_ERROR` / `LOG_CORE_INFO`. Called once at startup in debug builds from `engine.cpp`.

**Inputs struct fields of note:**

- `m_AccessKey` — AWS access key ID (`AKIA…`); maps to `queryData.m_ApiKey` in `Apply`.
- `m_SecretKey` — 40-character AWS secret; sourced from `m_Params["secret_access_key"]`.
- `m_SessionToken` — Optional STS temporary session token; when non-empty triggers an `x-amz-security-token` canonical header and an `X-Amz-Security-Token` output header.
- `m_Service` — Defaults to `"bedrock"` in `Apply` if `m_Params["service"]` is absent, making the default integration point explicit.
- `m_AmzDate` — Leave empty in `Apply` for live clock; supply a fixed value in tests.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `IAuthSigner` (`curlWrapper/authSigner.h`) — Abstract base that `SigV4Signer` fulfils. `CurlWrapper` holds a pointer to `IAuthSigner` and calls `Apply` before dispatching each request; `SigV4Signer` is registered as the signer for AWS-backed providers.
- `CurlWrapper::QueryData` — The data-transfer struct bridging the engine's request description to the curl layer. `Apply` reads `m_Url`, `m_Data`, `m_ApiKey`, and `m_Params` from it.
- `engine.cpp` — Calls `RunSelfTest()` at startup (debug builds). The `LOG_CORE_ERROR` / `LOG_CORE_INFO` macros resolve through `engine.h`.
- OpenSSL (`openssl/hmac.h`, `openssl/sha.h`) — Direct calls to `SHA256()` and `HMAC()` with `EVP_sha256()`. No EVP streaming API is used; the entire body is hashed in one shot.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`SigV4Signer` carries no mutable instance state. All working data lives on the stack inside `Sign()` and `Apply()`. The object is therefore safe to call concurrently from multiple threads without synchronisation. `Apply` does capture the current wall-clock time via `std::time` / `gmtime_r` (or `gmtime_s` on Windows) when `m_AmzDate` is empty; this is a syscall with no shared state inside the class. Ownership is managed by whichever component registers the signer with `CurlWrapper`; the interface contract is a pointer to `IAuthSigner`, so the concrete `SigV4Signer` instance must outlive the `CurlWrapper` that holds it.

---

**Notes:**

- The canonical headers set is fixed at four entries (`host`, `x-amz-content-sha256`, `x-amz-date`, and conditionally `x-amz-security-token`). No other request headers are signed. If `CurlWrapper` adds headers that AWS expects to be signed (e.g., `content-type`), they will be absent from `SignedHeaders` and the request will be rejected by AWS with a `SignatureDoesNotMatch` error.
- The minimal URL parser (`ParseUrl`) handles only the scheme-strip, host, path, and query decomposition sufficient for AWS service endpoints. It does not handle port numbers, userinfo, fragments, or IPv6 literals. A URL like `https://host:443/path` would include `:443` in `m_Host` and in the `host` canonical header, which would mismatch the actual `Host` header sent by curl and cause a signature failure.
- `UriEncode` uses uppercase hex digits (`%2F`, not `%2f`) for query parameters (`encodeSlash=true`) and leaves `/` unencoded in path segments (`encodeSlash=false`), matching the SigV4 specification exactly.
- `CanonicalQuery` sorts query parameter pairs lexicographically by the already-encoded key and then encoded value, which is correct per spec. Parameters are not de-duplicated before sorting.
- `RunSelfTest` checks the `Authorization` header prefix structure including `SignedHeaders=host;x-amz-content-sha256;x-amz-date` (no session token in that test). If the fixed header set or ordering changes, this prefix check will fail at startup.
- A missing-credentials guard in `Apply` pushes a deliberately malformed `Authorization` header rather than omitting the header or throwing. Callers will receive an HTTP 400/403 from AWS rather than a curl-level error; log output is the signal that credentials were not configured.


---

### 109_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlManager_h

<a id="109-doc-engine-curlwrapper-curlmanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/109_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlManager_h/docu.output.md`



## CurlManager

**Role:** `CurlManager` is a thin factory/accessor that provides each thread with its own lazily-initialised `CurlWrapper` instance. Its sole job is to ensure that libcurl handles are never shared between threads, sidestepping the re-entrancy and state-corruption issues that arise when a single curl handle is used concurrently. It lives inside the `AIAssistant` namespace alongside the rest of the engine's HTTP infrastructure.

**Public API:**

- `GetThreadCurl() → CurlWrapper&` — Returns a reference to the calling thread's private `CurlWrapper`. The object is constructed the first time a given thread calls this function and is destroyed automatically when that thread exits. Callers must not store the returned reference beyond the lifetime of the thread that obtained it, and must not pass it to another thread. No arguments; no locking; no heap allocation on the caller's side.

**Collaborators:**

- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — the actual libcurl wrapper type whose construction, configuration, and teardown are managed by `CurlWrapper` itself. `CurlManager` has no knowledge of curl internals; it only controls the per-thread storage policy for `CurlWrapper` instances.

**Threading / lifetime:**

The `thread_local` storage qualifier guarantees one `CurlWrapper` per OS thread. Construction is lazy and happens on first access within each thread. Destruction is automatic at thread exit, which means any in-flight requests on that thread must be completed or cancelled before the thread exits or the `CurlWrapper` destructor will run underneath them. Because the returned reference is thread-local, callers on different threads receive distinct objects — no synchronisation is needed around `GetThreadCurl()` itself. The class has no instance state; all methods are `static`, so `CurlManager` is never instantiated.

**Notes:**

- Storing the returned `CurlWrapper&` in a variable that outlives the calling thread (e.g. a global, a shared pointer, a cross-thread queue) is undefined behaviour — the underlying object will have been destroyed.
- Thread pools that reuse OS threads will reuse the same `CurlWrapper` across logically distinct tasks on that thread. If `CurlWrapper` carries per-request state (headers, cookies, auth tokens), callers are responsible for resetting that state between uses.
- `CurlManager` itself is not copyable, movable, or constructable in any meaningful way; it is purely a namespace-scoped utility class.


---

### 10_doc_application_cloud_azureSharedKeySigner_h

<a id="10-doc-application-cloud-azuresharedkeysigner-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/10_doc_application_cloud_azureSharedKeySigner_h/docu.output.md`



## AzureSharedKeySigner

**Role:** `AzureSharedKeySigner` is a stateless utility class inside the `AIAssistant` namespace that produces the HTTP headers required to authenticate requests against the Azure Blob Storage REST API using the Shared Key authorization scheme. Given an HTTP method, a full blob URL, an account name, and a Base64-encoded account key, it constructs the canonical string-to-sign mandated by the Azure Storage specification, computes an HMAC-SHA256 signature over it using OpenSSL, and returns the three headers the caller must attach to their outgoing HTTP request: `Authorization`, `x-ms-date`, and `x-ms-version`. The class has no instance state; every method is `static`.

---

**Public API:**

- `Sign(method, url, accountName, accountKey, extraHeaders, contentLength) → SignedRequest` — The sole entry point for callers. Parses `url` to extract host, path, and query string; stamps the current UTC wall-clock time as `x-ms-date` in RFC 1123 format; pins `x-ms-version` to the hard-coded string `"2024-11-04"`; merges `extraHeaders` with those two `x-ms-*` headers to form the canonicalized-headers input; then builds the Azure StringToSign in the exact fourteen-line order the specification requires. The decoded raw bytes of `accountKey` (Base64 input) are used as the HMAC key. Returns a `SignedRequest` whose `m_Headers` map contains exactly `Authorization`, `x-ms-date`, and `x-ms-version` — the caller is responsible for merging these with any other request headers before sending. `contentLength` should be `0` for GET, HEAD, and DELETE; passing `0` correctly emits an empty string in the Content-Length slot of the StringToSign as Azure requires. `extraHeaders` values are included in the canonical headers computation if their lowercased key begins with `x-ms-`, and their values (e.g. `Content-Type`, `Content-MD5`) are picked up for the corresponding StringToSign lines by exact key lookup — so callers must use the canonical HTTP capitalisation (`"Content-Type"`, not `"content-type"`) when passing those headers in `extraHeaders`.

- `SignedRequest::m_Headers` — A `std::map<std::string, std::string>` returned inside `SignedRequest`. Always contains the three keys listed above after a successful `Sign` call. Callers iterate or merge this map into their HTTP client's header collection.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **OpenSSL** (`openssl/hmac.h`, `openssl/evp.h`, `openssl/bio.h`, `openssl/buffer.h`) — used directly for `HMAC`/`EVP_sha256` in `HmacSha256` and for BIO chain-based Base64 encode/decode in `Base64Encode` / `Base64Decode`. No OpenSSL handles are exposed to callers; they are fully managed within each private method call.
- **`cloud/azureSharedKeySigner.h`** — the corresponding header that declares this class and `SignedRequest`; consumers only need to include the header.
- **HTTP client layer (caller-side)** — `Sign` produces headers but does not perform I/O. The caller is expected to pass the returned headers to whichever HTTP library (libcurl, Boost.Beast, etc.) is used for the actual request.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

All methods are `static` and the class holds no mutable state, so `Sign` is safe to call concurrently from multiple threads without synchronisation, subject to two constraints. First, OpenSSL's `HMAC` and BIO APIs are themselves thread-safe as long as the global OpenSSL library has been initialised (OpenSSL 1.1+ initialises automatically; earlier versions require explicit `SSL_library_init`). Second, `GetRfc1123Date` calls `gmtime_r` (POSIX) or `gmtime_s` (Windows) on a local `std::tm`, so there is no shared state there either. `SignedRequest` is a plain value type and is returned by value; ownership transfers entirely to the caller.

---

**Notes:**

- The `Date` header line in the StringToSign is deliberately emitted as an empty string (`"" + "\n"`). This is correct per the Azure specification when `x-ms-date` is present; using both would cause signature failures.
- `x-ms-version` is hard-coded to `"2024-11-04"`. If the Azure Storage service deprecates this API version or a newer feature requires a later version, this string must be updated in `Sign` — there is no way for the caller to override it.
- `CanonicalizedHeaders` only folds headers whose lowercased key starts with `"x-ms-"`. Any custom non-`x-ms-` header in `extraHeaders` is used only for StringToSign content lines (e.g. `Content-Type`) via the `getHeader` lambda; it does not appear in the canonicalized-headers block.
- `CanonicalizedResource` splits the query string on `&` and assumes simple `key=value` pairs. It does not URL-decode percent-encoded characters in parameter names or values before inserting them into the canonical resource string. Callers using percent-encoded query parameters (e.g. SAS tokens or `comp=` parameters) should verify that the raw query string passed through `url` matches exactly what Azure will reconstruct on its side.
- `Base64Decode` returns an empty `std::string` if `BIO_read` returns a negative value (malformed input). `Sign` will then call `HmacSha256` with an empty key, producing a deterministically wrong but non-crashing signature. There is no explicit error return path; callers should ensure `accountKey` is a valid Base64 string before calling `Sign`.
- The BIO chain in `Base64Encode` uses `BIO_FLAGS_BASE64_NO_NL` to suppress newlines, which is required for the Azure signature format. The BIO chain in `Base64Decode` mirrors this flag.


---

### 110_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlMultiDispatcher_h

<a id="110-doc-engine-curlwrapper-curlmultidispatcher-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/110_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlMultiDispatcher_h/docu.output.md`



## CurlMultiDispatcher

**Role:** `CurlMultiDispatcher` is the single network I/O layer for all AI provider HTTP requests in j9t. It owns one dedicated I/O thread that drives libcurl's multi-handle interface, multiplexing all concurrent requests to the same host over a single HTTP/2 TCP/TLS connection rather than blocking thread-pool threads on network I/O. It enforces per-(host, model-family) admission control via `RateLimitController` (AIMD concurrency cap), parses provider rate-limit response headers through the `IRateLimitStrategy` abstraction, automatically retries HTTP 429 and transient errors (400/500/502/503) with exponential backoff, and supports cascade cancellation of all requests belonging to a terminated workflow run. It is the only component that holds the `CURLM*` multi-handle and is the sole entry point for outbound AI HTTP traffic.

---

**Public API:**

- `CurlMultiDispatcher()` — Initialises the `CURLM*` multi-handle with `CURLPIPE_MULTIPLEX`, `MAX_HOST_CONNECTIONS=1`, and `MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS=100`, then starts the I/O thread. Asserts (via `CORE_ASSERT`) that `curl_multi_init()` succeeds. Must be constructed on a thread that can tolerate a new `std::thread` being launched.

- `~CurlMultiDispatcher()` — Calls `SignalStop()` then `WaitStop()` to drain in-flight requests, then cleans up the multi-handle with `curl_multi_cleanup()`. Blocking destructor — callers must not destroy this object while expecting fast teardown.

- `Submit(QueryData const& data, Callback callback) → void` — Enqueues a request onto the internal inbox and wakes the I/O thread via `curl_multi_wakeup()`. Thread-safe; may be called from any thread including from inside a completion callback. The `Callback` signature is `void(QueryResult, std::string responseBody)` and fires on the I/O thread when the request completes, is cancelled, or exhausts retries. Callbacks must be short-lived; file writes are acceptable but blocking operations will stall the entire I/O pipeline.

- `CancelByCancelKey(std::string const& cancelKey) → void` — Thread-safe. Pushes the key onto `m_PendingCancellations` and wakes the I/O thread. On the next I/O thread iteration, `DrainPendingCancellations()` walks all three locations — inbox, retry queue, and active `CURL*` handles — and cancels every request whose `QueryData::m_CancelKey` matches. Each cancelled request fires its callback with `QueryResult::Fail(CURLE_ABORTED_BY_CALLBACK, ...)`. A no-op if `cancelKey` is empty. Called by `AiRequestPool::CancelRequestsForRun` when a workflow run terminates.

- `SignalStop() → void` — Sets `m_Stopping` to `true` atomically and wakes the I/O thread. Non-blocking. After this call, the I/O thread will reject new inbox entries with `CURLE_ABORTED_BY_CALLBACK`, cancel all pending retry entries, and exit once `m_Active` drains to empty.

- `WaitStop() → void` — Blocks the calling thread until the I/O thread joins. Must be called after `SignalStop()` for a clean shutdown. Called automatically by the destructor.

- `GetDebugSnapshot() const → DebugSnapshot` — Returns a point-in-time snapshot of all counters, inbox size, active count, retry queue size, per-host legacy rate-limit state, and per-`QuotaKey` `RateLimitController` state. Acquires `m_InboxMutex` briefly for inbox size and `m_DebugMutex` for everything else. Surfaced via the `/api/debug/signals` endpoint.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **`CurlWrapper::QueryData`** (`curlWrapper.h`) — Input descriptor for each request. Key fields consumed by this class: `m_Url`, `m_Data` (POST body), `m_AuthStyle`, `m_CancelKey`, `m_QuotaKey`, `m_InterfaceType`, `m_TimeoutMs`, `m_MaxRetries429`, `m_MaxRetriesTransient`, `m_BaseRetryMs`, `m_MaxConcurrency`, `m_EstimatedInputTokens`.

- **`IAuthSigner`** (`curlWrapper/authSigner.h`) — Stateless strategy obtained via `IAuthSigner::Get(authStyle)`. Its `Apply()` method produces the per-request auth headers (API keys, AWS SigV4, Azure keys, etc.) that are attached to the `curl_slist` in `SetupEasyHandle()`. No per-provider branching exists in `CurlMultiDispatcher` itself.

- **`IRateLimitStrategy`** (`curlWrapper/rateLimitStrategy.h`) — Stateless strategy obtained via `IRateLimitStrategy::Get(interfaceType)`. Called in `ParseRateLimitHeaders()` to normalise provider-specific response headers into a `RateLimitObservation`. Also consulted for `InitialConcurrencyProbe()` when creating a new `RateLimitController`.

- **`RateLimitController`** (`rateLimitController.h`) — Per-`QuotaKey` stateful AIMD concurrency controller. Lives inside `m_Controllers` (keyed by `QuotaKey = "<host>|<modelFamily>"`). `ShouldAdmit(inflight, estimatedTokens)` gates every inbox drain; `Observe(observation, was429)` is called after every request completes to update the AIMD cap and streak counter.

- **`RateLimitObservation`** — Value type returned by `IRateLimitStrategy::Parse()`. Carries remaining requests, input/output/combined token counts, reset timestamps, retry-after duration, and consumed token counts. Fed into both the legacy `HostRateLimitState` and the `RateLimitController::Observe()`.

- **`Core::g_Core` / `ThreadPool`** (`core.h`) — Checked inside the static `MultiProgressCallback` to abort in-flight curl transfers when the engine's thread pool has stopped (`IsStopped() == true`). This is the only cross-component dependency inside a static libcurl callback.

- **`CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()`** — Used in `SetupEasyHandle()` to set `CURLOPT_CAINFO` when a custom CA bundle path is configured.

- **`QueryResult` / `QueryErrorCode`** — Result type passed to callbacks. Constructed via `QueryResult::Ok()` or `QueryResult::Fail(code, message)`.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- **One I/O thread, all curl handle mutations on it.** `m_Active`, `m_RetryQueue`, `m_HostRateLimits`, `m_Controllers`, the `CURLM*` handle, and all `curl_easy_*` / `curl_multi_*` calls are exclusively owned by and mutated on the I/O thread. The only cross-thread state is the inbox (`m_Inbox` / `m_InboxMutex`), the cancellation queue (`m_PendingCancellations` / `m_PendingCancellationsMutex`), the atomic lifetime counters, and `m_Stopping`.

- **Callbacks fire on the I/O thread.** Any callback that calls `Submit()` re-enters via the inbox path (lock + wakeup), which is safe. Callbacks must not call `WaitStop()` or otherwise join the I/O thread — that would deadlock.

- **`m_DebugMutex` is `std::recursive_mutex`** to allow the I/O thread to hold it at multiple levels of the same call chain (e.g. `DrainCompleted` → `ParseRateLimitHeaders`, both locking it). The debug endpoint on the API thread contends on this mutex rarely and briefly.

- **Lifetime:** `CurlMultiDispatcher` is typically owned by `AiRequestPool` or an equivalent singleton. It must outlive all callers that hold a `Callback` reference since callbacks fire asynchronously on the I/O thread. The destructor blocks until the I/O thread exits and all in-flight `CURL*` easy handles have been cleaned up.

- **`CurlWrapper::QueryData` is copied** into `PendingRequest` at `Submit()` time, so callers may destroy their local copy immediately after calling `Submit()`.

---

**Notes:**

- **Throttle gate stops the entire inbox on first blocked key.** In `DrainInbox()`, when a `RateLimitController::ShouldAdmit()` returns `false` for a request, all remaining items in the local drain batch are pushed back to the inbox unprocesed and the drain loop breaks. This means one throttled key can delay unrelated keys that happen to sit behind it in FIFO order. Requests are not reordered by key.

- **Retry entries re-enter the throttle gate.** `DrainRetryQueue()` moves ready entries back into `m_Inbox`, not directly into `m_Active`. This ensures retried requests pass through `ShouldAdmit()` again rather than bypassing the concurrency cap and potentially re-triggering 429 storms.

- **429 retry delay prefers provider-supplied reset times.** Exponential backoff (`baseRetryMs * 2^(retryCount-1)`) is used as a fallback; if the parsed `HostRateLimitState` has request or token reset timestamps, the delay is set to `max(requestsResetAt, tokensResetAt) - now + 100ms`, capped at 120 seconds.

- **Transient error retry (400/500/502/503) is limited.** Default `kDefaultMaxRetriesTransient = 2`; 429 default is `kDefaultMaxRetries429 = 10`. Per-request overrides come from `QueryData::m_MaxRetries429` and `m_MaxRetriesTransient`.

- **Token quota tracking takes the tightest bucket.** When `ParseRateLimitHeaders()` merges `m_RemainingCombinedTokens`, `m_RemainingInputTokens`, and `m_RemainingOutputTokens` into `HostRateLimitState::m_RemainingTokens`, it selects the minimum non-negative value. This ensures the throttle trips on whichever bucket is most constrained (relevant for Anthropic, which reports input and output token quotas separately).

- **`m_HostRateLimits` is transitional.** Comments in the code mark it as a Phase-2 legacy structure kept alive solely for the `/api/debug/signals` snapshot. The actual admission-control gating has moved to `m_Controllers`. Phase 5 is intended to retire `m_HostRateLimits` entirely.

- **`kMaxActivePerHost = 48`** is a hard ceiling on concurrent HTTP/2 streams per host, independent of provider quota. It applies as the `hardCap` argument to `RateLimitController` when `QueryData::m_MaxConcurrency` is not set.

- **`CURLMOPT_MAX_HOST_CONNECTIONS = 1`** means all requests to the same host share one TCP/TLS connection via HTTP/2 multiplexing. If the server does not support HTTP/2, libcurl falls back to HTTP/1.1 via ALPN negotiation, at which point parallelism degrades to one request per connection.

- **Shutdown does not wait for retries.** When `m_Stopping` is true, the I/O thread fires all pending retry entries with `CURLE_ABORTED_BY_CALLBACK` immediately rather than waiting for their `m_ReadyAt` timers. In-flight `m_Active` handles are allowed to complete naturally (or abort via `MultiProgressCallback`) before the thread exits.

- **`s_QueryCounter` in `DrainCompleted` is a `static std::atomic<uint32_t>`** — a process-wide monotonic query number used only for log correlation. It is not reset between `CurlMultiDispatcher` instances.

### 111_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlWrapper_h

<a id="111-doc-engine-curlwrapper-curlwrapper-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/111_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlWrapper_h/docu.output.md`



## CurlWrapper (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h` + `curlWrapper.cpp`)

**Role:** `CurlWrapper` is the single, low-level HTTP client used by j9t to POST JSON payloads to AI provider REST endpoints. Each instance owns one `CURL*` easy handle and a response accumulation buffer. It manages the one-time global `curl_global_init` call (guarded by a `static std::mutex`), resolves the correct CA bundle for the host OS at startup, attaches the appropriate authentication headers through `IAuthSigner`, handles graceful abort on thread-pool shutdown via a XFERINFO progress callback, and maps the result into a unified `QueryResult`/`QueryErrorCode` scheme that covers libcurl transport errors, HTTP status codes, and j9t-internal pre-flight failures.

---

**Public API:**

- `CurlWrapper()` — Calls `curl_global_init(CURL_GLOBAL_DEFAULT)` exactly once (double-checked lock on a function-local `static bool`), allocates a `curl_easy_handle` via `curl_easy_init`, and applies the resolved CA bundle path with `CURLOPT_CAINFO`. Sets `m_Initialized = true` only when both steps succeed. Logs libcurl version and SSL backend at first construction.

- `~CurlWrapper()` — Calls `curl_easy_cleanup(m_Curl)` if the handle is non-null. Does **not** call `curl_global_cleanup`; that is deferred to `GlobalCleanup()`.

- `IsInitialized() const → bool` — Returns `m_Initialized`. Callers should check this before calling `Query`; an uninitialised wrapper will return `QueryErrorCode::CurlNotInitialized` immediately from `Query`.

- `Query(QueryData const& queryData) → QueryResult` — The main work method. Validates the `QueryData` (non-empty URL, body, and API key), calls `IAuthSigner::Get(queryData.m_AuthStyle).Apply(...)` to populate authentication headers, builds a `CurlSlist`, sets all `curl_easy_setopt` options including `CURLOPT_HTTP_VERSION_2TLS`, applies the optional `m_TimeoutMs`, performs `curl_easy_perform`, inspects the `CURLcode` and then the HTTP response code, and returns a `QueryResult`. Response bytes are accumulated into `m_ReadBuffer` via a lambda write callback. A XFERINFO progress callback returns 1 (aborting the transfer) when `Core::g_Core->GetThreadPool().IsStopped()` is true. On failure, returns `QueryResult::Fail` with either the raw `CURLcode` (1–99), the HTTP status code (100–599), or a 1000+ custom code.

- `GetBuffer() → std::string&` — Returns a mutable reference to the internal response accumulation buffer. Callers (e.g. reply parsers) read the raw JSON from here after a successful `Query` call.

- `Clear()` — Clears `m_ReadBuffer`. Must be called between successive `Query` calls on the same instance; `Query` itself does **not** clear the buffer before performing the transfer.

- `static GlobalCleanup()` — Calls `curl_global_cleanup()`. Intended to be called once at process shutdown, after all `CurlWrapper` instances have been destroyed.

- `static GetCaBundlePath() → std::string const&` — Returns a lazily computed, cached path to the system CA bundle. Resolution order: `CURL_CA_BUNDLE` env → `SSL_CERT_FILE` env → `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt` (Debian/Ubuntu) → `/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt` (Fedora/RHEL) → `/etc/ssl/ca-bundle.pem` (openSUSE) → `/etc/ssl/cert.pem` (Alpine/macOS). Returns an empty string if none exist, which leaves libcurl to use its compiled-in default.

**`QueryData` fields worth noting:**

- `m_AuthStyle` — Selects the authentication signer (Bearer, XGoogApiKey, AnthropicXApiKey, AzureApiKey, AwsSigV4). `Query` passes this to `IAuthSigner::Get`.
- `m_TimeoutMs` — 0 means no timeout; positive values set `CURLOPT_TIMEOUT_MS`.
- `m_Params` — Arbitrary key/value map forwarded to signers that need extra context (e.g. AWS region, secret access key, session token for SigV4/Bedrock).
- `m_InterfaceType`, `m_QuotaKey`, `m_EstimatedInputTokens`, `m_CancelKey`, `m_MaxConcurrency`, `m_MaxRetries429`, `m_MaxRetriesTransient`, `m_BaseRetryMs` — Metadata consumed upstream by `CurlMultiDispatcher` and `AiRequestPool`; `CurlWrapper::Query` itself does not read them.
- `IsValid() const → bool` — Returns true only when URL, body (`m_Data`), and `m_ApiKey` are all non-empty. Logs at CRITICAL level for each empty field.

**`CurlSlist`:** RAII wrapper around `curl_slist*`. `Append(str)` calls `curl_slist_append`; destructor calls `curl_slist_free_all`. Used internally by `Query` to build the HTTP header list.

**`QueryErrorCode::Describe(int code) → std::string`:** Returns a human-readable label. Delegates codes 1–99 to `curl_easy_strerror`, provides explicit strings for common HTTP codes, and has a `switch` for all 1000+ custom codes.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `IAuthSigner` (`curlWrapper/authSigner.h`) — `Query` calls `IAuthSigner::Get(authStyle).Apply(queryData, authHeaders)` to produce the provider-specific auth header strings before building the `CurlSlist`. The signer for `AwsSigV4` reads `m_Params` for region and credentials.
- `Core` / `Core::g_Core` (`core.h`) — The XFERINFO progress callback dereferences `Core::g_Core` to read `GetThreadPool().IsStopped()`. If `g_Core` is null the check is skipped. `Query` also reads `Core::g_Core->Verbose()` to enable `CURLOPT_VERBOSE`.
- `CurlMultiDispatcher` / `AiRequestPool` — Higher-level callers that construct `QueryData`, call `Query`, then read `GetBuffer()` to feed the reply parser. They are responsible for calling `Clear()` between calls.
- `ReplyParser` (`json/replyParser.h`) — Included in the `.cpp`; the parsed result of `m_ReadBuffer` is consumed after `Query` returns.
- Tracy (`tracy/Tracy.hpp`) — `curl_easy_perform` is wrapped in a `ZoneScopedNC` zone named `"curl_easy_perform(m_Curl)"` coloured blue when `TRACY_ENABLE` is defined.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- One `CurlWrapper` instance **must not** be shared across threads. `CURL*` easy handles are not thread-safe for concurrent use. Each worker thread that issues HTTP requests should own its own instance.
- `curl_global_init` is called at most once, protected by a `static std::mutex` inside the constructor. Constructing multiple `CurlWrapper` objects concurrently on different threads is safe.
- `m_QueryCounter` is `static std::atomic<uint32_t>`, shared across all instances and all threads; increments in `Query` are for diagnostic logging only and carry no synchronisation guarantees about request ordering.
- The `GetCaBundlePath` result is a `static std::string` computed once via a immediately-invoked lambda; safe to read from multiple threads after first construction, but the first construction itself must not race (guaranteed by the C++11 static-local initialisation rules).
- `GlobalCleanup()` must only be called after all `CurlWrapper` instances are destroyed and no libcurl operation is in progress.
- Move construction/assignment transfer ownership of `m_Curl`; the default-generated move operations set the source `m_Curl` to null only if `CURL*` is a trivially movable pointer — callers should not use a moved-from instance.

---

**Notes:**

- `Query` does **not** call `Clear()` on `m_ReadBuffer` before performing the transfer. If the same instance is reused, the caller must call `Clear()` explicitly or the new response will be appended to stale data.
- HTTP/2 is requested via `CURLOPT_HTTP_VERSION_2TLS`; libcurl falls back to HTTP/1.1 through ALPN negotiation if the server does not support it. The actual protocol used is logged after each query.
- The `CURLOPT_POSTFIELDS` pointer points directly into `queryData.m_Data`. `queryData` must remain alive and unmodified for the entire duration of `curl_easy_perform`.
- HTTP status codes ≥ 400 cause `QueryResult::Fail` even when `curl_easy_perform` returns `CURLE_OK`. Callers must check `QueryResult::m_Ok`, not just the absence of a CURLcode error.
- A 429 response gets a dedicated, more descriptive log message mentioning billing balance, distinct from the generic HTTP error path.
- The `AuthStyle::AwsSigV4` path signs the request body and emits `Authorization`, `X-Amz-Date`, and `X-Amz-Content-Sha256` headers. The required auxiliary values (`region`, `secret_access_key`, `session_token`) must be present in `QueryData::m_Params`; their absence is a signer-level concern, not validated by `CurlWrapper` itself.
- `QueryData::m_InterfaceType` is stored as `int` rather than `ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType` to avoid a `configParser.h` include in this header. The value `-1` is the sentinel for "not set", mirroring `InterfaceType::InvalidAPI`.


---

### 112_doc_engine_curlWrapper_rateLimitController_h

<a id="112-doc-engine-curlwrapper-ratelimitcontroller-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/112_doc_engine_curlWrapper_rateLimitController_h/docu.output.md`



## RateLimitController

**Role:** `RateLimitController` is a per-(host, model-family) adaptive admission controller that decides whether the next AI request should be dispatched immediately or deferred, and updates its internal model after each completed request. It composes three orthogonal mechanisms described in *AI call performance optimization.md* §4: a token-bucket mirror that tracks the provider's stated quota headroom (§4.1), an AIMD (Additive Increase / Multiplicative Decrease) concurrency cap that converges on a sustainable in-flight count through trial and back-off (§4.2), and a server-directed wait floor derived from `Retry-After` headers and provider reset timestamps (§4.3). All three mechanisms are evaluated in priority order inside a single `ShouldAdmit` call with no blocking or sleeping.

**Public API:**

- `RateLimitController(int initialConcurrencyProbe, int hardCap)` — Constructs the controller. `initialConcurrencyProbe` seeds `m_CurrentConcurrencyCap` and is clamped to `[1, hardCap]`; `hardCap` is itself floored to 1 and stored as the immutable `m_HardCap`. In Phase 2 the dispatcher supplies `kMaxActivePerHost` for `hardCap`; in Phase 4 a config field `rate_limit.max_concurrency` may override it.

- `ShouldAdmit(int currentInflight, int64_t estimatedInputTokens) const → Decision` — Pure read of current state; no mutations. Returns a `Decision` with `m_Admit = true` only when all three guards pass. Checks are applied in this fixed order: (1) `currentInflight >= m_HardCap` → deny with reason `"hard cap"`; (2) `currentInflight >= m_CurrentConcurrencyCap` → deny with reason `"AIMD cap"`; (3) if `m_RequestsResetAt` is in the future and the projected remaining request count (`m_RemainingRequests - currentInflight`) drops to zero or below → deny with reason `"provider req quota"` and sets `m_NextAttemptAt` to `m_RequestsResetAt`; (4) if `m_TokensResetAt` is in the future and the tightest of the three remaining-token fields minus `estimatedInputTokens` is zero or below → deny with reason `"provider token quota"` and sets `m_NextAttemptAt` to `m_TokensResetAt`. `estimatedInputTokens` is expected to come from `strategy.EstimateInputTokens(prompt)` at the call site. `m_Reason` always points to a string literal; it never needs to be freed.

- `Observe(RateLimitObservation const& observation, bool was429) → void` — Mutates all internal state. Merges `observation` into `m_LastObservation` field-by-field using an "overwrite if known" rule: numeric fields overwrite only when `>= 0`; `std::optional` timestamp fields overwrite only when `has_value()`. If `observation.m_RetryAfter` is set, it is converted to an absolute `steady_clock` time point and applied as a forward floor to *both* `m_RequestsResetAt` and `m_TokensResetAt`, even if those fields already have values — the floor only moves forward, never backward. After merging, AIMD runs: `was429 == true` halves `m_CurrentConcurrencyCap` (floor 1) and resets `m_StreakSinceLast429` to 0; `was429 == false` increments the streak, and every `kStreakForIncrease` (5) clean completions increments `m_CurrentConcurrencyCap` by 1 up to `m_HardCap`, then resets the streak.

- `CurrentConcurrencyCap() const → int` — Returns the live AIMD cap; intended for dispatcher debug snapshots only.

- `StreakSinceLast429() const → int` — Returns the current clean-completion streak; debug/snapshot use only.

- `LastObservation() const → RateLimitObservation const&` — Returns a const reference to the merged observation state; debug/snapshot use only. Caller must not hold this reference across any subsequent `Observe()` call.

**Collaborators:**

- `RateLimitObservation` (`rateLimitObservation.h`) — The value type carrying all provider-sourced quota fields: `m_RemainingRequests`, `m_RemainingInputTokens`, `m_RemainingOutputTokens`, `m_RemainingCombinedTokens`, `m_RequestsResetAt`, `m_TokensResetAt`, `m_RetryAfter`, `m_ConsumedInputTokens`, `m_ConsumedOutputTokens`. `RateLimitController` stores one as `m_LastObservation` and merges incoming observations into it.

- `CurlMultiDispatcher` — The sole owner of `RateLimitController` instances, one per (host, model-family) key. It is responsible for calling `ShouldAdmit` before dispatching and `Observe` after a response completes or a 429 is received.

- *AI call performance optimization.md* §4 — The authoritative design document for the three-mechanism architecture; section references appear in comments throughout the source.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`RateLimitController` instances are owned exclusively by `CurlMultiDispatcher`. All access — both `ShouldAdmit` and `Observe` — occurs only while `CurlMultiDispatcher::m_DebugMutex` is held. **No internal synchronisation is provided.** There are no shared pointers, no reference counting, and no transfer of ownership. The class must not be accessed from any thread that does not hold that external mutex.

**Notes:**

- `ShouldAdmit` is `const` and takes a snapshot of `steady_clock::now()` internally; the comparison against reset timestamps uses that single snapshot, so there is no TOCTOU between the two quota checks within a single call.
- The token-bucket check in `ShouldAdmit` takes the *minimum* across whichever of `m_RemainingCombinedTokens`, `m_RemainingInputTokens`, and `m_RemainingOutputTokens` are non-negative. Fields that are negative (meaning the provider did not report them) are excluded from the minimum via the `consider` lambda. If none are non-negative, the token bucket check is skipped entirely.
- The request-quota check in `ShouldAdmit` adjusts for in-flight requests that the provider has not yet acknowledged: it subtracts `currentInflight` from `m_RemainingRequests`. This projection can be pessimistic but prevents piling additional requests onto an already-saturated quota window.
- `Observe` is designed to be called multiple times per request without producing inconsistent state — specifically to accommodate streaming responses where HTTP headers and body arrive separately. Calling it twice with the same fields produces the same final state as calling it once with both fields combined, because known fields simply overwrite and the `Retry-After` floor is monotonically non-decreasing.
- `kStreakForIncrease = 5` is a compile-time constant; there is currently no runtime configuration path for it. The hard cap (`m_HardCap`) is truly immutable after construction — it is `const`.
- `Decision::m_Reason` is always a pointer to a string literal in the translation unit. Callers must not free it and must not assume it is unique or stable across different `Decision` instances beyond the standard string literal lifetime.

### 112_doc_engine_engine_h

<a id="112-doc-engine-engine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/112_doc_engine_engine_h/docu.output.md`



### LOW: Potential Secrets Leakage via Logging
- **Location:** All LOG_* macros, throughout file (macros starting line ~14, usages throughout)
- **Issue:** Logging macros such as `LOG_CORE_INFO`, `LOG_CORE_WARN`, etc., may log sensitive data if invoked with secrets or key material, especially during startup or error conditions (e.g., the key load process in `engine` line ~109–154). There is no evidence in this file that secrets are logged, but the risk exists if calling code logs key content or sensitive parameters.
- **Impact:** Leaked secrets or credentials in logs could be accessed by unauthorized actors, especially in environments where logs are less protected than memory.
- **Fix:** Ensure all invocations of the logging macros—and the REST endpoint `/api/settings/keys/unlock`—never log secrets, API keys, master passwords, or decrypted material. Consider updating documentation and code comments to warn developers against this pattern.

### LOW: Use of `exit()` and `_exit()` in Macros and Watchdog
- **Location:** CORE_HARD_STOP macro (line ~37), engine shutdown watchdog lambda (line ~221)
- **Issue:** Use of `exit(EXIT_FAILURE)` and `_exit(EXIT_FAILURE)` for error/timeout handling. While these are appropriate for killing the process, if not all sensitive material is wiped from memory first, secrets may persist on crash dumps or residual memory.
- **Impact:** In rare edge cases (e.g., core dump enabled, or OS paging), secrets might be recoverable after a forced exit.
- **Fix:** Before calling `exit` or `_exit`, consider explicitly zeroing out memory used for secrets (e.g., via SecureZeroMemory/explicit_bzero for sensitive buffers, if applicable).

### NONE
No critical, high, or medium security issues identified in this file.

### 113_doc_engine_curlWrapper_rateLimitObservation_h

<a id="113-doc-engine-curlwrapper-ratelimitobservation-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/113_doc_engine_curlWrapper_rateLimitObservation_h/docu.output.md`



## RateLimitObservation

**Role:** `RateLimitObservation` is a plain data struct (defined in `rateLimitObservation.h`, namespace `AIAssistant`) that carries a single provider-agnostic snapshot of rate-limit state extracted from one HTTP response — its headers and optional body. It is the unit of communication between the per-provider extraction layer (`rateLimitStrategy.h` implementations) and the rate-limit controller (Phase 2). Every field has a well-defined "unknown" sentinel (`-1` for integers, `std::nullopt` for optionals) so that partial observations from providers that only populate a subset of headers can be safely merged without erasing previously known values.

**Public API:**

- `IsEmpty() → bool` — Returns `true` when every field in the struct holds its default sentinel value, meaning the HTTP response contributed no extractable rate-limit information whatsoever. Useful in the controller to skip the merge step entirely when a strategy could not parse anything from a response.

**Collaborators:**

- `rateLimitStrategy.h` (per-provider strategy implementations) — these are the producers; each strategy populates whichever fields it can extract from provider-specific response headers and body (e.g., OpenAI's `x-ratelimit-remaining-requests`, Anthropic's split input/output token headers, Gemini/Bedrock's `Retry-After`). Fields the provider does not supply are left at their sentinel defaults.
- Rate-limit controller (Phase 2, not in this file) — the consumer; it merges successive `RateLimitObservation` instances using a replacement-if-known, preservation-if-unknown policy so that a sparse observation never overwrites a previously known value with an unknown sentinel.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`RateLimitObservation` is a value type with no internal synchronisation. It is intended to be constructed on the stack or by value inside the strategy, returned by value, and consumed immediately by the controller. No heap ownership, no shared state, no locking concerns. Callers must not share a single instance across threads; pass by value or protect externally if sharing is required.

**Notes:**

- The integer sentinel is `-1`, not `0`. A value of `0` is a valid and meaningful observation (e.g., zero requests remaining), so callers must test `< 0`, not `== 0`, to detect "unknown."
- `m_RemainingCombinedTokens` is a distinct field from `m_RemainingInputTokens` / `m_RemainingOutputTokens` to accommodate providers like OpenAI that report a single aggregate token quota rather than split quotas. Strategies set whichever form the provider uses; the controller must handle both being populated simultaneously or only one of them.
- `m_ConsumedInputTokens` / `m_ConsumedOutputTokens` are sourced from the response body (`usage.input_tokens` / `usage.output_tokens`), not from headers. This intentional separation lets the controller independently track actual consumption versus provider-reported remaining capacity, which is critical when the provider's remaining-count header lags behind actual usage.
- `m_RequestsResetAt` and `m_TokensResetAt` use `std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point`. Strategies that parse wall-clock reset times from provider headers (typically ISO-8601 or Unix epoch values) must convert them to the steady clock before populating these fields to avoid cross-clock comparison bugs in the controller.
- `m_RetryAfter` carries a `std::chrono::milliseconds` duration (relative, not absolute), matching the semantics of the HTTP `Retry-After` header as a delay rather than a point in time. The controller is responsible for adding it to the current time when scheduling a retry.

### 114_doc_engine_curlWrapper_rateLimitStrategy_h

<a id="114-doc-engine-curlwrapper-ratelimitstrategy-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/114_doc_engine_curlWrapper_rateLimitStrategy_h/docu.output.md`



## IRateLimitStrategy / rateLimitStrategy

**Role:** This file defines the `IRateLimitStrategy` interface and its three concrete singleton implementations (`RateLimitStrategyEmpty`, `RateLimitStrategyOpenAI`, `RateLimitStrategyAnthropic`), which together give the dispatcher a per-provider policy for three things: extracting rate-limit state from raw HTTP responses (`Parse`), mapping model IDs to quota bucket keys (`DeriveQuotaKey`), and bounding early concurrency before any observations exist (`InitialConcurrencyProbe`). The factory `Get()` dispatches on `ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType` to the appropriate singleton, covering API1/API2/API6 (OpenAI family), API4 (Anthropic Messages), and API3/API5/Test (empty/stub). A default `EstimateInputTokens` implementation using a chars-divided-by-4 heuristic is provided in the base class so callers can pre-screen token costs before sending a request.

**Public API:**

- `Parse(headerBuffer, responseBody, httpStatus) → RateLimitObservation` — Parses the raw accumulated header buffer (lines in `"Name: Value\r\n"` format) and, where needed, the response body and HTTP status code into a `RateLimitObservation`. The OpenAI strategy reads `x-ratelimit-remaining-requests`, `x-ratelimit-remaining-tokens`, `x-ratelimit-reset-requests`, `x-ratelimit-reset-tokens`, and `retry-after`. The Anthropic strategy reads `anthropic-ratelimit-requests-remaining`, `anthropic-ratelimit-tokens-remaining`, `anthropic-ratelimit-input-tokens-remaining`, `anthropic-ratelimit-output-tokens-remaining`, the corresponding `-reset` headers (ISO 8601 UTC, converted to a steady-clock delta), and `retry-after` in integer seconds. For token-reset fields, the Anthropic strategy takes the latest value across all three token-bucket reset headers. The empty strategy always returns a default-constructed `RateLimitObservation`. No I/O or state mutation occurs; the method is pure relative to external state.

- `DeriveQuotaKey(model) → std::string` — Maps a model identifier string to the quota-bucket family name used to key the AIMD rate-limit controller. Both OpenAI and Anthropic strategies extract the substring up to and including the second hyphen (e.g., `"gpt-4o-mini"` → `"gpt-4o"`, `"claude-sonnet-4-6"` → `"claude-sonnet"`). Models with fewer than two hyphens are returned as-is. The empty strategy always returns an empty string, signalling to the controller that there is one bucket per host.

- `InitialConcurrencyProbe() → int` — Returns the hard upper bound on inflight requests before the first `RateLimitObservation` is received. OpenAI returns `8`; Anthropic and the empty strategy return `4`. The AIMD controller grows from this value on successful completions and halves it on a 429.

- `EstimateInputTokens(concatenatedPrompt) → int64_t` — Non-virtual default implementation: returns `prompt.size() / 4`. Called by `AiRequestPool::Submit` with the concatenated content of all message objects. Concrete strategies may override this with provider-specific tokenizers. The real token count from the response body's `usage` field supersedes this estimate once a request completes.

- `static Get(interfaceType) → IRateLimitStrategy const&` — Factory returning a reference to one of three file-scoped singletons: `s_OpenAI` for API1, API2, API6; `s_Anthropic` for API4; `s_Empty` for API3, API5, Test, `NumAPIs`, and `InvalidAPI`. A `static_assert` enforces that `NumAPIs == 7`; adding a new `InterfaceType` without updating this switch breaks the build.

**Collaborators:**

- `RateLimitObservation` (`rateLimitObservation.h`) — the value type `Parse` populates; carries optional remaining-request/token counts, reset time points (`std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point`), and an optional `retry-after` duration.
- `ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType` (`json/configParser.h`) — the enum over which `Get()` switches; must stay in sync with the `static_assert` on `NumAPIs`.
- `AiRequestPool::Submit` — the documented caller of `EstimateInputTokens`; responsible for concatenating message content before calling.
- The AIMD rate-limit controller (not in this file) — consumes the `RateLimitObservation` returned by `Parse`, reads `InitialConcurrencyProbe` at startup, and is keyed by `(host, DeriveQuotaKey result)`.
- Internal helpers `IterateHeaderLines`, `ParseOpenAIDurationMs`, and `ParseIso8601DeltaSec` are file-local (anonymous namespace) and not part of the public surface.

**Threading / lifetime:**

All three concrete strategy objects (`s_OpenAI`, `s_Anthropic`, `s_Empty`) are function-scope statics with no mutable state, making them safe to call concurrently from any thread without locking. `Get()` returns a `const&` to these statics; callers must not store a non-const pointer to them nor attempt to delete them. The objects live for the duration of the process. `Parse` captures `std::chrono::steady_clock::now()` at entry and uses it for all reset-time calculations within a single call, so all time points in one `RateLimitObservation` share a consistent `now` baseline.

**Notes:**

- `ParseIso8601DeltaSec` uses `timegm` (POSIX, not standard C++); it rejects deltas ≤ 0 or ≥ 7200 seconds as garbage, returning `-1`. Anthropic reset timestamps more than two hours in the future are silently dropped.
- `ParseOpenAIDurationMs` is purely additive and handles `ms`, `s`, and `m` unit suffixes in any order within the value string. Non-digit, non-unit characters are skipped, so a malformed value produces `0` (treated as no reset time).
- The Anthropic token-reset logic deliberately takes the maximum across `anthropic-ratelimit-tokens-reset`, `anthropic-ratelimit-input-tokens-reset`, and `anthropic-ratelimit-output-tokens-reset`, intentionally waiting for the slowest-refilling bucket.
- `retry-after` parsing on both strategies silently ignores values outside `(0, 7200)` seconds and swallows all exceptions from `std::stoi`/`std::stoll`, leaving the corresponding `RateLimitObservation` fields empty on any parse failure.
- API3 (Gemini), API5 (Bedrock), and the Test interface all receive the empty strategy. Comments in the code explicitly flag these as Phase 3 work pending verification of what headers those providers actually expose.
- The `static_assert(NumAPIs == 7, …)` is the enforcement mechanism for the "no default: arm" discipline rule; it is a compile-time tripwire, not a runtime check.

### 115_doc_engine_engine_h

<a id="115-doc-engine-engine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/115_doc_engine_engine_h/docu.output.md`



## engine.h / engine.cpp

**Role:** This file pair forms the top-level entry point of the j9t/JarvisAgent workflow engine. `engine.h` declares the single entry-point function `engine()` and defines the entire macro-based logging and assertion system used throughout the codebase. `engine.cpp` implements the full startup sequence: CLI argument parsing, `config.json` validation, key-loading strategy (encrypted store → plaintext `keys.json` → `OPENAI_API_KEY` environment variable), cloud connection loading from `connections.json`, `Core` initialization, `JarvisAgent` application construction and execution, and a deterministic 3-second shutdown watchdog that forces `_exit` if the graceful teardown sequence stalls.

---

**Public API:**

- `engine(int argc, char* argv[]) → int` — The sole exported function; called from `main()`. Parses `--help` / `-h`, `--version` / `-v`, and rejects any unknown `-` prefixed flags with a non-zero exit. If proceeding with startup, it checks for `config.json` in the current working directory, constructs a `Core` instance, runs `ConfigParser` and `ConfigChecker` on the config, initialises `KeyManager` through the three-tier key loading strategy, loads `connections.json` into `CloudConnectionManager`, calls `Core::Start()`, instantiates the `JarvisAgent` application via `JarvisAgent::Create()`, calls `app->OnStart()`, hands control to `Core::Run(app)`, and finally drives the ordered shutdown sequence (`app->OnShutdown()` then `engine->Shutdown()`). Returns `EXIT_SUCCESS` on clean exit, `EXIT_FAILURE` on config errors or unknown CLI flags.

**Logging macros (defined in `engine.h`):**

- `LOG_CORE_TRACE/INFO/WARN/ERROR/CRITICAL(...)` — Route to `Core::g_Logger->GetLogger()` (the engine/core spdlog channel). All expand to no-ops when `DISTRIBUTION_BUILD` is defined.
- `LOG_APP_TRACE/INFO/WARN/ERROR/CRITICAL(...)` — Route to `Core::g_Logger->GetAppLogger()` (the application-level spdlog channel). Same distribution-build stripping applies.
- `LOG_SECURITY_INFO/WARN(...)` — Route to `Core::g_Logger->GetSecurityLogger()`. Stripped in distribution builds.
- `CORE_ASSERT(x, str)` — Logs a critical message with file and line if `x` is false. Does **not** abort; execution continues. No-op in distribution builds.
- `CORE_HARD_STOP(str)` — Logs critical, prints to stdout, then calls `exit(EXIT_FAILURE)`. Effectively a fatal abort with a diagnostic. Expands to an empty block in distribution builds — callers must not rely on it terminating in release.
- `APP_ASSERT(x, str)` — Same behaviour as `CORE_ASSERT` but routes to the app logger channel. No-op in distribution builds.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `Core` (`core.h`) — Owns the global singleton `Core::g_Core` and `Core::g_Logger`. `engine()` creates it via `std::make_unique<Core>()`, calls `Start(engineConfig)`, `Run(app)`, and `Shutdown()` in strict sequence.
- `ConfigParser` (`json/configParser.h`) — Parses `config.json` into a `ConfigParser::EngineConfig` value type. Called once before `Core::Start()`.
- `ConfigChecker` (`json/configChecker.h`) — Validates the parsed `EngineConfig` in-place; sets `engineConfig.IsValid()` to false on any structural or semantic error.
- `KeyManager` — Accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`. Receives the resolved keys-file path and is driven through one of three load paths; status is explicitly set to `KeyManager::KeyLoadStatus::NoPassword` or `KeyManager::KeyLoadStatus::Ok`.
- `CloudConnectionManager` — Accessed via `engine->GetCloudConnectionManager()`. Populated from `connections.json` if the file exists; `ClearDirty()` is called after a successful load to suppress a spurious pending-save flag.
- `JarvisAgent` (`jarvisAgent.h`) — Concrete `AIAssistant::Application` subclass created by `JarvisAgent::Create()`. Lifecycle methods `OnStart()`, `OnShutdown()`, and `GetFatalStartupMessage()` are called by `engine()`.
- `SigV4Signer` (`curlWrapper/awsSigV4.h`) — `SigV4Signer::RunSelfTest()` is called between `Core::Start()` and application construction, but only in debug builds (`#ifndef NDEBUG`).
- `log/log.h` — Pulled into `engine.h`; provides the `Logger` abstraction that the logging macros delegate to.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `Core` is heap-allocated as a `std::unique_ptr<Core>` local to `engine()` and lives for the entire duration of the process after construction. The global raw pointer `Core::g_Core` is populated inside the `Core` constructor; all logging macros that dereference it are therefore only safe after the `Core` constructor completes and before `engine()` returns.
- The shutdown watchdog is a detached `std::thread` that waits on a `std::condition_variable` for up to 3 seconds. If `app->OnShutdown()` or `engine->Shutdown()` block longer than 3 seconds total, the watchdog calls `_exit(EXIT_FAILURE)`, bypassing all destructors. The watchdog is disarmed by setting `watchdogDiffused = true` under `watchdogMutex` and calling `watchdogCV.notify_one()`.
- Because the watchdog is detached, references it holds (`watchdogMutex`, `watchdogCV`, `watchdogDiffused`) must remain valid until either the watchdog fires or it is diffused. All three are stack locals in `engine()`; the detached thread must not outlive `engine()`'s stack frame, which is guaranteed only by the 3-second hard timeout path using `_exit` rather than `return`.
- `engine()` is single-threaded from the caller's perspective; it is intended to be called exactly once from `main()` and blocks until the application exits.

---

**Notes:**

- **Key loading priority is strictly ordered and mutually exclusive in effect.** If an encrypted `keys.json.enc` (or whatever `m_KeysFilePath` resolves to) exists, the `KeyManager` is placed in `NoPassword` state and neither the plaintext fallback nor the environment variable fallback is attempted — the `keysLoaded` flag stays false and the env-var branch is also guarded by `!std::filesystem::exists(keysFilePathAbsolute)`. The encrypted file's mere presence blocks the env-var fallback; this is intentional to prevent `OPENAI_API_KEY` from silently masking a locked sealed store and causing the dashboard to falsely report healthy key state.
- **`CORE_ASSERT` does not halt.** Unlike a standard assert, `CORE_ASSERT(x, str)` only logs; a false condition does not stop execution. Callers that need a hard stop must use `CORE_HARD_STOP`.
- **`CORE_HARD_STOP` is a no-op in distribution builds.** Code that calls `CORE_HARD_STOP` in non-distribution builds must handle the fall-through case in release, since the macro expands to `{}`.
- **`LOGGING_AND_ASSERTS` is the controlling guard**, not `NDEBUG`. It is enabled whenever `DISTRIBUTION_BUILD` is not defined, which means debug and RelWithDebInfo builds both get full logging. Do not use `NDEBUG` to infer whether logging macros are active.
- Fatal startup messages (e.g., a port-binding failure surfaced by `JarvisAgent`) are deliberately printed **after** `app->OnShutdown()` so that any curses/TUI teardown has already restored the terminal before the message appears.
- Raw `::write`/`_write` calls to `stderr` (via the `RAW_ENGINE` macro) are used during shutdown sequencing specifically because they are async-signal-safe and bypass any buffered I/O that may have been corrupted by the TUI layer at that point.

### 116_doc_engine_event_applicationEvent_h

<a id="116-doc-engine-event-applicationevent-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/116_doc_engine_event_applicationEvent_h/docu.output.md`



## AppErrorEvent

**Role:** `AppErrorEvent` is a lightweight, typed event class within the `AIAssistant` namespace that represents application-level error conditions propagated through the engine's event system. It carries a strongly-typed `ErrorCode` enum value and is dispatched like any other `Event` subclass, allowing event handlers elsewhere in the system to respond to specific error conditions (currently only `AppErrorBadCurl = 1000`, indicating a libcurl initialization or runtime failure) without direct coupling to the site where the error originates.

**Public API:**

- `AppErrorEvent(ErrorCode errorCode)` — Constructs the event with the specified `ErrorCode`. The sole constructor argument is stored directly; no heap allocation or external resource acquisition occurs. The only currently defined code is `AppErrorBadCurl (1000)`.
- `GetErrorCode() → int` — Returns the stored error code cast to `int`. The cast is explicit, so callers receiving an `int` must compare against known `ErrorCode` values or the raw integer `1000` to identify the condition.
- `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(AppError)` — Macro-generated overrides (from `event/event.h`) that supply `GetStaticType()`, `GetEventType()`, and `GetName()` returning the `AppError` event type identifier. Used by the dispatcher to route events by type.
- `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryApp)` — Macro-generated override that returns `EventCategoryApp` as the category bitmask, enabling category-level filtering in the event dispatcher.

**Collaborators:**

- `event/event.h` — Defines the `Event` base class and the `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE` / `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY` macros. The entire dispatch, type registration, and category filtering mechanism lives there; `AppErrorEvent` contributes nothing beyond the error code payload.
- libcurl — Indirectly implicated: `AppErrorBadCurl` is the only defined error code, suggesting the curl integration layer (wherever it lives in the engine) constructs and fires this event on curl failure rather than throwing or logging inline.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`AppErrorEvent` is a value-semantic, stack-friendly object with no shared or dynamic state. Lifetime follows standard `Event` dispatch conventions: the object is typically constructed at the error site, passed by reference into the event bus or dispatcher, and must remain alive for the duration of dispatch. Because the event system in j9t is generally single-threaded at the dispatch call site, no internal locking is provided or needed. If events are ever posted across threads, the caller is responsible for ensuring the object outlives the dispatch call.

**Notes:**

- `GetErrorCode()` returns `int`, not `ErrorCode`, so type-safe switching on the enum requires an explicit cast back on the caller side.
- Only one error code is currently defined (`AppErrorBadCurl = 1000`). The gap from 0 implies intentional namespace partitioning — values below 1000 are presumably reserved for other event or error categories.
- The class carries no message string, timestamp, or contextual payload beyond the code. Diagnostic detail must be captured elsewhere (e.g., logged before the event is fired) because the event itself provides no retrieval mechanism for it.

### 117_doc_engine_event_engineEvent_h

<a id="117-doc-engine-event-engineevent-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/117_doc_engine_event_engineEvent_h/docu.output.md`



## EngineEvent

**Role:** `EngineEvent` is a thin event class within the `AIAssistant` namespace that represents engine-level lifecycle signals — currently restricted to a single code, `EngineEventShutdown` (value 2000). It extends the base `Event` type (declared in `event/event.h`) and participates in the engine's event dispatch system by declaring its type and category via the `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE` and `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY` macros, which are expected to generate the boilerplate required for type identification and category filtering.

**Public API:**

- `EngineEvent(EngineEventCode engineEventCode)` — Constructs the event with a specific `EngineEventCode` value. Currently the only valid value is `EngineEventShutdown = 2000`. The code is stored internally and accessible via the getter.

- `GetEngineCode() const → int` — Returns the stored `EngineEventCode` cast to `int`. Callers should compare the returned value against the `EngineEventCode` enum members rather than raw integers, though the cast means both are possible.

- `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(EngineEvent)` — Macro-generated; expected to produce `GetStaticType()`, `GetEventType()`, and `GetName()` overrides that identify this event as `EngineEvent` within the dispatch system.

- `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryEngine)` — Macro-generated; expected to produce `GetCategoryFlags()` returning a bitmask that includes `EventCategoryEngine`, enabling category-based filtering in event listeners.

**Collaborators:**

- `Event` (`event/event.h`) — The base class providing the virtual interface and macro expansion points that `EngineEvent` satisfies. The dispatch, routing, and handling infrastructure is defined there.
- `EventCategoryEngine` — A category constant expected to be defined in `event/event.h` or an associated header; must exist for `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY` to compile.

**Threading / lifetime:** `EngineEvent` instances are value-like and carry no heap-allocated state. Ownership follows whatever convention the broader `Event` dispatch system uses — typically stack-allocated at the dispatch site or managed by the event queue. No synchronisation is performed inside this class; thread safety at the point of construction and dispatch is the caller's responsibility.

**Notes:**

- The `EngineEventCode` enum has only one member (`EngineEventShutdown = 2000`). The gap from zero implies that other event families occupy lower numeric ranges; this value was intentionally spaced to avoid collisions with those ranges.
- `GetEngineCode()` returns `int`, not `EngineEventCode`, so callers lose type safety on the return value. Compare against the enum explicitly to avoid silent mismatch if additional codes are added later.
- The file is a header-only definition (`#pragma once`, no corresponding `.cpp`). Any translation unit that includes it will compile the constructor and getter inline.

### 118_doc_engine_event_event_h

<a id="118-doc-engine-event-event-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/118_doc_engine_event_event_h/docu.output.md`



## Events.h (AIAssistant Event System)

**Role:** This header defines the foundational event model for the j9t / AIAssistant workflow engine. It declares the full enumeration of event types the engine recognises — ranging from keyboard and mouse input through filesystem notifications, embedded-Python crashes, and the full AI-call dispatch lifecycle (started, retrying, completed, failed) — plus bitmask categories for grouping them. It also provides the abstract `Event` base class that all concrete event objects must inherit, and the `EventDispatcher` utility that routes a single `Event` reference to a typed handler function at zero overhead when the type does not match.

**Public API:**

**`Event` (abstract base class)**

- `GetEventType() const → EventType` — pure virtual; every concrete subclass must return its `EventType` enumerator. Used by `EventDispatcher` for type matching.
- `GetName() const → const char*` — pure virtual; returns a human-readable string name for the event, typically the enumerator name via the `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE` macro.
- `GetCategoryFlags() const → int` — pure virtual; returns a bitmask of `EventCategory` bits. Used by `IsInCategory`.
- `IsInCategory(EventCategory category) const → bool` — returns true when the event's category bitmask includes the supplied category bit. Does not modify state.
- `IsHandled() const → bool` — returns the current handled flag. Once set, higher-level dispatch loops are expected to stop propagating the event.
- `SetHandled(bool handled = true)` — marks the event as handled (or un-handled if `false` is passed explicitly). Called by `EventDispatcher::Dispatch` automatically when the handler returns `true`.
- `ToString() const → std::string` — virtual; default implementation returns `GetName()`. Concrete subclasses may override to append payload data (key codes, file paths, AI model identifiers, etc.).

**`EventDispatcher`**

- `EventDispatcher(Event& event)` — constructor; binds the dispatcher to a single, caller-owned `Event` reference for its entire lifetime. The event must outlive the dispatcher.
- `Dispatch<T>(EventFn<T> func) → bool` — checks whether the bound event's runtime type matches `T::GetStaticType()`. If it matches, casts the event to `T&` and calls `func`; the return value of `func` is forwarded to `Event::SetHandled`. Returns `true` if the types matched (regardless of whether the handler returned `true` or `false`), `false` otherwise. Multiple `Dispatch` calls on the same dispatcher against different types are safe and expected.

**Macros**

- `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(type)` — injects `GetStaticType()` (static), `GetEventType()` (virtual override), and `GetName()` (virtual override) into a concrete subclass. `GetStaticType()` is the static method `EventDispatcher` compares against.
- `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(category)` — injects `GetCategoryFlags()` (virtual override) returning the supplied category bitmask expression.

**Collaborators:** Every concrete event class in the codebase (keyboard, mouse, timer, filesystem, AI lifecycle) must include this header and use both macros. The AI-call lifecycle events (`AiCallStarted`, `AiCallRetrying`, `AiCallCompleted`, `AiCallFailed`) are expected to interact with whatever module drives AI provider dispatch. Filesystem event types (`FileAdded`, `FileRemoved`, `FileModified`) correspond to the file-watching subsystem. `PythonCrashed` is consumed by the embedded Python integration layer.

**Threading / lifetime:** `Event` objects and the `EventDispatcher` are stack-allocated by convention (the dispatcher stores only a non-owning reference). Neither class has internal synchronisation. If an event is created on one thread and dispatched on another, the caller is responsible for all synchronisation. The `m_Handled` flag has no atomic guarantee; do not share a single `Event` instance across threads without external locking.

**Notes:**

- `EventDispatcher::Dispatch` returns `true` to mean "type matched and handler was invoked", not "event was handled". A handler that returns `false` still causes `Dispatch` to return `true` but leaves `m_Handled` as `false`. Callers that gate on `Dispatch`'s return value alone will misinterpret this.
- The `EventCategory` enum uses a plain (non-class) enum so its enumerators are implicitly convertible to `int`, which is intentional — it allows bitwise OR of multiple categories in a single `GetCategoryFlags()` return.
- `AiCallRetrying` is a distinct event type, meaning consumers can separately observe and count retry attempts from the engine's retry logic without parsing log strings.
- There is no event queue or priority mechanism in this file; it provides only the type system and single-event dispatch primitive. Any queuing, thread-safe posting, or ordered delivery is the responsibility of the higher-level event bus built on top of these primitives.
