### 140_doc_engine_entryPoint_cpp

<a id="140-doc-engine-entrypoint-cpp"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/140_doc_engine_entryPoint_cpp/docu.output.md`



## main.cpp

**Role:** This is the program entry point for the j9t workflow engine. The file's sole responsibility is to receive the operating-system-supplied argument vector and immediately delegate all initialisation, execution, and teardown to the `engine()` function declared in `engine.h`. It contains no application logic of its own.

**Public API:**

- `main(int argc, char* argv[]) → int` — Standard C++ program entry point. Forwards `argc` and `argv` unmodified to `engine()` and returns whatever integer `engine()` returns directly to the OS as the process exit code. No argument parsing, validation, or transformation occurs here.

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` / `engine()` — The only dependency. All meaningful behaviour lives in `engine()`. The signature `int engine(int argc, char* argv[])` is implied by the call site; callers should consult `engine.h` for the full contract of that function, including how it interprets the argument vector and what non-zero exit codes mean.

**Threading / lifetime:**

No threads are created here. Lifetime is the full process lifetime: `engine()` is called once and the process exits when it returns. No heap allocation, RAII objects, or static initialisers exist in this translation unit.

**Notes:**

- The return value of `engine()` is passed through verbatim to the OS; any error signalling or graceful-shutdown semantics are entirely owned by `engine()`.
- Because this file adds no logic between the OS and `engine()`, adding pre-engine setup (signal handlers, locale, logging bootstrap) would need to go here or inside `engine()` itself — there is currently no middle layer.
- The file is intentionally minimal; its only contractual obligation is that `engine()` exists with a compatible signature at link time.


---

### 14_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnector_h

<a id="14-doc-application-cloud-cloudconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/14_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## CloudConnector (cloud\_connector.h)

**Role:** This header defines the shared vocabulary and abstract interface for all cloud service connectors in the AIAssistant namespace. It declares `CloudAuthType` (an enum covering six authentication schemes), `CloudCredentials` (a runtime-only transport bundle of resolved secrets ready for immediate HTTP use), `CloudConnection` (the persisted configuration record loaded from `config.json`), `OAuth2ProviderInfo` (per-provider OAuth2 endpoint metadata consumed by the web server's generic authorize/callback handlers), and `ICloudConnector` (the pure-virtual interface every connector implementation must satisfy). The file contains no logic beyond the two inline string-conversion helpers `AuthTypeToString` and `StringToAuthType`; all actual credential resolution, token refresh, and connectivity testing live in concrete connector classes elsewhere.

**Public API:**

- `AuthTypeToString(CloudAuthType authType) → std::string` — Converts a `CloudAuthType` enumerator to its canonical lowercase string token (`"bearer"`, `"oauth2"`, `"jwt_rsa"`, `"basic_auth"`, `"sigv4"`, `"azure_shared_key"`). Used for serialization to `config.json` and REST API responses. Falls through to `"bearer"` for any unrecognised value, which can mask future enum additions silently.

- `StringToAuthType(std::string_view str) → CloudAuthType` — Inverse of `AuthTypeToString`. Parses the string tokens listed above and returns the corresponding enumerator. Any unrecognised string returns `CloudAuthType::BearerToken` without error; callers loading config should validate the raw string before calling this if strict rejection is required.

- `ICloudConnector::GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the connector's type identifier (e.g., `"polarion"`, `"s3"`, `"snowflake"`). Used by the connector registry to match `CloudConnection::m_Type` to the correct implementation.

- `ICloudConnector::TestConnection(CloudConnection const& connection, std::string& errorMessage) → bool` — Probes live connectivity for the given connection config. Returns `true` on success; populates `errorMessage` with a human-readable reason on failure. Implementations are expected to perform an actual network round-trip. No side effects on the connection or credential store are implied by this interface.

- `ICloudConnector::ResolveCredentials(CloudConnection const& connection, CloudCredentials& credentials, std::string& errorMessage) → bool` — Reads the named credential from `KeyManager` via `connection.m_KeyName`, performs any provider-specific transformation (OAuth token refresh, RSA-signed JWT generation, SigV4 key derivation), and writes the result into `credentials`. On failure returns `false` and populates `errorMessage`. The returned `CloudCredentials` is valid only for immediate use; callers must not cache it across request boundaries without re-resolving, since tokens may expire.

- `ICloudConnector::GetOAuth2ProviderInfo(CloudConnection const& connection, OAuth2ProviderInfo& info) const → bool` — Fills `info` with provider-specific OAuth2 URLs, default scopes, extra authorize parameters, and whether a client secret is required during token exchange. Returns `false` for connectors that do not support OAuth2; the default base-class implementation returns `false`. The web server's generic OAuth2 authorize and callback handlers call this to avoid hardcoding per-provider quirks.

**Collaborators:**

- **KeyManager** — Concrete connectors call into `KeyManager` (not declared in this file) to retrieve the raw secret referenced by `CloudConnection::m_KeyName`. The `ICredential` hierarchy (`ApiKeyCredential`, `OAuthCredential`, etc.) is the persisted side that `KeyManager` manages; `CloudCredentials` is what comes out after resolution.
- **Web server OAuth2 handlers** — The generic authorize/callback routes in `webServer` consume `OAuth2ProviderInfo` to drive OAuth2 flows without hardcoding provider endpoints.
- **Connector registry** — External code matches `CloudConnection::m_Type` to a registered `ICloudConnector` implementation using `GetType()`.
- **config.json** — `CloudConnection` is the in-memory representation of a persisted connection record. `AuthTypeToString`/`StringToAuthType` are the serialization bridge.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ICloudConnector` carries no threading contract at the interface level. Concrete implementations that maintain token caches or refresh timers must document their own locking. `CloudCredentials` is an unowned value type; callers own the instance and should treat it as ephemeral (valid for one request). `CloudConnection` is likewise a value type; the connector receives it by `const&` and must not hold a pointer or reference past the call's return. `ICloudConnector` instances are typically held in a registry with shared or unique ownership; check the registry implementation for lifetime rules.

**Notes:**

- `CloudCredentials` is explicitly documented as a runtime transport bundle, not a persisted type. Mixing it up with the `ICredential` hierarchy is the most likely source of confusion for new contributors.
- Both `AuthTypeToString` and `StringToAuthType` silently default to `BearerToken` for unknown values. Adding a new `CloudAuthType` enumerator without updating both functions will cause silent data loss during serialization and a silent fallback during deserialization.
- `OAuth2ProviderInfo::m_ExtraAuthorizeParams` is the extension point for provider-specific quirks (e.g., `access_type=offline` for Google). Connectors must populate it rather than expecting callers to know provider details.
- `CloudConnection::m_Params` is an open-ended `std::map<std::string, std::string>` for type-specific configuration. There is no schema enforcement at this layer; each connector is responsible for validating and interpreting its own keys.
- The default `GetOAuth2ProviderInfo` implementation on `ICloudConnector` returns `false` so non-OAuth2 connectors need not override it, but callers must handle `false` before dereferencing `info`.


---

### 15_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnectorRegistry_h

<a id="15-doc-application-cloud-cloudconnectorregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/15_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnectorRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



## CloudConnectorRegistry

**Role:** `CloudConnectorRegistry` is a simple ownership-taking registry that maps string type keys to `ICloudConnector` implementations within the `AIAssistant` namespace. It acts as the central lookup table through which the workflow engine resolves which cloud provider connector to use for a given request type. Connectors are registered once at startup (or on-demand) and retrieved by type string for the lifetime of the registry. Duplicate registrations are permitted but emit a warning and silently replace the existing entry.

**Public API:**

- `Register(std::unique_ptr<ICloudConnector> connector) → void` — Transfers ownership of a connector into the registry. The connector's type string is obtained by calling `connector->GetType()` immediately on entry; that string becomes the lookup key. Passing a null pointer is a no-op that emits `LOG_CORE_WARN`. If a connector with the same type is already present, the old instance is destroyed and replaced with the new one; a warning is logged before replacement. After this call the caller's `unique_ptr` is empty.

- `GetConnector(std::string const& type) const → ICloudConnector*` — Returns a raw, non-owning pointer to the connector registered under `type`, or `nullptr` if no such type is registered. The returned pointer is valid only as long as the registry is alive and no subsequent `Register` call replaces the same type.

- `GetRegisteredTypes() const → std::vector<std::string>` — Returns a snapshot of all currently registered type keys. Order is unspecified (inherits from `std::unordered_map` iteration order). The vector is freshly allocated on each call; callers should not assume stable ordering between calls.

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (declared in `cloudConnector.h`) — the interface all registered connectors implement. `CloudConnectorRegistry` depends on `ICloudConnector::GetType()` to derive the registration key; any connector whose `GetType()` returns an empty or colliding string will behave unexpectedly.
- `engine.h` — included in the `.cpp` translation unit, which is where the `LOG_CORE_WARN` / `LOG_CORE_INFO` macros are sourced from. The registry itself does not depend on the engine class directly; the include is purely for logging infrastructure.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`CloudConnectorRegistry` has no internal synchronisation. All calls to `Register`, `GetConnector`, and `GetRegisteredTypes` must be serialised by the caller. The registry exclusively owns every registered connector via `std::unique_ptr`; connectors are destroyed when the registry is destroyed or when a replacement registration for the same type occurs. Raw pointers returned by `GetConnector` are borrowed references — they are invalidated if the owning registry is destroyed or if `Register` is called with the same type string while another thread holds the pointer.

**Notes:**

- Re-registration silently destroys the previous connector for that type. If the old connector has outstanding borrowed raw pointers elsewhere in the engine those pointers become dangling. Callers must not cache the result of `GetConnector` across potential re-registration points.
- `GetRegisteredTypes` does not guarantee stable ordering between two calls even when the registry contents have not changed, because it iterates an `unordered_map`.
- There is no `Unregister` method; once a type is registered it can only be removed by replacing it with a new connector or by destroying the registry itself.
- The type key is captured at `Register` time from `ICloudConnector::GetType()`. If a connector's `GetType()` returns different values at different call sites (mutable state, virtual override mismatch), the key stored in the map will not match what `GetConnector` callers expect.


---

### 16_doc_application_cloud_cloudRetryPolicy_h

<a id="16-doc-application-cloud-cloudretrypolicy-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/16_doc_application_cloud_cloudRetryPolicy_h/docu.output.md`



## CloudRetryPolicy

**Role:** `CloudRetryPolicy` is a stateless utility class inside the `AIAssistant` namespace that wraps any callable returning an `AttemptResult` with configurable exponential-backoff retry logic. It is the single, authoritative place in the workflow engine where transient cloud/HTTP failures (rate limits, gateway errors, server-side 5xx responses) are absorbed and retried before propagating a failure up to the caller. It handles backoff computation, jitter, `Retry-After` header respect, and early-exit on non-retryable status codes, keeping HTTP transport clients free of that logic.

**Public API:**

- `Execute(fn, config) → AttemptResult` — Runs `fn()` up to `config.m_MaxRetries + 1` times. After each failed attempt it inspects `AttemptResult::m_HttpStatusCode`: if the code is non-zero and absent from `config.m_RetryableHttpCodes`, the loop exits immediately without sleeping (fast-fail path). Otherwise it sleeps for either `m_RetryAfterSeconds * 1000` ms (clamped to `m_MaxBackoffMs`) when the server supplied a `Retry-After` value, or for the jittered exponential backoff computed by `ComputeBackoffMs`. Logs each retry at `INFO` level via `LOG_APP_INFO`. Returns the `AttemptResult` of the last executed attempt — either the first success or the final failure after all retries are exhausted. Blocks the calling thread during all sleep intervals.

- `Execute(fn) → AttemptResult` — Convenience overload that constructs a default `Config` (`m_MaxRetries=3`, `m_InitialBackoffMs=1000`, `m_MaxBackoffMs=30000`, `m_JitterFactor=0.2`, retryable codes `{429, 500, 502, 503, 504}`) and delegates to the two-argument overload.

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` — Provides the `LOG_APP_INFO` macro used for retry diagnostics. Callers must ensure the engine logging subsystem is initialised before invoking `Execute`.
- `cloud/cloudRetryPolicy.h` — The paired header declaring `CloudRetryPolicy`, `Config`, and `AttemptResult`; included by any cloud transport or provider client that needs retry behaviour.
- HTTP transport / provider clients (callers) — Supply the `fn` lambda that performs the actual HTTP call and constructs an `AttemptResult` from the response, populating `m_HttpStatusCode`, `m_RetryAfterSeconds`, and `m_ErrorMessage`. This class does not perform any I/O itself.

**Threading / lifetime:**

- The class is entirely stateless; all methods are `static`. There is no instance to own or lifetime to manage.
- `ComputeBackoffMs` uses a `static thread_local std::mt19937` seeded from `std::random_device` on first use per thread. The RNG is safe to call concurrently from different threads without locking, but multiple concurrent `Execute` calls on the same thread share one RNG instance, which is expected and safe.
- `Execute` calls `std::this_thread::sleep_for` directly, so it **blocks the calling thread** for the full backoff duration. It must never be called on a thread that owns a mutex or drives an event loop, unless the caller accepts that latency. The total worst-case blocking time across all retries is bounded by `m_MaxRetries * m_MaxBackoffMs` (plus `Retry-After` overrides, which are also clamped to `m_MaxBackoffMs`).

**Notes:**

- A status code of `0` in `AttemptResult` is treated as a network-level or pre-HTTP error (no status code available). The non-retryable fast-exit check is skipped in this case (`result.m_HttpStatusCode != 0` guard), so network errors always exhaust all retries.
- `Retry-After` values are respected but clamped to `m_MaxBackoffMs` to prevent a misbehaving or hostile server from inducing unbounded waits. Jitter is **not** applied when `Retry-After` is present — the server-supplied value is used directly (after clamping).
- Exponential backoff saturates at `m_MaxBackoffMs` during the doubling loop, so very high attempt counts do not overflow `int`. After the loop, the jittered result is additionally clamped to `[0, m_MaxBackoffMs]` by `std::clamp`, making the upper bound doubly enforced.
- `fn` is taken by `const&` to `std::function`. If the lambda captures state that is mutated between attempts (e.g. a request ID or nonce), the caller is responsible for that mutation inside the lambda body.
- There is no cancellation mechanism. Once `Execute` enters a sleep interval it cannot be interrupted. Callers in latency-sensitive or shutdown paths should set a conservative `m_MaxRetries` or `m_MaxBackoffMs` rather than relying on external cancellation.


---

### 17_doc_application_cloud_cloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="17-doc-application-cloud-cloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/17_doc_application_cloud_cloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## ICloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `ICloudTaskExecutor` is an abstract base class (inside `namespace AIAssistant`) that sits between the workflow engine's generic `ITaskExecutor` interface and concrete cloud-operation implementations. Its `Execute()` override performs all of the plumbing that every cloud task needs — JSON-parsing the `connection` parameter, looking up the matching `CloudConnection` and `ICloudConnector`, resolving credentials, checking the circuit breaker, expanding `{{template}}` variables in `m_ParamsJson`, and wiring up cooperative cancellation — then delegates to the pure-virtual `ExecuteCloud()` that each subclass provides. It also supplies two protected static utilities: a path-traversal guard (`ValidateLocalPath`) and a per-instance response-file naming and writing scheme (`ResponseJsonFilename` / `WriteResponseJson`).

---

**Public API:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor(CloudConnectorRegistry& connectorRegistry, CloudConnectionManager& connectionManager)` — Constructor; stores references to both registries. Neither argument is owned; both must outlive the executor instance.

- `Execute(WorkflowDefinition const&, WorkflowRun&, TaskDef const&, TaskInstanceState&) → bool` — The sole public entry point, called by the workflow engine. Performs in order: (1) parses `TaskDef::m_ParamsJson` with simdjson to extract the required `"connection"` string; (2) calls `CloudConnectionManager::GetConnection()` to retrieve a `CloudConnection`; (3) calls `CloudConnectorRegistry::GetConnector()` to retrieve an `ICloudConnector` for that connection's type; (4) calls `ICloudConnector::ResolveCredentials()` to materialise `CloudCredentials`; (5) emits a `LOG_SECURITY_INFO` audit record; (6) checks `CloudCircuitBreaker::AllowRequest()` and short-circuits to `Failed` if the breaker is open; (7) JSON-escapes all values in `TaskInstanceState::m_InputValues` and runs `ExpandTemplate()` in `Lenient` mode against `m_ParamsJson`, replacing the task definition's params in a local copy; (8) calls the pure-virtual `ExecuteCloud()` with the expanded task def, the resolved connection, credentials, and the run's cancellation token; (9) records the success or failure with the circuit breaker. Sets `TaskInstanceState::m_LastErrorMessage` and `m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed` on any early-exit error. Returns `false` on any failure; returns whatever `ExecuteCloud()` returns on success.

---

**Protected API** (relevant to subclass authors):

- `ExecuteCloud(WorkflowDefinition const&, WorkflowRun&, TaskDef const&, TaskInstanceState&, CloudConnection const&, CloudCredentials const&, TaskCancellationToken const&) → bool` — Pure virtual. By the time this is called, `TaskDef::m_ParamsJson` has already been template-expanded with JSON-escaped input values. The `TaskCancellationToken` comes from `WorkflowRun::m_CancellationToken` if present, otherwise a local no-op token. Subclasses must not re-resolve credentials or re-check the circuit breaker.

- `ValidateLocalPath(std::string const& localPath, std::filesystem::path const& baseDir, std::string const& taskId) → bool` — Static. Rejects any path containing `".."` immediately. For all other paths, resolves `baseDir / localPath` lexically and verifies the result starts with `baseDir` (both normalised). Emits `LOG_SECURITY_INFO` with the tag `path_traversal_blocked` on rejection. Returns `true` for empty `localPath`.

- `ResponseJsonFilename(TaskInstanceState const& taskState) → std::string` — Static. Returns `"response.json"` for regular tasks. For per-item child instances whose `m_TaskInstanceId` contains `'#'`, returns `"response_<N>.json"` where `<N>` is the suffix after the last `'#'`. This ensures concurrent per-item children write to non-overlapping files.

- `WriteResponseJson(std::filesystem::path const& workDir, TaskInstanceState const& taskState, std::string const& responseBody) → void` — Static. Creates `workDir` (including parents) via `std::filesystem::create_directories`, then writes `responseBody` verbatim to the file named by `ResponseJsonFilename()`. Silently no-ops if `workDir` is empty; silently no-ops if directory creation fails (error code is checked but not propagated). File is opened with `std::ios::trunc`.

---

**Collaborators:**

| Collaborator | How it is used |
|---|---|
| `CloudConnectorRegistry` | `GetConnector(type)` maps a connection-type string to an `ICloudConnector*`. |
| `CloudConnectionManager` | `GetConnection(name)` retrieves a `CloudConnection` by name from config. |
| `ICloudConnector` | `ResolveCredentials()` is called to turn a `CloudConnection` into live `CloudCredentials`. |
| `CloudCircuitBreaker` | Obtained via `Core::g_Core->GetCloudCircuitBreaker()`; `AllowRequest`, `RecordSuccess`, `RecordFailure` wrap every `ExecuteCloud` call. |
| `TaskCancellationToken` | Read from `WorkflowRun::m_CancellationToken`; a local no-op token is used if the pointer is null. |
| `TemplateEngine` (`ExpandTemplate`) | Expands `{{key}}` placeholders in `m_ParamsJson` using `TemplateMode::Lenient` (unknown keys are left in place rather than causing failure). |
| `simdjson` | `ondemand` parser used only in `Execute()` to extract the `"connection"` field from params JSON. |
| `Core::g_Core` | Global singleton access point for `GetCloudCircuitBreaker()`; `Execute()` depends on it being non-null. |

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` holds non-owning references to `CloudConnectorRegistry` and `CloudConnectionManager`. Both must remain alive for the full lifetime of any executor instance.
- `Core::g_Core` is accessed as a raw global pointer inside `Execute()`; it must be initialised before any cloud task runs.
- No internal mutable state is modified after construction; the two member references are effectively read-only. Whether `Execute()` is safe to call concurrently on the same instance depends entirely on thread-safety of the injected collaborators and the subclass's `ExecuteCloud()` implementation — `ICloudTaskExecutor` itself adds no locking.
- `WorkflowRun::m_CancellationToken` is read (not written) inside `Execute()`; the pointed-to `TaskCancellationToken` must remain valid for the duration of the `ExecuteCloud()` call.

---

**Notes:**

- Template expansion happens on a local copy (`expandedTaskDef`) of `taskDefinition`, so the original `TaskDef` passed by the engine is never mutated. Subclasses receive the expanded copy.
- Template expansion uses `TemplateMode::Lenient`: a warning is logged but execution continues if expansion fails. Subclasses should not assume all placeholders have been resolved.
- JSON-escaping of input values (`"`, `\`, `\n`, `\r`, `\t`) is performed manually before substitution. Only those five characters are handled; other control characters (U+0000–U+001F except the above) are passed through unescaped, which may produce invalid JSON if input values contain them.
- `ValidateLocalPath` uses a string prefix check (`resolvedStr.find(baseDirStr) != 0`) rather than `std::filesystem::path::lexically_relative`. This is safe only when `baseDirStr` does not itself happen to be a prefix of an unrelated sibling directory name (e.g., `/data/run` vs `/data/run2`). Callers providing a `baseDir` that is a prefix of sibling paths should be aware of this.
- `WriteResponseJson` swallows directory-creation errors silently; if `workDir` cannot be created the response file is simply not written with no diagnostic. Subclasses that depend on the file being present for downstream template resolution will fail silently in that case.
- The circuit breaker key is the `connectionName` string, not the connector type. Multiple logically distinct connections sharing the same name would trip the same breaker — but connection names are expected to be unique across `CloudConnectionManager`.
- `ResponseJsonFilename` uses `rfind('#')`, so only the last `#` segment is used as the item index. Task instance IDs with multiple `#` characters (nested per-item expansion) produce a filename based on the outermost index only.


---

### 18_doc_application_cloud_dbQueryCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="18-doc-application-cloud-dbquerycloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/18_doc_application_cloud_dbQueryCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## DbQueryCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `DbQueryCloudTaskExecutor` is the JCWF cloud task executor for the `"db_query"` task type. It opens a synchronous PostgreSQL connection via libpq, executes a caller-supplied SQL string verbatim, and serialises the result set to a file on disk in the task's working directory. Output can be RFC 4180 CSV (the default) or a JSON array of objects. It is a concrete subclass of `ICloudTaskExecutor` and participates in the standard cloud task dispatch pipeline.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — Protected override called by the base-class dispatch loop. Reads `"query"`, `"format"`, and `"output_file"` from `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` using simdjson's on-demand parser, builds a libpq connection string via `PostgresConnector::BuildConnectionString`, issues the query with `PQexec`, and writes results to `workDir / outputFile`. On success it sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded` and populates `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` with a short summary (`rows=N cols=M format=F file=F`). On any failure it sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed`, writes a human-readable message to `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`, and returns `false`. The `cancellationToken` parameter is accepted but not consulted during query execution — a long-running query will not be interrupted. The constructor is inherited directly from `ICloudTaskExecutor` via `using`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — abstract base class that owns dispatch, parameter routing, and provides the `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` objects already resolved from the workflow's named connection registry.
- `PostgresConnector::BuildConnectionString` (`cloud/postgresConnector.h`) — translates a `CloudConnection` + `CloudCredentials` pair into a libpq connection string; this file does not construct credentials itself.
- `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` / `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — resolves the on-disk working directory where the output file is written, rooted under the workflow's base directory and the task's `m_WorkingDirectory` field.
- `libpq` (`libpq-fe.h`) — used directly: `PQconnectdb`, `PQexec`, `PQntuples`, `PQnfields`, `PQfname`, `PQgetvalue`, `PQgetisnull`, `PQclear`, `PQfinish`.
- `simdjson` — on-demand parser used to extract `"query"`, `"format"`, and `"output_file"` from the raw JSON params string stored in `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`.
- `engine.h` — pulled in for `LOG_APP_INFO`; the log line records connection name, row/column counts, and the resolved output path.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

The executor makes no use of shared mutable state beyond the `taskState` output parameter, which is owned by the caller for the duration of the call. The libpq connection (`PGconn*`) and result (`PGresult*`) are created, used, and freed entirely within the single `ExecuteCloud` invocation — there is no connection pooling or reuse. If the engine calls `ExecuteCloud` concurrently on separate instances, there is no contention within this class, but callers must ensure `taskState` is not shared across threads. The `TaskCancellationToken` is not checked, so cancellation requests issued after `ExecuteCloud` starts will not abort an in-flight query.

---

**Notes:**

- **No query sanitisation.** The SQL from `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` is passed to `PQexec` as a raw string. Injection safety is entirely the workflow author's responsibility.
- **NULL handling differs by format.** In CSV output, SQL `NULL` is silently emitted as an empty field with no distinguishing marker. In JSON output, SQL `NULL` is correctly emitted as the JSON `null` literal.
- **All JSON values are strings.** The JSON serialiser wraps every non-null cell value in double quotes regardless of the PostgreSQL column type. Consumers must not rely on native numeric or boolean JSON types in the output.
- **`kMaxCaptureChars = 1024`.** The summary written to `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` is capped at 1 024 characters; the summary string is short by construction, so truncation is not expected in practice but is enforced defensively.
- **`output_file` is not sanitised.** A path-traversal value (e.g., `"../../etc/passwd"`) would be passed directly to `workDir / outputFile`. The workflow engine or task author must validate this field upstream.
- **Output directory is created silently.** `std::filesystem::create_directories` is called with an ignored `std::error_code`; a subsequent file-open failure will surface the real error through `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`.
- **`PGRES_COMMAND_OK` is accepted.** DDL and DML statements that return no rows (e.g., `INSERT`, `CREATE TABLE`) succeed without writing any data to the output file beyond headers (CSV) or an empty array (JSON). The summary will correctly report `rows=0`.
- **`simdjson` on-demand parser requires the JSON to remain in scope.** `paddedJson` and `doc` are kept alive on the stack for the duration of all `getStringParam` calls; this is handled correctly as written.


---

### 19_doc_application_cloud_emailCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="19-doc-application-cloud-emailcloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/19_doc_application_cloud_emailCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## EmailCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `EmailCloudTaskExecutor` is the j9t workflow engine's task executor for email operations. It handles two JCWF task types — `email_send` (SMTP) and `email_read` (IMAP) — by inheriting from `ICloudTaskExecutor` and overriding the `ExecuteCloud` dispatch point. For outbound mail it constructs a fully-formed RFC 2822 message (plain text or `multipart/mixed` with Base64-encoded attachments) and streams it to an SMTP server via libcurl. For inbound mail it issues IMAP `SEARCH` and per-UID `FETCH` commands through `EmailConnector`, then parses raw message text to produce a JSON summary written to the task's working directory.

---

**Public API:**

- `EmailCloudTaskExecutor(…)` — inherited from `ICloudTaskExecutor` via `using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor`; no additional construction logic in this class.

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — protected override called by the engine dispatcher. Routes to `ExecuteEmailRead` when `taskDefinition.m_Type == TaskType::EmailRead`; otherwise executes `email_send` inline. Parses `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` with simdjson on every call. Sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded` or `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed` and populates `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` on failure. Writes `response.json` (and for reads, `emails_summary.json`) under the task's resolved working directory. Returns `true` on success, `false` on any failure. The `cancellationToken` parameter is accepted but not consulted in the current implementation.

- `ExecuteEmailRead(workflowDefinition, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials) → bool` — private; performs the full IMAP read flow: builds the IMAP base URL via `EmailConnector::BuildImapUrl`, issues `SEARCH ALL` via `EmailConnector::ImapCommand`, parses returned UIDs with `EmailConnector::ParseSearchUids`, trims the list to `max_messages` (default 10, keeping the most-recent tail), fetches each message body, applies an optional `subject_filter` substring match, and serialises results to `emails_summary.json` and `response.json`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class providing the `ExecuteCloud` virtual hook and the `WriteResponseJson` helper used to persist `response.json`.
- `EmailConnector` (`cloud/emailConnector.h`) — owns all IMAP URL construction (`BuildImapUrl`), SMTP URL construction (`BuildSmtpUrl`), raw IMAP command execution (`ImapCommand`), and UID list parsing (`ParseSearchUids`). The file-local `ImapCommand` and `ParseSearchUids` wrappers simply forward to this class.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — supplies the CA-bundle path via `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` applied to every outbound SMTP `CURL*` handle.
- `TaskPathResolver` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — resolves the workflow base directory and per-task working directory for reading attachment files and writing output files.
- `simdjson` — used for zero-copy parsing of `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` on every execution; `ondemand::parser` and `padded_string` are stack-allocated per call.
- `libcurl` — direct `curl_easy_*` usage for SMTP; IMAP calls are routed through `EmailConnector::ImapCommand`.
- OpenSSL (`BIO`/`EVP`/`BUF_MEM`) — used only for Base64 encoding of attachment data via the file-local `Base64Encode` helper.
- `engine.h` — provides `LOG_APP_INFO` / `LOG_APP_WARN` macros used throughout.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`EmailCloudTaskExecutor` holds no mutable member state of its own; all working data (the simdjson parser, the curl handle, the upload context, the JSON output buffers) is stack-allocated or locally scoped within each call to `ExecuteCloud` / `ExecuteEmailRead`. This means multiple workflow threads may call `ExecuteCloud` on the same instance concurrently without data races inside this class, provided the engine guarantees that the `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials` arguments are read-only from the caller's perspective (they are passed by const-ref). `TaskInstanceState& taskState` is written by the executor; callers must ensure it is not shared across concurrent calls. The `CURL*` handle is created, used, and destroyed within a single `ExecuteCloud` invocation — no handle is retained between calls.

---

**Notes:**

- `body_file` takes unconditional precedence over `body` for `email_send`: if `body_file` is present and readable its content replaces `body` entirely; if it is present but unreadable the task fails immediately rather than falling back to `body`.
- The sender address for `email_send` is resolved from `connection.m_Params["from"]` first; if that key is absent or empty, `credentials.m_Username` is used as the `MAIL FROM` envelope address.
- STARTTLS (`CURLUSESSL_ALL`) is enabled automatically when `smtp_port` equals `"587"`. Any other port (including `"465"` for implicit TLS) does not trigger this option; callers relying on implicit TLS must ensure `BuildSmtpUrl` in `EmailConnector` produces a `smtps://` scheme.
- For `email_read`, UID trimming is applied to the raw list returned by `SEARCH ALL` before subject filtering. The `subject_filter` is applied per-message after fetching, so messages that do not match are fetched over the network and then discarded; `max_messages` is therefore a fetch limit, not a result limit.
- `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` is capped at `kMaxCaptureChars` (1024 bytes) for both task types; the full JSON is always written to disk regardless of this cap.
- JSON for output files is built by manual string concatenation with the file-local `JsonEscapeEmail` function rather than through a JSON library. This covers the standard escape sequences and Unicode control characters below `U+0020`, but does not handle surrogate pairs or non-UTF-8 byte sequences in message headers or bodies.
- `curl_slist_free_all` and `curl_easy_cleanup` are called unconditionally after `curl_easy_perform`, even on failure, so there is no curl handle leak on error paths.
- The `WorkflowRun& workflowRun` and `TaskCancellationToken const& cancellationToken` parameters accepted by `ExecuteCloud` are currently unused inside this file.


---

### 20_doc_application_cloud_emailConnector_h

<a id="20-doc-application-cloud-emailconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/20_doc_application_cloud_emailConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## EmailConnector

**Role:** `EmailConnector` is the email integration backend for j9t's cloud connector subsystem. It implements `ICloudConnector` under the `AIAssistant` namespace and provides two distinct protocol surfaces: SMTP connectivity testing via libcurl's `CURLOPT_CONNECT_ONLY` mode, and IMAP polling used by both `email_read` task executors and `email_watch` triggers. The class is stateless — all persistent context lives in the `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials` structs passed at call time — and its static IMAP utilities are shared freely by any part of the engine that needs mailbox inspection without going through the connector abstraction.

---

**Public API:**

- `GetType() → std::string` — Returns the literal string `"email"`. This token is how the engine's connector registry identifies and dispatches to this class.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Looks up `connection.m_KeyName` in the global `KeyManager` via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider(...)`. Requires a `BasicAuthCredential` entry with a non-empty `m_Username`. On success, populates `credentials.m_AuthType`, `credentials.m_Username`, and `credentials.m_Password`. Fails fast and writes to `errorMessage` if the key is missing or has no username.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Validates that `smtp_host` is present, resolves credentials, constructs the SMTP URL via `BuildSmtpUrl`, then opens a real TCP+TLS connection to the server using `CURLOPT_CONNECT_ONLY`. On port 587 it activates `CURLUSESSL_ALL` (STARTTLS); on port 465 the `smtps://` scheme handles SSL at the transport layer. Connect timeout is 10 s, overall timeout 15 s. Does not send any email. Returns false and sets `errorMessage` on any `CURLcode` error.

- `static BuildSmtpUrl(connection) → std::string` — Derives an SMTP URL string from `m_Params` keys `smtp_host`, `smtp_port`, and `use_ssl`. Port defaults to `"587"`. Scheme is `smtps` only when `use_ssl != "false"` **and** port is exactly `"465"`; all other combinations produce `smtp://`.

- `static BuildImapUrl(connection) → std::string` — Derives an IMAP URL string from `imap_host`, `imap_port`, and `use_ssl`. Port defaults to `"993"`. Scheme is `imaps` whenever `use_ssl != "false"`.

- `static ImapCommand(url, username, password, customRequest, responseBody, errorMessage, useSsl) → bool` — Executes a single libcurl IMAP request against `url`, authenticating with the supplied credentials. `customRequest` is passed verbatim to `CURLOPT_CUSTOMREQUEST`; pass an empty string to use libcurl's default for the URL path. Response bytes are accumulated into `responseBody` via `ImapWriteCallback`. Timeout is 30 s. CA bundle path is sourced from `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` when non-empty. SSL can be disabled by passing `useSsl = false`, which explicitly sets `CURLUSESSL_NONE`.

- `static ParseSearchUids(searchResponse) → std::vector<std::string>` — Scans a raw IMAP server response for the line prefix `"* SEARCH "` and tokenises the remainder up to `\r` or `\n` into numeric UID strings. Non-digit tokens are silently skipped. Returns an empty vector if the prefix is absent (including when the server returns `* SEARCH` with no UIDs, i.e. an empty mailbox).

- `static CheckForNewMail(connection, credentials, folder, subjectFilter, lastSeenUid, hasNewMail, errorMessage) → std::string` — High-level IMAP poll. Builds the search URL as `BuildImapUrl(connection) + "/" + folder`, issues a `SEARCH ALL` command (optionally with `SUBJECT "<subjectFilter>"`), parses UIDs, and compares them against `lastSeenUid` as a numeric watermark. Returns the highest UID found as a string (to be persisted as the new watermark by the caller), or an empty string on communication failure. Sets `hasNewMail = true` only when at least one returned UID is numerically greater than `lastSeenUid`. On the very first call (`lastSeenUid` empty) it returns the current highest UID but leaves `hasNewMail = false`, seeding the watermark without firing for pre-existing mail.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — Base interface; `EmailConnector` overrides `GetType`, `TestConnection`, and `ResolveCredentials`.
- `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` — Plain data structs carrying `m_Params` (a string-to-string map), `m_KeyName`, `m_Name`, and `m_AuthType`. The full set of recognised `m_Params` keys is documented in the class-level comment in the header.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — Accessed as a singleton via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`. `GetProvider(name)` returns a pointer to a credential record; the pointer must be non-null and have a non-empty `m_Username` for email to proceed.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — Provides `GetCaBundlePath()`, which returns the path to the system or bundled CA certificate store used for TLS verification on all outbound libcurl handles.
- `Core` (`core.h`) / `Engine` (`engine.h`) — `Core::g_Core` is the global singleton through which `KeyManager` is reached. `engine.h` is included but `Engine` is not directly referenced in this file's logic; it is likely required for broader translation-unit consistency.
- `libcurl` — All network I/O is performed with raw `curl_easy_*` calls. There is no curl multi handle here; every call is synchronous.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`EmailConnector` has no member state; instances can be shared across threads without synchronisation. However, each call to `TestConnection` and `ImapCommand` creates and destroys its own `CURL*` handle, so libcurl must have been globally initialised (via `curl_global_init`) before any of these methods are invoked — that responsibility belongs to the engine startup sequence, not this class. `ResolveCredentials` reads through `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`, which must remain valid for the duration of the call; no reference to the returned `provider` pointer is retained after the function returns, but the pointer is dereferenced while under no explicit lock visible in this file, so callers must ensure `KeyManager` entries are not mutated concurrently.

---

**Notes:**

- **`SEARCH ALL` instead of `UID SEARCH UID <N>:*`:** The comment explicitly notes that UID-range SEARCH is omitted for compatibility with servers such as GreenMail. New-mail detection is done purely by numeric comparison of all returned UIDs against the watermark in application code. This means every poll fetches the full UID list for the folder; on large mailboxes this may be slow.
- **Watermark seeding on first call:** When `lastSeenUid` is empty, `CheckForNewMail` returns the current highest UID and sets `hasNewMail = false`. Callers must persist this returned string as the new watermark before the next poll, or the first-call seed behaviour will repeat indefinitely.
- **Inclusive boundary quirk:** The comment in `CheckForNewMail` notes that IMAP servers return the boundary UID itself in range searches, so the comparison is strict (`> lastSeenUid`, not `>=`). The numeric conversion uses `std::stoull`, which will throw `std::invalid_argument` or `std::out_of_range` on malformed UID strings; there is no guard against this.
- **SMTP scheme selection edge case:** `BuildSmtpUrl` only promotes the scheme to `smtps` when both `use_ssl != "false"` AND `port == "465"`. A caller that sets port 465 and explicitly sets `use_ssl = "false"` will still get `smtp://` — consistent — but a caller that sets a non-standard SSL port (e.g. `"8465"`) will also get `smtp://` regardless of `use_ssl`, which may not be the intended outcome.
- **`from` parameter not used in this file:** The `m_Params` key `"from"` is documented in the header but is never read in either the `.h` or `.cpp` shown here. It is presumably consumed by a separate `email_send` task executor elsewhere in the codebase.
- **No `CURLOPT_SSL_VERIFYPEER` override:** TLS certificate verification relies solely on the CA bundle supplied by `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()`. If that path is empty, libcurl falls back to its compiled-in defaults; peer verification is never explicitly disabled.


---

### 21_doc_application_cloud_gcsCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="21-doc-application-cloud-gcscloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/21_doc_application_cloud_gcsCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## GcsCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `GcsCloudTaskExecutor` is the Google Cloud Storage task executor for the j9t workflow engine. It handles exactly two GCS operations — object upload and object download — driven by workflow task definitions. It inherits from `ICloudTaskExecutor` and overrides `ExecuteCloud` to parse task parameters from JSON, resolve credentials and bucket from the active `CloudConnection`, build GCS JSON API URLs, perform the HTTP transfer via libcurl, and write a compact JSON result back to the task's working directory. It is registered for the JCWF task types `gcs_upload` and `gcs_download`.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — Protected override called by the `ICloudTaskExecutor` base. Reads the `operation`, `bucket`, `object_name`, and `local_path` keys from `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` using simdjson. For **upload**, reads the entire local file into memory and POSTs it to the GCS JSON API endpoint (`/upload/storage/v1/b/{bucket}/o?uploadType=media&name={object}`). For **download**, creates missing output directories, then streams the object to the local path via a dedicated file-writing curl handle using `?alt=media`. On success, sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded`, populates `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` with a JSON summary (capped at `kMaxCaptureChars` = 1024 characters), and calls `WriteResponseJson` to persist the full response in the task working directory. On any failure — JSON parse error, missing required parameters, curl error, or HTTP ≥ 400 — sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed`, populates `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`, and returns `false`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class; supplies constructor forwarding and the `WriteResponseJson` helper; dispatches into `ExecuteCloud`.
- `GcsConnector::BuildEndpointUrl(connection)` (`cloud/gcsConnector.h`) — builds the GCS base URL from the `CloudConnection`; allows overriding the endpoint (e.g., for emulator use).
- `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` — `connection.m_Params` is consulted for a default `bucket` key when the task params omit one. `credentials.m_Token` is used directly as the OAuth2 Bearer token in every request header.
- `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` / `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — resolves the filesystem path where the JSON response file is written.
- `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — supplies an optional CA bundle path applied to every curl handle; if empty, the system default is used.
- `simdjson` — used to parse `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`; the `ondemand` API is used with a locally scoped `parser` and `padded_string` on each call.
- `engine.h` — provides the `LOG_APP_INFO` macro used for progress logging.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`GcsCloudTaskExecutor` holds no mutable state of its own; all working state lives on the call stack inside `ExecuteCloud`. The class is safe to instantiate once and call concurrently from multiple workflow runner threads **as long as the `WorkflowRun`, `TaskDef`, and `TaskInstanceState` arguments are distinct per call** — simdjson's `ondemand::parser` and all curl handles are stack-local. The `cancellationToken` parameter is accepted but is **not checked** inside `ExecuteCloud`; long-running transfers cannot be interrupted mid-flight. Ownership of `WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, and related objects follows the `ICloudTaskExecutor` contract and is managed by the calling workflow engine, not by this class.

---

**Notes:**

- Uploads are **fully buffered in memory** before the POST; there is no streaming or chunked upload path. Uploading objects larger than available process memory will fail or crash. The download path has an explicit hard cap of **256 MB** (`kMaxDownloadBytes`) enforced via `CURLOPT_MAXFILESIZE_LARGE`; no equivalent cap exists for uploads.
- The `operation` key is **required** in `m_ParamsJson`. The comment block in the header lists `gcs_upload` / `gcs_download` as JCWF task types, but the runtime discriminator inside `ExecuteCloud` reads an `operation` field with values `"upload"` / `"download"` — not the task type string.
- Object names with special characters (slashes, spaces, Unicode) are URL-encoded using `curl_easy_escape` via a dedicated short-lived curl handle. If `curl_easy_init()` fails for the encoder handle, the raw unencoded object name is used as a fallback with no warning.
- The response body written to `m_CapturedStdout` is a **manually constructed JSON string**, not the actual GCS API response. The real API response from an upload is discarded after the HTTP status check.
- For downloads, parent directories of `local_path` are created with `std::filesystem::create_directories`; errors from directory creation are silently swallowed via `std::error_code ec` and do not cause task failure — the subsequent `fopen` will then fail with the real error.
- The request timeout for all operations is a uniform **300 seconds** (`kTimeoutSeconds`), covering both small metadata calls and large binary transfers.
- HTTP error response bodies are appended to `m_LastErrorMessage` only if they are non-empty and shorter than 500 characters; larger error bodies are silently truncated.


---

### 22_doc_application_cloud_gcsConnector_h

<a id="22-doc-application-cloud-gcsconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/22_doc_application_cloud_gcsConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## GcsConnector

**Role:** `GcsConnector` is the Google Cloud Storage implementation of the `ICloudConnector` interface within the j9t workflow engine's cloud abstraction layer. It handles service-account-based authentication against the GCS JSON API by minting RS256-signed JWTs via `JwtGenerator`, exchanging them for OAuth2 bearer tokens at `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token`, caching those tokens per key name with a 55-minute effective lifetime, and exposing the resolved credentials to callers so that upstream code can construct authorized GCS requests. It also supports a local fake-gcs-server mode where the JWT is used directly as the bearer token, bypassing the token exchange entirely.

---

**Public API:**

- `GetType() → std::string` — Returns the fixed connector type string `"gcs"`. Used by the connector registry to identify and dispatch to this implementation.

- `TestConnection(const CloudConnection& connection, std::string& errorMessage) → bool` — Validates that a `CloudConnection` is correctly configured and operational. Requires both `"bucket"` and `"service_account_email"` in `connection.m_Params`. Internally calls `ResolveCredentials` to obtain a valid token, then issues an HTTP GET to `/storage/v1/b/{bucket}` on the resolved endpoint. Populates `errorMessage` on any failure (missing params, credential resolution error, curl error, or HTTP ≥ 400). Side effect: may populate the token cache as a result of calling `ResolveCredentials`.

- `ResolveCredentials(const CloudConnection& connection, CloudCredentials& credentials, std::string& errorMessage) → bool` — Core method that produces a live OAuth2 bearer token into `credentials.m_Token` (with `credentials.m_AuthType` set to `CloudAuthType::OAuth2`). On a cache hit (token not yet within 5-minute expiry margin) it returns immediately without any network I/O. On a cache miss, it retrieves the `KeyPairCredential` identified by `connection.m_KeyName` from `KeyManager`, builds a JWT claims set (iss, sub via `service_account_email`; scope `devstorage.read_write`; aud `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token`; iat/exp), calls `JwtGenerator::Generate`, then calls `ExchangeJwtForAccessToken`. A successful token is stored in `m_TokenCache` keyed by `connection.m_KeyName`. Fails with a descriptive `errorMessage` if the key name is missing, the credential is absent from `KeyManager`, the PEM is empty, the email parameter is absent, JWT generation fails, or the token exchange fails.

- `BuildEndpointUrl(const CloudConnection& connection) → std::string` (static) — Returns the GCS API base URL. If `connection.m_Endpoint` is non-empty it is used as-is (with a trailing slash stripped); otherwise defaults to `"https://storage.googleapis.com"`. Callers use this to construct both token-exchange bypass logic and REST endpoint URLs.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` / `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` / `CloudAuthType` — base interface and data types from `cloud/cloudConnector.h` that define the contract this class fulfils.
- `KeyManager` (accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`) — provides `KeyPairCredential` instances by name; `GcsConnector` reads `m_PrivateKeyPem` directly from the returned provider pointer.
- `JwtGenerator::Generate` — signs the RS256 JWT header+payload with the PEM private key. Lives in `keys/jwtGenerator.h`.
- `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` — supplies the CA bundle path used for TLS verification on all outbound curl handles; sourced from `curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`.
- `simdjson` — used to parse the `access_token` field from the Google token endpoint JSON response.
- `libcurl` (raw `CURL*`) — used directly (not through any wrapper) for the token exchange POST and the `TestConnection` GET. Both calls set a 15-second timeout.
- `Core::g_Core` — global singleton providing access to `KeyManager`; `GcsConnector` depends on it being initialised before any credential resolution occurs.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`GcsConnector` instances are expected to be long-lived and shared across threads. The token cache (`m_TokenCache`) and its mutex (`m_CacheMutex`) are `mutable`, and both `ResolveCredentials` and `TestConnection` acquire `m_CacheMutex` via `std::lock_guard` for cache reads and writes. The two lock acquisitions in `ResolveCredentials` are intentionally non-contiguous: the cache is checked under lock, released before network I/O, then re-acquired to write the result. This means two threads racing on the same expired key can both perform a token exchange simultaneously; the second writer will simply overwrite the first with an equally valid token, which is safe but wastes one network round-trip. There is no external ownership transfer: `CloudCredentials` is filled by value. The raw `KeyPairCredential` pointer returned by `GetProvider` is used only within the same call stack and is not stored.

---

**Notes:**

- **Local testing bypass:** When `connection.m_Endpoint` is set to anything other than `"https://storage.googleapis.com"` (e.g. `"http://localhost:4443"` for fake-gcs-server), `ExchangeJwtForAccessToken` skips the HTTP POST entirely and returns the raw JWT string as the bearer token. This is the only way `ExchangeJwtForAccessToken` avoids a network call.
- **Token cache granularity is per `m_KeyName`, not per connection.** Two `CloudConnection` objects sharing the same `m_KeyName` but pointing to different buckets or even different service account emails will share the same cached token. Ensure `m_KeyName` uniquely identifies a service account credential across all connections.
- **Effective token lifetime is 55 minutes** (`kTokenLifetimeSeconds` 3600 minus `kTokenRefreshMarginSeconds` 300). The token itself is valid for 60 minutes at Google's end, but the cache entry expires 5 minutes early to pre-empt clock skew or slow request paths.
- **`m_KeyName` must reference a `KeyPairCredential`** (i.e. `m_PrivateKeyPem` must be non-empty). A symmetric or token-only credential will cause `ResolveCredentials` to fail with an explicit error message.
- **`TestConnection` re-uses `ResolveCredentials` internally**, so a successful test also warms the token cache.
- **No retry logic exists** in either the token exchange or the `TestConnection` probe; a transient network error returns `false` immediately.
- The `simdjson` parser and `padded_string` are stack-allocated inside `ExchangeJwtForAccessToken` on every call; there is no parser reuse between invocations.


---

### 23_doc_application_cloud_gitHubCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="23-doc-application-cloud-githubcloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/23_doc_application_cloud_gitHubCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## GitHubCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `GitHubCloudTaskExecutor` is the j9t workflow engine's concrete task executor for GitHub REST API operations. It handles five distinct issue-and-repository operations — creating issues, adding comments, closing issues, fetching file content, and listing open issues — all driven by a `"github_issue"` task definition's JSON parameter block. The class sits in the `AIAssistant` namespace, inherits from `ICloudTaskExecutor`, and overrides the single protected virtual `ExecuteCloud` method to implement all GitHub-specific HTTP logic, response capture, and file I/O.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — The sole entry point, called by the base class machinery when a `"github_issue"` task is dispatched. Reads the operation type and all other parameters from `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` using `simdjson::ondemand`. Issues one or more `curl_easy_perform` calls via the file-local `GitHubRequest` helper. On success, sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded`, populates `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` with up to `kMaxCaptureChars` (1 024) characters of the raw response body, and calls `WriteResponseJson` (provided by the base class) to persist the full response to the task's working directory. On any failure — JSON parse error, missing required parameter, `curl` transport error, or HTTP ≥ 400 — sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed`, writes a descriptive message to `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`, and returns `false`. The `cancellationToken` parameter is accepted but not currently consulted inside `ExecuteCloud`.

  Supported operations and their required/optional parameters:

  - `"create"` — requires `owner`, `repo`, `title`; optional `body`, `labels` (JSON array). Issues `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues`.
  - `"comment"` — requires `owner`, `repo`, `issue_number`, `body`. Issues `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{number}/comments`.
  - `"close"` — requires `owner`, `repo`, `issue_number`. Issues `PATCH /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{number}` with `{"state":"closed"}`.
  - `"get_file"` — requires `owner`, `repo`, `path`; optional `ref`. Issues `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` (with `?ref=` appended when provided), base64-decodes the `"content"` field from the JSON response using OpenSSL BIO, and writes the decoded bytes to `{workDir}/{filename}` (binary, truncated). The filename is taken from the last component of `path`.
  - `"list_issues"` — requires `owner`, `repo`. Issues `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues?state=open&per_page=100`. Response body is captured but not parsed further.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class providing the dispatch framework, `WriteResponseJson`, and constructor forwarding via `using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor`.
- `GitHubConnector::GetApiBaseUrl(connection)` (`cloud/gitHubConnector.h`) — resolves the correct API base URL (accommodates GitHub Enterprise via connection params rather than hard-coding `https://api.github.com`).
- `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` — `connection.m_Params` supplies fallback `"owner"` and `"repo"` values when the task params omit them; `credentials.m_Token` is passed as the `Bearer` token in every HTTP request.
- `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` / `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — used twice: once inside `"get_file"` to determine where to write the decoded file, and once at the end of every successful operation to locate the directory for `WriteResponseJson`.
- `simdjson` (ondemand API) — all JSON parsing of both task params and the `"get_file"` response body. A separate `simdjson::ondemand::parser` instance is constructed locally for the response document in `"get_file"`.
- `libcurl` (direct, not via `CurlWrapper`) — `GitHubRequest` calls `curl_easy_init` / `curl_easy_perform` / `curl_easy_cleanup` directly. It does consult `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` for the CA bundle path, so the broader `CurlWrapper` infrastructure still influences TLS behavior.
- OpenSSL BIO (`openssl/bio.h`, `openssl/evp.h`, `openssl/buffer.h`) — used only in `Base64Decode` for the `"get_file"` operation; the BIO chain is `BIO_f_base64` → `BIO_new_mem_buf`, read with `BIO_FLAGS_BASE64_NO_NL` after stripping embedded newlines from GitHub's multi-line base64 payload.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`GitHubCloudTaskExecutor` holds no mutable state of its own; all working variables are stack-local within `ExecuteCloud`. Instances can therefore be used concurrently from different threads as long as each call receives its own `taskState` and the base class infrastructure provides thread-safe `WorkflowRun` access. The file-local `GitHubRequest` function creates and destroys its own `CURL*` handle per call — no shared curl state. `simdjson::ondemand::parser` instances are also stack-local and not shared. Ownership of `GitHubCloudTaskExecutor` objects follows whatever policy the base `ICloudTaskExecutor` factory establishes; this file imposes no additional constraints.

---

**Notes:**

- `owner` and `repo` resolution has a defined precedence: task-level params override `connection.m_Params`. For `"comment"`, `"close"`, and `"list_issues"`, if both sources are empty the URL will be malformed (no explicit guard beyond what the GitHub API returns as an error).
- The `"get_file"` path has a silent failure mode: if `std::ofstream` fails to open (e.g., permissions), the error is not reported back through `taskState`; the task still succeeds as far as the engine is concerned.
- `kMaxCaptureChars = 1024` caps `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` for all operations uniformly; the full response is still written to disk via `WriteResponseJson`.
- `"list_issues"` is hard-coded to `state=open&per_page=100` with no pagination support; callers expecting more than 100 open issues will receive a truncated result without any warning.
- JSON for request bodies is assembled by hand with `JsonEscape` rather than through a library. `JsonEscape` escapes `"`, `\`, `\n`, `\r`, `\t` only; other control characters (U+0000–U+001F) pass through unescaped, which may cause GitHub API to reject the payload.
- The `cancellationToken` is threaded through the signature but never checked; long-running curl operations (up to the 30-second timeout) cannot be interrupted by workflow cancellation.
- HTTP response bodies longer than 500 characters are not appended to `m_LastErrorMessage` on `"create"` failure; other operations do not include the body in the error message at all, which can make debugging non-2xx responses harder.
