### 24_doc_application_cloud_gitHubConnector_h

<a id="24-doc-application-cloud-githubconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/24_doc_application_cloud_gitHubConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## GitHubConnector

**Role:** `GitHubConnector` is a concrete `ICloudConnector` implementation that authenticates against GitHub, GitLab, or GitHub Enterprise REST APIs using a Bearer token (Personal Access Token). Its two responsibilities are resolving a named credential from the engine's `KeyManager` into a `CloudCredentials` struct and verifying that credential is live by issuing a `GET /user` request. It is a thin adapter — it does not implement any repository operations itself; it only covers the credential and connectivity layer that higher-level cloud operations depend on.

**Public API:**

- `GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the string `"github"`, which is the connector type identifier used by the engine's connector registry to look up and instantiate this class.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Calls `ResolveCredentials` to obtain the PAT, then issues a synchronous `GET /user` (or the GitLab/GHE equivalent path) with a 15-second timeout. Returns `true` only if the HTTP response code is below 400. On failure, populates `errorMessage` with a human-readable reason including the HTTP status code and up to 500 bytes of the response body when relevant. Specifically distinguishes HTTP 401 with a PAT-specific hint. Allocates and tears down a `CURL*` handle inline — no handle reuse.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Looks up `connection.m_KeyName` in `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`. On success, sets `credentials.m_AuthType = CloudAuthType::BearerToken` and copies the PAT into `credentials.m_Token`. Fails explicitly if `m_KeyName` is empty, if the named provider does not exist in `KeyManager`, or if the provider's `m_ApiKey` field is empty.

- `GetApiBaseUrl(connection) → std::string` (static) — Returns `connection.m_Endpoint` with any trailing slash stripped, falling back to `DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL` (`"https://api.github.com"`) when `m_Endpoint` is empty. Used by both `TestConnection` and any callers that need to construct endpoint URLs for this connection.

- `DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL` (static constexpr) — The string `"https://api.github.com"`. Exposed so callers and other connectors can reference the canonical default without hard-coding it.

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — base interface that mandates `GetType`, `TestConnection`, and `ResolveCredentials`. `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials` structs are defined there; callers must populate `m_KeyName`, `m_AuthType`, `m_Endpoint`, and `m_Params` (`"owner"`, `"repo"`) before passing a `CloudConnection` in.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider(name)`. Returns a provider object whose `m_ApiKey` field must be non-empty for GitHub auth to succeed. `GitHubConnector` is a read-only consumer of this subsystem.
- `Core` (`core.h`) / `Engine` (`engine.h`) — `Core::g_Core` is a global singleton pointer; `GetKeyManager()` is called on it directly. If `g_Core` is null or destroyed the call will crash, so `TestConnection` and `ResolveCredentials` must only be called after engine initialisation.
- `libcurl` — used directly (not through `CurlWrapper`'s higher-level API) for the `GET /user` probe. `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` is called to obtain the CA bundle path for TLS verification.
- `simdjson` — included but not used in this file's current implementation; likely reserved for parsing `GET /user` response fields in future work.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`GitHubConnector` carries no mutable state of its own and all methods are stateless with respect to the object. However, `TestConnection` and `ResolveCredentials` both dereference `Core::g_Core` (a global raw pointer) and read from `KeyManager` without any visible locking inside this file. Callers must ensure the engine is fully initialised and `KeyManager` contents are stable for the duration of the call. `TestConnection` performs a blocking network call (up to 15 seconds); it must not be invoked on a thread where blocking is prohibited. The `CURL*` handle and `curl_slist*` are allocated and freed within the same call frame, so there is no cross-call curl state to worry about. The `CloudCredentials` output of `ResolveCredentials` contains the raw PAT string — callers are responsible for not persisting it beyond the required scope.

**Notes:**

- `m_AuthType` on the incoming `CloudConnection` is documented as required to be `BearerToken`, but `ResolveCredentials` does not validate this field — it unconditionally sets `credentials.m_AuthType = CloudAuthType::BearerToken` on output. A misconfigured connection with a different auth type will silently succeed credential resolution.
- `simdjson` is pulled in as a dependency but produces no observable behaviour in the current translation unit. If `GET /user` response parsing is added later, the response body is already accumulated in `responseBody` inside `TestConnection`.
- The `writeCallback` lambda is cast to a raw function pointer (`size_t (*)(void*, size_t, size_t, void*)`). This is only valid because the lambda captures nothing; adding a capture would make the cast undefined behaviour.
- For GitLab the equivalent of `GET /user` is `GET /api/v4/user`, which will be reached correctly as long as `m_Endpoint` is set to `"https://gitlab.com/api/v4"` — the `/user` suffix is appended unconditionally, so the base URL must not already include it.
- The `"owner"` and `"repo"` entries in `m_Params` are declared in the header-level comment as meaningful keys, but `GitHubConnector` itself never reads them. They are conventions for higher-level code that builds repository-scoped URLs on top of this connector.


---

### 25_doc_application_cloud_googleSheetsCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="25-doc-application-cloud-googlesheetscloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/25_doc_application_cloud_googleSheetsCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## GoogleSheetsCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** Implements the `ICloudTaskExecutor` interface to drive Google Sheets API v4 read and write operations within the j9t workflow engine. The class handles two JCWF task types — `sheets_read` and `sheets_write` — by parsing task parameters from a JSON blob, resolving credentials and connection metadata, issuing raw HTTPS requests to the Sheets REST API via libcurl, and serialising results to the task's working directory as CSV or JSON files.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — The sole override of `ICloudTaskExecutor::ExecuteCloud`. Dispatches to read or write logic based on the `"operation"` field in `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` (defaults to `"read"` if absent). On success, sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded`, populates `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` with a compact JSON summary (capped at 1024 characters), and calls `WriteResponseJson` to persist the raw API response. On any failure — bad JSON params, missing required fields, curl error, HTTP ≥ 400, or file I/O error — sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed`, writes a human-readable message into `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`, and returns `false`. The `cancellationToken` parameter is accepted but not currently checked inside `ExecuteCloud`; cancellation is enforced at the `ICloudTaskExecutor` layer above.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class; provides the `ExecuteCloud` override point, `WriteResponseJson`, and the constructor that callers use directly via the inherited `using` declaration.
- `GoogleSheetsConnector::GetApiBaseUrl(connection)` (`cloud/googleSheetsConnector.h`) — returns the API root URL (typically `https://sheets.googleapis.com/v4/spreadsheets`), allowing the connector to override the endpoint (e.g. for testing or private deployments).
- `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` / `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — resolve the filesystem path under which output files and input CSV files are expected to live.
- `simdjson` — used for both task-param parsing (`taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`) and API response parsing. Two separate `ondemand::parser` / `ondemand::document` instances are created to avoid re-entry conflicts.
- `libcurl` (via the file-local `SheetsRequest` helper) — each call to `SheetsRequest` calls `curl_easy_init` / `curl_easy_perform` / `curl_easy_cleanup` inline; there is no connection pooling or shared CURL handle.
- `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — provides the CA bundle path injected into every request via `CURLOPT_CAINFO`.
- `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials` — carry the named connection parameters (`m_Params["spreadsheet_id"]`, `m_AuthType`) and the bearer token / API key (`m_Token`). Auth mode is determined by comparing `credentials.m_AuthType == CloudAuthType::BearerToken` against `connection.m_AuthType != CloudAuthType::OAuth2`; API-key auth appends `?key=<token>` to the URL, while OAuth2 sets an `Authorization: Bearer` header.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`GoogleSheetsCloudTaskExecutor` holds no member state beyond what `ICloudTaskExecutor` provides. All local state (curl handles, string buffers, parsers, file streams) is stack-allocated within `ExecuteCloud`. The class is therefore safe to call concurrently from multiple threads as long as each call operates on a distinct `taskState` and `workflowRun`. No internal mutex is used. The `simdjson ondemand::parser` and `ondemand::document` objects are not thread-safe across instances, but because they are local variables here there is no sharing concern. libcurl's global state must have been initialised before any call (`curl_global_init`) — this is the engine's responsibility, not this class's.

---

**Notes:**

- **spreadsheet_id resolution order:** task param `"spreadsheet_id"` takes precedence; if absent, the value from `connection.m_Params["spreadsheet_id"]` is used; if that is also missing, the task fails immediately.
- **Operation inference:** if `"operation"` is missing from the params JSON, `ExecuteCloud` silently defaults to `"read"`. There is no validation that the JCWF task type name (`sheets_read` vs `sheets_write`) is consistent with this default.
- **JSON output format treats row 0 as headers:** when `output_format` is `"json"`, the first row of the API response is consumed as the key set for all subsequent row objects. If the spreadsheet range starts with data rows rather than a header row, the first data row will be misinterpreted as column names.
- **Sparse rows not padded:** during JSON output, the loop `for (size_t c = 0; c < headers.size() && c < rows[r].size(); ++c)` silently truncates rows that are shorter than the header row and drops extra cells in rows that are wider. No padding or error is emitted.
- **Write uses PUT (full overwrite):** `sheets_write` issues `PUT /values/{range}`, which replaces the specified range entirely. There is no APPEND mode.
- **HTTP error body truncation:** when an HTTP ≥ 400 response is received, the raw body is appended to `m_LastErrorMessage` only if it is fewer than 500 bytes; larger error bodies are silently discarded.
- **`kMaxCaptureChars = 1024`:** `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` is always trimmed to this limit regardless of the actual summary size.
- **No retry logic:** a transient network failure or a 429/503 from the Sheets API causes an immediate task failure with no back-off or retry.
- **CR/LF normalisation on write:** `ParseCsvLine` strips trailing `\r` from lines but does not handle embedded newlines inside quoted CSV fields, consistent with the comment in the source that it handles "simple" CSV only.
- **`cancellationToken` is unused inside this method** — long-running large reads or writes cannot be interrupted mid-transfer.


---

### 26_doc_application_cloud_googleSheetsConnector_h

<a id="26-doc-application-cloud-googlesheetsconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/26_doc_application_cloud_googleSheetsConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## GoogleSheetsConnector

**Role:** `GoogleSheetsConnector` is the Google Sheets–specific implementation of `ICloudConnector` within the j9t cloud integration layer. It handles credential resolution (supporting both API key authentication for read-only public sheets and OAuth2 for private read/write sheets), constructs the correct Sheets API v4 base URL from a `CloudConnection` configuration, populates Google-specific OAuth2 provider metadata, and validates a live connection by issuing a real HTTP GET against the spreadsheet's metadata endpoint via libcurl. It does not perform any sheet data reads or writes itself; it exists solely to authenticate, validate, and describe the connection.

**Public API:**

- `GetType() → std::string` — Returns the fixed string `"google_sheets"`. Used by the connector registry or factory to identify this connector type. No side effects.

- `TestConnection(CloudConnection const& connection, std::string& errorMessage) → bool` — Validates a live connection to the configured Google Sheets spreadsheet. Requires `connection.m_Params["spreadsheet_id"]` to be present and non-empty, otherwise fails immediately. Calls `ResolveCredentials` internally, then issues a `GET /{spreadsheet_id}?fields=properties.title` request against the Sheets API v4 base URL. For API key auth, appends the key as `?key=` query parameter; for OAuth2, sends an `Authorization: Bearer` header. Sets a 15-second timeout. On any cURL transport error or HTTP 4xx/5xx response, populates `errorMessage` and returns `false`. On success returns `true`; `errorMessage` is not modified in that path.

- `ResolveCredentials(CloudConnection const& connection, CloudCredentials& credentials, std::string& errorMessage) → bool` — Resolves the appropriate credential for the connection. If `connection.m_AuthType == CloudAuthType::OAuth2`, queries `OAuthTokenManager::GetAccessToken` using `connection.m_KeyName`; on success, populates `credentials` with `CloudAuthType::OAuth2` and the access token. If OAuth2 is not configured or `m_AuthType` is not OAuth2, falls back to looking up `connection.m_KeyName` in `KeyManager` and reading `provider->m_ApiKey`, populating `credentials` with `CloudAuthType::BearerToken`. Returns `false` with a descriptive `errorMessage` if `m_KeyName` is empty, if the key is not found in `KeyManager`, or if neither an API key nor OAuth token is available.

- `GetOAuth2ProviderInfo(CloudConnection const& connection, OAuth2ProviderInfo& info) const → bool` — Fills `info` with Google-specific OAuth2 endpoints and parameters. Hardcodes `m_AuthorizeUrl` to `https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth`, `m_TokenUrl` to `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token`, `m_DefaultScopes` to `DEFAULT_OAUTH_SCOPES` (`https://www.googleapis.com/auth/spreadsheets`), and sets extra authorize params `access_type=offline`, `prompt=consent`, `include_granted_scopes=true` to force refresh token issuance. Sets `m_RequiresClientSecret = true`. The `connection` parameter is unused. Always returns `true`.

- `GetApiBaseUrl(CloudConnection const& connection) → std::string` — Static helper. Returns `connection.m_Endpoint` with any trailing slash stripped, or falls back to `DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL` (`https://sheets.googleapis.com/v4/spreadsheets`) if the endpoint is empty. Used internally by `TestConnection` and available to other callers constructing request URLs.

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — Abstract base class providing the interface `GoogleSheetsConnector` implements. Defines `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials`, `CloudAuthType`, and `OAuth2ProviderInfo` types used throughout.
- `Core::g_Core` / `Core` (`core.h`) — Global engine core singleton accessed directly via `Core::g_Core`. Used to reach both `KeyManager` and `OAuthTokenManager`.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — Queried via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider(m_KeyName)` to retrieve API key credentials. The returned pointer is expected to have a valid `m_ApiKey` field.
- `OAuthTokenManager` (`keys/oauthTokenManager.h`) — Queried via `Core::g_Core->GetOAuthTokenManager().GetAccessToken(m_KeyName, errorMessage)` for OAuth2 flows. Returns an empty string if no valid token exists.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — Used only to retrieve the CA bundle path via `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` for TLS certificate validation in `TestConnection`.
- `libcurl` — Used directly (not abstracted) in `TestConnection` for the HTTP request. `curl_easy_init`, `curl_easy_setopt`, `curl_easy_perform`, `curl_easy_getinfo`, `curl_easy_cleanup`, and `curl_slist_*` are all called inline.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`GoogleSheetsConnector` holds no instance state whatsoever; all methods either operate on their arguments or call into global singletons. It is safe to use from multiple threads concurrently as long as the underlying singletons (`Core::g_Core`, `KeyManager`, `OAuthTokenManager`) are themselves thread-safe. `TestConnection` performs a synchronous, blocking HTTP call (15-second timeout) on whichever thread calls it; callers must not invoke it on latency-sensitive or UI threads. Ownership of the connector instance is expected to follow whatever the connector registry in `cloudConnector.h` mandates; the class itself imposes no additional lifetime constraints.

**Notes:**

- The auth type disambiguation in `ResolveCredentials` is asymmetric: if `m_AuthType == CloudAuthType::OAuth2` but `OAuthTokenManager` returns an empty token, the error is cleared and the code falls through to attempt an API key lookup. This means a misconfigured OAuth2 credential will silently try API key auth rather than failing immediately.
- In `TestConnection`, the `useApiKeyParam` flag is true only when `credentials.m_AuthType == CloudAuthType::BearerToken` AND `connection.m_AuthType != CloudAuthType::OAuth2`. This is necessary because `ResolveCredentials` can store an API key in a `BearerToken`-typed credential even when the connection was nominally set up for OAuth2, and in that scenario the code still sends a Bearer header rather than a `?key=` param.
- The `access_type=offline` and `prompt=consent` extra params in `GetOAuth2ProviderInfo` are essential for obtaining a `refresh_token` from Google on repeat authorizations. Without them, Google omits the refresh token after the first authorization, which would break token renewal.
- `DEFAULT_OAUTH_SCOPES` grants full read/write access to all spreadsheets in the authenticated user's account. There is no narrower per-spreadsheet scope available in the Google Sheets API v4.
- `TestConnection` appends the response body to `errorMessage` only if it is non-empty and fewer than 500 characters, to avoid flooding logs with large HTML error pages from Google's API.
- The `connection` parameter in `GetOAuth2ProviderInfo` is explicitly cast to `(void)connection`, confirming it is intentionally unused; all returned values are hardcoded constants regardless of connection configuration.


---

### 27_doc_application_cloud_jiraCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="27-doc-application-cloud-jiracloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/27_doc_application_cloud_jiraCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## JiraCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `JiraCloudTaskExecutor` is the j9t workflow engine's task executor for all Jira issue operations. It handles the `jira_issue` JCWF task type and implements five distinct Jira REST API v3 operations — create, update, transition, comment, and get — by dispatching authenticated HTTP requests through libcurl, serialising request bodies manually as JSON, and writing API responses back into the task's working directory and captured-stdout slot on `TaskInstanceState`.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — The sole override of `ICloudTaskExecutor::ExecuteCloud`. Parses `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` with simdjson to extract the `operation` key and all per-operation parameters, builds an appropriate Jira REST API v3 request, executes it via the file-static `JiraRequest`, and updates `taskState.m_State` to either `Succeeded` or `Failed`. On success, the raw JSON response body (capped at `kMaxCaptureChars` = 1024 characters) is written to `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`, and the full body is persisted to disk via `WriteResponseJson`. Returns `false` and populates `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` on any failure: JSON parse error, missing required parameters, curl transport error, or HTTP ≥ 400 status code.

---

**Internal helpers (file-static, not exposed on the class):**

- `JsonEscape(input) → std::string` — Escapes `"`, `\`, `\n`, `\r`, and `\t` for safe embedding inside a JSON string literal. Used everywhere a task-param string is spliced into a manually constructed JSON body.

- `JiraRequest(method, url, credentials, responseBody, httpCode, requestBody) → bool` — Thin libcurl wrapper. Initialises a fresh `CURL*` per call (no connection reuse), sets a 30-second timeout, follows redirects, applies the CA bundle from `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()`, and authenticates either via HTTP Basic (`CloudAuthType::BasicAuth` uses `m_Username`/`m_Password`) or Bearer token. Returns `false` only on a curl transport error; HTTP error codes are surfaced through the `httpCode` out-parameter and must be checked by the caller.

---

**Collaborators:**

| Collaborator | Role |
|---|---|
| `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) | Base class; provides the `ExecuteCloud` contract and the `WriteResponseJson` utility used to persist the response to disk. |
| `JiraConnector::GetBaseUrl(connection)` (`cloud/jiraConnector.h`) | Constructs the Jira instance base URL from the `CloudConnection` object. |
| `CloudCredentials` | Carries `m_AuthType`, `m_Username`, `m_Password`, and `m_Token`; consumed directly by `JiraRequest`. |
| `CloudConnection` | Provides `m_Params` map, which is checked for a default `project_key` when the `create` operation omits it from task params. |
| `TaskPathResolver` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) | Resolves the workflow base directory and the task-specific working directory path where the response JSON is written. |
| `simdjson` (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) | Parses `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` on every invocation; uses `ondemand` API. |
| `libcurl` | Transport layer for all Jira REST calls; managed entirely within `JiraRequest`. |
| `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` | Supplies the CA bundle path injected into every curl handle for TLS verification. |

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`JiraCloudTaskExecutor` is a thin stateless executor; all state lives in the parameters passed to `ExecuteCloud`. The class inherits its constructor from `ICloudTaskExecutor` via `using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor`. Each call to `ExecuteCloud` allocates and immediately destroys a `CURL*` handle inside `JiraRequest` — there is no shared curl state, so concurrent calls on different task instances are safe as long as the caller ensures the `TaskInstanceState` and `WorkflowRun` references are not shared across threads. `simdjson::ondemand::parser` and `simdjson::padded_string` are stack-allocated per invocation; the `doc` object holds an internal pointer into `paddedJson`, so `paddedJson` must not be destroyed before all `doc` accesses complete — both live in the same stack frame, satisfying this invariant.

---

**Notes:**

- **ADF requirement:** Jira REST API v3 requires Atlassian Document Format (ADF) for description and comment body fields, not plain text. The executor wraps all text in a minimal single-paragraph ADF document (`doc → paragraph → text`). Multi-paragraph or rich ADF is not supported; a description containing embedded newlines has them collapsed to spaces before serialisation, because ADF `text` nodes reject embedded newlines.

- **`description_file` parameter:** The `create` operation supports a `description_file` key that reads description content from a file path, taking precedence over the inline `description` key. This mirrors the pattern used by `polarion_write`'s `field_value_file` and is the intended mechanism for piping AI-generated output into a Jira issue via `{{upstreamTask.output_file}}` template substitution.

- **`update` fields passthrough:** The `fields` parameter for the `update` operation is expected to be a pre-formed JSON object string. It is embedded verbatim into the request body as `{"fields": <raw-value>}` without any validation or re-serialisation. Malformed JSON here will produce a Jira 400 error at runtime.

- **Synthetic response bodies:** For `update` and `transition` operations, Jira's API returns an empty 204 body on success. The executor synthesises a minimal JSON object (`{"ok":true,"issue_key":"...","operation":"update"}`) so that downstream tasks reading `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` or the written response file always receive parseable JSON.

- **Error message truncation:** HTTP error response bodies are appended to `m_LastErrorMessage` only if they are non-empty and under 500 bytes, preventing unbounded error strings for large HTML error pages.

- **Response capture cap:** `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` is limited to 1024 characters (`kMaxCaptureChars`). The full unbounded response is still written to disk by `WriteResponseJson`; the cap applies only to the in-memory captured field.

- **No cancellation handling:** `cancellationToken` is accepted as a parameter to satisfy the interface contract but is never checked inside `ExecuteCloud`. Long-running curl requests cannot be interrupted mid-flight by cancellation.

### 28_doc_application_cloud_jiraConnector_h

<a id="28-doc-application-cloud-jiraconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/28_doc_application_cloud_jiraConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## JiraConnector

**Role:** `JiraConnector` is a concrete `ICloudConnector` implementation that connects j9t's workflow engine to Atlassian Jira instances via the Jira REST API v3. It handles credential resolution from the engine's `KeyManager`, supports both Jira Cloud (Basic Auth: email + API token) and Jira Data Center (Bearer token / PAT), and validates a live connection by issuing an authenticated HTTP GET to `/rest/api/3/myself`. It does not perform any Jira business operations (issue creation, queries, etc.) itself — its sole responsibilities are type identification, credential wiring, and connectivity verification.

**Public API:**

- `GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the string `"jira"`. Used by the connector registry to match a `CloudConnection` to this implementation. No inputs, no side effects.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Issues a live HTTP GET to `<endpoint>/rest/api/3/myself` using resolved credentials. Requires `connection.m_Endpoint` to be non-empty. Calls `ResolveCredentials` internally. Uses a raw `CURL*` handle with a 15-second timeout and automatic redirect following. Returns `true` only when the HTTP response code is below 400. On failure, writes a human-readable diagnostic into `errorMessage`, including the HTTP status code and up to 500 bytes of the response body when the code is ≥ 400.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Looks up the provider named by `connection.m_KeyName` in `KeyManager`. If the provider has both `m_Username` and `m_Password` set, populates `credentials` for `CloudAuthType::BasicAuth` (Jira Cloud path). If only `m_ApiKey` is set, populates `credentials` for `CloudAuthType::BearerToken` (Jira Data Center path). Returns `false` if the key name is empty, the provider is not found, or the provider has neither credential combination. Writes a descriptive error into `errorMessage` on all failure paths.

- `static GetBaseUrl(connection) → std::string` — Returns `connection.m_Endpoint` with any trailing `/` stripped. Static utility used internally by `TestConnection` and available to other callers that need to construct Jira API URLs from the same connection object.

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — Base interface this class implements. Defines `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials`, and `CloudAuthType`.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — Accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider(name)` to fetch stored credentials. The provider object exposes `m_Username`, `m_Password`, and `m_ApiKey`.
- `Core` (`core.h`) / `Engine` (`engine.h`) — `Core::g_Core` is the global singleton entry point used to reach `KeyManager`. `engine.h` is included but not directly referenced in the visible code; likely pulled in transitively.
- `libcurl` — Used directly (not through `CurlWrapper`'s higher-level API) for the test connection HTTP call. `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` is called to obtain the CA bundle path for TLS verification.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — Provides `GetCaBundlePath()` only; the full curl session is managed manually in `TestConnection`.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`JiraConnector` carries no instance state — all methods operate solely on their arguments or on the global `Core::g_Core` singleton. It is safe to call from any thread provided `Core::g_Core` and its `KeyManager` are thread-safe (that guarantee is owned by those subsystems, not this class). `TestConnection` performs a synchronous, blocking network call on whatever thread invokes it; callers must not invoke it on a latency-sensitive or UI thread. The raw `CURL*` handle is created, used, and destroyed entirely within the scope of a single `TestConnection` call — there is no persistent curl state.

**Notes:**

- The credential resolution priority is fixed: if a provider has both `m_Username`/`m_Password` and `m_ApiKey`, the Basic Auth path wins because the `if` branch for username+password is checked first. A provider with only `m_ApiKey` populated (and empty username/password) correctly falls through to the Bearer path.
- Response body is only appended to `errorMessage` when its length is strictly less than 500 bytes, preventing large HTML error pages from polluting diagnostics.
- HTTP 401 is given a distinct, more actionable error message than the generic `>= 400` path.
- No CA bundle is set when `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` returns an empty string, which means curl falls back to its compiled-in default. This may cause TLS failures in environments with custom certificate authorities if the bundle path is not configured.
- The `m_AuthType` field on `CloudConnection` is documented in the header comment as an input hint, but `ResolveCredentials` ignores it entirely — the actual auth type is inferred from whichever credential fields are populated in the `KeyManager` provider.
- `connection.m_Params["project_key"]` is documented in the header as a supported configuration key but is not consumed anywhere in this file; it is intended for use by higher-level Jira operation code that builds on this connector.


---

### 29_doc_application_cloud_oneDriveCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="29-doc-application-cloud-onedrivecloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/29_doc_application_cloud_oneDriveCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## OneDriveCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `OneDriveCloudTaskExecutor` is a concrete `ICloudTaskExecutor` subclass responsible for transferring files between the local workflow working directory and Microsoft OneDrive via the Microsoft Graph API. It handles exactly two operations — `upload` and `download` — dispatched by the j9t workflow engine when it encounters task definitions of type `onedrive_upload` or `onedrive_download`. All HTTP I/O is performed synchronously using libcurl; authentication relies on a bearer token supplied through `CloudCredentials`. On success the raw Graph API JSON response (or a synthetic JSON object for downloads) is captured into `TaskInstanceState` and also written to disk via `WriteResponseJson`.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — Protected override called by the base class after it has resolved the named `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials`. Parses `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` with simdjson to extract `operation`, `remote_path`, and `local_path`. For `upload`: resolves `local_path` relative to the task working directory, reads the entire file into a `std::string`, and issues a `PUT` to `<graphBase>/me/drive/root:/{remote_path}:/content` with `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`. For `download`: creates any missing output directories, then streams the Graph API response directly to disk via `GraphDownload`. Sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded` or `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed` and populates `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` on failure. Returns `false` on any error, `true` on success.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class that resolves the named connection and credentials before forwarding to `ExecuteCloud`; inherits its constructor via `using`.
- `OneDriveConnector::GetGraphBaseUrl(connection)` (`cloud/oneDriveConnector.h`) — provides the Graph API root URL (e.g. `https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0`) from the `CloudConnection` object.
- `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory(workflowDefinition)` and `TaskPathResolver::ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath(baseDir, workingDirectory)` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — translate workflow-relative paths to absolute filesystem paths for both upload source and download destination.
- `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — supplies the CA bundle path for TLS verification; if non-empty it is applied to every libcurl handle via `CURLOPT_CAINFO`.
- `simdjson` — used on-demand to parse `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`; the parser and padded string are stack-allocated per call.
- `WriteResponseJson(workDir, taskState, responseBody)` — free function (declared in `engine.h` or a related header) that persists the response JSON to the task working directory after a successful operation.
- `LOG_APP_INFO` — engine-wide logging macro used to emit one info line per successful transfer.
- `CloudCredentials::m_Token` — the bearer token string embedded directly into the `Authorization` header; no token-refresh logic exists in this class.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`OneDriveCloudTaskExecutor` carries no mutable member state of its own; all working data is stack-allocated per `ExecuteCloud` call. The class is therefore safe to call from multiple threads provided each call operates on distinct `TaskInstanceState` and `WorkflowRun` objects — consistent with j9t's general per-task-instance execution model. The two static helpers `GraphRequest` and `GraphDownload` are internal to the translation unit and likewise stateless. libcurl handles (`CURL*`) are created, used, and destroyed within each helper call; there is no shared curl state. Ownership of all inputs (`WorkflowDefinition`, `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials`) remains with the caller for the duration of the call.

---

**Notes:**

- **Upload reads the entire file into memory** as a `std::string` before the PUT. There is no chunked or streaming upload path; very large files will exhaust process heap. The 256 MB `kMaxDownloadBytes` guard (`CURLOPT_MAXFILESIZE_LARGE`) applies only to downloads, not uploads.
- **Download size cap is 256 MB** (`kMaxDownloadBytes`). If Graph returns a file larger than this limit, libcurl aborts the transfer and `GraphDownload` returns a curl error, not an HTTP 400-range code. The partial file is left on disk at `outputPath`.
- **The `operation` key is not mentioned in the header-level comment** which lists only `connection`, `remote_path`, and `local_path`. Callers must also supply `"operation": "upload"` or `"operation": "download"` in the params JSON or the task will fail immediately.
- **simdjson on-demand parsing is document-order sensitive.** The `getStringParam` lambda re-enters `doc` by key after a full `iterate()`. If simdjson's on-demand iterator has advanced past a key the lookup will silently return an empty string. Ensure params JSON presents keys in the order they are accessed (`operation` → `remote_path` → `local_path`) or switch to `simdjson::ondemand::object` with explicit field extraction.
- **HTTP 4xx/5xx responses for uploads** truncate the response body to 500 characters when appending to `m_LastErrorMessage`; download failures do not include the response body at all (the file handle is closed before the HTTP code is checked, so body content is lost).
- **`cancellationToken` is accepted but never consulted** inside `ExecuteCloud`. Long-running transfers (up to the 300-second `kTimeoutSeconds` wall-clock limit) will not be interrupted even if the workflow run is cancelled.
- **The response captured in `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`** is capped at `kMaxCaptureChars` (1024 bytes). For downloads, the captured value is always the synthetic string `{"ok":true,"operation":"download","remote_path":"..."}`, not actual Graph API response body.
- **TLS verification** depends entirely on `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` returning a valid path. If it returns an empty string, libcurl falls back to its compiled-in default CA store; there is no explicit `CURLOPT_SSL_VERIFYPEER` override, so the default (verify = on) applies.


---

### 30_doc_application_cloud_oneDriveConnector_h

<a id="30-doc-application-cloud-onedriveconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/30_doc_application_cloud_oneDriveConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## OneDriveConnector

**Role:** `OneDriveConnector` is the Microsoft OneDrive integration point within j9t's cloud connector subsystem. It implements `ICloudConnector` to provide OAuth 2.0 authentication and connectivity verification for OneDrive via the Microsoft Graph API. Specifically, it resolves live access tokens from `OAuthTokenManager`, constructs tenant-aware Microsoft Identity Platform authorization and token endpoint URLs, fills in `OAuth2ProviderInfo` for the PKCE public-client flow, and validates a live connection by issuing a `GET /me/drive` request against the Graph API.

---

**Public API:**

- `GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the fixed string `"onedrive"`. Used by the connector registry to dispatch to this implementation when a `CloudConnection` has type `"onedrive"`.

- `TestConnection(CloudConnection const& connection, std::string& errorMessage) → bool` — Validates a fully configured OneDrive connection end-to-end. Requires `client_id` to be present in `connection.m_Params`. Calls `ResolveCredentials` to obtain a live Bearer token, then performs a synchronous `GET {graphBaseUrl}/me/drive` via a raw `curl_easy_*` call with a 15-second timeout. Returns `true` only on HTTP 2xx. Writes a human-readable diagnostic into `errorMessage` on failure, including the HTTP status code and up to 500 characters of the response body when available. HTTP 401 receives a dedicated message noting that the token may be expired.

- `ResolveCredentials(CloudConnection const& connection, CloudCredentials& credentials, std::string& errorMessage) → bool` — Populates `credentials.m_AuthType` with `CloudAuthType::OAuth2` and `credentials.m_Token` with a current access token fetched from `OAuthTokenManager::GetAccessToken` using `connection.m_KeyName` as the credential key. Returns `false` if `m_KeyName` is empty or if the token manager returns an empty token. Does not perform any network call itself; token refresh is delegated entirely to `OAuthTokenManager`.

- `GetOAuth2ProviderInfo(CloudConnection const& connection, OAuth2ProviderInfo& info) const → bool` — Fills `info` with the Microsoft Identity Platform authorize URL, token URL, default scopes (`"Files.ReadWrite offline_access"`), an extra authorize parameter `response_mode=query`, and sets `m_RequiresClientSecret = false` to signal PKCE public-client behaviour. Reads `tenant_id` from `connection.m_Params`; falls back to `"common"` if absent or empty.

- `GetGraphBaseUrl(CloudConnection const& connection) → std::string` (static) — Returns `connection.m_Endpoint` with any trailing slash stripped, or `DEFAULT_GRAPH_BASE_URL` (`"https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0"`) if `m_Endpoint` is empty. Used internally by `TestConnection` and available to callers building Graph API paths.

- `GetAuthorizeUrl(std::string const& tenantId) → std::string` (static) — Constructs `https://login.microsoftonline.com/{tenantId}/oauth2/v2.0/authorize`. Substitutes `"common"` when `tenantId` is empty.

- `GetTokenUrl(std::string const& tenantId) → std::string` (static) — Constructs `https://login.microsoftonline.com/{tenantId}/oauth2/v2.0/token`. Same empty-string fallback as `GetAuthorizeUrl`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — Base interface this class implements; defines `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials`, `CloudAuthType`, and `OAuth2ProviderInfo`.
- `OAuthTokenManager` (`keys/oauthTokenManager.h`) — Provides `GetAccessToken(keyName, errorMessage)`. `ResolveCredentials` delegates all token lifecycle (caching, refresh) to this manager, accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetOAuthTokenManager()`.
- `Core` / `Engine` (`core.h`, `engine.h`) — `Core::g_Core` is the global singleton gateway to `OAuthTokenManager`. `OneDriveConnector` does not store its own reference; it calls through `g_Core` on each invocation.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — Queried for the CA bundle path via `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()`. The path is applied to the raw CURL handle in `TestConnection` when non-empty.
- `libcurl` — Used directly (not through any wrapper) inside `TestConnection` for the `GET /me/drive` probe request. The class does **not** use a persistent handle; it initialises and cleans up a `CURL*` per call.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`OneDriveConnector` carries no mutable state of its own; all data lives in the `CloudConnection` argument passed per call. Instances may therefore be shared across threads without synchronisation concerns at this class's level. However, `TestConnection` and `ResolveCredentials` both call through `Core::g_Core`, so thread-safety of `OAuthTokenManager::GetAccessToken` is governed by that class's own locking, not by `OneDriveConnector`. The raw `CURL*` handle in `TestConnection` is stack-local and never shared. Ownership of `CloudCredentials` and `OAuth2ProviderInfo` output objects is the caller's responsibility; this class only writes into them and does not retain references.

---

**Notes:**

- `TestConnection` uses a raw `curl_easy_*` call rather than the project's `CurlWrapper` abstraction for the actual HTTP operation. This means features provided by `CurlWrapper` (connection pooling, uniform error handling, etc.) are bypassed here; the call is entirely fire-and-forget with a hardcoded 15-second timeout.
- HTTP 401 is given a distinct, more actionable error message than other 4xx/5xx codes. No automatic token refresh is attempted inside `TestConnection`; if the token is stale, the call fails and the caller is expected to trigger re-authentication through the OAuth flow.
- `m_RequiresClientSecret = false` in `GetOAuth2ProviderInfo` explicitly signals PKCE flow. Callers constructing the token exchange must not include a client secret; including one would be a protocol error for public clients registered in Azure AD without a secret.
- `GetGraphBaseUrl` silently strips a trailing slash from a custom `m_Endpoint`. Callers appending paths (e.g. `"/me/drive"`) must include the leading slash in their path string, not in `m_Endpoint`.
- The `tenant_id` value `"common"` selects Microsoft's multi-tenant / personal-account endpoint. For single-tenant enterprise deployments, `tenant_id` must be set to the specific Azure AD tenant GUID or verified domain, or tokens issued by other tenants will be rejected by Graph.
- The response body is included in `errorMessage` only when it is non-empty and fewer than 500 bytes; larger error payloads are silently truncated to just the HTTP status code in the message.


---

### 31_doc_application_cloud_polarionConnector_h

<a id="31-doc-application-cloud-polarionconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/31_doc_application_cloud_polarionConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## PolarionConnector

**Role:** `PolarionConnector` is the cloud-connector adapter that integrates Polarion ALM into j9t's generic cloud abstraction layer. It implements the `ICloudConnector` interface to give the rest of the engine a uniform way to validate and authenticate against a Polarion server using a Personal Access Token (PAT) stored in the `KeyManager`. The class does not maintain any per-instance state; it is a stateless policy object that delegates HTTP work to `PolarionClient` and credential lookup to `KeyManager`.

**Public API:**

- `GetType() → std::string` — Returns the fixed string `"polarion"`. Used by the cloud connector registry to match a `CloudConnection` record whose type field equals this string. No side effects.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Validates that the supplied `CloudConnection` is correctly configured and that the Polarion server is reachable. Sequentially checks that `connection.m_Endpoint` is non-empty, that `connection.m_Params["project_id"]` is present and non-empty, calls `ResolveCredentials` internally to confirm the PAT exists, then issues a real HTTP GET against `/rest/v1/projects/{project_id}/workitems?query=type:requirement&page[size]=1&page[number]=1` via `PolarionClient::HttpGet`. Any failure populates `errorMessage` with a human-readable explanation and returns `false`. A trailing slash on `m_Endpoint` is stripped before the URL is assembled. On success returns `true`; the response body is discarded (the call is purely a connectivity probe).

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Looks up the credential named by `connection.m_KeyName` from `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`. On success it populates `credentials.m_AuthType` with `CloudAuthType::BearerToken` and `credentials.m_Token` with the provider's `m_ApiKey` (the PAT). Fails with a descriptive `errorMessage` if `m_KeyName` is empty, if the provider entry does not exist in `KeyManager`, or if the provider's `m_ApiKey` field is empty.

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — base interface that defines the contract (`GetType`, `TestConnection`, `ResolveCredentials`) and the `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials`, and `CloudAuthType` types.
- `PolarionClient` (`workflow/filter/polarionClient.h`) — performs the actual HTTP GET and provides the static `UrlEncode` helper used when building the test URL. `PolarionConnector` instantiates `PolarionClient` on the stack inside `TestConnection`; no shared instance is kept.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider(name)` to retrieve the named API-key credential. The returned pointer is a raw non-owning pointer into `KeyManager`'s internal store.
- `Core` (`core.h`, `engine.h`) — provides the global singleton `Core::g_Core` through which `KeyManager` is reached.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`PolarionConnector` carries no member state, so multiple threads may safely call different instances simultaneously. However, `ResolveCredentials` dereferences `Core::g_Core` and calls into `KeyManager::GetProvider`, which returns a raw pointer. Callers must ensure that `KeyManager` is not mutated (e.g. credentials reloaded) while that pointer is in use, as there is no locking inside `ResolveCredentials`. The `PolarionClient` instance created in `TestConnection` is stack-local and does not escape. Ownership of the `PolarionConnector` object itself is governed by the cloud connector registry that registers it; the class imposes no additional lifetime requirements.

**Notes:**

- The connectivity probe in `TestConnection` always queries `type:requirement` work items with a page size of 1. This is a hard-coded heuristic; if a project contains no requirements the HTTP call may still succeed with an empty result set, which is treated as a successful connection test.
- `PolarionClient::UrlEncode` is called on the `project_id` value but the query string parameters `page[size]` and `page[number]` are percent-encoded as literals (`%5Bsize%5D`, `%5Bnumber%5D`) directly in the string rather than through `UrlEncode`. Callers constructing similar URLs elsewhere should be aware of this inconsistency.
- `ResolveCredentials` is `public` and callable independently of `TestConnection`. The engine's broader credential-resolution path (e.g., when the workflow runner needs a token at execution time) calls it directly without also triggering `TestConnection`.
- `m_KeyName` must identify a credential of the `api_key` type; `ResolveCredentials` reads only `m_ApiKey` from the provider and does not inspect username/password or OAuth fields. Supplying a non-API-key credential will result in an empty-token error.
- The class emits no log output; all diagnostics are returned through the `errorMessage` output parameter.


---

### 32_doc_application_cloud_polarionWriteTaskExecutor_h

<a id="32-doc-application-cloud-polarionwritetaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/32_doc_application_cloud_polarionWriteTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## PolarionWriteTaskExecutor

**Role:** `PolarionWriteTaskExecutor` is the j9t workflow task executor for all write-side (and one read-side) interactions with a Siemens Polarion ALM instance. It handles task type `"polarion_write"` and dispatches to five distinct Polarion REST API operations — updating a work item field, creating a work item, uploading an attachment, downloading an attachment, and fetching linked work items — based on the `"operation"` key in the task's JSON params. The class bridges the generic cloud task infrastructure (`ICloudTaskExecutor`) with the Polarion-specific HTTP client (`PolarionClient`), resolves credential and connection metadata from the established `CloudConnection`, constructs or validates JSON:API request bodies, and writes the raw API response both to `TaskInstanceState::m_CapturedStdout` (truncated) and to a JSON file in the task's working directory.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — Protected override called by the `ICloudTaskExecutor` base after it has resolved the named cloud connection and its credentials. Reads all operation parameters from `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` using simdjson on-demand parsing, performs the selected Polarion operation via `PolarionClient`, writes response output, and updates `taskState.m_State` to `Succeeded` or `Failed`. Returns `true` on success, `false` on any validation or API failure. On failure, `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` is populated with a human-readable description before returning. The constructor is inherited directly from `ICloudTaskExecutor` via `using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class that owns connection lookup, credential resolution, and calls into `ExecuteCloud`. Also provides `WriteResponseJson`, used here to persist the full response body to the task working directory.
- `PolarionClient` (`workflow/filter/polarionClient.h`) — performs all actual HTTP operations: `UpdateWorkItem`, `CreateWorkItem`, `UploadAttachment`, `DownloadAttachment`, and `FetchLinkedWorkItems`. Constructed on the stack inside `ExecuteCloud`; no shared state.
- `CloudConnection` — supplies `m_Endpoint` (the Polarion base URL) and `m_Params`, from which `"project_id"` is required. Missing `project_id` is a hard failure.
- `CloudCredentials` — supplies `m_Token`, passed verbatim as the bearer token to all `PolarionClient` calls.
- `TaskPathResolver` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — resolves `workflowBaseDir` and the task-specific working directory used when writing the response JSON file. If `workflowBaseDir` is empty, the file-write step is silently skipped.
- `simdjson` — used for on-demand JSON parsing of `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`. A fresh `simdjson::ondemand::parser` and `simdjson::padded_string` are allocated per invocation.
- `TaskInstanceState` — mutated in place: `m_State`, `m_LastErrorMessage`, and `m_CapturedStdout` are all written by this class.
- `engine.h` — provides `LOG_APP_INFO` logging macro used for structured info logging after each successful operation.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`PolarionClient` is instantiated on the stack inside `ExecuteCloud` and is not shared. The executor itself holds no mutable member state, so separate task runs using distinct instances are safe to execute concurrently. However, `TaskInstanceState` and `WorkflowRun` are passed by reference and must not be shared across concurrent calls to the same task instance. The simdjson on-demand parser is also stack-local and not thread-safe to share. The base class (`ICloudTaskExecutor`) governs the lifetime of the executor object; callers should consult that class for ownership semantics. `cancellationToken` is accepted but not checked inside `ExecuteCloud` in the current implementation — long-running attachment transfers will not respond to cancellation mid-flight.

---

**Notes:**

- `"linked_items"` is named as a write operation but is semantically a read (`FetchLinkedWorkItems`). This is an architectural oddity; it lives in this executor purely because it shares the same connection and credentials flow.
- The convenience path `field_name` + `field_value_file` reads the entire file into memory as a string before JSON-escaping and embedding it in the request body. There is no size limit enforced on this file, so very large files will produce an oversized JSON:API body.
- `field_value_file` takes precedence over `field_value` when both are supplied; `field_value` is silently overwritten.
- `"file_name"` in `upload_attachment` defaults to `std::filesystem::path(filePath).filename()` if omitted, using the local filesystem basename.
- `download_attachment` does not produce a Polarion JSON:API response; instead, a synthetic `{"ok":true,"file_path":"..."}` object is constructed and treated as the response body for output and capture purposes.
- `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` is capped at `kMaxCaptureChars` (1024 characters) via `substr`. The full response is written to the working-directory file without truncation via `WriteResponseJson`.
- `BuildFieldUpdateBody` hardcodes `"type":"text/plain"` for the field value wrapper. Polarion rich-text or enumeration fields that require a different type cannot be set through the `field_name`/`field_value` convenience path; callers must supply a raw `"body"` instead.
- The `JsonEscape` helper handles the full set of JSON string escape sequences including all control characters below U+0020, but it does not validate or re-encode non-ASCII UTF-8 byte sequences — they pass through byte-for-byte.


---

### 33_doc_application_cloud_postgresConnector_h

<a id="33-doc-application-cloud-postgresconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/33_doc_application_cloud_postgresConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## PostgresConnector

**Role:** `PostgresConnector` is a concrete implementation of `ICloudConnector` that wires the j9t workflow engine to PostgreSQL databases via the libpq C API. It is responsible for three tightly coupled concerns: identifying itself as the `"postgres"` connector type, resolving database credentials from the engine's `KeyManager`, and performing a live connectivity probe against a target PostgreSQL server. It lives in the `AIAssistant` namespace under `cloud/postgresConnector.h/.cpp` and is registered (externally, not shown here) so the cloud connector subsystem can dispatch `CloudConnection` records whose type matches `"postgres"` to this class.

---

**Public API:**

- `GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the fixed string `"postgres"`. Used by the connector registry to match `CloudConnection` records to this implementation. No side effects.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Looks up `connection.m_KeyName` in the global `KeyManager` (`Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider()`). On success, populates `credentials.m_AuthType` with `CloudAuthType::BasicAuth` and copies `m_Username` / `m_Password` from the retrieved provider. Returns `false` and populates `errorMessage` if the key name is empty, the provider is not found, or the resolved username is empty. The password is allowed to be empty (passwordless auth). Does not touch the network.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Validates that the `"database"` param is present, calls `ResolveCredentials`, builds a libpq keyword/value connection string via `BuildConnectionString`, opens a synchronous connection with `PQconnectdb`, then executes `SELECT 1` to confirm the session is functional. Both the `PGconn` and `PGresult` handles are created and destroyed within this call — no connection is retained after it returns. Returns `false` with a descriptive `errorMessage` on any failure at any stage. Hard-codes a `connect_timeout=10` (seconds).

- `static BuildConnectionString(connection, credentials) → std::string` — Pure function (no global state reads) that constructs a libpq keyword/value format connection string. Parses `connection.m_Endpoint` as `host:port` using `rfind(':')` on the last colon, validating that the suffix is all-digit before treating it as a port; otherwise the entire endpoint is used as the host and port defaults to `5432`. Reads `"database"` (default `"postgres"`) and `"sslmode"` (default `"prefer"`) from `connection.m_Params`. All values are wrapped in libpq single-quote escaping that backslash-escapes `\` and `'` characters. The `connect_timeout=10` keyword is always appended. The password keyword is omitted entirely if `credentials.m_Password` is empty.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — abstract base class; `PostgresConnector` overrides its three virtual methods. `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials` are data types defined there; callers must populate `CloudConnection.m_Endpoint`, `m_KeyName`, and `m_Params` before calling any method.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — accessed through `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`. `ResolveCredentials` calls `GetProvider(m_KeyName)` and reads `m_Username` / `m_Password` directly off the returned provider pointer. If the `KeyManager`'s provider map changes concurrently, the raw pointer dereference is unsafe (see Threading).
- `Core` (`core.h`) — provides the singleton `g_Core` through which `KeyManager` is reached. `Core::g_Core` must be non-null when `ResolveCredentials` or `TestConnection` is called.
- `libpq` (PostgreSQL C library) — used directly via `PQconnectdb`, `PQstatus`, `PQexec`, `PQresultStatus`, `PQerrorMessage`, `PQresultErrorMessage`, `PQclear`, `PQfinish`. The calling code must link against `libpq`.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`PostgresConnector` itself carries no mutable state; all instance methods are effectively stateless with respect to the object itself, making the object safe to share across threads for concurrent calls to `GetType` and `BuildConnectionString`. However, `ResolveCredentials` and `TestConnection` both dereference a raw pointer returned by `KeyManager::GetProvider` without any visible locking. If the `KeyManager` can invalidate or reallocate provider entries concurrently, callers must ensure external synchronization or hold a lock that covers the `GetProvider` call through the credential field reads. `TestConnection` opens and closes its `PGconn` within the stack frame — no heap ownership escapes the call. `Core::g_Core` must outlive any call into this class.

---

**Notes:**

- The host/port split uses `rfind(':')` (last colon), which correctly handles IPv4 addresses like `192.168.1.1:5432` but will misparse bare IPv6 addresses (`::1` or `[::1]`). IPv6 endpoints must either omit the port or be passed pre-formatted in a way libpq accepts via the `host=` keyword, which may itself not accept bracketed IPv6 in all libpq versions.
- `BuildConnectionString` defaults the database to `"postgres"` when the `"database"` param is absent or empty, but `TestConnection` explicitly rejects a missing or empty `"database"` param before calling `BuildConnectionString`. The default in `BuildConnectionString` is therefore unreachable through `TestConnection`; it is only relevant if `BuildConnectionString` is called directly by other code.
- The single-quote escape function wraps values in libpq's `'value'` quoting style. This is correct for libpq keyword/value connection strings but is distinct from URI-format connection strings; the two formats must not be mixed.
- `connect_timeout=10` is hard-coded and not configurable via `m_Params`. Long-running DNS lookups or firewalled hosts will block the calling thread for up to 10 seconds during `TestConnection`.
- An empty password is silently accepted by `ResolveCredentials` but causes the `password=` keyword to be omitted from the connection string entirely, which is the correct behavior for passwordless or trust-authenticated PostgreSQL users.

### 34_doc_application_cloud_providerRateLimitPolicy_h

<a id="34-doc-application-cloud-providerratelimitpolicy-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/34_doc_application_cloud_providerRateLimitPolicy_h/docu.output.md`



## ProviderRateLimitPolicy

**Role:** `ProviderRateLimitPolicy` is a thread-safe, per-provider rate-limiting layer that sits on top of `CloudRetryPolicy`. It maintains a registry of named provider types (e.g. `"Slack"`, `"GitHub"`, `"GraphAPI"`, `"Snowflake"`) and, for each, enforces two independent throttling dimensions: a minimum inter-request interval and a burst-count cap over a rolling time window. Callers query how long to wait before sending a request and record each dispatched request so the policy can update its internal timing state. The embedded `CloudRetryPolicy::Config` per provider also lets the same registration call supply provider-specific HTTP status codes that should trigger retry logic upstream.

**Public API:**

- `RegisterProvider(providerType, config) → void` — Inserts or replaces the `ProviderState` for the named provider. `config` is moved in. Any existing burst count and timing state stored for that key is reset to the new `ProviderConfig` defaults because the entire `ProviderState` struct is reconstructed by copy-assignment of `m_Config` only; `m_LastRequestTime`, `m_BurstCount`, and `m_BurstWindowStart` are zero-initialised by their default member initialisers. Call this once at startup per provider before any concurrent use.

- `GetRetryConfig(providerType) const → CloudRetryPolicy::Config` — Returns the `CloudRetryPolicy::Config` embedded in the registered `ProviderConfig` for `providerType`. If the provider is not registered, returns a default-constructed `CloudRetryPolicy::Config`. This is a pure read once the lock is acquired; no state is mutated.

- `GetThrottleDelayMs(providerType) → int` — Returns the number of milliseconds the caller must wait before sending the next request to `providerType`. Returns `0` if the provider is unregistered or neither throttle dimension is active. Evaluates minimum-interval first, then burst-limit. **Does not mutate `m_LastRequestTime` or `m_BurstCount`**; the caller must separately call `RecordRequest` after the request is actually dispatched. If the burst window has expired when this is called, it resets `m_BurstCount` to `0` and restarts `m_BurstWindowStart` as a side effect.

- `RecordRequest(providerType) → void` — Stamps `m_LastRequestTime` to `now` and increments `m_BurstCount` for the named provider. Silently no-ops if the provider is not registered. Must be called after each successfully dispatched request to keep throttle calculations accurate.

**Collaborators:**

- `CloudRetryPolicy` / `CloudRetryPolicy::Config` (`cloud/cloudRetryPolicy.h`) — `ProviderConfig` embeds a `CloudRetryPolicy::Config` so that retry-status-code configuration and backoff parameters travel alongside rate-limit configuration as a single registration unit. `ProviderRateLimitPolicy` does not inherit from `CloudRetryPolicy`; the comment in the header says it *extends* that policy in a semantic sense, but the relationship is composition, not inheritance.

**Threading / lifetime:**

- All public methods acquire `m_Mutex` via `std::lock_guard` before touching `m_Providers`, making the class safe to call concurrently from multiple threads.
- `GetRetryConfig` takes the lock on a `const` instance via a `mutable std::mutex`, so `const`-qualified references remain callable from read-only contexts without UB.
- `GetThrottleDelayMs` mutates burst-window state under the lock, so it is not logically `const` despite only being a query from the caller's perspective — do not assume it is side-effect-free.
- `ProviderRateLimitPolicy` has no virtual destructor and no explicit ownership semantics; it is typically owned by whatever subsystem manages outbound provider connections and should outlive all threads that call into it.
- There is no condition variable or async notification; callers are responsible for sleeping the returned delay before sending.

**Notes:**

- The two throttle dimensions are evaluated independently and only the **first** triggered condition's delay is returned. If both `m_MinRequestIntervalMs` and `m_BurstLimit` would independently demand a wait, `GetThrottleDelayMs` returns the minimum-interval delay and never reaches the burst check. Callers cannot rely on a single call capturing both constraints simultaneously; they must re-call after sleeping to catch the second constraint.
- Burst-window reset happens inside `GetThrottleDelayMs`, not inside `RecordRequest`. If `GetThrottleDelayMs` is never called (e.g. caller skips the check), the window never resets automatically, leaving `m_BurstCount` stale.
- `RecordRequest` increments `m_BurstCount` unconditionally without checking whether a new burst window should have started. If `RecordRequest` is called but `GetThrottleDelayMs` was not called first in the same window cycle, burst counts can accumulate past a window boundary without being cleared.
- When `m_BurstCount >= m_BurstLimit`, the returned delay is `max(remaining, 1)`, guaranteeing at least 1 ms is returned rather than 0, which prevents the caller from treating a saturated burst window as "send immediately".
- `RegisterProvider` replaces only `m_Config` inside an existing `ProviderState`; because `m_Providers[providerType]` default-constructs the state on first insertion and the assignment only touches `m_Config`, re-registering a provider mid-flight will preserve any ongoing burst window timing from before the re-registration.


---

### 35_doc_application_cloud_redmineCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="35-doc-application-cloud-redminecloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/35_doc_application_cloud_redmineCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## RedmineCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `RedmineCloudTaskExecutor` is the j9t cloud task executor for the JCWF task type `"redmine_issue"`. It bridges the j9t workflow engine to the Redmine REST API, supporting two operations: listing issues for a project (`list_issues`, via `GET /issues.json`) and updating an existing issue with notes and/or a new assignee (`update_issue`, via `PUT /issues/{id}.json`). The class inherits from `ICloudTaskExecutor` and overrides the single `ExecuteCloud` hook; all HTTP traffic is performed inline via libcurl with a 30-second timeout. On success the raw Redmine response body is written to `response.json` in the task's working directory and the first 1 024 bytes are captured into `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`.

---

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — The sole entry point, called by the cloud task dispatch layer. Parses `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` with simdjson to extract the `"operation"` key, then dispatches to one of two sub-paths:

  - **`list_issues`** — Builds a URL of the form `<baseUrl>/issues.json?project_id=<ident>&status_id=<status>&limit=<n>` and fires a `GET`. `project_identifier` is taken from the task params first and falls back to `connection.m_Params`; `status` defaults to `"open"`; `limit` defaults to `25`. The raw JSON response body is written to `response.json` and truncated to 1 024 bytes in `m_CapturedStdout`.

  - **`update_issue`** — Requires `issue_id` (string or integer). Optionally accepts `notes` (inline string) or `notes_file` (path relative to j9t cwd; trailing/leading whitespace is stripped). Also accepts `assigned_to_id` (inline string or integer) or `assigned_to_id_file` (one integer per line, whitespace-trimmed). Both pairs can be combined; at least one of the four must be present. Constructs a JSON body `{"issue":{"notes":"…","assigned_to_id":N}}` and fires a `PUT`. Because Redmine returns HTTP 204 with an empty body on success, the executor synthesises `{"ok":true,"operation":"update_issue","issue_id":<id>}` as the response payload so downstream steps always receive parseable JSON.

  Returns `true` on success and sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded`. Returns `false` with `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed` and a descriptive message in `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` on any parse error, missing required parameter, curl failure, or HTTP ≥ 400 response. `workflowRun` and `cancellationToken` are accepted but currently unused (cast to `void`).

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class; provides the `ExecuteCloud` virtual hook, the `WriteResponseJson` helper that writes `response.json`, and the constructor forwarded via `using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor`.
- `RedmineConnector::GetBaseUrl(connection)` (`cloud/redmineConnector.h`) — extracts the scheme+host base URL string from the `CloudConnection` record.
- `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` — `connection.m_Params` holds per-connection key/value strings (e.g. the default `project_identifier`); `credentials.m_Token` is sent as the `X-Redmine-API-Key` HTTP header.
- `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` / `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — resolves the filesystem path where `response.json` is written.
- `simdjson::ondemand` — used to parse `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`; a fresh `parser` and `padded_string` are allocated per call.
- `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` — provides the CA bundle path set into `CURLOPT_CAINFO`; if the returned string is empty, the option is omitted and libcurl uses its compiled-in default.
- `engine.h` / `LOG_APP_INFO` — structured logging macro used to emit an info-level entry on successful operations.
- Static file-local helpers: `JsonEscape` (RFC-correct escaping of `"`, `\`, control characters), `ReadFileToString`, `TrimWhitespace`, and `RedmineRequest` (thin libcurl wrapper).

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`RedmineCloudTaskExecutor` has no mutable instance state of its own; all working data is stack-allocated per `ExecuteCloud` call. The libcurl handle is created and destroyed within the static `RedmineRequest` function (not reused), so concurrent calls on different executor instances are safe with respect to curl state. However, libcurl itself requires that `curl_global_init` has been called once before any use — this is the responsibility of the engine startup code, not this class. The `simdjson::ondemand::parser` and `padded_string` are also stack-local and not shared. No locks are held or required by this class directly.

---

**Notes:**

- `cancellationToken` is accepted but entirely ignored; long-running Redmine requests (up to the 30-second curl timeout) cannot be interrupted mid-flight.
- `notes_file` and `assigned_to_id_file` paths are treated as relative to the j9t process current working directory, not to the workflow's working directory. If the file cannot be opened, `ReadFileToString` returns an empty string; for `notes_file` this is treated as a fatal error, but for `assigned_to_id_file` an unreadable file silently results in an empty `assignedToId` string, which may then cause the "requires at least one" validation to fire if `notes` is also empty.
- `assigned_to_id` is emitted as an unquoted JSON integer if and only if every character in the resolved string is an ASCII digit. A non-numeric value (e.g. a username string) is emitted as a quoted JSON string, which Redmine will reject — there is no validation or error returned before the HTTP call.
- HTTP error bodies are only appended to `m_LastErrorMessage` when the body is strictly less than 500 bytes; larger error payloads are silently discarded from the message (but do not affect the failure path).
- The synthesised 204 response body (`{"ok":true,…}`) is written to both `m_CapturedStdout` and `response.json`, so downstream tasks that key off `response.json` will see valid JSON regardless of Redmine's no-content response.
- `kMaxCaptureChars` is 1 024; `m_CapturedStdout` is always a prefix of the raw response, not a summary, so it may truncate mid-JSON for large `list_issues` responses.
- The `doc` simdjson document object is captured by reference in the `getStringParam` lambda. simdjson on-demand parsers are forward-only; re-querying a key that was already iterated past will silently return an empty result. The code queries `operation` first, then all sub-operation keys in order — callers adding new params must maintain compatible document traversal order.


---

### 36_doc_application_cloud_redmineConnector_h

<a id="36-doc-application-cloud-redmineconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/36_doc_application_cloud_redmineConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## RedmineConnector

**Role:** `RedmineConnector` is the j9t cloud connector plugin for Atlassian-style Redmine project management instances, communicating exclusively via the Redmine REST API. It implements the `ICloudConnector` interface to give the engine a uniform way to validate and authenticate against a self-hosted or remote Redmine server. Its two responsibilities are credential resolution — pulling an API key out of `KeyManager` and packaging it for use by action executors — and connection testing, which performs a live HTTP probe against the authenticated Redmine instance to confirm reachability and key validity before any workflow step runs.

**Public API:**

- `GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the fixed string `"redmine"`. Used by the connector registry to identify and look up this plugin by name in connection configuration.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Validates a fully configured `CloudConnection` end-to-end. First checks that `m_Endpoint` is non-empty, then calls `ResolveCredentials` internally, then issues a live `GET /users/current.json` request to the Redmine instance using libcurl directly (15-second timeout, redirect following enabled, CA bundle from `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()`). Sends `X-Redmine-API-Key` and `Accept: application/json` headers. Returns `false` and populates `errorMessage` on any of: missing endpoint, unresolvable credentials, curl transport failure, HTTP 401 (dedicated message), or any HTTP 4xx/5xx. The response body is appended to `errorMessage` when it is non-empty and under 500 bytes. Returns `true` only on HTTP 2xx.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Looks up `connection.m_KeyName` in the global `KeyManager` via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider()`. Fails with a descriptive message if the key name is empty, the provider is not found, or the provider's `m_ApiKey` field is empty. On success, writes `CloudAuthType::BearerToken` into `credentials.m_AuthType` and the raw API key string into `credentials.m_Token`. Note: despite using `BearerToken` as the auth-type enum value, the token is **not** sent as a Bearer token; the calling executor is expected to read `m_Token` and place it in an `X-Redmine-API-Key` header instead.

- `GetBaseUrl(connection) → std::string` *(static)* — Returns `connection.m_Endpoint` with any trailing `/` stripped. Safe to call on an empty endpoint; callers should guard for that case before constructing URLs.

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — base interface this class satisfies; defines `CloudConnection`, `CloudCredentials`, `CloudAuthType`, and the virtual API contract.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — accessed through the global `Core::g_Core` singleton to retrieve a `KeyProvider*` by name; the provider's `m_ApiKey` field carries the Redmine API access key.
- `Core` / `Engine` (`core.h`, `engine.h`) — provides access to `g_Core`, the process-wide singleton through which `KeyManager` is reached.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — consulted only for `GetCaBundlePath()` to obtain a platform-appropriate CA certificate bundle path for TLS verification in `TestConnection`.
- **libcurl** — used directly via raw `curl_easy_*` calls in `TestConnection`; the connector does not use `CurlWrapper` session management for this probe, managing its own `CURL*` handle and `curl_slist*` inline.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`RedmineConnector` holds no instance state; every method either reads only from its arguments or accesses global singletons through `Core::g_Core`. `TestConnection` performs a synchronous, blocking HTTP call on the calling thread and must not be invoked from a thread where blocking I/O is prohibited. Callers are responsible for ensuring `Core::g_Core` and `KeyManager` are fully initialised before calling any method. The class itself is safe to share or copy freely since it carries no mutable members.

**Notes:**

- The auth-type mismatch is intentional but fragile: `credentials.m_AuthType` is set to `CloudAuthType::BearerToken` purely as a carrier convention; the actual wire header is `X-Redmine-API-Key`, not `Authorization: Bearer`. Any executor consuming these credentials must check that the cloud connection type is `"redmine"` and build the correct header explicitly rather than using generic bearer-token logic.
- `TestConnection` allocates its own raw `CURL*` and `curl_slist*` handles and cleans them up inline. If `curl_easy_perform` throws or returns early due to a future refactor, the `curl_slist_free_all` / `curl_easy_cleanup` calls could be skipped; there is no RAII guard here.
- The `project_identifier` key in `connection.m_Params` is documented in the header as the default project for list operations, but `RedmineConnector` itself never reads it; it is consumed downstream by action nodes that hold a reference to the connection.
- HTTP response body is included in `errorMessage` only when its length is strictly less than 500 bytes, silently truncating diagnostic information for large error payloads (e.g., HTML error pages from a misconfigured proxy).
