- The CA bundle path is only applied when `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` returns a non-empty string; on platforms where it returns empty, TLS certificate verification falls back to libcurl's compiled-in default, which may silently pass or fail depending on the build environment.


---

### 37_doc_application_cloud_s3CloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="37-doc-application-cloud-s3cloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/37_doc_application_cloud_s3CloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## S3CloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `S3CloudTaskExecutor` is the j9t workflow-engine task executor responsible for all Amazon S3 (and S3-compatible) object-storage operations within a workflow run. It handles four operations — `upload`, `download`, `list`, and `delete` — driven entirely by the task's JSON params blob. The class sits in the `AIAssistant` namespace, is registered under JCWF task type `"s3"`, and extends `ICloudTaskExecutor` so the engine dispatches it automatically whenever a workflow task declares that type. All HTTP communication is performed via raw libcurl calls with AWS Signature Version 4 authentication applied by `SigV4Signer`.

---

**Public API:**

`S3CloudTaskExecutor` inherits its constructor from `ICloudTaskExecutor` via `using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor` and exposes no additional public methods. Its sole behavioural entry point is the protected override listed below, which the engine calls internally.

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — Parses `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` with simdjson, resolves the target bucket (task param, then `connection.m_Params["bucket"]`), resolves the AWS region (from `connection.m_Params["region"]`, defaulting to `"us-east-1"`), and dispatches to one of four operation branches. On success it sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded`, writes a compact JSON result string into `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` (capped at `kMaxCaptureChars` = 1 024 characters), and calls `WriteResponseJson` to persist the full response into the task's working directory. On any error it sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed`, populates `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` with a descriptive string, and returns `false`. The `cancellationToken` parameter is accepted but not actively polled inside this implementation.

  Operation specifics:

  - **upload** — Requires `key` and `file_path`. Reads the entire file into a `std::string` in memory, computes its SHA-256 hex digest via `SigV4Signer::Sha256Hex`, then issues a SigV4-signed HTTP `PUT` with `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`. The response body from S3 is discarded on success; a synthetic JSON `{"ok":true,...}` is stored in `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`.
  - **download** — Requires `key` and `file_path`. Creates the output directory via `std::filesystem::create_directories` if needed, then streams the object directly to a `FILE*` opened in binary-write mode via `S3Download`. Enforces a hard 256 MB cap (`kMaxDownloadBytes`) via `CURLOPT_MAXFILESIZE_LARGE`; downloads exceeding this limit fail at the curl layer.
  - **list** — Uses the S3 ListObjectsV2 query-string API (`?list-type=2`). Accepts optional `prefix` and `max_keys` params (default 1 000). The raw XML response body from S3 is passed through as `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`; it is not parsed or transformed.
  - **delete** — Requires `key`. Issues an HTTP `DELETE`; synthesises a `{"ok":true,...}` result on HTTP 2xx.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class; provides the `WriteResponseJson` helper used after every successful operation, as well as the `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials` structures.
- `SigV4Signer` (`cloud/sigV4Signer.h`) — signs every outbound request. `SigV4Signer::Sign` produces a `SignedRequest` whose `m_Headers` map is appended to the curl header list. `SigV4Signer::Sha256Hex` computes upload payload digests. `SigV4Signer::EmptyPayloadHash` is used for `GET`, `DELETE`, and list requests.
- `S3Connector` (`cloud/s3Connector.h`) — `S3Connector::BuildEndpointUrl(connection, bucket)` constructs the base URL; this is the only place the endpoint is derived, so custom S3-compatible endpoints (e.g. MinIO) are supported if the connection is configured appropriately.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — provides `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()`, which supplies a custom CA bundle path to every curl handle; if the returned string is empty no `CURLOPT_CAINFO` override is applied.
- `TaskPathResolver` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` and `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` locate the filesystem directory into which the response JSON file is written.
- `simdjson` — used for all JSON parsing of `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`. A local `ondemand::parser` and `padded_string` are constructed per call; the document is iterated in a forward-only pass.
- `engine.h` — pulls in `LOG_APP_INFO` used for the per-operation info log lines.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`S3CloudTaskExecutor` holds no mutable member state of its own; all working data (curl handles, file handles, string buffers) is stack-local or locally allocated within `ExecuteCloud`. The class itself is therefore safe to call from any thread, subject to whatever serialisation the engine applies at the `ICloudTaskExecutor` dispatch level. Each `ExecuteCloud` invocation allocates and destroys its own `CURL*` handle(s), so concurrent calls on different instances (or even the same instance) do not share curl state. The `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials` arguments are passed by const reference and must remain valid for the duration of the call; ownership stays with the caller (the engine). The `TaskInstanceState` reference is written exclusively by this method during its execution and must not be concurrently accessed by other threads for that task instance.

---

**Notes:**

- **Upload is fully in-memory.** The entire file is read into a `std::string` before the PUT is issued. There is no streaming upload path and no explicit size guard, so uploading very large files will exhaust process heap. The 256 MB download cap has no equivalent on the upload side.
- **`list` returns raw S3 XML**, not JSON. Downstream workflow steps that consume `m_CapturedStdout` from a list operation must parse XML, not JSON. All other operations return synthesised JSON.
- **`m_CapturedStdout` is truncated to 1 024 bytes.** The full response is still written to the working-directory file via `WriteResponseJson`; only the in-memory task-state field is capped.
- **`max_keys` is parsed with `std::stoi` without exception handling.** A non-numeric `max_keys` param will throw `std::invalid_argument` and unwind through `ExecuteCloud` uncaught at this layer.
- **Bucket resolution order:** task param `"bucket"` takes precedence over `connection.m_Params["bucket"]`. If neither is present the task fails immediately before any network I/O.
- **Region defaults silently to `"us-east-1"`** if `connection.m_Params["region"]` is absent. This will produce authentication failures against buckets in other regions without any explicit diagnostic indicating the region mismatch.
- **The `cancellationToken` is ignored inside the curl calls.** Long-running uploads or downloads (timeout ceiling: 300 seconds) will not be interrupted by workflow cancellation.
- **HTTP errors on upload** include up to 500 characters of the S3 XML error body in `m_LastErrorMessage`; other operations do not include the body.
- **`S3Download` opens the output file before issuing the HTTP request.** If the request ultimately returns HTTP 4xx/5xx, the output file is still created (and possibly partially written) on disk before `S3Download` returns `false`.


---

### 38_doc_application_cloud_s3Connector_h

<a id="38-doc-application-cloud-s3connector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/38_doc_application_cloud_s3Connector_h/docu.output.md`



## S3Connector

**Role:** `S3Connector` is a concrete `ICloudConnector` implementation that connects j9t's workflow engine to AWS S3 and any S3-compatible object store (MinIO, Wasabi, Cloudflare R2, etc.). It handles the three responsibilities that the cloud subsystem requires of every connector: identifying itself by type string, resolving AWS credentials from the `KeyManager`, and verifying that a given `CloudConnection` configuration actually reaches a live bucket. All HTTP signing is delegated to `SigV4Signer`; all HTTP I/O is done with raw libcurl (with a CA-bundle path sourced from `CurlWrapper`).

---

**Public API:**

- `GetType() → std::string` — Returns the fixed string `"s3"`. Used by the connector registry to match a `CloudConnection`'s type field to this implementation. No inputs; no side effects.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Looks up the credential named by `connection.m_KeyName` in `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()` and populates `credentials.m_AccessKeyId` and `credentials.m_SecretKey`. Accepts two storage conventions from the `KeyManager` provider: (1) `m_Username` / `m_Password` treated as access-key-ID / secret-key directly, or (2) `m_ApiKey` in `"ACCESS_KEY_ID:SECRET_KEY"` colon-delimited format. Always sets `credentials.m_AuthType` to `CloudAuthType::SigV4`. Returns `false` and writes a human-readable message into `errorMessage` if the key name is empty, the provider is not found, or the `api_key` format is malformed. Does not perform any network I/O.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Performs a live connectivity check by issuing a signed `GET /?list-type=2&max-keys=1` against the configured bucket. Requires `region` and `bucket` to be present and non-empty in `connection.m_Params`; fails fast otherwise. Calls `ResolveCredentials` internally, builds the endpoint URL via `BuildEndpointUrl`, signs the request with `SigV4Signer::Sign`, and executes the request with a raw `CURL*` handle (15-second timeout, redirect following enabled). Returns `false` for any `CURLcode` error or any HTTP status ≥ 400; on HTTP errors, appends the raw response body to `errorMessage` if it is under 500 bytes. Callers must treat a `true` return as "bucket reachable and credentials accepted."

- `BuildEndpointUrl(connection, bucket) → std::string` *(static)* — Constructs the base URL for a bucket. If `connection.m_Endpoint` is non-empty (custom endpoint), strips any trailing slash and appends `"/" + bucket`, producing path-style URLs for S3-compatible services. If empty, constructs the AWS virtual-hosted-style URL `https://{bucket}.s3.{region}.amazonaws.com`, defaulting `region` to `"us-east-1"` when the `"region"` param is absent. Does not validate that the resulting URL is reachable.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` / `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` / `CloudAuthType` — base interface and data types defined in `cloud/cloudConnector.h`. `S3Connector` fulfils the interface contract for the cloud connector registry.
- `KeyManager` (accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`) — sole source of credentials. The connector never handles raw secret material except transiently during `ResolveCredentials`.
- `SigV4Signer` (`cloud/sigV4Signer.h`) — produces the signed header map (`m_Headers`) and the empty-payload hash sentinel used for `GET` requests. `S3Connector` does not implement any signing itself.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — supplies the CA bundle path via `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()`, which is applied to the raw `CURL*` handle when non-empty.
- `Core` / `Engine` (`core.h`, `engine.h`) — `Core::g_Core` is the global singleton through which `KeyManager` is reached.
- libcurl — used directly (not through `CurlWrapper`'s higher-level API) for the `TestConnection` HTTP request.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`S3Connector` carries no mutable instance state; all data flows through parameters. It is therefore safe to call its methods concurrently from multiple threads provided the `Core::g_Core` singleton and `KeyManager` are themselves thread-safe for reads (which is a precondition imposed by the broader engine, not enforced here). The raw `CURL*` handle in `TestConnection` is created, used, and destroyed within a single call frame — it is never shared. No ownership transfer occurs: `credentials` is an output parameter populated by value, and `connection` is always taken by `const&`. `S3Connector` instances are expected to be owned and managed by the cloud connector registry; their lifetime must not exceed that of `Core::g_Core`.

---

**Notes:**

- `BuildEndpointUrl` always produces path-style URLs for custom endpoints (`endpoint/bucket`) but virtual-hosted-style for AWS (`bucket.s3.region.amazonaws.com`). If a custom endpoint already contains a bucket prefix this will double it — callers must pass a bare endpoint URL.
- `TestConnection` uses an inline lambda cast to a raw function pointer (`WriteFunc`) for the libcurl write callback. This is only valid because the lambda captures nothing; adding a capture would be a silent compile error on some toolchains.
- The `api_key` colon-split requires the colon to be neither the first nor the last character (`colonPos > 0 && colonPos < size - 1`); an `api_key` of exactly `":"` or one with a leading/trailing colon is rejected with an error message.
- HTTP errors in `TestConnection` include the raw XML S3 error body in `errorMessage` only when it is strictly under 500 bytes, preventing oversized messages from propagating to callers or logs.
- `connection.m_AuthType` is documented as required to be `SigV4`, but `ResolveCredentials` does not validate the incoming `m_AuthType` field — it unconditionally overwrites `credentials.m_AuthType`. Misconfigured connections with a different declared auth type will still be processed as SigV4.
- `region` defaults silently to `"us-east-1"` inside `BuildEndpointUrl` but is a hard required parameter in `TestConnection`. These two code paths are inconsistent: a caller that uses `BuildEndpointUrl` directly without going through `TestConnection` will not get a validation error for a missing region.


---

### 39_doc_application_cloud_sigV4Signer_h

<a id="39-doc-application-cloud-sigv4signer-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/39_doc_application_cloud_sigV4Signer_h/docu.output.md`



## SigV4Signer

**Role:** `SigV4Signer` is a stateless utility class inside the `AIAssistant` namespace that implements AWS Signature Version 4 request signing using OpenSSL's HMAC-SHA256 primitives. Its sole job is to produce the `Authorization`, `X-Amz-Date`, `X-Amz-Content-Sha256`, and `Host` headers required to authenticate HTTP requests against S3-compatible AWS APIs. It is used by the rest of the j9t engine wherever cloud storage interactions occur, and it has no mutable state — every method is `static`.

---

**Public API:**

- `Sign(method, url, region, service, accessKeyId, secretKey, payloadHash, extraHeaders) → SignedRequest` — The primary entry point. Executes all six SigV4 steps: parses the URL into host/path/query, captures the current UTC timestamp, assembles the canonical request (sorted lowercase headers, URI-encoded path, canonical query string), builds the credential scope and string-to-sign, derives the four-part HMAC signing key chain (`AWS4` + secretKey → date → region → service → `aws4_request`), and produces the final hex signature. Returns a `SignedRequest` whose `m_Headers` map is ready to be merged directly into an outgoing HTTP request. The `extraHeaders` parameter (e.g., `Content-Type`) is lowercased and included in both the canonical headers block and the `SignedHeaders` list. Calling code must pass `"UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD"` as `payloadHash` when the body is streamed or unknown at signing time; otherwise it must pass the pre-computed SHA-256 hex digest of the exact request body.

- `Sha256Hex(data) → std::string` — Computes the SHA-256 hex digest of an arbitrary byte string using OpenSSL's `EVP_DigestInit_ex` / `EVP_DigestUpdate` / `EVP_DigestFinal_ex` pipeline. Allocates and frees an `EVP_MD_CTX` on every call. Used internally by `Sign` to hash the canonical request, and available publicly for callers that need to pre-hash a request body before passing it to `Sign`.

- `EmptyPayloadHash() → std::string const&` — Returns a cached (function-local `static`) SHA-256 hex digest of the empty string. Use this constant when making S3 requests with no body (e.g., `GET`, `DELETE`, `HEAD`). Avoids recomputation on every call.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **OpenSSL (`<openssl/evp.h>`, `<openssl/hmac.h>`)** — All cryptographic operations go through OpenSSL. `Sha256Hex` uses the `EVP_MD_CTX` API directly. `HmacSha256` and `HmacSha256Hex` use the one-shot `HMAC()` function. No other crypto library is involved.
- **Callers in j9t's cloud layer** — Any component making authenticated S3-compatible calls (e.g., file upload/download, presigned-style workflows) is expected to call `Sign` and then attach `SignedRequest::m_Headers` to its HTTP request. The class itself does not perform any networking; it only produces headers.
- **`cloud/sigV4Signer.h`** — The accompanying header file that declares the class, `SignedRequest`, and the `UrlParts` private struct.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

Because every method is `static` and the class holds no instance state, there are no ownership or lifetime concerns for `SigV4Signer` objects — none are ever created. The only shared state is the function-local `static std::string` inside `EmptyPayloadHash()`, which is initialised once at first call and is safe to read concurrently after that (C++11 guarantees thread-safe static initialisation). `GetAmzDate()` and `GetDateStamp()` call `std::chrono::system_clock::now()` on every invocation; they are thread-safe but each invocation captures a fresh timestamp, so two calls within the same `Sign()` execution use the same `amzDate` variable — there is no race within a single `Sign` call.

---

**Notes:**

- `ParseUrl` strips the standard ports 443 and 80 from the `Host` value, matching the AWS requirement that the `Host` header must not include the default port. Non-standard ports (e.g., custom S3-compatible endpoints on port 9000) are preserved verbatim.
- The canonical URI is produced by calling `UriEncode(path, encodeSlash=false)`, meaning forward slashes in the S3 key path are not percent-encoded, which is the correct AWS S3 behaviour. If your key itself contains characters that must be double-encoded, verify the path arriving in `url` is already in its decoded form before calling `Sign`.
- `CanonicalQueryString` parses and re-encodes query parameters but does **not** pre-decode percent-encoded values already present in the query string — it encodes the raw token as received. Pass a decoded query string if your URL has pre-encoded parameters, or pass them already in the correct form.
- The signing key derivation happens from scratch on every `Sign` call. There is no caching of derived keys. High-frequency callers making many requests per second (e.g., multipart upload loops) may want to cache the `kSigning` key across calls that share the same `secretKey`, `region`, `service`, and `dateStamp`.
- `HmacSha256` returns raw bytes as a `std::string`; `HmacSha256Hex` returns the hex encoding of those bytes. The raw-byte overload is used as intermediate input in the key derivation chain; callers outside this class should use `HmacSha256Hex` if they need a printable digest. Both are `private`.
- Timestamp capture uses `std::chrono::system_clock` converted to `std::time_t` and then to UTC via `gmtime_r` (POSIX) or `gmtime_s` (Windows), selected at compile time via `#ifdef _WIN32`. There is no monotonic clock involved; wall-clock skew between the signing host and AWS servers must be within ±5 minutes or AWS will reject the request.


---

### 40_doc_application_cloud_slackCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="40-doc-application-cloud-slackcloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/40_doc_application_cloud_slackCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## SlackCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** `SlackCloudTaskExecutor` is the j9t workflow engine's integration point for the Slack Web API. It extends `ICloudTaskExecutor` to handle two JCWF task types: `slack_message`, which POSTs a message to a Slack channel via `chat.postMessage`, and `slack_read`, which retrieves channel history via `conversations.history`, filters it, and writes structured output files to the task's working directory. All HTTP traffic is performed synchronously through libcurl with a 30-second timeout, using Bearer token authentication drawn from a `CloudCredentials` object supplied by the base-class framework.

**Public API:**

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — The single protected override called by the base class dispatcher. Inspects `taskDefinition.m_Type` and delegates to either `ExecuteSlackPost` or `ExecuteSlackRead`. `workflowRun` and `cancellationToken` are accepted but currently unused (both cast to `void`). Returns `true` on success and `false` on any failure, with `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` and `taskState.m_State` set accordingly. Callers must not rely on partial side-effects when `false` is returned.

**Private methods (called only from `ExecuteCloud`):**

- `ExecuteSlackPost(workflowDefinition, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials) → bool` — Parses `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` to extract `channel`, `text`/`text_file`, and optionally `thread_ts`/`thread_ts_file`. If `text_file` is set it reads the file relative to the j9t process working directory and trims trailing whitespace. Constructs a JSON body, calls `PerformSlackRequest` against `<base_url>/chat.postMessage`, validates the Slack `ok` envelope via `CheckSlackOk`, stores the raw response (up to 1 024 characters) in `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`, sets `taskState.m_State = Succeeded`, and writes a `response.json` to the task working directory via `WriteResponseJson`.

- `ExecuteSlackRead(workflowDefinition, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials) → bool` — Parses params for `channel` (must be a channel ID starting with `C`, not a `#name`; the method explicitly rejects `#`-prefixed values), `limit` (default 10), `oldest` (optional timestamp filter), and `exclude_bots` (default `true`). Issues a GET to `<base_url>/conversations.history`, re-parses the response with a second `simdjson` parser instance to walk the `messages` array, drops bot messages when `exclude_bots` is true, and drops any message where `ts` or `text` is empty. Writes four output files to the task working directory: `messages_summary.json` (array of `{ts, user, text}`), `latest_message.txt`, `latest_ts.txt`, and `response.json` (`{ok, count, channel, latest_ts}`). Also stores up to 1 024 characters of the summary JSON in `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`.

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) — base class; provides the `ExecuteCloud` virtual dispatch contract and `WriteResponseJson` helper used by both task handlers.
- `SlackConnector::GetApiBaseUrl(connection)` (`cloud/slackConnector.h`) — supplies the resolved Slack API base URL for the named connection; all endpoint paths are appended to whatever this returns.
- `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` — carry the named connection configuration and the Bearer token (`credentials.m_Token`) used in the `Authorization` header.
- `WorkflowDefinition` / `TaskDef` / `TaskInstanceState` — workflow-engine value types; `TaskDef.m_ParamsJson` is the raw parameter JSON, `TaskDef.m_WorkingDirectory` is the relative output path, and `TaskInstanceState` receives state, error messages, and captured stdout.
- `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` / `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — used to construct the absolute filesystem path for output files.
- `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — provides an optional custom CA bundle path set on the curl handle; empty means system default.
- `simdjson` (ondemand API) — used for all JSON parsing of both task params and Slack API responses. Because simdjson ondemand is a streaming parser, two separate `parser`/`document` instances are required whenever the same JSON buffer must be read in non-sequential field order (e.g., reading `ok` then `error` in `CheckSlackOk`).
- `libcurl` — all HTTP is performed directly via `curl_easy_*` calls inside the file-local `PerformSlackRequest` function; there is no connection pooling or reuse across invocations.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`SlackCloudTaskExecutor` instances are constructed and consumed by the workflow engine per the `ICloudTaskExecutor` contract. The class holds no mutable member state of its own; all state is passed in through function arguments or written to `taskState`. There is no internal synchronisation. `ExecuteCloud` performs synchronous blocking I/O (both HTTP via curl and file I/O via `std::fstream`) and must not be called from a thread where blocking is prohibited. The `cancellationToken` parameter is accepted by signature but is not checked during execution, so long-running requests (up to the 30-second curl timeout) cannot be interrupted once started.

**Notes:**

- `slack_read` silently drops messages that have an empty `ts` or `text` after filtering. Slack system messages (join/leave events) and messages whose body is only attachments with no `text` field fall into this category and will not appear in output files.
- The Slack API returns `conversations.history` results newest-first; `messages[0]` after filtering is therefore the most recent message, and that is what `latest_message.txt` and `latest_ts.txt` capture.
- `text_file` and `thread_ts_file` paths are resolved relative to the j9t process current working directory at execution time, not relative to the workflow definition directory.
- `CheckSlackOk` requires two separate `simdjson::ondemand::parser` instances because the ondemand API does not allow re-reading fields out of document order; attempting to read `error` after `ok` on the same document would return an error.
- HTTP responses with status codes below 400 are not considered transport failures by `PerformSlackRequest`; the Slack-level `ok: false` check in `CheckSlackOk` is the second gate. A 200 response with `ok: false` (e.g., `invalid_auth`, `channel_not_found`) is caught there, not at the HTTP level.
- `kMaxCaptureChars` (1 024) caps what is stored in `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`; the full response or summary is still written to disk regardless of that limit.
- If `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` returns an empty path, output files are silently skipped with no error — the task still reports `Succeeded`.


---

### 41_doc_application_cloud_slackConnector_h

<a id="41-doc-application-cloud-slackconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/41_doc_application_cloud_slackConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## SlackConnector

**Role:** `SlackConnector` is a concrete implementation of `ICloudConnector` that integrates j9t's cloud connection subsystem with the Slack Web API. It handles two responsibilities: resolving a Bot token from the engine's `KeyManager` into a `CloudCredentials` struct, and verifying that token is live by calling Slack's `auth.test` endpoint over libcurl. It is registered under the connector type identifier `"slack"` and is the only point in the codebase that constructs Slack-specific HTTP requests.

**Public API:**

- `GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the string `"slack"`, which the connector registry uses to match a `CloudConnection` to this implementation. No side effects.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Calls `ResolveCredentials` internally, then issues an HTTP POST to `<base>/auth.test` with a 15-second timeout. On success (HTTP < 400 and `{"ok": true}` in the body) returns `true`. On any failure — libcurl transport error, HTTP 4xx/5xx, JSON parse failure, or Slack-level `ok: false` — writes a human-readable diagnosis into `errorMessage` and returns `false`. Allocates and frees a `CURL*` handle inline; the handle does not outlive the call. The Slack-level `error` field from the response body is captured and surfaced in `errorMessage` when present.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Looks up `connection.m_KeyName` in `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`. If found, copies `provider->m_ApiKey` into `credentials.m_Token` and sets `credentials.m_AuthType` to `CloudAuthType::BearerToken`. Fails (returns `false`, populates `errorMessage`) if `m_KeyName` is empty, the provider is not registered, or the provider's `m_ApiKey` is empty. No network I/O.

- `GetApiBaseUrl(connection) → std::string` *(static)* — Returns `connection.m_Endpoint` stripped of a trailing slash, or `DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL` (`"https://slack.com/api"`) when `m_Endpoint` is empty. Used internally and can be called by callers that need to construct additional Slack API URLs against the same configured base.

- `DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL` *(static constexpr)* — The string `"https://slack.com/api"`. Exposed so other code can reference the canonical default without hard-coding it.

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — Abstract base class supplying the `GetType`, `TestConnection`, and `ResolveCredentials` interface that the connector registry calls polymorphically.
- `CloudConnection` / `CloudCredentials` / `CloudAuthType` — Data types declared in the connector header. `CloudConnection.m_Endpoint`, `m_KeyName`, `m_Name`, and `m_AuthType` are read; `CloudCredentials.m_Token` and `m_AuthType` are written by `ResolveCredentials`.
- `Core::g_Core` / `KeyManager` (`core.h`, `keys/keyManager.h`) — `ResolveCredentials` dereferences the global `Core` singleton to reach `KeyManager::GetProvider(name)`, which returns a raw pointer to a credential record whose `m_ApiKey` field holds the Bot token (expected format: `xoxb-...`).
- `libcurl` — Used directly (not through `CurlWrapper`) for the `auth.test` POST. `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` is called to set `CURLOPT_CAINFO` when a custom CA bundle is configured.
- `simdjson` (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) — Used in `TestConnection` to parse the JSON response from `auth.test` and inspect the `ok` and `error` fields via the on-demand API.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — Consulted only for `GetCaBundlePath()`; the actual curl handle is managed manually inside `TestConnection`.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`SlackConnector` carries no mutable instance state; all data lives in the call stack or in parameters. It is safe to call from multiple threads simultaneously provided the underlying `KeyManager` provider pointer remains valid for the duration of `ResolveCredentials`. The `KeyManager` lookup returns a raw non-owning pointer — callers must not hold it beyond the scope in which it is used. `Core::g_Core` is a global singleton and must be fully initialised before any method on this class is called. The `CURL*` handle created inside `TestConnection` is local to that invocation and is always cleaned up before return, including on error paths.

**Notes:**

- Slack returns HTTP 200 even for authentication failures; the `ok` boolean in the JSON body is the authoritative success signal. `TestConnection` checks both the HTTP status code and `ok`, so a 200 with `ok: false` is correctly treated as failure.
- The `CURLOPT_POST` + `CURLOPT_POSTFIELDS ""` pattern sends an empty body. `auth.test` accepts this; the token is conveyed entirely via the `Authorization: Bearer` header.
- `connection.m_AuthType` is documented in comments as required to be `BearerToken`, but `ResolveCredentials` does not validate it — the connector unconditionally sets `credentials.m_AuthType = BearerToken` regardless of what the caller supplies in `connection.m_AuthType`.
- The `simdjson` on-demand parser requires the input string to be padded; a `simdjson::padded_string` is constructed from `responseBody` on the stack, so the padded copy's lifetime correctly covers the document traversal.
- There is no retry logic, backoff, or rate-limit handling in `TestConnection`; transient network failures will surface immediately as errors.


---

### 42_doc_application_cloud_snowflakeCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="42-doc-application-cloud-snowflakecloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/42_doc_application_cloud_snowflakeCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## SnowflakeCloudTaskExecutor

**Role:** Implements the `"snowflake_query"` workflow task type by driving Snowflake's SQL REST API (v2) end-to-end: it submits a SQL statement, polls for completion when the result is not immediately available, cancels on workflow cancellation, and writes the full result set to disk as either RFC 4180 CSV or a JSON array-of-objects. It inherits the cloud-task lifecycle from `ICloudTaskExecutor`, which handles connection lookup, credential resolution, and top-level error routing before handing control to `ExecuteCloud`.

---

**Public API:**

- `SnowflakeCloudTaskExecutor(/* ICloudTaskExecutor ctor args */)` — Inherited constructor via `using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor`. No additional construction logic in this class.

- `ExecuteCloud(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState, connection, credentials, cancellationToken) → bool` — Protected override called by the base class. Parses `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` with simdjson, builds and POSTs a statement-submission body to `/api/v2/statements`, optionally polls `/api/v2/statements/{handle}` at `poll_interval`-second intervals until the `"Statement executed successfully."` message appears or a timeout/cancellation fires, then deserialises the `resultSetMetaData` + `data` arrays and writes output to `workDir / outputFile`. Returns `true` and sets `taskState.m_State = Succeeded` on success; sets `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` and `taskState.m_State = Failed` and returns `false` on any error. Side effects: creates the task working directory, writes the output file (CSV or JSON), writes `response.json` via `WriteResponseJson`, and populates `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` with a compact JSON summary (capped at 1 024 chars).

---

**File-local helpers (not part of the public API but important to understand):**

- `SnowflakeRequest(method, url, jwt, responseBody, httpCode, requestBody)` — Thin libcurl wrapper that fires a single synchronous HTTP request with `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>` and `X-Snowflake-Authorization-Token-Type: KEYPAIR_JWT`. Uses `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` for CA bundle override. Hard timeout of 60 seconds per individual HTTP call (`kCurlTimeoutSeconds`). Creates and tears down a fresh `CURL*` handle on every call — no connection reuse.

- `CsvEscape(value)` — RFC 4180 quoting: wraps a field in double-quotes and doubles internal double-quotes only when the field contains `,`, `"`, `\n`, or `\r`. Empty strings for SQL NULLs pass through as empty CSV fields.

---

**Collaborators:**

| Collaborator | Role |
|---|---|
| `ICloudTaskExecutor` (`cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h`) | Base class; resolves `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials` before calling `ExecuteCloud`. |
| `SnowflakeConnector::BuildApiBaseUrl` (`cloud/snowflakeConnector.h`) | Converts the raw connection endpoint string into the HTTPS base URL used for all REST calls. |
| `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` / `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) | Resolves the filesystem directory where output files are written. |
| `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) | Supplies the optional CA bundle path injected into every libcurl handle. |
| `simdjson::ondemand` | All JSON parsing — submit response, poll responses, and result set extraction. A new `parser` + `document` pair is allocated per parse site; they are not reused across calls. |
| `engine.h` | Provides `LOG_APP_INFO` and presumably `WriteResponseJson`. |
| `CloudConnection::m_Params` | Fallback source for `warehouse`, `database`, `schema` when task params omit them. |
| `TaskCancellationToken` | Checked before each poll sleep; triggers a `POST /api/v2/statements/{handle}/cancel` when set. |

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ExecuteCloud` is synchronous and blocking. It sleeps the calling thread for `poll_interval` seconds between polls (default 2 s, minimum clamped to 1 s). Callers must run this on a thread that can block for up to `timeout` seconds (default 3 600 s). There is no internal locking; the base class is responsible for any concurrency controls around `taskState` and `workflowRun`. The `CURL*` handle is stack-local and cleaned up before the function returns, so there are no curl lifetime issues visible to callers. All `simdjson` parsers are also stack-local per invocation and not shared across threads.

---

**Notes:**

- **Poll timeout is wall-clock via `steady_clock`**, not a Snowflake server-side timeout. The server-side `timeout` param in the POST body instructs Snowflake to abort after that many seconds independently; if Snowflake aborts server-side the poll will detect a non-success `code` and fail the task.

- **Synchronous short-circuit:** if the submit response already contains `"message": "Statement executed successfully."`, the polling loop is skipped entirely and `responseBody` still holds the submit response when the result set is parsed. If polling does occur, `responseBody` is overwritten on each poll and the final successful poll response is used for result parsing. Both paths feed into the same result-extraction block.

- **Snowflake status codes recognised during polling:** `"090001"` and `"333334"` are treated as "still running" and allow the loop to continue. Any other non-empty `code` field is treated as a query failure.

- **NULL handling:** SQL NULL values in the `data` array are not returned as JSON `null` by the API at the string level — they arrive as absent or empty string values. The code pushes an empty `std::string` for any cell that fails `get_string()`. In CSV output this produces an empty field; in JSON output an empty string produces `null` (the `rows[r][c].empty()` branch emits the literal `null` token).

- **JSON output is not fully escaped for Unicode.** The manual character-switch escaper handles `"`, `\`, `\n`, `\r`, `\t` but does not encode non-ASCII bytes or control characters outside those five. This is safe for well-formed UTF-8 content that Snowflake returns but would produce non-compliant JSON for raw binary or unusual control characters.

- **`kMaxCaptureChars = 1024`** caps what goes into `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`; the full result is always written to disk regardless.

- **`output_format`** is matched with a plain string equality check (`== "json"`). Any value other than `"json"` (including misspellings) silently falls through to CSV.

- **The 60-second per-request curl timeout** is independent of `poll_interval` and `timeout`. A Snowflake endpoint that hangs the TCP connection will abort after 60 s; this does not count against the overall statement timeout tracked by `steady_clock`.


---

### 43_doc_application_cloud_snowflakeConnector_h

<a id="43-doc-application-cloud-snowflakeconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/43_doc_application_cloud_snowflakeConnector_h/docu.output.md`



## SnowflakeConnector

**Role:** `SnowflakeConnector` is a concrete `ICloudConnector` implementation that enables j9t to authenticate against and validate connectivity to Snowflake data warehouses via the Snowflake SQL REST API (v2). It is entirely stateless: it holds no mutable member data, derives everything it needs from the `CloudConnection` descriptor passed into each call, and produces short-lived RSA JWT bearer tokens on demand. Its two responsibilities are credential resolution (mint a signed JWT from a stored RSA private key) and connection testing (fire a `SELECT 1` against `/api/v2/statements` and validate the HTTP response).

---

**Public API:**

- `GetType() const → std::string` — Returns the fixed discriminator string `"snowflake"`. Used by the connector registry to map a `CloudConnection::m_Type` value to this implementation. No inputs; no side effects.

- `ResolveCredentials(connection, credentials, errorMessage) → bool` — Validates that `connection.m_KeyName` is non-empty, looks up the corresponding `KeyPairCredential` in `KeyManager`, confirms the credential carries a non-empty RSA private key PEM, then calls `JwtGenerator::GenerateSnowflakeJwt` with the `account` and `user` params to produce a 1-hour JWT. On success, populates `credentials.m_AuthType = JwtRsa` and `credentials.m_Token` with the signed JWT string and returns `true`. On any failure, populates `errorMessage` with a human-readable reason and returns `false`. Does not perform any network I/O.

- `TestConnection(connection, errorMessage) → bool` — Validates that `connection.m_Endpoint` is non-empty, calls `ResolveCredentials` internally to obtain a fresh JWT, constructs a JSON request body for `SELECT 1` (optionally embedding `warehouse`, `database`, and `schema` context fields when those params are present), and POSTs to `{BuildApiBaseUrl(endpoint)}/api/v2/statements` using a raw `CURL*` handle. Sets a 30-second curl timeout. Sends four mandatory headers: `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>`, `Content-Type: application/json`, `Accept: application/json`, and `X-Snowflake-Authorization-Token-Type: KEYPAIR_JWT`. Inspects the HTTP response code: returns `true` for any HTTP < 400, and returns `false` with a specific message distinguishing 401/403 auth errors from generic 4xx/5xx errors. The response body is captured and appended to `errorMessage` when it is non-empty and under 500 characters.

- `BuildApiBaseUrl(endpoint) → std::string` (static) — Normalises an endpoint string into a fully qualified Snowflake REST base URL. Strips trailing slashes, passes through strings that already begin with `https://` or `http://` unchanged, and otherwise returns `"https://" + endpoint + ".snowflakecomputing.com"`. Pure function; no side effects.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ICloudConnector` (`cloud/cloudConnector.h`) — Base interface; `SnowflakeConnector` overrides `GetType`, `TestConnection`, and `ResolveCredentials`. `CloudConnection` and `CloudCredentials` structs are defined there and carry all configuration.
- `KeyManager` (`keys/keyManager.h`) — Accessed via `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider(name)`. Must contain a `KeyPairCredential` whose `m_PrivateKeyPem` field holds the RSA private key in PEM format. `ResolveCredentials` fails fast if this lookup returns null or the PEM is empty.
- `JwtGenerator` (`keys/jwtGenerator.h`) — `JwtGenerator::GenerateSnowflakeJwt(account, user, privateKeyPem, errorMessage)` performs the actual RSA-SHA256 signing and Snowflake-specific JWT claim construction. Any signing failure propagates its error string through `errorMessage`.
- `Core` (`core.h`) — Global singleton `Core::g_Core` is the access path to `KeyManager`. The connector has a hard dependency on this being initialised before either public method is called.
- `CurlWrapper` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` is consulted to set `CURLOPT_CAINFO`; if it returns an empty string, the system default CA bundle is used. The connector allocates its own `CURL*` handle directly rather than using any pooling abstraction from `CurlWrapper`.
- `simdjson` — Header is included but not actively used in the current implementation; response body parsing is not performed (only HTTP status codes are examined).

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`SnowflakeConnector` is stateless and holds no instance data beyond the vtable, so a single instance can safely be called from multiple threads simultaneously provided the underlying dependencies are thread-safe. The critical constraint is `Core::g_Core`: it must be fully initialised before any call to `ResolveCredentials` or `TestConnection`; there is no null-check on the pointer. `KeyManager::GetProvider` returns a raw `const*` pointer into internal storage; the caller must not store or use that pointer beyond the scope of `ResolveCredentials` without external locking. Each `TestConnection` call creates and destroys its own `CURL*` handle on the calling thread, so there is no shared curl state between calls.

---

**Notes:**

- The JSON request body in `TestConnection` is assembled by raw string concatenation without any escaping. If `warehouse`, `database`, or `schema` param values contain double-quote characters or backslashes, the resulting JSON will be malformed. This is currently safe only for well-controlled config values.
- `simdjson` is included but the response body from Snowflake is never parsed. A successful `SELECT 1` is confirmed purely by HTTP status code < 400; the actual result set is discarded.
- `BuildApiBaseUrl` is idempotent: passing an already-complete URL (e.g. from an older config that stored the full hostname) will not double-wrap it with the `.snowflakecomputing.com` suffix.
- The `X-Snowflake-Authorization-Token-Type: KEYPAIR_JWT` header is mandatory for Snowflake to accept JWT bearer tokens; omitting it causes a 400 or 401 even with a valid token.
- The JWT generated by `JwtGenerator::GenerateSnowflakeJwt` has a 1-hour expiry baked in (per the comment in `ResolveCredentials`). Callers should not cache the returned `CloudCredentials` across that boundary without re-calling `ResolveCredentials`.
- `connection.m_AuthType` is documented as needing to be `JwtRsa`, but `ResolveCredentials` does not validate this field; it only enforces it by setting `credentials.m_AuthType = JwtRsa` on output. A misconfigured `m_AuthType` on the input connection is silently ignored.


---

### 44_doc_application_cloud_taskCancellationToken_h

<a id="44-doc-application-cloud-taskcancellationtoken-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/44_doc_application_cloud_taskCancellationToken_h/docu.output.md`



## TaskCancellationToken

**Role:** `TaskCancellationToken` is a lightweight cooperative cancellation primitive used to signal in-flight cloud operations that they should abort. It lives in the `AIAssistant` namespace and is designed for use with long-running tasks such as AI provider calls and workflow-run cloud steps. The token starts in a non-cancelled state and transitions to cancelled exactly once when `Cancel()` is called. As of the current code, cancellation propagation is a Phase 9 deliverable: the token exists and is functional in isolation but is not yet wired into the `POST /api/workflow-runs/{runId}/cancel` HTTP endpoint or the existing run-cancel mechanism.

**Public API:**

- `TaskCancellationToken()` — Default-constructs the token in the non-cancelled state. No parameters, no side effects beyond zeroing the internal atomic flag.
- `IsCancelled() const → bool` — Returns `true` if `Cancel()` has been called on this token, `false` otherwise. Uses `memory_order_relaxed`, so it is a low-overhead polling check suitable for tight loops inside cloud task handlers. Callers must not rely on this for synchronisation with other memory operations; it signals state only.
- `Cancel() → void` — Atomically sets the cancelled flag to `true`. Uses `memory_order_relaxed`. Once set, the flag is never cleared; cancellation is irreversible for a given token instance. Has no direct effect on any running thread — it is the responsibility of the long-running operation to poll `IsCancelled()` and react accordingly.

**Collaborators:** No direct dependencies on other classes are present in this header. The intended integration point is the workflow-run cancel mechanism (Phase 9), where the HTTP handler for `POST /api/workflow-runs/{runId}/cancel` is expected to acquire a reference to the token associated with the target run and call `Cancel()` on it. Cloud task executor code is expected to hold a reference to the same token and poll `IsCancelled()` at yield points.

**Threading / lifetime:** The atomic flag makes concurrent calls to `IsCancelled()` and `Cancel()` from different threads safe without external locking. However, `memory_order_relaxed` means there are no happens-before guarantees with respect to other memory operations; callers that need to coordinate additional state changes around cancellation must supply their own fences or use a stronger memory order. Ownership of the token is not dictated by this header; in practice the token should be shared between the task executor and the cancellation initiator, making `std::shared_ptr<TaskCancellationToken>` the natural vehicle. The token has no destructor side effects and is trivially destructible.

**Notes:** The use of `memory_order_relaxed` for both the load and the store is deliberate for performance in polling loops, but it means a thread that calls `Cancel()` cannot assume any previously written memory is visible to the thread reading `IsCancelled()` at that moment. Do not use this token as a general synchronisation barrier. The transition from `false` to `true` is permanent within a token's lifetime; if a reset is ever needed for retries, a new token instance must be created and distributed. Until Phase 9 integration is complete, calling `Cancel()` via the HTTP endpoint has no effect on running tasks even though the token class itself works correctly.


---

### 45_doc_application_content_chunkPlanner_h

<a id="45-doc-application-content-chunkplanner-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/45_doc_application_content_chunkPlanner_h/docu.output.md`



## ChunkPlanner

**Role:** `ChunkPlanner` (in `AIAssistant::`) is a stateless, structure-aware text partitioning utility used during Phase 6 of the workflow pipeline to split a document body into chunks that each fit within an AI model's usable context window. Given a raw body string, a maximum context size in tokens, and the token cost already consumed by the surrounding prompt, it produces an ordered list of non-overlapping substrings that collectively cover the full input. The planner cascades through progressively finer splitting strategies — markdown section boundaries (via `MarkdownSectionSplitter`), then blank-line-delimited paragraphs, then sentence-ending punctuation — only subdividing as deeply as necessary. Token counts throughout are approximated with the `chars ÷ 4` (ceiling) heuristic.

**Public API:**

- `EstimateTokens(text) → uint64_t` — Returns `(text.size() + 3) / 4`, a ceiling integer-division estimate of the number of tokens in `text`. Pure function; no allocation side effects beyond the call itself. Used both externally and internally by `AppendPacked` during recursive packing.

- `Plan(body, maxContextTokens, promptOverheadTokens) → std::vector<std::string>` — Produces the ordered chunk list. `body` is the full document text. `maxContextTokens` is the hard token budget of the target model context window. `promptOverheadTokens` is the estimated cost of the fixed prompt wrapper that will surround each chunk. The effective per-chunk budget is `maxContextTokens - promptOverheadTokens`, clamped to a minimum of 128 tokens; if `maxContextTokens` is zero the body is returned as a single chunk untouched. If the entire body already fits in the budget, a single-element vector containing the unchanged body is returned immediately with no splitting work. Otherwise, `MarkdownSectionSplitter::Split` is called at heading depth 3, and the resulting section bodies are packed into chunks via `AppendPacked`. Returns an empty vector only when `body` is empty.

**Collaborators:**

- `MarkdownSectionSplitter` (`content/markdownSectionSplitter.h`) — Called inside `Plan` to extract `MarkdownSection` objects (each exposing an `m_Body` field) by splitting on `#`, `##`, and `###` headings at depth 3. This is the primary structural decomposition step and the only external collaborator.
- `SplitByParagraphs` (anonymous namespace) — Falls back to blank-line splitting when a markdown section exceeds the budget. Uses `std::istringstream` line-by-line; accumulates lines into paragraphs delimited by empty lines.
- `SplitBySentences` (anonymous namespace) — Final fallback; splits character-by-character on `.`, `!`, `?`, and `\n`. If a single sentence still exceeds the budget it is emitted as an oversized chunk without further splitting.
- `AppendPacked` (anonymous namespace) — Greedy bin-packing loop that accumulates pieces until adding the next would exceed the budget, then flushes. Recurses into `SplitByParagraphs` or `SplitBySentences` for pieces that individually exceed the budget.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ChunkPlanner` has no instance state; both public methods are `static`. There are no shared mutable data structures, no caches, and no locks. Callers may invoke `Plan` and `EstimateTokens` concurrently from any number of threads without synchronisation. All returned `std::string` chunks are new, independently owned values.

**Notes:**

- The `chars ÷ 4` heuristic is calibrated for English text. Code-heavy or non-Latin content will produce systematic underestimates, potentially yielding chunks that exceed the real token budget when submitted to a model.
- When `promptOverheadTokens >= maxContextTokens` the fallback budget is `maxContextTokens / 4`, not zero; the floor is then further clamped to 128. Callers passing a zero `maxContextTokens` receive the whole body in one chunk, bypassing all splitting logic — useful for models with unlimited context or for passthrough paths.
- Oversized atomic pieces (a single sentence that exceeds the budget) are emitted whole rather than hard-truncated. Downstream callers must tolerate chunks larger than the requested budget in this degenerate case.
- `SplitByParagraphs` emits a trailing accumulator even when it does not end with a blank line, preserving content at the end of sections that lack a terminal newline.
- The fallback chain is strictly one-level deep per recursive call: sections → paragraphs → sentences. There is no byte-level or token-level hard split beyond sentence granularity.
- If `MarkdownSectionSplitter::Split` returns an empty list, `chunks` will be empty after `AppendPacked`, and `Plan` recovers by returning `{body}` as a safety net.


---

### 46_doc_application_content_markdownSectionSplitter_h

<a id="46-doc-application-content-markdownsectionsplitter-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/46_doc_application_content_markdownSectionSplitter_h/docu.output.md`



## MarkdownSectionSplitter

**Role:** `MarkdownSectionSplitter` is a stateless utility within the `AIAssistant` namespace that breaks a raw Markdown string into semantically meaningful sections by detecting ATX-style headings (`#` through `######`). Its primary purpose inside j9t is to chunk long AI-related documents — prompts, knowledge-base entries, response bodies — into independently addressable pieces before further processing such as embedding, storage, or injection into context windows. It applies a "shallowest useful split" heuristic: it tries heading levels 1 through `maxHeadingLevel` in order and returns the first level that produces more than one section, avoiding over-segmentation on deeply nested documents.

---

**Public API:**

- `MarkdownSectionSplitter::Split(markdown, maxHeadingLevel = 6) → std::vector<MarkdownSection>` — Accepts a full Markdown document as a `const std::string&` and an optional integer ceiling on which heading levels are considered split boundaries. Iterates heading levels 1 to `clampedMax` (clamped to `[1, 6]` regardless of caller input), calling the internal `SplitAtLevel` for each; returns the first result that contains more than one section. If no level produces a split, returns a single `MarkdownSection` with `m_HeadingLevel = 0` and `m_Body` equal to the entire input string verbatim. Returns an empty vector only when `markdown` is itself empty. Has no side effects; does not mutate any state.

---

**Collaborators:** This file has no external collaborators beyond the C++ standard library (`<string>`, `<vector>`, `<sstream>`). It is consumed by whatever component in j9t needs to segment Markdown content — expected callers are document ingestion pipelines, AI context builders, or knowledge-base indexers. The `MarkdownSection` struct is the sole data contract between this splitter and its callers.

---

**Threading / lifetime:** `MarkdownSectionSplitter` has no instance state; `Split` is a `static` member function and is safe to call concurrently from multiple threads without any locking. All returned `MarkdownSection` objects are value types owning their own `std::string` members — callers own the returned vector outright and there are no shared references back into the input string or any internal buffer.

---

**Notes:**

- **Fence detection is line-prefix only.** `SplitAtLevel` toggles `insideFence` whenever a line *starts* with ` ``` ` or `~~~` (checked via `rfind(..., 0)`). This means a fence opened with ` ```cpp ` is recognised correctly, but an indented fence opener (e.g., four-space-indented code block containing a fence marker) would not be detected and could leak a false-positive fence toggle. Info strings after the fence marker do not interfere with toggle logic because the check is purely prefix-based.
- **Heading detection is strict.** `DetectHeadingLevel` requires the `#` run to be followed immediately by a space or tab; `#NoSpace` is not treated as a heading, matching the CommonMark spec. Trailing spaces, tabs, and carriage returns are stripped from `m_HeadingText`, making the text safe for comparison or display on both Unix and Windows line endings.
- **Heading line is included in `m_Body`.** For sections with `m_HeadingLevel > 0`, `m_Body` begins with the raw heading line (e.g., `## Introduction\n`). Callers that want only the body content below the heading must strip the first line themselves.
- **Preamble sections.** Content that appears before the first qualifying heading is captured in a `MarkdownSection` with `m_HeadingLevel = 0` and `m_HeadingText` empty. It is emitted as the first element of the returned vector when non-empty.
- **`maxHeadingLevel` clamping.** Values less than 1 are silently raised to 1; values greater than 6 are silently lowered to 6. Callers should not rely on out-of-range values to signal special behaviour.
- **Fallback section carries no heading metadata.** When the entire document is returned as a single section (no split found), `m_HeadingLevel` is 0 and `m_HeadingText` is empty even if the document contains headings deeper than `maxHeadingLevel`. Callers must not infer that a level-0 section means the document is heading-free.


---

### 47_doc_application_file_fileWatcher_h

<a id="47-doc-application-file-filewatcher-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/47_doc_application_file_fileWatcher_h/docu.output.md`



## FileWatcher

**Role:** `FileWatcher` is a polling-based filesystem monitor that runs on a background thread and pushes `FileAddedEvent`, `FileModifiedEvent`, and `FileRemovedEvent` events into the engine's central event queue whenever files appear, change, or disappear under one or more watched directory roots. It supports a fixed *primary root* supplied at construction time and an arbitrarily extensible set of *additional roots* registered at runtime, making it usable both as a dedicated queue-folder monitor (with a fixed root that triggers engine shutdown when it vanishes) and as a multi-path trigger watcher serving `TriggerEngine` and the adhoc workflow manager.

**Public API:**

- `FileWatcher(const fs::path& pathToWatch, std::chrono::milliseconds interval = 100ms) → (ctor)` — Stores the primary root and polling interval. Passing an empty path creates a rootless watcher where all paths must be supplied via `AddPath`; an empty primary root also disables the "primary gone → `EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown`" behaviour. Does not start the background thread.

- `Start() → void` — Submits the `Watch()` loop to `Core::g_Core->GetThreadPool()` via `std::future`. Idempotent if already running (checked via `m_Running`). An initial scan of the primary root fires `FileAddedEvent` for every pre-existing valid file before the polling loop begins, so files dropped while j9t was offline are not silently missed.

- `Stop() → void` — Calls `SignalStop()` then `WaitStop()` sequentially; blocks until the background task completes. Also called by the destructor, so explicit `Stop()` before destruction is optional but harmless.

- `SignalStop() → void` — Sets `m_Running = false` and notifies `m_StopCV`, causing the watch loop to exit at its next condition-variable wake point. Non-blocking; returns before the thread has finished.

- `WaitStop() → void` — Calls `m_WatchTask.wait()` to join the background task, then invalidates the future to prevent a double-log on a subsequent `Stop()` or destructor call. Safe to call even if `Start()` was never called (checks `m_WatchTask.valid()`).

- `AddPath(const fs::path& root) → bool` — Registers an additional directory root for observation. Canonicalises the path via `fs::canonical`, rejects empty paths, non-directories, duplicates of the primary root, and duplicates of already-added roots. Thread-safe. The new root becomes active on the next poll tick (≤ `m_Interval`). Returns `false` on any rejection condition.

- `RemovePath(const fs::path& root) → bool` — Unregisters a root previously added with `AddPath`. Uses `fs::weakly_canonical` so the call succeeds even after the directory has already been deleted from disk. Enqueues the canonical path in `m_PendingRemovals`; the watch thread drains this on its next tick and silently prunes the internal file-time map entries under that root without firing `FileRemovedEvent` for any of those files. The primary root cannot be removed. Returns `false` if the path is empty, cannot be canonicalised, or is not found in `m_AdditionalRoots`.

**Collaborators:**

- `Core::g_Core` — Singleton core. `Start()` borrows its thread pool to run `Watch()`. `Watch()` calls `Core::g_Core->PushEvent(...)` to dispatch all three event types plus `EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown`.
- `FileAddedEvent`, `FileModifiedEvent`, `FileRemovedEvent` (from `event/events.h`) — Event types pushed into the engine queue; downstream consumers drive actual workflow logic.
- `EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown` — Pushed when the primary root directory disappears from disk; consumed by the engine to initiate an orderly shutdown.
- `TriggerEngine` and `AdhocWorkflowManager` — Primary callers of `AddPath`/`RemovePath`. The adhoc manager uses these to register per-run queue folders and is responsible for any cleanup signalling when a run folder is torn down before calling `RemovePath`.
- `auxiliary/file.h`, `engine.h` — Implementation-side includes; `core.h` provides the `g_Core` global and `LOG_APP_INFO`.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`FileWatcher` is typically owned by whatever subsystem creates it (queue manager, trigger engine). `Start()` fires an async task managed by `m_WatchTask`; the task holds a raw `this` pointer via lambda capture, so the owning object must not be destroyed while the watch thread is running — always call `Stop()` (or let the destructor do so) before the object is released. `m_RootsMutex` guards `m_AdditionalRoots` and `m_PendingRemovals`; it is held by both caller threads (via `AddPath`/`RemovePath`) and the watch thread (via `SnapshotAllRoots`/`DrainPendingRemovals`). `m_StopMutex` + `m_StopCV` are used exclusively to interrupt the poll sleep; they are independent of `m_RootsMutex`. `m_Running` is `std::atomic<bool>` and is read/written without holding any mutex. The internal `files` map (path → `last_write_time`) lives entirely on the watch thread and requires no locking.

**Notes:**

- Files whose names begin with `.` are silently excluded by `IsValidFile`; this is intentional to suppress Geany-style temporary files created in-place during editing.
- `RemovePath` intentionally does **not** fire `FileRemovedEvent` for files under the removed root. Callers that need downstream notification must emit their own signals before or after calling `RemovePath`.
- The initial scan in `Watch()` fires `FileAddedEvent` for every file already present under the primary root (`fireAdd=true`), but subsequent scans also pass `fireAdd=true` for additional roots on every tick, meaning a file first seen in any root on any scan cycle generates a `FileAddedEvent` regardless of when the root was registered.
- If the primary root disappears, `EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown` is pushed on every subsequent tick until `Stop()` is called, because the check is inside the `while (m_Running)` loop with no debounce or one-shot guard.
- Removal detection iterates the entire `files` map on every tick using `fs::exists`. For large file trees this is an O(n) filesystem stat pass per interval.
- `WaitStop()` intentionally invalidates `m_WatchTask` after joining to prevent a spurious "File watcher stopped" log line from appearing on the terminal after `Core::Shutdown` tears down the TUI; callers relying on `m_WatchTask.valid()` as a liveness sentinel after `WaitStop()` will see `false`.
- The directory iterator uses `fs::directory_options::skip_permission_denied` and silences per-entry errors with an `error_code` parameter, so partial permission failures produce no events and no exceptions. A `fs::filesystem_error` thrown during iteration (e.g. directory mutated mid-scan by a reaper process) is caught and the entire scan cycle is silently retried next tick.


---

### 48_doc_application_file_scriptRegistry_h

<a id="48-doc-application-file-scriptregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/48_doc_application_file_scriptRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



## ScriptRegistry

**Role:** `ScriptRegistry` is the central catalogue of all engine-managed scripts (`.py`, `.sh`, `.ps1`) that the AI assistant is allowed to discover and invoke. It parses a structured comment header embedded in each script file to extract metadata — a short description, parameters, outputs, and a list of top-level callable functions — and keeps that metadata in a thread-safe in-memory map. The registry serves two downstream consumers: the AI context pipeline (via a Markdown table injected into prompts) and the REST API (via a raw entry snapshot). It is also the live target for file-watcher events; individual files can be added, updated, or removed without a full rescan.

---

**Public API:**

- `ScanDirectory(scriptsDir) → void` — Clears the entire registry, then recursively walks `scriptsDir` for files whose extension is `.sh`, `.py`, or `.ps1`. Each qualifying file is passed through `ParseHeader`; only files that contain a valid `@jarvis-script` marker within their first 20 lines **and** a non-empty `@short` field are inserted. Logs the final count at INFO level. If `scriptsDir` does not exist or is not a directory the map is left empty and a WARN is emitted. Acquires `m_Mutex` for the clear-and-populate step.

- `AddOrUpdate(filePath) → void` — Intended for file-watcher callbacks when a single file is created or modified. Re-parses `filePath` via `ParseHeader`. If parsing succeeds, the entry is inserted or replaced under its absolute path key. If parsing fails (marker removed, `@short` absent, file unreadable) and the file was previously registered, it is silently removed from the map. Files whose extension is not `.sh`, `.py`, or `.ps1` are ignored without acquiring the lock.

- `Remove(filePath) → void` — Intended for file-watcher delete events. Erases the entry keyed on `filePath.string()` if present. No-op if the path was never registered.

- `SerializeMarkdownTable() → std::string` — Renders all registered entries as a GitHub-flavoured Markdown table sorted lexicographically by `m_FilePath`. Columns are script path, `@short` description, and comma-separated `@params`. Intended for injection into AI prompt context. Returns a fixed `"## Available Scripts\n\n(none)\n"` string when the map is empty.

- `GetEntries() → std::vector<ScriptRegistryEntry>` — Returns a by-value snapshot of every entry in the registry. Iteration order is unspecified (hash map). Used by the REST API layer.

- `Size() → size_t` — Returns the current count of registered scripts. Acquires `m_Mutex`.

- `FindByModulePath(modulePath) → ScriptRegistryEntry const*` — Converts a Python dotted module path (e.g. `"scripts.parseLog"`) to a file path by replacing every `.` with `/` and appending `.py`, then delegates to `FindByFilePath`. Returns `nullptr` if not found. Only meaningful for Python scripts; calling it with a shell module name will not find anything.

- `FindByFilePath(filePath) → ScriptRegistryEntry const*` — Looks up an entry first by exact key match, then by suffix match: the stored key is checked to see whether it ends with `filePath` preceded by a `/` or the beginning of the string. This allows callers to pass a relative path even when entries were registered with absolute paths. Returns `nullptr` if no match is found. The returned pointer is into the internal map and is invalidated by any subsequent mutating call.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` — Provides `LOG_CORE_WARN` and `LOG_CORE_INFO` macros used throughout for operational logging. No other coupling to the engine is introduced in this file.
- File-watcher subsystem — Expected to call `AddOrUpdate` and `Remove` on filesystem events. The registry does not register itself with any watcher; the watcher must hold a reference and call these methods directly.
- AI prompt-building layer — Calls `SerializeMarkdownTable()` to embed script metadata in LLM context windows.
- REST API handler — Calls `GetEntries()` to expose the script catalogue over HTTP.
- Embedded Python execution layer — Uses `FindByModulePath` to resolve a Python module identifier to a concrete `ScriptRegistryEntry` before invoking the interpreter.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ScriptRegistry` is designed to be shared across threads. Every public method acquires `m_Mutex` (declared `mutable`) before touching `m_Entries`. There are no recursive lock acquisitions; `ParseHeader` is a static helper that performs only file I/O and is called **before** the lock is taken in `AddOrUpdate` and `ScanDirectory`, so the lock is never held during blocking disk reads.

Ownership is not managed by the class itself — callers are responsible for keeping the `ScriptRegistry` instance alive. The raw pointers returned by `FindByModulePath` and `FindByFilePath` point directly into `m_Entries` and are only valid as long as no mutating method (`AddOrUpdate`, `Remove`, `ScanDirectory`) is called on the registry. Callers that need to retain the data past a potential mutation must copy the `ScriptRegistryEntry` by value.

---

**Notes:**

- **Header detection limits:** `ParseHeader` reads at most the first 20 lines searching for `@jarvis-script` and parses at most up to line 50 for `@`-fields. Any comment block that spans beyond line 50 is silently truncated. The header block ends immediately when a non-comment line (one whose first non-whitespace character is not `#`) is encountered after the marker, which means a blank line inside the comment block also terminates parsing.

- **`@short` is mandatory.** A file that contains `@jarvis-script` but omits `@short` is rejected with a WARN and is **not** inserted into the registry, even if all other fields are present.

- **Exported function detection is extension-specific.** For `.py` files, only top-level `def` lines (zero leading whitespace, `def ` prefix) are recorded in `m_ExportedFunctions`. For `.sh` files, only `function funcname` syntax is detected; the alternative `funcname()` POSIX form is **not** currently matched. `.ps1` files receive no function extraction at all.

- **Map key is the absolute file path string** as returned by `std::filesystem::path::string()`. `FindByFilePath`'s suffix-matching logic compensates for callers that supply relative paths, but the match is purely string-based and is not path-normalised, so a trailing slash difference or a `./` prefix on the query will cause the suffix check to fail.

- **`ScanDirectory` calls `m_Entries.clear()` under the lock** before iterating the filesystem. This means there is a window during a rescan where the registry is empty and concurrent `FindBy*` calls return `nullptr`. Callers that cannot tolerate momentary empty results should coordinate externally.

- **`@params` and `@outputs` are multi-valued fields.** Each continuation line (or each inline value after the tag) appends a new element to the respective vector, with leading `- ` stripped from list-style items. `@short` and `@description` instead concatenate continuation lines with a single space separator.


---

### 49_doc_application_jarvisAgent_h

<a id="49-doc-application-jarvisagent-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/49_doc_application_jarvisAgent_h/docu.output.md`



## JarvisAgent / jarvisAgent.h + jarvisAgent.cpp

**Role:** `JarvisAgent` is the top-level application class for the j9t workflow engine. It owns every major subsystem — the HTTP/WebSocket server, Python interpreter pool, workflow registry and runtime manager, cron/file-watch trigger engine, AI request pool, curl dispatcher, cloud connector registry, script watcher, task executor registry, and the TUI status renderer — and sequences their startup, per-frame update, event routing, and two-phase shutdown. It is the single object that the engine loop calls into; all other components are either directly owned by it or reachable through it via the global `App::g_App` pointer.

---

**Public API:**

- `OnStart() → void` — Called once by the engine loop before the first `OnUpdate`. Records `m_StartupTime`, sets `App::g_App = this`, wires `StatusRenderer` callbacks into `TerminalManager`, scans `scripts/` and `workflows/` directories, starts `FileWatcher` for scripts, creates and starts `WebServer` (aborts with `m_IsFinished = true` on failure), initialises `PythonEnginePool` from `scripts/main.py` (continues without Python on failure), constructs `AiRequestPool` and `CurlMultiDispatcher`, then delegates to `InitializeWorkflows()`. On Windows, also probes the preferred shell via `ShellTaskExecutor::ProbeWindowsShell`.

- `OnUpdate() → void` — Called every engine tick. Returns immediately if `m_IsFinished` or a quit has been requested. Ticks `AiRequestPool::Update()`, advances cron/file-watch triggers via `TriggerEngine::Tick(now)`, calls `WorkflowRuntimeManager::Update()` and broadcasts run-state snapshots to connected dashboard WebSocket clients when the return value indicates a state change. Calls `CheckIfFinished()` at the end (currently a no-op; Ctrl+C is handled by the engine loop).

- `OnEvent(std::shared_ptr<Event>&) → void` — Routes engine events through `EventDispatcher`. Handles: `EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown` (sets `m_IsFinished`); `FileAddedEvent`, `FileModifiedEvent`, `FileRemovedEvent` (forwarded to `TriggerEngine::NotifyFileEvent` with startup-suppress logic, and to `ScriptRegistry::AddOrUpdate`/`Remove` when the path is under `scripts/`); `PythonCrashedEvent` (stops the pool); `AiCallStartedEvent`, `AiCallCompletedEvent`, `AiCallFailedEvent` (broadcast to dashboard WebSocket via `WebServer`). Events not consumed by a handler fall through to `PythonEnginePool::OnEvent`.

- `OnShutdown() → void` — Executes a strict two-phase shutdown. **Phase 1 (signal, non-blocking):** `Core::SignalShutdown`, `AiRequestPool::Shutdown`, `CurlMultiDispatcher::SignalStop`, optionally `WebServer::ShutdownAssistantController` (studio build), `WorkflowRuntimeManager::SignalStop`, `PythonEnginePool::SignalStop`, `FileWatcher::SignalStop`, `WebServer::SignalStop`. **Phase 2 (wait and reset, blocking, ordered):** `WorkflowRuntimeManager::WaitStop` → `AiRequestPool::reset` → `CurlMultiDispatcher::WaitStop` → `PythonEnginePool::WaitStop` → `FileWatcher::WaitStop` → `WebServer::WaitStop` → `TriggerEngine::reset`. Raw `write`/`_write` calls to stderr bracket each step so hang points are visible even if spdlog is broken.

- `IsFinished() const → bool` — Returns `m_IsFinished`. Set to `true` on `EngineEventShutdown` or when `WebServer::Start()` fails.

- `GetStartupTimestamp() const → int64_t` — Returns `m_StartupTime` as nanoseconds since the Unix epoch.

- `Create() → std::unique_ptr<Application>` — Static factory used by the engine entry-point to construct the application object.

- `GetWebServer() const → WebServer*` — Raw non-owning pointer; valid from after `OnStart` until `OnShutdown` phase 2.

- `GetPythonEnginePool() → PythonEnginePool*` — May return a valid but non-running pool if initialisation failed.

- `GetWorkflowRegistry() → WorkflowRegistry*` — Populated by `InitializeWorkflows`; null before that call completes.

- `GetWorkflowRuntimeManager() const → WorkflowRuntimeManager*` — Non-owning pointer; reset to null in phase 2 of `OnShutdown`.

- `GetAiRequestPool() const → AiRequestPool*` — Reset to null early in phase 2.

- `GetCurlMultiDispatcher() const → CurlMultiDispatcher*` — Reset to null in phase 2.

- `GetStatusRenderer() → StatusRenderer&` — Reference to the value-member; always valid for the lifetime of `JarvisAgent`.

- `GetScriptRegistry() → ScriptRegistry*` — Registry of on-disk Python/shell scripts under `scripts/`.

- `GetWorkflowFileIndex() → WorkflowFileIndex*` — Basename-to-path index for the `workflows/` directory.

- `GetInternalTaskRegistry() → IInternalTaskRegistry*` — Pointer to the value-member `InternalTaskRegistry`; valid for the full object lifetime.

---

**Collaborators:**

| Collaborator | Relationship |
|---|---|
| `Application` (base) | CRTP-style lifecycle contract; engine calls `OnStart/OnUpdate/OnEvent/OnShutdown/IsFinished`. |
| `Core` / `Core::g_Core` | Global engine singleton. Provides `TerminalManager`, `KeyManager`, `CloudCircuitBreaker`, `CloudConnectorRegistry`, `CloudConnectionManager`, `ThreadPool`, config, and the global shutdown flag. Must exist before `OnStart`. |
| `WebServer` | Owned. Serves the REST/WebSocket dashboard and MCP endpoint. Receives pointers to `WorkflowRegistry`, `WorkflowRuntimeManager`, and `TriggerEngine` after construction. |
| `WorkflowRuntimeManager` | Owned. Executes and tracks live workflow runs. Late-bound to `SubWorkflowTaskExecutor` and `WebServer` after construction. |
| `WorkflowRegistry` | Owned. Loads and validates JCWF workflow definitions from disk. |
| `TriggerEngine` | Owned. Fires cron and file-watch triggers; callback enqueues a run via `WorkflowRuntimeManager::EnqueueWorkflowRun`. |
| `TaskExecutorRegistry` | Singleton (not owned). `InitializeWorkflows` registers all executor types: `Shell`, `AiCall`, `Python`, `Internal`, `SubWorkflow`, and all cloud types. |
| `AiRequestPool` | Owned. Manages in-flight AI provider HTTP calls; ticked each frame. |
| `CurlMultiDispatcher` | Owned. Drives libcurl's multi interface for async HTTP. |
| `PythonEnginePool` | Owned. N sub-interpreters. Initialised from `scripts/main.py`; receives file and crash events. |
| `ScriptRegistry` | Owned. In-memory index of `scripts/` content, kept live by `m_ScriptFileWatcher`. |
| `FileWatcher` (`m_ScriptFileWatcher`) | Owned. Polls `scripts/` at 100 ms intervals; emits `FileAdded/Modified/Removed` events routed through `OnEvent`. |
| `WorkflowFileIndex` | Owned. Basename-to-absolute-path map for `workflows/`; populated at startup only (not live-watched). |
