| `SubWorkflowTaskExecutor` | Shared ownership (`shared_ptr`). Registered in `TaskExecutorRegistry` and held by `JarvisAgent` so `SetRuntimeManager` can be called after `WorkflowRuntimeManager` is constructed. |
| `StatusRenderer` | Value member. Wired with lambda providers at startup for snapshot and last-runs data. |
| `InternalTaskRegistry` | Value member. Wrapped with a no-op deleter `shared_ptr` when passed to `InternalTaskExecutor`. |
| All cloud connectors/executors | Constructed in `InitializeWorkflows` and registered into `Core`-owned `CloudConnectorRegistry` and the singleton `TaskExecutorRegistry`. |
| `App::g_App` | Static raw pointer set to `this` on `OnStart`, cleared to `nullptr` in `OnShutdown` phase 2 (before `PythonEnginePool` stops). |

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `JarvisAgent` itself is constructed and driven exclusively on the engine's main thread. All `OnStart`/`OnUpdate`/`OnEvent`/`OnShutdown` calls are single-threaded from the caller's perspective.
- Background threads are owned by subsystems (`WorkflowRuntimeManager`, `PythonEnginePool`, `FileWatcher`, `WebServer`, `CurlMultiDispatcher`). The two-phase shutdown protocol (`SignalStop` then `WaitStop`) ensures these threads are joined before their owning `unique_ptr` is reset. The ordering in phase 2 is intentional and must not be rearranged: `WorkflowRuntimeManager` before `AiRequestPool` before `CurlMultiDispatcher`; `WebServer` before `TriggerEngine` (in-flight web requests may hold raw pointers into `TriggerEngine`).
- `App::g_App` is a raw global pointer with no locking. It is set before background threads start and cleared after `CurlMultiDispatcher` is stopped but before `PythonEnginePool` stops. Code running on Python threads that dereferences `App::g_App` must tolerate it being null.
- `m_InternalTaskRegistry` is a stack-allocated value member. The `shared_ptr` wrapping it uses a no-op deleter, so callers receiving it from `GetInternalTaskRegistry()` must not outlive `JarvisAgent`.
- `SubWorkflowTaskExecutor` is held as `shared_ptr` by both `JarvisAgent` and `TaskExecutorRegistry`. It must have `SetRuntimeManager(nullptr)` semantics or simply be reset after `WorkflowRuntimeManager` is destroyed; the current code resets `m_WorkflowRuntimeManager` in phase 2 before `m_SubWorkflowExecutor` is released, so the raw pointer it holds becomes dangling if the executor is invoked after phase 1 signals have been sent — callers must not dispatch new sub-workflow tasks after `WorkflowRuntimeManager::SignalStop`.

---

**Notes:**

- **Startup-suppress for file events:** During the first 10 seconds after `m_StartupTime`, any non-delete file event for a file whose `last_write_time` predates `m_StartupTime` is silently dropped. This prevents pre-existing files from firing `file_watch` triggers on startup. The comparison converts `filesystem::file_time_type` to `system_clock` via the anonymous `ToSystemClock` helper.
- **WebServer failure is fatal:** If `WebServer::Start()` returns false, `m_FatalStartupMessage` is set and `m_IsFinished` is set to `true` before any other subsystem is started, so `OnUpdate` will never be called. Callers must handle this by checking `IsFinished()` immediately after `OnStart`.
- **Python failure is non-fatal:** A failed `PythonEnginePool::Initialize` logs a critical message and continues; `m_PythonEnginePool` remains valid but its `IsRunning()` will return false. Events are still forwarded to it.
- **Slack and Email executors are registered under two `TaskType` values each** (`SlackMessage`/`SlackRead`; `EmailSend`/`EmailRead`) using the same `shared_ptr` instance. Replacing one registration silently affects both.
- **`OnUpdate` Python tick is disabled.** The call to `m_PythonEnginePool->OnUpdate()` is commented out. Python execution is entirely event- and task-driven.
- **`CheckIfFinished` is a no-op.** The function body contains only a comment. Ctrl+C termination is handled upstream in the engine run loop; `JarvisAgent` only sets `m_IsFinished` via `OnEvent` shutdown dispatch or WebServer start failure.
- **Raw stderr writes in `OnShutdown`** use the `RAW_ONSHUTDOWN` macro (POSIX `write` / Win32 `_write`) to emit progress markers that survive spdlog failures or deadlocks. These are intentional diagnostics, not dead code.
- **`WorkflowFileIndex` is static after startup.** It scans `workflows/` once in `OnStart` and is never updated by the file watcher. Workflows added at runtime are not reflected in the index until the next restart.
- **`assistant/sessions` directory** is created unconditionally at startup. Failure (e.g. permission denied) is silently ignored — the `std::error_code` is checked for logging only, not for aborting startup.


---

### 50_doc_application_json_jcwfGenerationGuide_generated_h

<a id="50-doc-application-json-jcwfgenerationguide-generated-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/50_doc_application_json_jcwfGenerationGuide_generated_h/docu.output.md`



## jcwf_generation_guide.hpp

**Role:** This header is an auto-generated, compile-time-embedded string constant that holds the complete JCWF (JC Workflow) generation reference document. It exposes the entire text of `doc/jcwf_generation_guide.md` as a single `constexpr char const*` named `kJcwfGenerationGuide` inside the `AIAssistant` namespace. Its purpose is to make the workflow authoring guide available to any C++ translation unit that needs to inject it into an AI prompt, display it in a UI, or feed it to an AI assistant as in-context documentation — without requiring a filesystem read at runtime.

**Public API:**

- `AIAssistant::kJcwfGenerationGuide` — A `constexpr char const*` holding the full JCWF generation guide as a raw string literal. No functions, no methods, no constructors. Callers include this header and read the pointer directly. The string is immutable and statically allocated; its address is stable for the lifetime of the process. The value is a concatenation of three adjacent raw string literals (using the `JCWF` delimiter) that the compiler joins at translation time into a single contiguous null-terminated string.

**Collaborators:**

- `doc/jcwf_generation_guide.md` — The authoritative source from which this file is generated. Do not edit this header directly; regenerate via `premake5.lua` prebuild step.
- `sub-jcwf_generation_guide.md` — Referenced within the guide text for sub-workflow canvas content; a separate companion document not embedded here.
- `JC_Workflow_Specification.md` — Cited repeatedly inside the guide for full specification details (§3.3.1 cloud task types, etc.); not embedded in this header.
- Consumers in the `AIAssistant` subsystem — any component building AI prompts about JCWF authoring (for example, a workflow-generation AI assistant or a Studio help panel) would include this header and pass `kJcwfGenerationGuide` as context to the model.

**Threading / lifetime:**

The constant has static storage duration. It requires no synchronization, no heap allocation, and no initialization order considerations. It is safe to read from any thread at any time after program startup. No ownership transfer occurs; callers borrow the pointer and must not free or mutate it.

**Notes:**

- The string is split across three adjacent raw string literals with the `R"JCWF(...)JCWF"` delimiter. This is purely a C++ source-length workaround; the compiler produces one contiguous string. The split points are mid-table in §3.2 and mid-task in Example B §11, so if the guide text is searched or printed it is fully intact.
- The guide covers JCWF v1.0 and v1.1. Features gated to v1.1 (`filters`, `control_nodes`, `controlflow`) require `"version": "1.1"` in the canvas JSON; the runtime rejects them on v1.0 documents.
- The `file_inputs` path-relativity rule is a frequent source of bugs: values in `file_inputs` are resolved relative to `working_directory`, so bare filenames are correct and prefixing with the working directory path doubles the path at runtime (pitfall #10 in §12).
- Shell task `params.command` must start with `scripts/` — this is a security enforcement by the runtime, not merely a convention.
- `ai_call` tasks must never declare `file_outputs` pointing inside their own queue folder with unrecognized or `PROB_`-prefixed filenames; the runtime categorizes such files as fresh prompts and fires extra AI queries (§3.2 table).
- The `output_schema` and chunking features on `ai_call` are mutually exclusive; the guide explicitly states that schema wins and chunking is skipped if the prompt exceeds the context window.
- Webhook trigger `params.secret` is mandatory; the validator rejects empty or absent secrets at load time, and the runtime validates `X-Webhook-Signature: sha256=<hex>` on every incoming request (§8).
- `python` task functions are called programmatically via `module.function(**kwargs)`, not via CLI; `sys.argv`, `argparse`, and `main()` entry points are incorrect patterns for this task type (§3.3).


---

### 51_doc_application_json_jcwfSchema_generated_h

<a id="51-doc-application-json-jcwfschema-generated-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/51_doc_application_json_jcwfSchema_generated_h/docu.output.md`



## jcwf_schema.hpp

**Role:** This header is an auto-generated, compile-time constant that embeds the complete JSON Schema for the JC Workflow File (JCWF) format v1.1 directly into the `AIAssistant` namespace as a single `inline constexpr` C-string. It is the canonical machine-readable contract for every valid `.jcwf` document consumed or produced by the j9t engine. Because it is generated by the `premake5.lua` prebuild step from `doc/jcwf.schema.json`, it serves as the single source of truth shared between the schema document and the compiled binary without any runtime file I/O.

**Public API:**

- `kJcwfSchemaJson` — `inline constexpr char const*` holding the full JSON Schema text (JSON Schema draft 2020-12). No functions are declared; the entire surface area is this one string constant. Consumers pass it to a JSON Schema validator (e.g., `nlohmann::json_schema`, `rapidjson`, or a Python `jsonschema` bridge) to validate workflow documents at load time. It can also be serialised to disk or served over HTTP as the schema endpoint without any heap allocation.

**Collaborators:**

- **`doc/jcwf.schema.json`** — the authoritative upstream source; this header is a verbatim embedding of that file. Any schema change must be made there and the prebuild step re-run; editing this header directly is explicitly forbidden by the file comment.
- **`premake5.lua` prebuild rule** — reads `doc/jcwf.schema.json` and regenerates this header. Engineers adding new task types, trigger kinds, or top-level properties must update the schema JSON and rebuild.
- **Workflow loader / validator** (caller, not present in this file) — whatever component in `AIAssistant` parses `.jcwf` files is expected to reference `kJcwfSchemaJson` to validate incoming documents before constructing runtime objects.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`kJcwfSchemaJson` is a `constexpr` string literal with static storage duration. It requires no initialisation, no destruction, and no locking. Any number of threads may read it simultaneously at any point during program lifetime. No ownership transfer ever occurs.

**Notes:**

- The `version` property enforces the regex `^1\.[01]$`, meaning only `"1.0"` and `"1.1"` are accepted. Documents claiming any other version string will fail validation; the runtime must not silently accept them.
- `manual_start` defaults to `true` at the schema level. If a loader does not apply JSON Schema defaults explicitly, it must supply this default itself or workflows will behave as if manually started when the field is absent.
- The `task.mode` field (`"single"` | `"per_item"`) has a schema default of `"single"`, but `per_item` mode implies the task is invoked once per element produced by the filter named in `task.filter`. Loaders that ignore `mode` will silently process only the first item.
- `expose_error_signal` appears twice in the `task` definition (once at the top-level properties block and once inside `params`). The schema does not deduplicate these; the effective default is `false` from the second occurrence.
- `controlflow_edge.kind` admits three values: `"normal"`, `"error_signal"`, and `"on_error"`. The distinction between `error_signal` (a task exposing an error output port) and `on_error` (a fallback edge taken when a task fails) is load-bearing for runtime error routing and must not be conflated.
- `queue_binding` file references (`stng_files`, `task_files`, `cntx_files`, `prob_files`, `prov_files`) accept either a bare string path or an object with `path` + `content` (inline content). Validators that only handle the string form will silently accept non-conforming documents; the runtime must handle both shapes.
- The `filter.source.kind` values `"query"` and `"polarion_query"` require a `connection` string and `query` field respectively. The schema marks neither as `required` inside the source object beyond `kind` itself, so validators will not catch missing connection parameters — the runtime loader must enforce these conditionally.
- Do **not** edit this file by hand. The prebuild step overwrites it unconditionally; manual changes will be lost on the next build.


---

### 52_doc_application_json_jsonObjectParser_h

<a id="52-doc-application-json-jsonobjectparser-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/52_doc_application_json_jsonObjectParser_h/docu.output.md`



## JsonObjectParser

**Role:** `JsonObjectParser` is a single-use, recursive pretty-printer and structural walker for `simdjson` on-demand JSON values. Given a key name and a `simdjson::ondemand::value`, it immediately traverses the value's full type hierarchy — scalars, nested objects, and arrays — and emits each node to the application log at an indented level that mirrors the JSON depth. It is used inside the `AIAssistant` engine wherever a JSON blob needs to be dumped in human-readable, hierarchically indented form (e.g. for inspecting AI provider responses or webhook payloads). Error detection is surfaced back to the caller through `HasError()` after construction completes.

**Public API:**

- `JsonObjectParser(std::string_view key, simdjson::ondemand::value value, std::string const& warningText, uint32_t indentLevel = 0) → void` — The only meaningful entry point. Stores copies of `key`, `warningText`, and `indentLevel`, retains the `simdjson::ondemand::value` view (not a copy of the underlying bytes), then immediately calls `Parse()`. All traversal happens synchronously inside this constructor. `key` is the label that will appear in log output (e.g. a JSON field name or an array-index string like `"[2]"`). `warningText` is substituted into any `LOG_APP_WARN` message that is emitted on a simdjson error, giving the caller control over contextual error messaging. `indentLevel` seeds the two-space-per-level indentation; callers pass 0 for the root and the class increments it internally for recursion.

- `HasError() const → bool` — Returns `true` if any `simdjson::simdjson_error` was caught at any depth during construction, or if any individual field or array-element reported an error code during iteration. A `false` return means the entire subtree was traversed without a simdjson exception or error result. Because parsing is fully complete by the time the constructor returns, this can be queried immediately after construction.

**Collaborators:**

- **simdjson (`simdjson::ondemand`)** — The entire traversal is built on the simdjson on-demand API. `simdjson::ondemand::value`, `::object`, `::array`, and `::json_type` are the core types consumed. The class depends on simdjson's iterator-based field and element access and on `simdjson::error_message()` for human-readable error strings.
- **`engine.h`** — Pulled in by the `.cpp` to gain access to `LOG_APP_INFO` and `LOG_APP_WARN`, the engine's logging macros. No other part of the engine is directly called; the dependency is purely for logging infrastructure.
- **Recursive `JsonObjectParser` instances** — `ParseObject` and `ParseArray` each construct new `JsonObjectParser` objects on the stack for every nested field or element, incrementing `indentLevel` by 1 each time. There is no explicit recursion depth limit; deeply nested JSON will produce a correspondingly deep call stack.

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `simdjson::ondemand::value` is a *view* into the parent document's backing buffer. The stored `m_Value` member is valid only for the duration of the calling iteration loop in the parent (e.g. a `for (auto field : obj)` loop). Callers must not hold a `JsonObjectParser` instance past the point where the underlying simdjson document or iterator is advanced or destroyed. Because `Parse()` runs entirely inside the constructor, the safe pattern is to construct and immediately discard (or query `HasError()` on) the object before advancing the parent iterator.
- There is no internal synchronization. Each `JsonObjectParser` instance is entirely stack-local and self-contained; concurrent use of distinct instances is safe as long as each has its own simdjson document context. Sharing a single simdjson on-demand document across threads is not safe per simdjson's own rules.
- `JsonObjectParser` owns `m_Key` and `m_WarningText` by value (copied from the constructor arguments), so the caller's `std::string_view key` source does not need to outlive construction.

**Notes:**

- All numbers are extracted via `get_double()` and converted with `std::to_string`, meaning integers stored in JSON (e.g. `42`) will be logged with decimal notation (e.g. `42.000000`). There is no integer-type branch.
- The `m_HasError` flag is set to `true` on any `simdjson::simdjson_error` thrown during `Parse()`, but errors encountered in `ParseObject` or `ParseArray` during field/element iteration are logged and skipped with `continue` rather than propagating `m_HasError` back to the parent `JsonObjectParser`. A parent instance therefore reports `HasError() == false` even if a nested recursive parse of one of its fields set its own `m_HasError`; error state does not bubble up the recursion chain.
- `ParseArray` and `ParseObject` log errors per-element and continue iteration rather than aborting, so a partially corrupt object or array will still produce log output for all valid siblings.
- The `default` branch in the type switch handles any future simdjson `json_type` values that fall outside the six known types; it logs `[unknown type]` without setting `m_HasError`.
- `PrintIndented()` returns a string of `2 × indentLevel` spaces on every call rather than caching it; for deeply nested documents this is called twice per node (once for the header log line and implicitly again for any warn path), but this is purely a log-path cost and is unlikely to matter in practice.


---

### 53_doc_application_json_replyParser_h

<a id="53-doc-application-json-replyparser-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/53_doc_application_json_replyParser_h/docu.output.md`



## ReplyParser

**Role:** `ReplyParser` is the abstract base class for all AI-provider response parsers within the `AIAssistant` namespace. Its single responsibility is to accept a raw JSON string returned by an AI provider's HTTP endpoint and expose a uniform, provider-agnostic interface for extracting reply content, error state, token usage, finish reason, system fingerprint, and structured output. Concrete parsing logic is entirely delegated to five provider-specific subclasses (`ReplyParserAPI1` through `ReplyParserAPI5`). The static factory method `Create` is the sole entry point for obtaining a live parser; it selects the correct subclass based on the configured `InterfaceType`, with `API6` (Azure OpenAI) deliberately reusing `ReplyParserAPI1` because Azure returns OpenAI-compatible response bodies.

---

**Public API:**

- `ReplyParser(std::string const& jsonString)` — Constructor. Stores the raw JSON string in `m_JsonString`. Does **not** trigger parsing; that happens in the concrete subclass constructor.

- `HasError() → bool` — Returns `m_HasError`. Set to `true` by subclasses when JSON parsing fails or the provider body signals an error condition. Callers should check this before calling `GetContent`.

- `HasContent(size_t index = 0) → size_t` *(pure virtual)* — Implemented by each concrete parser. Callers pass an index to query whether a response choice at that position exists. Return semantics (count vs. boolean-as-size_t) are defined per subclass.

- `GetContent(size_t index = 0) → std::string` *(pure virtual)* — Returns the text of the completion choice at `index`. Concrete parsers extract the appropriate JSON field (e.g., `choices[index].message.content` for OpenAI-style bodies).

- `GetError() → AiError` — Base implementation returns a generic `AiError` with `Kind::Provider` and a fixed message `"reply parser reported error"` when `m_HasError` is true; returns a default-constructed `AiError` otherwise. Concrete subclasses may override to return provider-specific error codes or messages.

- `GetUsage() → AiUsage` — Base implementation returns a default-constructed `AiUsage` (all fields zeroed). Concrete subclasses override to populate prompt/completion token counts from the provider body.

- `GetFinishReason() → std::string` — Base returns an empty string. Overridden by concrete parsers that expose a finish-reason field (e.g., `"stop"`, `"length"`, `"tool_calls"`).

- `GetSystemFingerprint() → std::string` — Base returns an empty string. Overridden by parsers for providers that include a system fingerprint in the response (OpenAI does; others may not).

- `GetStructuredOutput() → std::optional<std::string>` — Base returns `std::nullopt`. Overridden by parsers that recognise structured/JSON-mode output fields in the response body.

- `static Create(ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType const& interfaceType, std::string const& jsonString) → std::unique_ptr<ReplyParser>` — Factory. Allocates and returns the concrete subclass matching `interfaceType`. Returns a null `unique_ptr` and emits a `LOG_APP_CRITICAL` log entry if `interfaceType` is unrecognised. Callers **must** null-check the return value. `API6` maps to `ReplyParserAPI1`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType` (`json/configParser.h`) — Enum that drives subclass selection in `Create`. The enum values `API1`–`API6` must remain in sync with the set of concrete parsers.
- `AiError`, `AiUsage` (`workflow/aiReply.h`) — Plain data types returned by `GetError` and `GetUsage`. `AiError::Kind::Provider` is the only `Kind` the base class ever sets.
- `ReplyParserAPI1` – `ReplyParserAPI5` (`json/replyParserAPI1.h` – `json/replyParserAPI5.h`) — The five concrete parsers. Each inherits `ReplyParser` and is responsible for its own JSON parsing, typically in its constructor.
- `engine.h` — Included in the `.cpp`; provides the `LOG_APP_CRITICAL` macro used in the default branch of `Create`.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `Create` returns a `std::unique_ptr<ReplyParser>`, so the caller owns the object exclusively. There is no shared ownership and no internal reference counting.
- `ReplyParser` objects are not thread-safe. Parsing is expected to complete during construction of the concrete subclass; thereafter the object is effectively read-only. Concurrent read access to a fully-constructed parser from multiple threads is safe in practice, but this is not formally guaranteed by the class.
- The destructor is `virtual` and defaulted, ensuring correct destruction through a base pointer.

---

**Notes:**

- `Create` returns a null `unique_ptr` for an unsupported `InterfaceType` rather than throwing. Every call site must check `replyParser != nullptr` before use or risk an immediate null-pointer dereference.
- `API6` is not a distinct parser; it explicitly reuses `ReplyParserAPI1`. This is an intentional architectural decision documented in the switch statement comment. If `ReplyParserAPI1`'s behaviour changes, `API6` (Azure OpenAI) is silently affected.
- The `m_State` member (`Undefined`, `ParseOk`, `ParseFailure`, `ReplyOk`, `ReplyError`) is declared `protected` and available to all subclasses, but the base class never reads it in any of its own method bodies. Subclasses are responsible for setting it to reflect parse outcome; the base-class `HasError` uses only `m_HasError`, not `m_State`.
- Adding a new provider requires: a new `InterfaceType` enum value in `ConfigParser::EngineConfig`, a new concrete parser header and translation unit, and a new `case` in `ReplyParser::Create`. Forgetting the `Create` case causes a `LOG_APP_CRITICAL` log at runtime and a null return — no compile-time diagnostic.
- The base-class defaults for `GetFinishReason`, `GetSystemFingerprint`, `GetUsage`, and `GetStructuredOutput` intentionally return empty/null values so that callers can safely call them on any concrete parser without checking whether the provider exposes those fields.


---

### 54_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI1_h

<a id="54-doc-application-json-replyparserapi1-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/54_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI1_h/docu.output.md`



## ReplyParserAPI1

**Role:** `ReplyParserAPI1` is a concrete parser for the OpenAI Chat Completions v1 JSON response format (the `chat.completion` object shape used by GPT-4 and compatible endpoints). It derives from `ReplyParser`, accepts the raw JSON body as a `std::string` at construction, immediately parses it via simdjson's on-demand API, and exposes the result through the standardised `ReplyParser` virtual interface (`GetContent`, `GetError`, `GetUsage`, `GetFinishReason`). It handles both the happy-path `choices` array and the error-path `error` object emitted by the OpenAI API, classifying provider errors into a typed `ErrorType` enum and mapping them to conventional HTTP status codes in `GetError`.

---

**Public API:**

- `ReplyParserAPI1(std::string const& jsonString)` — Constructor. Takes the complete, unmodified JSON response body. Calls `Parse()` immediately and synchronously before returning; by the time the object is usable all internal state is final. No lazy parsing.

- `GetErrorInfo() const → ErrorInfo const&` — Returns a reference to the raw error fields extracted from the JSON `"error"` object: `m_Message`, `m_Type`, `m_Code`, `m_Param`. Meaningful only when `GetError().m_Kind == AiError::Kind::Provider` or `m_HasError` is true (inherited flag); returns a default-constructed (empty) struct on a successful reply.

- `GetErrorType() const → ErrorType` — Returns the classified error enum: `InvalidRequestError`, `AuthenticationError`, `PermissionError`, `RateLimitError`, `ServerError`, `InsufficientQuota`, or `Unknown`. Derived by matching the raw `"type"` string from the JSON error object.

- `HasContent() const → size_t` — Returns the number of entries in `m_Reply.m_Choices`. Zero indicates either a parse failure or an error reply; non-zero indicates a usable response. Overrides `ReplyParser::HasContent`.

- `GetContent(size_t index = 0) const → std::string` — Returns `m_Choices[index].m_Message.m_Content`. Logs `LOG_APP_ERROR` and returns an empty string if `index` is out of range. Default index 0 covers the common single-choice case. Overrides `ReplyParser::GetContent`.

- `GetError() const → AiError` — Constructs and returns an `AiError` struct. If `m_HasError` is false returns a default-constructed (zero/empty) struct. When an error exists, sets `m_Kind = AiError::Kind::Provider`, copies `m_ErrorInfo.m_Message`, and populates `m_HttpStatus` from the `ErrorType` mapping: `RateLimitError` → 429, `AuthenticationError` → 401, `PermissionError` → 403, `ServerError` → 500. `InvalidRequestError`, `InsufficientQuota`, and `Unknown` leave `m_HttpStatus` at its default (0). Overrides `ReplyParser::GetError`.

- `GetUsage() const → AiUsage` — Maps `m_Reply.m_Usage.{m_PromptTokens, m_CompletionTokens, m_TotalTokens}` (stored as `uint64_t`) into `AiUsage.{m_InputTokens, m_OutputTokens, m_TotalTokens}` (stored as `int32_t`) via `static_cast`. Returns zeroed struct when the reply contained no `"usage"` field or was discarded due to an error. Overrides `ReplyParser::GetUsage`.

- `GetFinishReason() const → std::string` — Returns `m_Choices.front().m_FinishReason` (e.g. `"stop"`, `"length"`). Returns an empty string when `m_Choices` is empty. Only the first choice's finish reason is exposed regardless of how many choices exist. Overrides `ReplyParser::GetFinishReason`.

- `GetSystemFingerprint() const → std::string` — Always returns an empty string. The OpenAI v1 API does emit `"system_fingerprint"` but this implementation does not parse or store it. Overrides `ReplyParser::GetSystemFingerprint`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ReplyParser` (`json/replyParser.h`) — Abstract base class providing the virtual interface, the `m_JsonString` storage used during parsing, the `m_HasError` flag, and the `State` enum (`ParseFailure`, `ReplyOk`, `ReplyError`) written to `m_State` by `Parse()`.

- `simdjson` (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) — Used in on-demand mode. A local `ondemand::parser` and `padded_string` are created per call to `Parse()`; the padded string copies the input so the original `jsonString` argument does not need to outlive the constructor call. All simdjson iterators are consumed within the private parse methods and are not retained.

- `JsonObjectParser` (`json/jsonObjectParser.h`) — Instantiated inline for every JSON key that does not match a known field (catch-all else branches in all four parse methods). Its purpose is diagnostic logging of unrecognised fields; the result object is discarded immediately.

- `AiError` / `AiUsage` — Engine-level value structs (from `engine.h`) returned by `GetError()` and `GetUsage()` respectively; callers consuming the `ReplyParser` interface receive these types.

- `CORE_ASSERT` (from `core.h`) — Used to assert JSON type expectations (e.g. `"id"` must be a string). In a debug build a type mismatch will abort; in a release build behaviour is undefined if a provider returns unexpected types.

- `LOG_APP_INFO` / `LOG_APP_ERROR` / `LOG_APP_CRITICAL` — Logging macros used extensively during parsing. Every successfully read field is logged at INFO level; errors and discarded replies at ERROR/CRITICAL.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ReplyParserAPI1` has no internal synchronisation and is not thread-safe. It is intended to be constructed, used, and destroyed on a single thread. The JSON string passed to the constructor is copied into the base class (`m_JsonString`) and then further copied into a `simdjson::padded_string` during `Parse()`; the caller's string does not need to remain alive after the constructor returns. All parse state is final after construction; no mutation occurs through any of the public query methods. Callers may therefore freely call the const query methods concurrently after construction without locking, as long as no other thread is constructing or destroying the object simultaneously.

---

**Notes:**

- **Error reply discards the success reply.** When `m_HasError` is true, the locally constructed `reply` object (populated by `ParseContent`) is silently discarded at the end of `Parse()` and `m_Reply` retains its default-constructed (empty) state. Callers must check `GetError()` or `HasContent()` before trusting content.

- **Usage is written directly to `m_Reply.m_Usage` during parsing** even when an error is also present, because `ParseUsage` modifies `m_Reply` directly rather than the local `reply` copy. In practice, error responses from OpenAI do not include a `"usage"` field, but if one appeared it would be stored even though the reply is subsequently discarded.

- **`GetSystemFingerprint` is a stub.** The OpenAI API includes `"system_fingerprint"` in its responses; the parser skips it (lands in the `JsonObjectParser` catch-all) and the accessor always returns `""`.

- **`CORE_ASSERT` type checks are debug-only guards.** If a provider returns a field with a different JSON type than expected (e.g. `"created"` as a string), a debug build asserts; a release build will call the wrong simdjson getter and produce undefined or garbage values rather than a clean parse error.

- **`GetFinishReason` only surfaces choice 0.** The `choices` array can contain multiple entries but `GetFinishReason` hard-codes `.front()`. Callers needing per-choice finish reasons must use `GetErrorInfo`/`HasContent` and access the underlying `Reply` struct, which is private. There is no public accessor for individual `Choice` objects.

- **`int32_t` truncation in `GetUsage`.** Token counts stored as `uint64_t` in `Reply::Usage` are cast to `int32_t` in `GetUsage`. Extremely large token counts (above ~2.1 billion, unlikely in practice) would silently truncate.


---

### 55_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI2_h

<a id="55-doc-application-json-replyparserapi2-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/55_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI2_h/docu.output.md`



## ReplyParserAPI2

**Role:** `ReplyParserAPI2` is a concrete subclass of `ReplyParser` that deserializes the OpenAI Responses API (API v2) JSON format — the format used by models such as GPT-5 — into structured C++ types. Construction immediately triggers a full parse of the supplied JSON string. The class maps the top-level `output` array (which can contain both `reasoning` and `message` items), the `usage` object, and any `error` object into internal structs, then exposes the results through the `ReplyParser` virtual interface consumed by the rest of the engine. Only output items that carry at least one non-empty `content` entry are retained; reasoning-only items are silently discarded.

**Public API:**

- `ReplyParserAPI2(std::string const& jsonString) → (constructor)` — Stores the raw JSON string in the base-class member `m_JsonString` and immediately calls `Parse()`. On return, `m_State` is either `ReplyParser::State::ReplyOk`, `ReplyParser::State::ReplyError`, or `ReplyParser::State::ParseFailure`. Callers must not reuse the instance with a different JSON string; construct a fresh object instead.

- `GetErrorInfo() const → ErrorInfo const&` — Returns a reference to the populated `ErrorInfo` struct containing the raw `message`, `type`, `code`, and `param` fields from the provider error object. The reference is valid for the lifetime of the `ReplyParserAPI2` instance. Returns a default-constructed (all-empty-string) `ErrorInfo` when no error was present.

- `GetErrorType() const → ErrorType` — Returns the strongly-typed `ErrorType` enum value derived from the `"type"` string in the error object. Possible values are `Unknown`, `InvalidRequestError`, `AuthenticationError`, `PermissionError`, `RateLimitError`, `ServerError`, and `InsufficientQuota`. Returns `ErrorType::Unknown` when no error was parsed or the type string is unrecognised.

- `HasContent() const → size_t` — Returns `m_Reply.m_Output.size()`, i.e. the count of output items that survived filtering (items with at least one non-empty `content` entry). Returns 0 on parse failure or when the provider returned an error.

- `GetContent(size_t index = 0) const → std::string` — Returns the `text` field of the first `content` element whose `type` is `"output_text"` and whose text is non-empty, within the output item at `index`. Logs `LOG_APP_ERROR` and returns an empty string if `index` is out of range or no matching content entry exists.

- `GetError() const → AiError` — Converts the internal `ErrorInfo` and `ErrorType` into the engine-wide `AiError` value type. Sets `AiError::m_Kind` to `Kind::Provider` and synthesises a canonical HTTP status code: 429 for `RateLimitError`, 401 for `AuthenticationError`, 403 for `PermissionError`, 500 for `ServerError`. `m_HttpStatus` is left at its default (zero) for all other error types. Returns a default-constructed `AiError` (no error) when `m_HasError` is false.

- `GetUsage() const → AiUsage` — Returns a copy of the token-usage counters cast from `uint64_t` to `int32_t`. No overflow guard is applied; token counts that exceed `INT32_MAX` will wrap silently.

- `GetFinishReason() const → std::string` — Returns `m_Reply.m_Status`, which corresponds to the top-level `"status"` field of the response (e.g. `"completed"`). Not derived from an individual output item's status field.

**Collaborators:**

- `ReplyParser` (`json/replyParser.h`) — Abstract base class that owns `m_JsonString`, `m_State`, and `m_HasError`. `ReplyParserAPI2` sets these members during `Parse()` and delegates virtual dispatch to its overrides.
- `simdjson` — Used for all JSON traversal via `simdjson::ondemand::parser`, `padded_string`, and `ondemand::document`. The parser is stack-allocated inside `Parse()` and does not persist after the constructor returns.
- `JsonObjectParser` (`json/jsonObjectParser.h`) — Instantiated inline for any unrecognised JSON key at all levels (top-level, output, usage, error). Acts as a diagnostic sink; its construction presumably logs the unexpected field.
- `AiError` and `AiUsage` — Engine-wide value types returned by `GetError()` and `GetUsage()` respectively; defined in `engine.h`.
- `LOG_APP_INFO`, `LOG_APP_WARN`, `LOG_APP_ERROR`, `LOG_APP_CRITICAL` — Logging macros from `core.h` used throughout parsing to trace field values and report anomalies.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ReplyParserAPI2` has no internal synchronisation and is not thread-safe. The `simdjson::ondemand::parser` and its `padded_string` are entirely local to the `Parse()` call and do not escape the constructor. The object is intended to be constructed, queried, and destroyed on the same thread. `GetErrorInfo()` returns a `const&` tied to the object's lifetime; callers must not hold that reference beyond the object's scope. Ownership is caller-managed; the class does not register itself anywhere and carries no shared state.

**Notes:**

- Output items from the `output` array are only appended to `m_Reply.m_Output` if they contain at least one non-empty `content` entry. Items of type `"reasoning"` typically have no `content` and are therefore discarded with a `LOG_APP_WARN`. Callers using `HasContent()` or `GetContent()` will never see reasoning-only items.
- When `m_HasError` is true the successfully-parsed `reply` local variable inside `Parse()` is thrown away and `m_Reply` remains default-constructed. `GetContent()` will return empty and `HasContent()` will return 0 in this state.
- `GetFinishReason()` returns the top-level `"status"` field, not the per-message `"status"` inside each output item. These can differ if, for example, one message is still `"in_progress"` while the overall response is `"completed"`.
- Usage token counts are stored as `uint64_t` internally but cast to `int32_t` in `GetUsage()` without any bounds check.
- `Parse()` allocates a second `simdjson::ondemand::document` by calling `parser.iterate(json)` a second time after already calling it once for the error-check step; the first `doc` variable is otherwise unused. This is benign but wastes one iteration.
- `ParseError()` guards `param` against null with an explicit `is_null()` check, but no such guard is applied to any other field. A null value for `message`, `type`, or `code` will trigger a simdjson type error and likely surface as a parse anomaly rather than a clean error state.


---

### 56_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI3_h

<a id="56-doc-application-json-replyparserapi3-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/56_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI3_h/docu.output.md`



## ReplyParserAPI3

**Role:** `ReplyParserAPI3` is the Google Gemini-native JSON reply parser within the `AIAssistant` namespace. It inherits from `ReplyParser` and is responsible for deserialising a raw Gemini REST API response body (the `candidates` / `usageMetadata` / `error` JSON schema) into strongly-typed C++ structs, then exposing the results through the provider-agnostic `ReplyParser` virtual interface. Parsing is triggered eagerly in the constructor; after construction the object is effectively a read-only value holder.

---

**Public API:**

- `ReplyParserAPI3(std::string const& jsonString)` — Constructor. Copies `jsonString` into the base-class `m_JsonString`, then immediately calls `Parse()`. On return, `m_State` is one of `ReplyParser::State::ReplyOk`, `ReplyParser::State::ReplyError`, or `ReplyParser::State::ParseFailure`, and all data members are fully populated. No lazy evaluation occurs.

- `GetErrorInfo() const → ErrorInfo const&` — Returns a reference to the internal `ErrorInfo` struct containing the numeric HTTP-style error `m_Code`, human-readable `m_Message`, and Google status string `m_Status`. The reference is valid for the lifetime of the `ReplyParserAPI3` instance. Only meaningful when `m_HasError` (base-class flag) is `true`; callers should check `GetError().m_Kind` or `m_State` first.

- `HasContent() const → size_t` — Returns the number of successfully parsed candidates stored in `m_Reply.m_Candidates`. A candidate is only retained if it contains at least one non-empty `parts` array; empty-part candidates are silently discarded during parsing.

- `GetContent(size_t index = 0) const → std::string` — Returns the text from the first non-empty `Part` inside `candidates[index].content.parts`. If `index` is out of range, or every part's `m_Text` is empty, logs an error and returns an empty string. Only the first non-empty part's text is returned even when multiple parts are present.

- `GetError() const → AiError` — Returns a default-constructed `AiError` (no error) when `m_HasError` is false. When `m_HasError` is true, returns an `AiError` with `m_Kind = AiError::Kind::Provider`, `m_Message` from `m_ErrorInfo.m_Message`, and `m_HttpStatus` from `m_ErrorInfo.m_Code`.

- `GetUsage() const → AiUsage` — Maps `usageMetadata` token counts to the engine-wide `AiUsage` struct: `m_PromptTokenCount` → `m_InputTokens`, `m_CandidatesTokenCount` → `m_OutputTokens`, `m_TotalTokenCount` → `m_TotalTokens`. Token counts are stored internally as `uint64_t` and cast to `int32_t` on return; overflow is silent if the provider ever returns values above `INT32_MAX`.

- `GetFinishReason() const → std::string` — Returns `m_FinishReason` from the first candidate (`front()`). Returns an empty string when there are no candidates. Common values from Gemini are `"STOP"`, `"MAX_TOKENS"`, and `"SAFETY"`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ReplyParser` (`json/replyParser.h`) — Abstract base class. Provides `m_JsonString` (the raw JSON buffer), `m_HasError` (bool flag set to `true` on error detection), and `m_State` (`ReplyParser::State` enum). `ReplyParserAPI3` writes these members directly during `Parse()`.

- `simdjson` (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) — Used exclusively through the `ondemand` API. A local `ondemand::parser` and `padded_string` are stack-allocated inside `Parse()`; the document and all derived iterators are consumed before `Parse()` returns. No simdjson state escapes the private parse methods.

- `JsonObjectParser` (`json/jsonObjectParser.h`) — Instantiated inline for every unrecognised JSON key in all four parse helpers. Its role appears to be diagnostic logging or soft-error handling of unknown fields; it is not stored and goes out of scope immediately.

- `AiError`, `AiUsage` — Engine-wide plain structs returned by `GetError()` and `GetUsage()` respectively, translating provider-specific data into the common interface consumed by higher-level engine components.

- `core.h` / `engine.h` — Included in the `.cpp`; provide `LOG_APP_INFO`, `LOG_APP_WARN`, `LOG_APP_ERROR`, and `LOG_APP_CRITICAL` macros used throughout the parse methods.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ReplyParserAPI3` objects are not thread-safe. All mutation happens exclusively in the constructor via `Parse()`; after construction the object is immutable and read-only accessor calls are safe to call from any thread as long as the object is not concurrently destroyed. The object owns all its data by value (`m_Reply`, `m_ErrorInfo`); no pointers to external storage are retained after the constructor returns. The simdjson `padded_string` and `ondemand::parser` are strictly stack-local to `Parse()` and are destroyed before the constructor exits.

---

**Notes:**

- **Double iteration over the JSON string:** `Parse()` calls `parser.iterate(json)` twice — once to check for a parse error, and then again immediately on a freshly assigned `ondemand::document`. This is redundant but not harmful because `padded_string json` is re-passed unchanged; it is worth knowing because it is slightly unexpected for readers familiar with simdjson's single-pass design.

- **Error path discards the parsed reply:** When an `"error"` key is found, `m_HasError` is set to `true` and `m_State` is set to `ReplyParser::State::ReplyError`. At the end of `Parse()`, the locally built `reply` object is discarded and `m_Reply` is left default-constructed. `GetUsage()` will therefore always return zero token counts on an error response, even if `usageMetadata` was present in the JSON.

- **ParseUsageMetadata writes directly to `m_Reply`:** Unlike `ParseCandidates`, which writes into a local `Reply& reply` argument, `ParseUsageMetadata` writes token counts directly into `m_Reply.m_UsageMetadata`. Because `m_Reply` is then only updated with `std::move(reply)` if there is no error, `usageMetadata` values are always populated on the live `m_Reply` regardless of whether the candidate data was merged — a mild structural inconsistency that callers should be aware of if querying usage on a partially-failed parse.

- **Candidate filtering:** A candidate parsed from the JSON is silently dropped (with a `LOG_APP_WARN`) if its `parts` array was absent or empty. `HasContent()` reflects only retained candidates.

- **`GetContent` returns on first non-empty part:** When multiple parts exist, only the text of the first non-empty part is returned. Subsequent parts in the same candidate are unreachable through the public API.

- **`std::cout` side effect:** Both `ParseCandidates` and `ParseError` write directly to `std::cout` (not through the logging macros) for content text and error messages. Callers running in environments where stdout is captured or suppressed should be aware of this.


---

### 57_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI4_h

<a id="57-doc-application-json-replyparserapi4-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/57_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI4_h/docu.output.md`



## ReplyParserAPI4

**Role:** `ReplyParserAPI4` is the Anthropic `/v1/messages` response parser within the `AIAssistant` namespace. It accepts a raw JSON string from an Anthropic API HTTP response, parses it once at construction time using simdjson's on-demand parser, and exposes the result through the shared `ReplyParser` interface. It handles both the success shape (`id`, `model`, `content[]`, `stop_reason`, `usage`) and the Anthropic error envelope (`{"type":"error","error":{...}}`), mapping Anthropic-specific error type strings to canonical HTTP status codes stored in an `AiError` value object.

---

**Public API:**

- `ReplyParserAPI4(std::string const& jsonString)` — Constructor. Takes the full JSON response body as a `std::string`. Immediately calls `Parse()`, which populates `m_Reply`, `m_ErrorInfo`, `m_HasError`, and `m_State` before the constructor returns. There is no deferred or lazy parsing; the object is fully resolved after construction.

- `GetErrorInfo() const → ErrorInfo const&` — Returns the raw Anthropic error envelope fields (`m_Type` and `m_Message`) as extracted from the JSON. Only meaningful when `m_HasError` is true. Returns a default-constructed `ErrorInfo` (empty strings) on success.

- `HasContent() const → size_t` — Returns the count of `ContentBlock` entries in `m_Reply.m_Content` whose `m_Type` is `"text"` and whose `m_Text` is non-empty. Non-text blocks (e.g. `"tool_use"`) and empty text blocks are excluded from the count.

- `GetContent(size_t index = 0) const → std::string` — Returns the text of the `index`-th non-empty text block, using the same filtering logic as `HasContent()`. If `index` is out of range, logs an `APP_ERROR` and returns an empty string. Does not throw.

- `GetError() const → AiError` — Converts `m_ErrorInfo` into a provider-agnostic `AiError`. Sets `AiError::Kind::Provider` and maps known Anthropic error type strings to HTTP status codes: `rate_limit_error` → 429, `authentication_error` → 401, `permission_error` → 403, `overloaded_error` / `api_error` → 503. Unrecognised error types leave `m_HttpStatus` at its default. Returns a default `AiError` (no error) when `m_HasError` is false.

- `GetUsage() const → AiUsage` — Returns an `AiUsage` struct with `m_InputTokens`, `m_OutputTokens`, and `m_TotalTokens` (computed as their sum). Token counts are extracted as `uint64_t` from the JSON and narrowed to `int32_t` via `static_cast`; values exceeding `INT32_MAX` will silently overflow.

- `GetFinishReason() const → std::string` — Returns `m_Reply.m_StopReason` verbatim. Possible Anthropic values: `"end_turn"`, `"max_tokens"`, `"stop_sequence"`, `"tool_use"`. Returns an empty string if the field was absent or parsing failed.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ReplyParser` (`json/replyParser.h`) — Abstract base class that owns `m_JsonString`, `m_HasError`, and `m_State`. `ReplyParserAPI4` reads and writes all three members directly. The full interface contract (`HasContent`, `GetContent`, `GetError`, `GetUsage`, `GetFinishReason`) is defined there.
- `simdjson` — On-demand parser used inside `Parse()`. A `simdjson::ondemand::parser` and `simdjson::padded_string` are constructed as local variables on the stack; no simdjson state outlives `Parse()`.
- `core.h` / `engine.h` — Pulled in by the `.cpp`. `LOG_APP_ERROR` is used for out-of-range index access and simdjson iteration failures; these macros are assumed to come from `core.h` or `engine.h`.
- `AiError`, `AiUsage` — Value types (not defined in this file) that form the currency between the parser and the rest of the engine. Both are returned by value.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ReplyParserAPI4` objects are not thread-safe. All state is set during construction by `Parse()` and is subsequently read-only, so concurrent reads after construction are safe provided no thread is still constructing the object. The object owns `m_Reply` and `m_ErrorInfo` by value; there are no heap-allocated members beyond the `std::string` and `std::vector` fields they contain. Callers own the object and are responsible for its lifetime; no internal back-references or callbacks exist.

---

**Notes:**

- **simdjson single-pass workaround:** Anthropic's JSON top-level `"type"` field is read first to detect the error envelope. Because simdjson's on-demand parser is strictly single-pass, the entire JSON string must be re-parsed from scratch after that check. `Parse()` explicitly constructs a new `ondemand::parser` and a second `padded_string` to reset the cursor. This is an intentional, documented workaround in the code comments.
- **`HasContent` / `GetContent` filtering is not index-stable relative to `m_Reply.m_Content`:** The `index` argument to `GetContent` counts only non-empty text blocks, skipping tool-use and other block types. The raw `m_Reply.m_Content` vector retains all block types, including `"tool_use"`, but those are never surfaced through the `ReplyParser` interface.
- **Token count narrowing:** `m_Usage.m_InputTokens` and `m_OutputTokens` are stored as `uint64_t` but cast to `int32_t` in `GetUsage()`. For any response with more than ~2.1 billion tokens this will produce a wrong (negative) value silently.
- **Parse failure handling:** A simdjson iteration error or a non-object root sets `m_State = ReplyParser::State::ParseFailure` and `m_HasError = true`. In the simdjson error case, `m_ErrorInfo` is also populated with `m_Type = "parse_failure"` and the simdjson error message string, making it inspectable via `GetErrorInfo()`.
- **`GetContent` out-of-range:** Returns an empty `std::string` and logs at error level. Callers should gate access with `HasContent()` to avoid triggering the log noise.

### 58_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI5_h

<a id="58-doc-application-json-replyparserapi5-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/58_doc_application_json_replyParserAPI5_h/docu.output.md`



## ReplyParserAPI5 (json/replyParserAPI5.h + json/replyParserAPI5.cpp)

**Role:** `ReplyParserAPI5` is the AWS Bedrock response parser in the `AIAssistant` namespace. Because Bedrock passes through each model family's native JSON body without wrapping it in a Bedrock-specific envelope, this class must first identify which model family produced the response and then delegate all parsing work to the appropriate concrete parser. On construction it runs a structural key sniff via the file-local `DetectFamily` function: presence of `"content"` routes to `ReplyParserAPI4` (Anthropic Claude on Bedrock), `"generation"` routes to the file-local `LlamaBedrockReply`, and `"results"` routes to the file-local `TitanBedrockReply`. Every public method is a thin forward to the chosen delegate stored in `m_Delegate`.

**Public API:**

- `ReplyParserAPI5(std::string const& jsonString)` — Constructs the parser and immediately sniffs the JSON shape using `DetectFamily`, which performs a full simdjson `ondemand` parse of `jsonString` to check for discriminating top-level keys. Allocates the matching delegate via `std::make_unique`. If the shape is unrecognised, sets `m_HasError = true` and `m_State = State::ParseFailure` on the base class; `m_Delegate` is left null. No logging is performed here; error propagation is deferred to callers (see `GetError`).

- `HasContent() const → size_t` — Returns 0 if `m_Delegate` is null, otherwise forwards to the delegate. Delegates return 1 when a non-empty generation/content string was parsed, 0 otherwise.

- `GetContent(size_t index = 0) const → std::string` — Returns the generated text at `index`. Only a single result is ever produced by these parsers (all delegates return a single string regardless of `index`), so callers should pass 0 or omit the argument. Returns an empty string if `m_Delegate` is null.

- `GetError() const → AiError` — If `m_Delegate` is null (unrecognised shape), synthesises an `AiError` with `Kind::Parse` and the message `"Bedrock reply: unrecognized response shape"`. Otherwise forwards to the delegate's `GetError`. This is the only place the unrecognised-shape error message is materialised; it surfaces to `AiRequestPool::OnRequestFailed` for structured logging with run/workflow/task context.

- `GetUsage() const → AiUsage` — Forwards to the delegate to retrieve input/output/total token counts. Returns a zero-initialised `AiUsage` if `m_Delegate` is null. Token field mapping per family: Llama uses `prompt_token_count` / `generation_token_count`; Titan uses `inputTextTokenCount` / `tokenCount` (inside the first `results` element); Anthropic usage comes from `ReplyParserAPI4`'s own logic.

- `GetFinishReason() const → std::string` — Forwards stop/completion reason to the delegate. Llama returns the `stop_reason` string; Titan returns `completionReason` from the first results entry; Anthropic delegates to `ReplyParserAPI4`. Returns empty string if `m_Delegate` is null.

**Collaborators:**

- `ReplyParser` (`json/replyParser.h`) — Abstract base class providing the `m_JsonString`, `m_HasError`, `m_State`, and the `State` enum (`ParseFailure`, `ReplyOk`, `ReplyError`). Both `ReplyParserAPI5` and the two file-local concrete classes inherit from it.
- `ReplyParserAPI4` (`json/replyParserAPI4.h`) — Reused directly for Anthropic-on-Bedrock responses. `ReplyParserAPI5` does not subclass it; it owns it behind `m_Delegate`.
- `LlamaBedrockReply` (file-local, anonymous namespace) — Handles `{"generation":..., "stop_reason":..., "prompt_token_count":..., "generation_token_count":...}`. Sets `m_HasError` and `m_State = ReplyError` when `generation` is absent or empty.
- `TitanBedrockReply` (file-local, anonymous namespace) — Handles `{"results":[{"outputText":..., "completionReason":..., "tokenCount":...}], "inputTextTokenCount":...}`. Only the first element of `results` is consumed; this matches Bedrock's non-batch invocation behaviour.
- `simdjson` (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) — Used for all JSON parsing via the `ondemand` API. `DetectFamily` creates its own short-lived `ondemand::parser`; each concrete delegate also creates its own.
- `engine.h` — Included in the `.cpp`; provides `AiError`, `AiUsage`, and related engine-level types consumed by the parser interface.
- `AiRequestPool::OnRequestFailed` — Upstream consumer that attaches run/workflow/task context to errors returned by `GetError()`. Not directly called here but named in a source comment as the structured-logging sink.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ReplyParserAPI5` is a value-like parse result object with no shared mutable state. It is constructed synchronously, fully populated during construction, and then read-only. `m_Delegate` is a `std::unique_ptr` owned exclusively by the `ReplyParserAPI5` instance; no sharing occurs. There are no locks and no async operations. Callers may read any public method from any thread after construction completes, provided they do not share a single instance across threads with concurrent mutation (which the class never performs anyway). Lifetime is straightforward: destroy the `ReplyParserAPI5` and its delegate is destroyed with it.

**Notes:**

- `DetectFamily` parses `jsonString` a second time independently of the delegate constructor, meaning `jsonString` is parsed twice for every Bedrock response. This is a known cost of the sniff-then-delegate design.
- The detection heuristic relies purely on the presence of top-level JSON keys (`content`, `generation`, `results`) and has no model-ID awareness. A future Bedrock model family that happens to use one of those keys would be misclassified silently.
- `TitanBedrockReply` unconditionally breaks after the first element of `results`. This matches single-invocation Bedrock behaviour but would silently drop additional results if the response were ever produced by a batch path.
- Token counts are cast from `int64_t` to `uint64_t` and then to `int32_t` inside `GetUsage`. Extremely large token counts could wrap; this mirrors the same pattern used elsewhere in the codebase.
- When `m_Delegate` is null, `HasContent()` returns `size_t(0)`, `GetContent()` returns `""`, `GetUsage()` returns zero-initialised `AiUsage`, and `GetFinishReason()` returns `""`. Only `GetError()` produces a non-default value, making it the required call for diagnosing unknown-family failures.
- No logging is emitted at the parser level by design; the comment in the constructor explicitly defers diagnostic output to `AiRequestPool::OnRequestFailed` so that run/workflow/task IDs can be attached.


---

### 59_doc_application_json_requestBuilder_h

<a id="59-doc-application-json-requestbuilder-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/59_doc_application_json_requestBuilder_h/docu.output.md`



## IRequestBuilder / requestBuilder

**Role:** This file defines the `IRequestBuilder` polymorphic interface and its six concrete implementations, one per AI provider wire protocol supported by j9t. Its sole job is to translate a provider-agnostic `AiInvocation` envelope plus a model string into three provider-specific artefacts: a JSON request body, a fully resolved endpoint URL, and an auth style token that tells `CurlWrapper` how to sign or authenticate the outgoing HTTP request. The factory method `IRequestBuilder::Create` selects the correct concrete class from a `ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType` enum value, keeping all protocol knowledge localised here and invisible to callers.

**Public API:**

- `BuildBody(AiInvocation const& envelope, std::string const& model) → std::string` — Serialises the invocation into a provider-specific JSON string. Reads `envelope.m_Messages` (concatenating all message content with newline separators), `envelope.m_Settings.m_Temperature`, `envelope.m_Settings.m_Seed` (API1 only), and `envelope.m_Settings.m_MaxTokens` (API4, API5, and Bedrock sub-builders). All user-supplied text is passed through `JsonHelper::SanitizeForJson` before embedding. For API5 (Bedrock), the model string prefix (`anthropic.`, `meta.llama`, `amazon.titan`, `amazon.nova`) selects the sub-builder; an unrecognised prefix logs an error via `LOG_APP_ERROR` and falls back to the Anthropic body shape. No I/O, no state mutation.

- `ResolveUrl(std::string const& baseUrl, std::string const& model) → std::string` — Computes the final endpoint URL. API1, API2, API4, and API6 return `baseUrl` unchanged (the caller is expected to supply the complete URL, including any query parameters such as Azure's `api-version`). API3 (Google) appends `/models/{model}:generateContent`. API5 (Bedrock) strips a trailing slash then appends `/model/{model}/invoke`. No network I/O.

- `GetAuthStyle() → CurlWrapper::AuthStyle` — Returns the enum value that `CurlWrapper` uses to attach credentials. Concrete mappings: API1 → `Bearer`; API2 → `Bearer`; API3 → `XGoogApiKey`; API4 → `AnthropicXApiKey`; API5 → `AwsSigV4`; API6 → `AzureApiKey`.

- `GetExtraHeaders() → std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>` — Default implementation returns an empty map. No concrete class in this file overrides it; the hook exists for future providers that need custom HTTP headers beyond the auth header.

- `SupportsNativeJsonSchema() → bool` — Indicates whether the provider's API natively understands JSON Schema constraints in the request body. API1, API2, API3 return `true`; API4 and API5 return `false`.

- `SupportsForcedToolShim() → bool` — Indicates whether the caller may inject a synthetic tool-use shim to coerce structured output from providers that lack native JSON Schema support. API1, API2, API4, API5 return `true`; API3 returns `false`.

- `static Create(ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType interfaceType) → std::unique_ptr<IRequestBuilder>` — Factory. Instantiates and returns the matching concrete builder. Returns `nullptr` and logs `LOG_APP_ERROR` for any unrecognised enum value. Caller owns the returned object.

**Collaborators:**

- `AiInvocation` (`workflow/aiInvocation.h`) — Source of all request parameters: message list, temperature, seed, max tokens. `BuildBody` reads these fields directly; it does not modify them.
- `JsonHelper` (`json/jsonHelper.h`) — Used exclusively for `SanitizeForJson`, which escapes user text for safe embedding in raw JSON string literals. Called by every `BuildBody` implementation and the three Bedrock free functions.
- `CurlWrapper::AuthStyle` (`curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`) — The enum returned by `GetAuthStyle`; consumed by `CurlWrapper` (or a layer above it) to decide which HTTP credential header or signing scheme to apply.
- `ConfigParser::EngineConfig::InterfaceType` (`json/configParser.h`) — The discriminant for `Create`. The enum values API1–API6 are the only coupling between configuration and this file.
- `engine.h` — Included in the `.cpp` for `LOG_APP_ERROR`; no class from it is used directly.

**Threading / lifetime:**

All six concrete builder classes are stateless (no member variables). `BuildBody` and `ResolveUrl` are `const` and perform no I/O or synchronisation, making every method safe to call concurrently from multiple threads without locking. `IRequestBuilder::Create` returns a `std::unique_ptr`; ownership transfers entirely to the caller and the object can be destroyed on any thread. There is no shared mutable state between instances.

**Notes:**

- `RequestBuilderAPI6` (Azure OpenAI) inherits from `RequestBuilderAPI1` rather than `IRequestBuilder` directly. It reuses `BuildBody` and `ResolveUrl` verbatim and overrides only `GetAuthStyle` to return `AzureApiKey`. The base class is therefore deliberately not marked `final`.
- API2's body uses an `"input"` key with `"store": false` rather than the `"messages"` array used by API1. This matches the OpenAI Responses API shape, not the Chat Completions shape.
- API4 (Anthropic direct) and all Bedrock sub-builders require `max_tokens` in the body; they default to 4096 (Anthropic) or 2048 (Llama/Titan/Nova) when `m_MaxTokens` is absent. API1 and API2 omit `max_tokens` entirely.
- Temperature is omitted from the JSON body when it is exactly `0.0` (floating-point equality). A caller that genuinely wants to pass temperature 0 to a provider must set it to a small epsilon or accept that the field will be absent.
- The Bedrock URL construction in `ResolveUrl` strips exactly one trailing slash; multiple trailing slashes would produce a malformed URL.
- `GetExtraHeaders` is not overridden by any current builder. Callers that check capability flags (`SupportsNativeJsonSchema`, `SupportsForcedToolShim`) before constructing requests must not assume these flags are consistent across builder instances obtained from different `Create` calls.


---

### 60_doc_application_json_schemaValidator_h

<a id="60-doc-application-json-schemavalidator-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/60_doc_application_json_schemaValidator_h/docu.output.md`



## SchemaValidator (`json/schemaValidator.h` + `json/schemaValidator.cpp`)

**Role:** `SchemaValidator` is a self-contained JSON Schema Draft 2020-12 *subset* validator used by the AI workflow engine to verify that documents (typically AI model tool-call responses or structured outputs) conform to a declared schema before the engine acts on them. It is constructed once per schema, parses and pre-validates the schema at construction time, and then exposes a reentrant `Validate` call for repeated document checking. Errors are surfaced as structured `ValidationError` records with JSON-pointer paths, and a dedicated formatting helper converts those records into a human/model-readable string suitable for feeding back into an AI prompt.

---

**Public API:**

- `SchemaValidator(std::string schemaJson)` — Constructor. Parses `schemaJson` using simdjson's DOM parser, recursively walks every sub-schema to detect unsupported keywords (see Notes), and pre-builds the `$defs` lookup table. If anything fails, `m_IsLoaded` is set to `false` and `m_LoadError` holds the reason. No exceptions are thrown; callers must check `IsLoaded()` before using the object.

- `IsLoaded() const → bool` — Returns `true` only if the schema was parsed successfully and passed the supported-keyword check. This flag is mirrored from `Impl` into the outer class at construction so it can be read without going through the pImpl.

- `LoadError() const → std::string const&` — Returns the diagnostic string produced during construction if `IsLoaded()` is `false`; empty string on success.

- `Validate(std::string const& documentJson) const → ValidationResult` — Parses `documentJson` with a fresh per-call `simdjson::dom::parser`, then recursively validates the resulting DOM against the pre-loaded schema. Returns a `ValidationResult` with `m_Ok = true` and an empty `m_Errors` vector on success, or `m_Ok = false` with one or more `ValidationError` entries (each carrying a JSON-pointer path and a message) on failure. If the validator was never successfully loaded, returns an immediate failure without attempting to parse the document. This method is `const` and allocates a local parser per call, making it safe to call from multiple threads simultaneously (see Threading).

- `static FormatErrorsForModel(std::vector<ValidationError> const& errors) → std::string` — Converts a `ValidationError` list into a newline-delimited, bullet-prefixed string of the form `  - at '<path>': <message>`. Paths that are empty are normalised to `/`. Intended to be injected directly into an AI model's correction prompt.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **simdjson (`simdjson/simdjson.h`)** — All JSON parsing (both schema and document) is done via `simdjson::dom::parser` and `simdjson::dom::element`. The schema parser and its root element live inside `Impl` for the full lifetime of the validator; each `Validate` call constructs its own local `dom::parser` for the document so the two parses do not share state.
- **`engine.h`** — Pulled in by the `.cpp` for the `LOG_APP_INFO` macro, used only to log a warning when an unknown-but-not-rejected schema keyword is encountered. No other engine functionality is used.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`SchemaValidator` is non-copyable (copy constructor and copy-assignment are explicitly deleted). Move semantics are not declared, so the object should be heap-allocated and shared via `std::shared_ptr` or `std::unique_ptr` if it needs to outlive its creating scope.

`Validate` is `const` and creates its document parser on the stack, so **multiple threads may call `Validate` concurrently on the same instance without external locking**, provided construction has already completed. The `Impl` pImpl is written only during construction; after that, `Impl` state is read-only during validation. `FormatErrorsForModel` is static and stateless; it is trivially thread-safe.

---

**Notes:**

- **Rejected keywords cause a hard load failure, not a silent skip.** Any schema containing `allOf`, `not`, `format`, `if`/`then`/`else`, `contains`, `uniqueItems`, `minItems`, `maxItems`, `multipleOf`, `const`, `patternProperties`, `propertyNames`, `readOnly`, or `writeOnly` will produce a non-empty `LoadError()` and `IsLoaded() == false`. Unknown keywords that are neither in the supported nor the rejected set are logged via `LOG_APP_INFO` and silently ignored — they do not cause a load failure.
- **`$ref` resolution is limited to `#/$defs/<name>` only.** Remote URIs, relative references, and any other `$ref` form will produce a runtime `ValidationError` with message `"unresolved $ref: ..."` when encountered during `Validate`.
- **`type` can be a string or an array of strings** in the schema. Both forms are handled; the validator stops checking further type alternatives as soon as a match is found.
- **`integer` type semantics:** a JSON `double` value is accepted as `integer` if and only if `floor(value) == value && isfinite(value)`. Raw `int64_t`/`uint64_t` DOM elements are always considered integers.
- **`number` is a superset of `integer`** in the type checker: a value that resolves as `integer` also satisfies a `"type": "number"` constraint.
- **`enum` comparison is string-based:** both the candidate and each enum value are serialised to strings via `operator<<` on `simdjson::dom::element` and compared lexicographically. This means `1` and `1.0` may or may not compare equal depending on how simdjson serialises them; schemas that mix numeric representations in `enum` arrays should be tested carefully.
- **`pattern` uses `std::regex` (ECMAScript dialect) not ECMA-262/JSON Schema's required ECMA 262 regexp engine.** An invalid regex in the schema produces a `ValidationError` at validation time rather than a load-time failure, because keyword checking at load time does not attempt to compile pattern strings.
- **`anyOf` failure reporting appends the errors from the *last* sub-schema only**, not all branches. Callers formatting errors for debugging or model feedback should be aware they are seeing a partial picture.
- **`additionalProperties` accepts `false` (deny all extras), `true` / absent (allow all extras), or a schema object (validate extras against that schema).** The check iterates actual document fields and matches them against `properties` keys; any unmatched field is subject to the `additionalProperties` rule.
- The schema's raw JSON string is kept alive in `m_SchemaJson` inside `Impl` because `simdjson::dom::parser` requires the backing buffer to remain valid for the lifetime of all `dom::element` handles derived from it.


---

### 61_doc_application_log_statusRenderer_h

<a id="61-doc-application-log-statusrenderer-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/61_doc_application_log_statusRenderer_h/docu.output.md`



## StatusRenderer

**Role:** `StatusRenderer` is a terminal UI component that renders a fixed two-row status bar for the j9t workflow engine. It aggregates runtime state drawn from multiple engine subsystems — key management, in-flight AI requests, active workflow runs, MCP connectivity, cloud connector circuit-breaker health, cumulative run counters, and Python engine availability — and formats them into printable strings suitable for a TUI display. It mirrors the same signals that the web dashboard consumes from `/api/status` and `/api/settings/keys/status`, so both views reflect identical system state. The class itself owns no engine state; it relies entirely on two injected provider callbacks to pull a snapshot on each render.

**Public API:**

- `SetRuntimeSnapshotProvider(RuntimeSnapshotProvider provider) → void` — Installs the callback that returns a `RuntimeSnapshot` struct. Called once at startup. The provider is invoked on every `BuildStatusLines` call, so it must be cheap (a snapshot copy, not a live query). Takes the mutex internally while storing the provider.

- `SetLastRunsProvider(LastRunsProvider provider) → void` — Installs the callback that accepts a `maxCount` and returns a `std::vector<LastRunSummary>`. The count passed at call time is always 3. Same locking and performance constraints as above.

- `Start() → void` — No-op in the current implementation. Present for lifecycle symmetry with other renderer types; callers should still call it for forward-compatibility.

- `Stop() → void` — No-op in the current implementation. Same rationale as `Start()`.

- `BuildStatusLines(std::vector<std::string>& outLines, int maxColumns) → void` — The primary rendering method. Clears `outLines` and appends exactly two UTF-8 strings. Row 1 contains edition label, keys status LED, AI-in-flight count with braille spinner, active workflow run count, MCP connection state, cloud circuit-breaker state, and cumulative succeeded/failed counters. Row 2 contains up to three most-recent run summaries (glyph + display ID + optional `[adhoc]` tag + relative timestamp) and, when keys are locked or no providers are loaded, appends an unlock hint because the TUI has no unlock affordance of its own. Both rows are hard-truncated to `maxColumns` columns via `SafeTruncateUtf8`. Providers are copied out under the mutex before being called, so the lock is not held during provider invocation.

- `GetRowCount() → size_t` — Always returns 2. Callers should use this rather than hard-coding the count so layout code stays correct if the row count changes.

**Collaborators:**

- `AiRequestPool` — The `RuntimeSnapshot::aiInflight` field is expected to be populated by calling `AiRequestPool::GetDirectDispatchInflight()` inside the registered snapshot provider.
- `WorkflowRuntimeManager` — `activeRuns` maps to `WorkflowRuntimeManager::GetActiveRunsSnapshot().size()`; `totalCompleted` and `totalFailed` come from `WorkflowRuntimeManager::GetRunCounters`.
- `WebServer` — `mcpConnected` reflects `WebServer::IsMcpConnected()`.
- `PythonEnginePool` — `pythonRunning` reflects pool health; when false, Row 1 appends a `!! Python offline` warning.
- Key manager (unnamed in this file) — `keysStatus` is a string token (`ok`, `no_password`, `wrong_password`, `no_keys_file`) matching the `/api/settings/keys/status` contract. `hasProviders` indicates whether the key manager has at least one provider loaded.
- Cloud connector subsystem — `cloudHealthy`, `cloudRecovering`, and `cloudOpen` reflect circuit-breaker state across cloud integrations; only the highest-severity bucket is shown on Row 1.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`m_Mutex` protects `m_SnapshotProvider`, `m_LastRunsProvider`, `m_SpinnerIndex`, and `m_LastSpinnerUpdate`. `BuildStatusLines` acquires the mutex twice: once briefly to copy both providers out, and once briefly to advance the spinner state. Provider callbacks are executed outside the lock, so they may themselves acquire locks in the engine subsystems without risk of deadlock with `StatusRenderer`. `SetRuntimeSnapshotProvider` and `SetLastRunsProvider` may be called from any thread. `BuildStatusLines` is expected to be called from the TUI render thread; calling it concurrently from multiple threads is safe but not meaningful. `StatusRenderer` instances are expected to outlive any thread that calls `BuildStatusLines`. There is no internal thread; the class is purely driven by the caller's render loop.

**Notes:**

- The braille spinner (`⣾…⣷` then `⠁…⠈`, 16 frames) advances at most once per 100 ms regardless of how frequently `BuildStatusLines` is called. It is stationary when `aiInflight == 0`.
- `SafeTruncateUtf8` counts codepoints, not display columns. Multi-column glyphs (CJK wide characters, certain emoji) will cause the truncated line to exceed `maxColumns` terminal columns visually even though the byte truncation is correct. This is a known limitation.
- `FormatRelative` uses `timegm` on POSIX and `_mkgmtime` on Windows to parse ISO 8601 UTC timestamps. If the timestamp is malformed or `timegm` returns `-1`, the relative time field is silently omitted rather than showing an error.
- The keys-blocked hint on Row 2 fires when `keysStatus != "ok"` **or** `hasProviders == false`. This means the hint can appear even when keys are unlocked if no providers have been configured yet, which is intentional — `ai_call` tasks will be skipped in either condition.
- The edition label (`j9t Studio edition` vs `j9t Engine edition`) is controlled by the compile-time define `J9T_STUDIO`, matching the `edition` field in `/api/status`, so TUI and web dashboard labels are always consistent.
- `Start()` and `Stop()` are deliberately empty; do not rely on them for any resource acquisition or release.


---

### 62_doc_application_python_pythonEngine_h

<a id="62-doc-application-python-pythonengine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/62_doc_application_python_pythonEngine_h/docu.output.md`



## PythonEngine (`pythonEngine.h` / `pythonEngine.cpp`)

**Role:** `PythonEngine` is a self-contained wrapper around a single CPython sub-interpreter. It owns one dedicated `std::thread` that serialises all Python execution for that interpreter, acquires and releases the sub-interpreter's GIL exclusively on that thread, and exposes two categories of work to the rest of the engine: lifecycle hook dispatch (`OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, `OnEvent`, `OnShutdown`) and synchronous workflow task execution. Because each instance runs its own sub-interpreter, multiple `PythonEngine` instances can execute Python code truly in parallel — there is no shared GIL contention between them. The class is created and initialised by `PythonEnginePool`; callers outside that pool should treat it as an opaque execution unit reached through `ExecuteWorkflowTask`.

---

**Public API:**

- `PythonEngine(size_t engineIndex) → void` — Stores the index used in log messages and load-balancing metrics. No Python state is touched here.

- `SetupSubInterpreter(const string& scriptDir, const string& moduleName, bool loadHooks) → bool` — Must be called on the thread that already holds this sub-interpreter's GIL (the pool's setup thread). Installs a `ctypes`-based stdout/stderr redirect that forwards Python output to `JarvisRedirectPython`; adds `scriptDir` and its parent directory to `sys.path`; if `loadHooks` is true, imports `moduleName` and resolves `OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, `OnEvent`, and `OnShutdown` callables, storing borrowed-then-incref'd `PyObject*` handles for each. Returns `false` and logs on any Python-level failure.
