- `SetInterpreterState(PyInterpreterState* interpState) → void` — Transfers the `PyInterpreterState*` created by `PythonEnginePool` into the engine so `WorkerLoop` can call `PyThreadState_New`. Must be called before `StartWorkerThread`.

- `StartWorkerThread() → void` — Spawns the internal `std::thread`. Sets `m_Running = true`. Must be called after `SetInterpreterState` and after `SetupSubInterpreter` has returned.

- `SignalStop() → void` — Enqueues a `Shutdown` task (if `OnShutdown` was resolved) so Python receives a clean callback, then sets `m_StopRequested = true` and notifies the condition variable. Non-blocking; pair with `WaitStop`.

- `WaitStop() → void` — Joins the worker thread. Safe to call only after `SignalStop`. Sets `m_Running = false` on return.

- `OnStart() → void` — Enqueues a `PythonTask::Type::OnStart` item. No-op if `m_Running` is false. The hook is only called if `m_OnStartFunc` was resolved during setup.

- `OnUpdate() → void` — Enqueues a `PythonTask::Type::OnUpdate` item. Intended for periodic tick calls; the queue is unbounded, so high-frequency callers should check `GetQueueDepth()` first.

- `OnEvent(shared_ptr<Event> eventPtr) → void` — Enqueues an `OnEvent` task carrying the event pointer. `BuildEventDict` converts the `Event` to a Python dict; `FileSystemEvent` gets an extra `"path"` key. Other event subclasses receive only the `"type"` key.

- `ExecuteWorkflowTask(const TaskDef& taskDef, const string& workingDir, const unordered_map<string,string>& inputs, const unordered_map<string,string>& context, unordered_map<string,string>& outputsOut, string& errorMessage, string& capturedStdout, string& capturedStderr) → bool` — The primary integration point for the workflow engine. Constructs a `WorkflowTaskRequest`, enqueues it, then **blocks the calling thread** on `std::future<bool>::get()` until the worker finishes. On success, `outputsOut` receives the string-coerced key/value pairs from the Python dict returned by the task function. `capturedStdout` and `capturedStderr` always contain whatever the task printed, even on failure. Returns `false` and populates `errorMessage` if the engine is stopped, params JSON is missing/malformed, the module or function cannot be resolved, or Python raises an exception.

- `IsRunning() → bool` — Read of `m_Running`; no lock; treat as advisory.

- `GetQueueDepth() → size_t` — Lock-guarded read of `m_TaskQueue.size()`. Used by `PythonEnginePool` for least-loaded dispatch.

- `GetEngineIndex() → size_t` — Returns the index assigned at construction.

- `GetTasksCompleted() → size_t` — Atomically-safe read (written only on the worker thread; read externally for metrics). Returns cumulative `WorkflowTask` completions.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `PythonEnginePool` — Creates, owns, and sets up all `PythonEngine` instances. Calls `SetupSubInterpreter` while holding the correct GIL, then calls `SetInterpreterState` and `StartWorkerThread`. Performs load-balanced dispatch via `GetQueueDepth`.
- `TaskDef` (from `workflow/workflowTypes.h`) — Provides `m_Id` (used in log messages and the `_task_id` context key) and `m_ParamsJson` (JSON string that must contain `"module"` and `"function"` string fields).
- `Event` / `FileSystemEvent` / `PythonCrashedEvent` (from `event/`) — `Event` is the base type dispatched via `OnEvent`. `FileSystemEvent` is down-cast in `BuildEventDict` to add a path field. `PythonCrashedEvent` is pushed to the global core event queue by the C-exported `JarvisPyStatus` when Python-side code calls the status hook.
- `AIAssistant::Core::g_Core` — Accessed directly in `JarvisPyStatus` to push a `PythonCrashedEvent`. This is a global pointer; `JarvisPyStatus` is callable from Python via `ctypes` without going through the normal C++ call stack.
- `JarvisRedirectPython` / `JarvisPyStatus` — C-exported symbols (dllexport on Windows) that Python installs via `ctypes` during `SetupSubInterpreter`. They route Python stdout/stderr through `std::cout` (which feeds `TerminalLogStreamBuf`) and signal fatal Python errors respectively.
- `log/log.h` — Structured logging via `LOG_APP_INFO` / `LOG_APP_ERROR` macros throughout.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `PythonEngine` is owned exclusively by `PythonEnginePool`. Callers must not delete or move it while the worker thread is live.
- `SetupSubInterpreter` must be called on the **main/pool thread** while that sub-interpreter's GIL is held. It must not be called after `StartWorkerThread`.
- All Python API calls (`PyObject_Call`, `PyImport_ImportModule`, etc.) happen exclusively on the worker thread inside `WorkerLoop`, which restores the GIL via `PyEval_RestoreThread` before each task and saves it via `PyEval_SaveThread` after. No Python API is called on any other thread once the worker is running.
- `ExecuteWorkflowTask` blocks its caller until the worker signals the `std::promise`. If the engine is stopped mid-wait, the drain loop in `WorkerLoop` resolves all outstanding promises with `false` so callers are never deadlocked.
- `m_Running` is written on the main thread (in `StartWorkerThread` and `WaitStop`) and read on arbitrary caller threads. There is no lock around it; callers should treat it as a best-effort advisory check.
- `m_StopRequested` is set under `m_QueueMutex` in `SignalStop` and read under the same mutex in `WorkerLoop`. The worker checks it both at the top of each iteration and after completing each task to avoid stalling on a long event backlog during shutdown.

---

**Notes:**

- **Hot-reload on every `WorkflowTask`:** Before importing the task module, `ExecuteWorkflowTaskOnWorker` unconditionally deletes the module (and its parent package for dotted names like `scripts.mymod`) from `sys.modules`. This means the `.py` file is re-read from disk on every workflow task invocation. This is intentional for development-time hot-reload but has measurable overhead in production.
- **`context` dict is always injected.** The `taskWantsContextInput` constant is hardcoded to `true`. The fallback heuristic that retried without a context dict when the first call raised an exception mentioning `"context"` and `"missing"/"required"` is still present in the code path guarded by `if (resultObject == nullptr && !taskWantsContextInput)`, but it is permanently unreachable. The reserved keys `_task_id` and `_task_working_directory` are always present in the context dict.
- **stdout/stderr capture is per-task, not per-interpreter.** Capture uses `PyRun_SimpleString` against `__main__`, which means the tee objects live in the `__main__` namespace. If a task itself spawns Python threads that write to stdout while the tee is active, their output is also captured. The teardown `PyRun_SimpleString` deletes the tee objects by name; if `PyRun_SimpleString` fails silently, the tee leaks into the next task.
- **Shutdown drain guarantees no future-deadlock.** Any `WorkflowTask` requests still in the queue when the worker exits the main loop have their promises fulfilled with `false` and `m_ErrorMessage = "PythonEngine shutting down"`. Callers blocking on `resultFuture.get()` will unblock immediately.
- **Windows Debug build workaround:** The `#undef _DEBUG` / `#define _DEBUG` bracket around `<Python.h>` forces linkage against `python314.lib` (release) in debug builds because `python314_d.lib` is typically absent in standard Python distributions.
- **`PyThreadState_DeleteCurrent` is used instead of `Py_EndInterpreter`** on worker thread exit. The sub-interpreter's main-thread state (created by `PythonEnginePool`) is still alive at that point, and `Py_EndInterpreter` requires it to be the sole remaining state. Sub-interpreter teardown is deferred to `Py_Finalize` at process exit.
- **`BuildEventDict` performs a `dynamic_cast`** to detect `FileSystemEvent`. Any other `Event` subclass silently receives only the `"type"` key; there is no registry of additional fields. Adding a new event type with Python-visible data requires adding another `dynamic_cast` branch here.


---

### 63_doc_application_python_pythonEnginePool_h

<a id="63-doc-application-python-pythonenginepool-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/63_doc_application_python_pythonEnginePool_h/docu.output.md`



## PythonEnginePool

**Role:** `PythonEnginePool` manages a fixed-size pool of `PythonEngine` instances, each backed by a CPython sub-interpreter, and serves as the single entry point for executing embedded Python within the j9t workflow engine. It owns the global CPython interpreter lifetime (`Py_Initialize` / `Py_Finalize` equivalent), creates sub-interpreters via `Py_NewInterpreterFromConfig` (Python 3.12+) or the legacy `Py_NewInterpreter` (Python < 3.12, clamped to one engine), and distributes incoming workflow tasks across engines using a minimum-queue-depth load balancing strategy. Hook callbacks (`OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, `OnEvent`) are always routed exclusively to the primary engine at index 0.

---

**Public API:**

- `Initialize(scriptPath, engineCount) → bool` — The primary setup call. Parses `scriptPath` into a directory and stem module name, calls `Py_Initialize()`, then creates `engineCount` sub-interpreters in a loop. For each sub-interpreter it calls `PythonEngine::SetupSubInterpreter` (stdout redirection, `sys.path` injection, import hooks for the primary engine only) and then `PythonEngine::StartWorkerThread`. Returns `false` and logs an error if `engineCount` is zero, `Py_Initialize` fails, or no engines are created successfully. On Python < 3.12, silently clamps `engineCount` to 1 and logs a warning. Must not be called while `m_Running` is true; re-entrant calls are no-ops that return `true`.

- `Stop() → void` — Convenience combinator that calls `SignalStop()` then `WaitStop()` in sequence. Safe to call from the destructor.

- `SignalStop() → void` — Iterates all engines and calls `PythonEngine::SignalStop()` on each, requesting their worker threads to drain and exit. Non-blocking; does not wait for completion.

- `WaitStop() → void` — Calls `PythonEngine::WaitStop()` on each engine (blocks until the worker thread joins), then clears `m_Engines` (destroying all `PythonEngine` objects) and sets `m_Running = false`. Logs a single info message on completion.

- `OnStart() → void` — Forwards to `m_Engines[0]->OnStart()`. Routes script lifecycle hook to the primary engine only. No-op if the pool is empty.

- `OnUpdate() → void` — Forwards to `m_Engines[0]->OnUpdate()`. Routes the per-frame/tick hook to the primary engine only. No-op if the pool is empty.

- `OnEvent(eventPtr) → void` — Moves `eventPtr` into `m_Engines[0]->OnEvent()`. Routes event delivery to the primary engine only. No-op if the pool is empty.

- `IsRunning() → bool` — Returns `m_Running`. True only after a successful `Initialize` and before `WaitStop` completes.

- `GetEngineCount() → size_t` — Returns the number of engines actually constructed (may be less than the requested `engineCount` if some sub-interpreters failed to initialize).

- `GetTasksCompleted(engineIndex) → size_t` — Delegates to `PythonEngine::GetTasksCompleted()` for the engine at `engineIndex`. Returns 0 for out-of-range indices.

- `ExecuteWorkflowTask(taskDefinition, taskWorkingDirectory, inputValues, contextValues, outputValuesOut, errorMessage, capturedStdout, capturedStderr) → bool` — Selects the least-loaded engine via `SelectEngine()` (minimum `GetQueueDepth()` scan) and delegates directly to `PythonEngine::ExecuteWorkflowTask`. Returns `false` immediately with an error message if the pool is not running. Output parameters `outputValuesOut`, `errorMessage`, `capturedStdout`, and `capturedStderr` are written by the selected engine.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `PythonEngine` (`pythonEngine.h` / `pythonEngine.cpp`) — The per-interpreter worker. `PythonEnginePool` creates, owns, and destroys all `PythonEngine` instances via `std::unique_ptr`. Calls `SetupSubInterpreter`, `SetInterpreterState`, `StartWorkerThread`, `SignalStop`, `WaitStop`, `GetQueueDepth`, `GetTasksCompleted`, and all hook/task dispatch methods on it.
- `Event` (`engine.h`) — Passed by `shared_ptr` through `OnEvent`; the pool moves ownership into the primary engine.
- `TaskDef` (`engine.h`) — Passed by const-ref into `ExecuteWorkflowTask`; defines the task descriptor used by the engine.
- CPython C API (`Python.h`) — Used directly for `Py_Initialize`, `PyThreadState_Get`, `PyThreadState_Swap`, `Py_NewInterpreterFromConfig` (3.12+), `Py_NewInterpreter` (< 3.12), `Py_EndInterpreter`, `PyEval_SaveThread`, `PyEval_RestoreThread`, and `PyStatus_IsError`.
- `log/log.h` — Macro-based structured logging (`LOG_APP_INFO`, `LOG_APP_WARN`, `LOG_APP_ERROR`) used throughout initialization and shutdown.
- `<filesystem>` — Used only during `Initialize` to decompose `scriptPath` into directory and stem.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`PythonEnginePool` is intended to be owned by a single controlling thread (the application main thread or workflow coordinator). `Initialize` must complete before any other public method is called from any thread. The pool itself performs no internal locking; the caller is responsible for serializing calls to `Initialize`, `Stop`, `SignalStop`, `WaitStop`, and the hook callbacks relative to each other.

During `Initialize`, all CPython sub-interpreter setup occurs on the calling thread while holding the GIL. After `PyEval_SaveThread()` is called at the end of `Initialize`, worker threads launched by `StartWorkerThread` each create their own `PyThreadState` against their sub-interpreter's `PyInterpreterState` (stored via `SetInterpreterState`). The main-thread `PyThreadState` objects created for each sub-interpreter during setup (`subTS`) are intentionally not cleaned up until process exit.

`ExecuteWorkflowTask` can be called concurrently from multiple threads; `SelectEngine` performs an unsynchronized scan of queue depths (read-only), so calls from multiple threads may transiently route to the same engine. `PythonEngine` itself is responsible for queue synchronization.

The destructor calls `Stop()` if `m_Running` is true, making the pool safe to destroy without an explicit shutdown call, but callers should prefer explicit `Stop()` for deterministic ordering.

---

**Notes:**

- The Python version gate (`PY_VERSION_HEX < 0x030C0000`) enforces a hard clamp to one engine on Python < 3.12. Callers passing `engineCount > 1` against an older runtime will silently get a pool of one without an error return — only a warning log.
- On Python 3.12, sub-interpreters are created with `PyInterpreterConfig_SHARED_GIL`. True parallel execution via per-interpreter GIL requires Python 3.13+; the comment in the source explicitly flags this as the upgrade path (`PyInterpreterConfig_OWN_GIL`).
- `check_multi_interp_extensions = 0` suppresses the extension compatibility check, meaning sub-interpreters can coexist with arbitrary single-phase C extensions loaded in the main interpreter. This trades safety for compatibility.
- Hook callbacks (`OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, `OnEvent`) are hard-wired to engine 0 regardless of pool size. If engine 0 fails to initialize and `m_Engines` is populated starting from engine 1, this invariant breaks; the pool does not reorder engines to guarantee index-0 is always present.
- If some but not all sub-interpreters fail during `Initialize`, the pool proceeds with the subset that succeeded and returns `true`. Callers should check `GetEngineCount()` against the requested count if exact sizing matters.
- `WaitStop` destroys all `PythonEngine` unique_ptrs via `m_Engines.clear()` but does not call `Py_FinalizeEx`. CPython global state is not torn down on pool shutdown; this is intentional for single-process lifetime management but means Python cannot be re-initialized in the same process after `Stop`.


---

### 64_doc_application_session_fileWriter_h

<a id="64-doc-application-session-filewriter-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/64_doc_application_session_fileWriter_h/docu.output.md`



## FileWriter (`session/fileWriter.h` / `session/fileWriter.cpp`)

**Role:** `FileWriter` is a process-wide singleton responsible for all file output produced by the AI Assistant workflow engine. It provides two write operations — a plain content write and a header-annotated write — both of which ensure the destination directory exists before opening the file, truncate-write the content atomically under a mutex, and log every significant step (directory creation attempts, success or failure, final write confirmation) through the engine's `LOG_APP_INFO` / `LOG_APP_ERROR` macros. It is the single choke-point through which session output files reach disk, keeping file-I/O concerns out of the rest of the pipeline.

**Public API:**

- `Get() → FileWriter&` — Returns the Meyer's-singleton instance. Safe to call from any thread after static initialisation. Never returns null. Callers must not store the reference beyond the lifetime of the process.

- `Write(const std::filesystem::path& filePath, const std::string& content) → void` — Resolves the parent directory of `filePath` to an absolute, lexically-normalised path, calls `fs::create_directories` on it (logging before and after), then opens `filePath` with `std::ios::out | std::ios::trunc` and writes `content` verbatim. If the file cannot be opened, logs an error and returns silently without throwing. Acquires `m_Mutex` for the entire operation.

- `WriteWithHeader(const std::filesystem::path& filePath, const std::string& content, const std::string& model) → void` — Identical directory-creation and file-write logic to `Write`. The `model` parameter is accepted but **not currently used** in the output — no header is prepended to the file in the current implementation despite the method name. Acquires `m_Mutex` for the entire operation.

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` — Provides the `LOG_APP_INFO` and `LOG_APP_ERROR` macros used throughout both methods for structured diagnostic output. No other symbols from `engine.h` are used in this file.
- `<filesystem>` / `<fstream>` / `<chrono>` / `<iomanip>` — Standard library. `<chrono>` and `<iomanip>` are included but unused in the current implementation, suggesting future timestamped-header work was planned.
- Callers in the session layer (anything that produces AI response output files) are the expected consumers; nothing in this file reaches back into the request or auth layers.

**Threading / lifetime:**

- The singleton is default-constructed on first call to `Get()` and lives until process exit. Copy construction and copy assignment are deleted; no move semantics are defined.
- `m_Mutex` serialises all calls to both `Write` and `WriteWithHeader`. Concurrent writers across threads block on entry; there is no try-lock or timeout, so a slow or blocked filesystem write will stall every other writer.
- Callers must not hold a reference to the returned `FileWriter&` across calls that might outlive the static instance (i.e., across `main` exit).

**Notes:**

- `WriteWithHeader` does **not** write a header. The `model` argument is silently ignored and no timestamp, model name, or metadata is prepended to the file. If callers depend on a header being present for downstream parsing, this is a bug rather than a feature gap.
- Both methods include `[folder creation debug]` log lines that appear to be temporary diagnostic instrumentation left in production code; they fire on every write, not just on first creation.
- `<chrono>` and `<iomanip>` are included in the `.cpp` but produce no code — dead includes, likely remnants of an earlier or planned header-generation implementation.
- All `std::filesystem` exceptions (permission errors, bad paths, etc.) are caught as `std::exception` and logged; the error is swallowed and the caller receives no indication of failure. Callers cannot distinguish a successful write from a silently failed one.
- The directory existence check (`existedBefore` / `existsAfter`) introduces a TOCTOU window: another process can remove or create the directory between the `fs::exists` probe and `create_directories`. This affects log accuracy only, not correctness.


---

### 65_doc_application_task_carMaintenanceTask_h

<a id="65-doc-application-task-carmaintenancetask-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/65_doc_application_task_carMaintenanceTask_h/docu.output.md`



## CarMaintenanceTask

**Role:** `CarMaintenanceTask` is a concrete `ITask` implementation within the `AIAssistant` workflow engine that acts as a dispatch node immediately downstream of an AI classification step. It reads a structured JSON file produced by a prior classifier task (one that declared an `output_schema`), extracts the `"category"` field, and writes a fixed-text maintenance guidance snippet — engine notes, tire notes, or a rephrase prompt — to an output file. It does not call any AI provider itself; it is pure post-processing logic that routes classifier output to canned response text on disk.

**Public API:**

- `Execute(inputFilePaths, outputFilePaths, errorMessageOut) → bool` — The sole entry point. Reads `inputFilePaths[0]` as a UTF-8 / binary JSON file, parses it with simdjson to extract `category`, selects the matching guidance text, then writes that text to `outputFilePaths[0]`, creating any missing parent directories. Returns `true` on success; on any failure populates `errorMessageOut` with a human-readable message prefixed `"CarMaintenanceTask: "` and returns `false`. Both vectors must be non-empty; only index `[0]` of each is consumed — additional entries are silently ignored.

**Collaborators:**

- `ITask` (`task/taskBase.h`) — abstract base that defines the `Execute` contract; `CarMaintenanceTask` is `final` and overrides only that method.
- **simdjson** (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) — used inside `ExtractCategoryFromStructuredJson` via an on-demand parser against a `simdjson::padded_string`. The parser instance and padded string are stack-allocated per call, so there is no retained simdjson state between invocations.
- `std::filesystem` / `std::fstream` — used directly for all I/O; no external I/O abstraction layer is involved.
- **Upstream classifier task** — the JSON contract is `{"category": "engine" | "tires" | "rephrase"}`. Any deviation (missing field, wrong type, extra nesting) causes `Execute` to return `false`.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`CarMaintenanceTask` carries no mutable member state; the default constructor and destructor are trivial. All private helper methods are `static`. A single instance can be reused across sequential calls to `Execute`, but because no synchronisation is present, concurrent calls on the same instance against overlapping output paths would produce a data race on the filesystem. The object is inexpensive to construct per-task invocation if the caller prefers that pattern. Ownership follows whatever the task-scheduling layer imposes on `ITask*` pointers; this class imposes no additional lifetime requirements.

**Notes:**

- The three accepted category strings are case-sensitive and exact: `"engine"`, `"tires"`, `"rephrase"`. Any other value — including `"Engine"` or `"tire"` (singular) — causes `Execute` to fail with an *unexpected category* error. The upstream classifier schema must guarantee this exact vocabulary.
- `TryReadAllText` opens files in `std::ios::binary` mode. It pre-reserves string capacity using `fs::file_size`, but falls back gracefully if that call returns an error code (no reserve, normal read proceeds). A `bad()` bit on the stream after reading triggers failure; `eof` alone does not.
- `TryWriteAllText` calls `fs::create_directories` on the output file's parent path before opening the stream. If the parent path is empty (i.e. the output is a bare filename in the working directory) that step is skipped entirely. The output file is opened with `std::ios::trunc`, so a pre-existing file at the output path is silently overwritten.
- The guidance text returned by `MakeEngineManualText`, `MakeTireMaintenanceText`, and `MakeRephraseRequestText` is entirely hard-coded in the translation unit. There is no localisation, templating, or runtime configuration.
- simdjson's on-demand API requires the input buffer to outlive the document iteration. `ExtractCategoryFromStructuredJson` satisfies this: `padded` and `doc` are both on the stack within the same scope, and `categoryOut` is populated via `std::string::assign` before either goes out of scope, so there is no dangling `string_view`.


---

### 66_doc_application_task_internalTaskRegistry_h

<a id="66-doc-application-task-internaltaskregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/66_doc_application_task_internalTaskRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



## InternalTaskRegistry / IInternalTaskRegistry

**Role:** This header defines the task-factory registry used by the j9t workflow engine to map action-name strings to concrete `ITask` implementations at runtime. `IInternalTaskRegistry` provides the polymorphic interface through which the rest of the system requests task creation, while `InternalTaskRegistry` is the concrete, thread-safe implementation that maintains an `unordered_map` of string-keyed factory callables. Callers register a zero-argument factory lambda (or any `std::function<std::unique_ptr<ITask>()>`) under an action name, and later retrieve a freshly constructed `ITask` instance by passing that same name to `CreateTask`.

**Public API:**

- `IInternalTaskRegistry::CreateTask(action) → std::unique_ptr<ITask>` — Pure virtual. Implementations must return a new `ITask` instance corresponding to `action`, or `nullptr` if the action is unknown. Ownership of the returned task is transferred to the caller.

- `InternalTaskRegistry::RegisterFactory(action, factory) → void` — Inserts or overwrites the factory callable associated with `action` in the internal map. `factory` must be a valid, callable `std::function<std::unique_ptr<ITask>()>`; storing an empty or null `std::function` is not guarded against and will cause `CreateTask` to throw `std::bad_function_call` at invocation time. Acquires `m_Mutex` for the duration of the write. Safe to call concurrently with other `RegisterFactory` or `CreateTask` calls.

- `InternalTaskRegistry::CreateTask(action) → std::unique_ptr<ITask>` — Looks up `action` in the factory map under `m_Mutex`, returns `nullptr` if not found, otherwise invokes the stored factory and returns the resulting `unique_ptr`. Each call produces an independent `ITask` instance; the registry retains no ownership of created tasks.

**Collaborators:**

- `ITask` / `taskBase.h` — The base interface that every registered factory must produce. The registry has no knowledge of concrete task types; that coupling lives in whatever code calls `RegisterFactory`.
- Consumers of `IInternalTaskRegistry` (expected to be a task dispatcher or workflow orchestrator elsewhere in `AIAssistant`) depend only on the interface, enabling test doubles to be injected without touching the concrete registry.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`m_Mutex` is declared `mutable` so that `CreateTask` (a `const` method) can acquire it legally. Both `RegisterFactory` and `CreateTask` take a `std::scoped_lock` on `m_Mutex`, making them safe to call from multiple threads simultaneously. The registry itself has no internal thread of its own and imposes no async constraints on callers. The `InternalTaskRegistry` instance must outlive all threads that call into it; there is no reference-counting mechanism on the registry. Returned `unique_ptr<ITask>` objects are fully owned by the caller and have no back-reference to the registry.

**Notes:**

- Registering the same action name a second time silently overwrites the previous factory with no warning. Callers relying on stable factory associations across registration calls must coordinate externally.
- If a stored `TaskFactory` is a default-constructed (empty) `std::function`, `CreateTask` will invoke it and trigger `std::bad_function_call`; the registry does not validate factories on insertion.
- `CreateTask` returns `nullptr` for unrecognised actions rather than throwing; callers must check the return value before dereferencing.
- The factory is invoked while `m_Mutex` is held. Factories that themselves call back into the same `InternalTaskRegistry` instance (e.g., to create sub-tasks) will deadlock, because `std::mutex` is not recursive.
- This is a header-only implementation (`#pragma once`, all method bodies inline), so there is no corresponding `.cpp` translation unit.


---

### 67_doc_application_task_taskBase_h

<a id="67-doc-application-task-taskbase-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/67_doc_application_task_taskBase_h/docu.output.md`



## ITask

**Role:** `ITask` is the abstract base interface for all executable task types within the `AIAssistant` workflow engine. Every concrete task — whether it invokes an AI provider, transforms a file, runs a Python snippet, or calls an external integration — must implement this interface. It defines a single uniform contract that the engine's task scheduler and pipeline runners use to dispatch work without knowing the specific task type.

**Public API:**

- `Execute(inputFilePaths, outputFilePaths, errorMessageOut) → bool` — Runs the task. `inputFilePaths` is a list of filesystem paths the task is expected to read from; `outputFilePaths` is a list of paths the task is expected to write to. On success, returns `true` and leaves `errorMessageOut` in an unspecified (typically empty) state. On failure, returns `false` and writes a human-readable diagnostic string into `errorMessageOut`. The method is responsible for all I/O, computation, and side effects specific to the concrete task type. Callers must treat `errorMessageOut` as meaningful only when the return value is `false`.

**Collaborators:** Concrete implementations of `ITask` will depend on whatever subsystems they wrap — AI provider clients, secret stores, webhook dispatchers, file watchers, or embedded Python runtimes. The interface itself has no dependencies beyond `<filesystem>` and `<string>`. The engine's task runner or pipeline coordinator holds and calls `ITask` instances polymorphically, so it is the primary consumer of this interface.

**Threading / lifetime:** `ITask` carries a virtual destructor, making it safe to delete concrete instances through an `ITask*`. Ownership is expected to rest with whatever container (task queue, pipeline stage, smart pointer) the engine uses to schedule work. The interface imposes no thread-safety guarantees on `Execute`; whether a given concrete implementation may be called concurrently is determined entirely by that implementation. Callers should assume `Execute` is not re-entrant unless a concrete class explicitly documents otherwise.

**Notes:** The path vectors are passed by const reference, so `Execute` must not mutate them. The interface does not define cancellation, progress reporting, or async continuation; tasks run to completion synchronously within a single `Execute` call. Any task needing async behaviour must implement it internally and block until completion before returning. `errorMessageOut` is a pure out-parameter — implementations should not rely on its incoming value.


---

### 68_doc_application_web_aiJcwfService_h

<a id="68-doc-application-web-aijcwfservice-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/68_doc_application_web_aiJcwfService_h/docu.output.md`



## AiJcwfService

**Role:** `AiJcwfService` is the AI-powered authoring service for JCWF (JC Workflow Format) documents. It exposes three async operations — explain an existing workflow in natural language, generate a new workflow from a natural language prompt, and fix a failed script using its stderr output — plus a synchronous interface connectivity probe and a static JCWF validator. All async operations run on detached background threads, communicate with the AI backend through `AiRequestPool`, write debug-replay transcript files to disk under the queue directory, and deliver progress and results to WebSocket clients via a caller-supplied `BroadcastFn` callback. The generate path implements an optional multi-batch fan-out strategy for large workflows, followed by script generation (with structural validation, automated fix, and AI review cycles), JCWF schema validation, and a single AI-driven fix pass before broadcasting the final result.

---

**Public API:**

- `SetBroadcastFn(BroadcastFn broadcastFn) → void` — Installs the WebSocket broadcast callback. Protected by `m_BroadcastMutex`. Must be called before any async operation if broadcast messages are required; replacing it mid-flight is safe but the old callback may still be in use on a background thread at the moment of replacement.

- `ExplainAsync(std::string const& jcwfJsonText) → void` — Launches a background thread that runs two sequential AI calls: Stage 1 produces a structured natural language summary of the workflow; Stage 2 reviews and corrects Stage 1's output against both the raw JSON and the embedded generation guide. Broadcasts `ai-explain-progress` during execution and `ai-explain-result` on completion. If Stage 2 fails, the Stage 1 result is broadcast as a fallback rather than surfacing an error.

- `GenerateAsync(std::string const& prompt, std::string const& currentJcwfJson) → void` — Launches a background thread executing a five-stage pipeline: (1) decompose the prompt into a structured task breakdown; (2) generate JCWF JSON — either as a single AI call or fanned out across multiple batches when the task count exceeds `config.m_JcwfBatchSize`; (3) generate any companion shell/Python/PowerShell scripts referenced in the produced JCWF that do not already exist on disk, each subject to structural validation, an optional AI fix call, and an AI review call; (4) validate the JCWF via `ValidateJcwf`; (5) issue a targeted or full-JCWF AI fix call if any errors or warnings are present, then re-validate. If `currentJcwfJson` is non-empty the AI is instructed to modify the existing workflow rather than create a new one. Broadcasts `ai-generate-progress` (with `stage`/`totalStages` fields) and `ai-generate-result`. The result payload includes the raw JCWF JSON and a `scripts` array of `GeneratedScript` objects for any newly produced scripts.

- `FixFailedScriptAsync(std::string const& scriptPath, std::string const& stderrContent, std::string const& taskType) → void` — Launches a background thread that reads the script at `scriptPath` from disk, builds a fix prompt that includes the stderr output and host OS description, calls the AI, strips markdown fences from the response, and broadcasts `ai-fix-script-result` with the fixed content for frontend review. Does not write the fixed script to disk; that decision is left to the caller. Broadcasts `ai-fix-script-progress` during execution.

- `TestAiInterface(size_t interfaceIndex, std::string& outResponsePreview, std::string& outError, int64_t& outLatencyMs) → bool` — Synchronous connectivity probe. Looks up the interface at `interfaceIndex` in `config.m_ApiInterfaces`, resolves the API key via `KeyManager`, constructs a minimal `AiInvocation` envelope with the prompt `"Say hello"`, delegates request body and URL assembly to `IRequestBuilder::Create`, and issues a direct `CurlManager::GetThreadCurl().Query` call with a 30-second timeout (bypassing `AiRequestPool` entirely). Returns true on HTTP-level success; `outResponsePreview` receives the first 200 characters of the AI's reply; `outLatencyMs` is wall-clock time measured around the curl call. Intended to be called from a Crow HTTP handler thread.

- `ValidateJcwf(std::string const& jcwfJsonText, std::string& outValidationSummary, ScriptRegistry const* scriptRegistry = nullptr, std::vector<GeneratedScript> const* pendingScripts = nullptr, std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>* outIssues = nullptr) → bool` (static) — Parses the JCWF text with `WorkflowJsonParser`, then runs `WorkflowValidator::Validate`. Returns true if there are no errors (warnings are tolerated). `outValidationSummary` receives a formatted multi-line string with severity, code, message, path, task ID, suggested fix, and context for every error and warning. `outIssues` receives the raw `WorkflowValidationIssue` vector if non-null. Accepts `pendingScripts` so scripts that have been generated but not yet written to disk can be treated as present during validation. Retrieves a `WorkflowFileIndex` pointer from `JarvisAgent::g_App` if available.

- `Shutdown() → void` — Sets `m_ShuttingDown` to true and joins all threads in `m_BackgroundThreads` under `m_ThreadsMutex`. Called automatically by the destructor. Background threads check `m_ShuttingDown` between stages and broadcast a shutdown error if it fires mid-flight.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `AiRequestPool` — obtained via `JarvisAgent::GetAiRequestPool()`; receives `AiInvocation` envelopes via `Submit` and delivers `AiReply` objects to a callback. The pool handles interface selection, retry logic, structured-output schema validation, and cancellation.
- `AiInvocation` / `AiReply` / `Message` — envelope and result types defined in `workflow/aiInvocation.h`. The envelope carries `m_InterfaceName`, `m_QueueFolder`, `m_ProbName`, `m_Timeout`, `m_OutputSchemaJson`, and the assembled `m_Messages` vector.
- `WorkflowJsonParser` / `WorkflowValidator` — parse and validate JCWF JSON; `WorkflowValidator::Validate` populates `WorkflowValidationIssue` vectors. `WorkflowValidator::HasErrors` is used to decide whether to mark a result as failed when a fix call itself fails.
- `ScriptRegistry` — obtained via `JarvisAgent::GetScriptRegistry()`; its `SerializeMarkdownTable()` output is injected into generation prompts so the AI knows which scripts already exist.
- `WorkflowFileIndex` — obtained via `JarvisAgent::GetWorkflowFileIndex()`; `ScanDirectory` is called before generation to refresh the listing, then `SerializeMarkdownListing()` is injected into prompts.
- `IRequestBuilder` / `ReplyParser` — used exclusively in `TestAiInterface` to build request bodies and parse responses outside the pool path.
- `CurlManager` / `CurlWrapper` — `CurlManager::GetThreadCurl()` is called in `TestAiInterface` for a direct synchronous HTTP request.
- `KeyManager` — resolves API keys and provider params (including AWS SigV4 fields) by key name from `config.m_ApiInterfaces[i].m_KeyName`.
- `Core::g_Core` / `JarvisAgent::g_App` — global singletons providing configuration, queue path, and subsystem access.
- `kJcwfGenerationGuide` (from `json/jcwfGenerationGuide.generated.h`) — the JCWF authoring guide embedded at build time by `tools/generateEmbeddedHeaders.py` from `doc/jcwf_generation_guide.md`. No disk fallback exists.
- `kJcwfSchemaJson` (from `json/jcwfSchema.generated.h`) — the JCWF JSON Schema, also embedded at build time. Passed as `outputSchemaJson` to `RunSingleAiCall` on the single-call generate path and the full-JCWF fix path so `AiRequestPool` can validate and retry structured output.
- `simdjson` — used throughout anonymous-namespace helpers to extract task IDs, script paths, function names, and file I/O metadata from JCWF JSON without a full parse-to-object round-trip.
- `AiCallTaskExecutor::WriteTextFile` — used to write STNG/TASK/CNTX/PROB transcript files to disk.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- All three `*Async` methods acquire `m_ThreadsMutex` and push a `std::thread` into `m_BackgroundThreads`. Threads are never joined mid-run; they accumulate until `Shutdown()` is called. `JoinFinishedThreads()` is a no-op stub — thread count is therefore bounded only by the number of user-initiated requests outstanding at the time of shutdown.
- `RunSingleAiCall` blocks its calling background thread using `std::future::wait_for` against the `AI_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (120 000 ms) deadline. The `AiRequestPool::Submit` callback fires on a separate curl I/O thread and fulfils the promise; the promise/future pair is heap-allocated (`std::shared_ptr`) to remain valid across the callback boundary.
- `Broadcast` acquires `m_BroadcastMutex` on every call. Background threads may hold this lock for the duration of the callback; callers who install a `BroadcastFn` that does non-trivial work (e.g. iterates WebSocket connections) should be aware of this.
- `TestAiInterface` is explicitly documented as a blocking call intended for Crow HTTP handler threads. It must not be called from the main thread because it will block for up to 30 seconds.
- `m_ShuttingDown` and `m_NextRequestSeq` are `std::atomic`; all other shared state (`m_BroadcastFn`, `m_BackgroundThreads`) is protected by their respective mutexes.
- `AiJcwfService` is non-copyable. Ownership is expected to rest with a single long-lived object (typically the application singleton). Callers must not destroy the service while background threads are running without first calling `Shutdown()`.

---

**Notes:**

- **Fan-out threshold and batch size:** `GenerateAsync` switches from a single AI call to a multi-batch fan-out when `taskIds.size() > config.m_JcwfBatchSize` *and* `currentJcwfJson` is empty. Batch 0 always produces a complete JCWF (workflow-level fields + first batch of tasks); subsequent batches produce `{ "tasks": { ... } }` fragments. `MergeJcwfFragments` splices fragments by string manipulation — it locates the `tasks` object via brace-matching (`FindMatchingBrace`) rather than a JSON parser, so malformed JSON in any fragment will produce a silently broken result.
- **Transcript files are debug artifacts, not dispatch inputs:** `RunSingleAiCall` writes STNG/TASK/CNTX/PROB files to `<queueBase>/_ai_jcwf_service/<subfolderName>/` and clears the directory on every call. These files are not read back; the authoritative content is the assembled `AiInvocation` envelope.
- **Script generation is non-fatal:** if generating, fixing, or reviewing any individual companion script fails, `GenerateAsync` logs a warning and continues with the remaining scripts. The final broadcast always includes whatever scripts were successfully produced.
- **Structured output schema enforcement:** `kJcwfSchemaJson` is passed to `RunSingleAiCall` only on the single-call generate path and the full-JCWF fix path. The fan-out batch calls and targeted fix calls do **not** pass a schema, meaning `AiRequestPool`'s schema-validation retry logic is not active for those calls.
- **Targeted vs full-JCWF fix:** the fix stage uses targeted patching (`PatchTasksIntoJcwf`) only when `useFanOut` is true *and* every validation issue has a non-empty `m_TaskId`. Otherwise it sends the entire JCWF for repair. `PatchTasksIntoJcwf` also operates via string manipulation, so task ID collisions or unusually formatted JSON can cause silent patch failures (logged at WARN level).
- **`ExplainAsync` Stage 2 fallback:** if the refinement AI call fails, Stage 1's output is broadcast as `ok:true` rather than an error. Callers cannot distinguish a reviewed result from a raw Stage 1 result.
- **`ValidateJcwf` is static and callable without a service instance:** it is used internally by `GenerateAsync` and exposed publicly for use by assistant tool handlers that have access only to the class header.
- **Host OS detection runs at call time:** `GetHostOsDescription()` is called inside background threads during `ExplainAsync`/`GenerateAsync`. On Linux it reads `/etc/os-release`; on macOS it forks `sw_vers`; on Windows it returns a static string. The fork in `GetHostOsDescription` is inside a background thread, which is safe but noteworthy.
- **`m_NextRequestSeq` provides collision-free subfolder names:** it is incremented atomically with `fetch_add` and incorporated into every `RunSingleAiCall` subfolder name (e.g. `gen_3_decompose`). Sequences restart from 1 on each process start.


---

### 69_doc_application_web_mcpKeyManager_h

<a id="69-doc-application-web-mcpkeymanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/69_doc_application_web_mcpKeyManager_h/docu.output.md`



## McpKeyManager

**Role:** `McpKeyManager` is the authoritative credential store for MCP API keys inside the `AIAssistant` workflow engine. It manages the full lifecycle of MCP API keys — enrollment, activation, authentication, rotation, and revocation — and persists all state as an AES-256-GCM + PBKDF2 encrypted JSON blob on disk (delegating crypto to `KeyEncryption`, the same mechanism used by `keys.json.enc`). Raw keys are never stored; only their SHA-256 hashes are retained. The class also tracks short-lived enrollment tokens that let an admin pre-authorise a user without handing over a real key, and maintains a best-effort in-memory disk-usage ledger keyed by user identity.

---

**Public API:**

- `Load(path, masterPassword) → bool` — Reads the encrypted file at `path`, decrypts it with `masterPassword` via `KeyEncryption::Decrypt`, parses the JSON, and populates `m_Keys`, `m_Enrollments`, and `m_DiskUsageByUser`. Sets `m_Loaded = true` on success. Existing in-memory state is replaced entirely. Returns `false` on file-open failure, decryption failure, JSON parse failure, or unsupported version number.

- `Save(path, masterPassword) → bool` — Serialises current state to JSON, encrypts with `KeyEncryption::Encrypt`, and writes to `path` (truncating). Returns `false` if encryption fails or the file cannot be opened. On success also sets `m_Loaded = true`. **Callers are responsible for calling `Save` after any mutating operation; mutations are not automatically persisted.**

- `IsLoaded() → bool` — Returns whether `m_Loaded` is set. Thread-safe; acquires the mutex.

- `CreateEnrollment(req) → std::string` — Generates a random `enroll_` + 64-hex-char token, stores only its SHA-256 hash as an `EnrollmentRecord`, and returns the raw token to the caller (shown to the admin once). `req.m_User` and `req.m_Role` must be non-empty or the call returns an empty string. TTL defaults to 30 minutes if `m_EnrollmentTtlMinutes` is zero or negative. The resulting key's lifetime defaults to 90 days if `m_KeyExpiryDays` is zero or negative.

- `ActivateEnrollment(rawEnrollmentToken) → std::optional<ActivateResult>` — Validates that the token begins with `enroll_`, purges expired enrollments inline, and finds the matching hash using `CRYPTO_memcmp`. On match, generates a new `mcp_` key, appends it to `m_Keys`, removes the enrollment record, and returns an `ActivateResult` containing the key ID, the raw key (shown once), and the populated `Record`. Returns `std::nullopt` if the token is unrecognised, expired, or entropy generation fails.

- `Authenticate(rawKey) → std::optional<AuthResult>` — Validates the `mcp_` prefix, derives the key ID from the first 8 hex chars after the prefix, finds the matching `Record`, and does a constant-time hash comparison. On success updates `m_LastUsedAt` in-memory (not persisted until `Save`) and returns an `AuthResult` containing a copy of the record and `m_DaysUntilExpiry` (negative if already past). Does **not** check `m_Enabled` or expiry — callers must inspect `m_Record.m_Enabled` and `m_DaysUntilExpiry` themselves.

- `CreateBootstrapAdminKey() → std::optional<ActivateResult>` — One-shot helper for the first-run flow. Succeeds only when `m_Keys` is empty; returns `std::nullopt` on any subsequent call. Creates an admin key with user identity `"boss"`, adhoc enabled, 1 024 MB quota, `ttl_72h` cleanup policy, and a 90-day expiry. The raw key is returned in `ActivateResult::m_RawKey`.

- `SelfRenew(currentRawKey) → std::optional<RenewResult>` — Lets a key holder rotate their own credential without admin involvement. Calls `Authenticate` internally (acquires and releases the mutex), then re-acquires it to append the new key and apply a 24-hour grace period to the old one. The grace period only shortens an existing expiry, never extends it. Returns `std::nullopt` if authentication fails, the key is disabled, or the key is already expired (`m_DaysUntilExpiry < 0`). The replacement key always gets a fresh 90-day window.

- `ListKeys() → std::vector<Record>` — Returns a snapshot copy of `m_Keys`. No raw keys or hashes with any special sensitivity are omitted from the returned structs — `m_KeyHash` fields are included. Callers rendering UI should treat `m_KeyHash` as metadata, not a secret.

- `UpdateKey(keyId, fields) → bool` — Applies whichever `std::optional` fields in `UpdateFields` are engaged to the record identified by `keyId`. Returns `false` if the key ID is not found. Any subset of role, adhoc flag, quota, cleanup policy, enabled flag, description, and expiry can be updated in one call.

- `RevokeKey(keyId) → bool` — Erases the record from `m_Keys` immediately; no grace period. Returns `false` if the ID is not found. Contrast with `SelfRenew`, which keeps the old key alive for 24 hours.

- `RecordDiskUsage(user, bytes)` — Overwrites (not increments) the disk-usage entry for `user` in `m_DiskUsageByUser`. Callers must supply the current total, not a delta.

- `GetDiskUsage(user) → size_t` — Returns the last value recorded for `user`, or `0` if never recorded. Thread-safe.

- `PurgeExpiredEnrollments()` — Removes all `EnrollmentRecord` entries whose `m_ExpiresAt` is in the past. Called opportunistically inside `ActivateEnrollment` and `Authenticate`; also safe to call from a maintenance path. No background thread is started by the class itself.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `KeyEncryption` (`keys/keyEncryption.h`) — Provides `Encrypt(json, password) → vector<uint8_t>` and `Decrypt(blob, password) → string`. The encrypted format is identical to `keys.json.enc` used elsewhere in the engine.
- `simdjson` (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) — Used for JSON parsing in `ParseFromJson`. The serialiser is hand-rolled via `std::ostringstream` with `JsonEscape`.
- OpenSSL (`openssl/rand.h`, `openssl/sha.h`, `openssl/crypto.h`) — `RAND_bytes` for key/token generation, `SHA256` for hashing, `CRYPTO_memcmp` for constant-time comparison.
- `engine.h` — Provides the `LOG_CORE_*` macros used for all diagnostic output. No other engine functionality is used.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

All public methods acquire `m_Mutex` (a `std::mutex`) before touching `m_Keys`, `m_Enrollments`, or `m_DiskUsageByUser`. `SelfRenew` is the only method that calls another locking public method (`Authenticate`) and then re-acquires the mutex; this is safe because `Authenticate` fully releases the lock before returning. There are no recursive lock attempts. The class has no internal threads and no async callbacks; the caller's thread performs all work synchronously. `McpKeyManager` is typically owned by a long-lived singleton or engine-level object; no special destruction protocol is required. `Save` must be called explicitly after mutations — there is no destructor-time flush.

---

**Notes:**

- **Raw keys are shown exactly once.** `ActivateEnrollment`, `SelfRenew`, and `CreateBootstrapAdminKey` all return the raw key in their result structs. Once the caller discards this value it cannot be recovered from the store.
- **`Authenticate` does not enforce `m_Enabled` or expiry.** It returns an `AuthResult` even for disabled or expired keys; the caller (typically the HTTP handler) must check `m_Record.m_Enabled` and `m_DaysUntilExpiry` and act accordingly. This is intentional to allow the caller to return differentiated error responses.
- **Key ID collision is theoretically possible.** The key ID is only the first 8 hex chars (4 bytes) of the 32-byte random value. With large key counts this prefix space may be insufficient; `FindByKeyIdUnlocked` returns the first match by linear scan.
- **`ParseFromJson` rejects records missing `key_id` or `key_hash`** and silently skips them; similarly for enrollment records missing `token_hash`. No other validation is performed on loaded records (e.g., expiry or role values are not checked).
- **`ParseFromJson` fails fast on any version other than `1`**, treating a missing `version` field as `1`.
- **`IsPast` returns `false` on unparseable timestamps**, meaning a malformed `expires_at` makes a token or key appear non-expired. Operators should ensure ISO-8601 UTC format (`%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ`) in any hand-edited data.
- **Disk usage is stored per user identity string, not per key ID.** Multiple keys for the same user share one usage counter. `RecordDiskUsage` is a set, not an accumulate — callers must track the running total externally.
- **The grace-period logic in `SelfRenew` only shortens expiry.** If the old key had an expiry more than 24 hours in the future at renewal time, the old key's expiry is shortened to `now + 24h`. If it already expires within 24 hours, it is left unchanged.
- **`m_LastUsedAt` is updated in-memory on every successful `Authenticate` call** but is not persisted until the next `Save`. High-frequency authentication without periodic saves will result in stale `last_used_at` values on disk.


---

### 70_doc_application_web_webServer_h

<a id="70-doc-application-web-webserver-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/70_doc_application_web_webServer_h/docu.output.md`



## WebServer (`web/webServer.h` / `web/webServer.cpp` / `web/webServer_studio.cpp` / `web/webServer_helpers.h`)

**Role:** `WebServer` is the single HTTP/WebSocket gateway for the entire j9t process. It owns a `crow::SimpleApp` instance, registers every REST route and both WebSocket endpoints (`/ws` and, in Studio builds, `/ws/assistant`), enforces a two-tier auth pipeline (MCP bearer tokens + session cookies), manages the encrypted MCP key store and dashboard session store, accumulates log lines and broadcast payloads for push to connected browser clients, and exposes the full operational API — workflow CRUD (Studio only), run control, artifact download, adhoc workflow submission, AI interface and provider configuration, cloud connection management, OAuth PKCE flows, and key-store lifecycle. It is also the only entity that talks directly to `WorkflowRegistry`, `WorkflowRuntimeManager`, and `TriggerEngine` on behalf of HTTP callers, holding raw non-owning pointers to each that are injected post-construction.

---

**Public API:**

- `WebServer()` — Constructs the Crow app, installs the `CrowSpdlogHandler` to redirect Crow logs through spdlog (preventing raw stderr writes from corrupting a TUI), calls `RegisterRoutes()` and `RegisterWebSocket()`, and in Studio builds wires the `AiJcwfService` broadcast callback into `m_PendingBroadcasts`.

- `~WebServer()` — Calls `Stop()`.

- `bool Start() → bool` — Reads TLS config from `Core::g_Core->GetConfig()`, pre-tests port availability with a raw `bind()` call (fails fast if a second JA instance is running), optionally calls `m_Server.ssl_file()`, attempts to initialise the MCP key store using a cached master password if one is available, then launches Crow on a dedicated `m_ServerThread` in multithreaded mode. Returns `false` and logs a critical error on misconfiguration or port conflict.

- `void Stop()` — Calls `SignalStop()` then `WaitStop()`.

- `void SignalStop()` — Sets `m_Running = false`, shuts down `AiJcwfService` and `AssistantController` (Studio), force-closes all WebSocket connections by calling `conn.close()` on each member of `m_Clients`, then calls `m_Server.stop()`.

- `void WaitStop()` — Joins `m_ServerThread`.

- `void SetWorkflowRegistry(WorkflowRegistry*)` — Stores the registry pointer, propagates it to `AssistantController` (Studio), creates and initialises `AdhocWorkflowManager` (including its reaper thread) if not already present, and refreshes the `ScriptCatalog`. Must be called from a single owner thread before concurrent requests arrive.

- `void SetWorkflowRuntimeManager(WorkflowRuntimeManager*)` — Stores the pointer, propagates it to `AssistantController` (Studio), and installs a terminal-observer lambda on the runtime manager that calls `AdhocWorkflowManager::OnRunCompleted` for any run whose ID starts with `adhoc_`.

- `void SetTriggerEngine(TriggerEngine*)` — Stores the pointer under `m_Mutex`.

- `void BroadcastWorkflowRunsSnapshot()` — Pulls `GetActiveRunsSnapshot()` from the runtime manager, serialises it as `{"type":"workflowRunsSnapshot", "runs":[...]}` with per-task stdout/stderr capped at 512 KB each, sanitises for valid UTF-8, and enqueues the JSON string into `m_PendingBroadcasts`. No-op when the runtime manager is not attached.

- `void BroadcastWorkflowRunsLastSnapshot()` — Same as above but serialises the last-run-per-workflow map and aggregate completed/failed counters as `{"type":"workflowRunsLastSnapshot", ...}`.

- `void Broadcast(std::string const& jsonMessage)` — Appends a raw JSON string to `m_PendingBroadcasts` under `m_Mutex`. Early-exits if `m_ClientCount` is zero (lock-free check).

- `void BroadcastJSON(std::string const& jsonString)` — Identical to `Broadcast`; updates `m_WsTotalBroadcastsEnqueued` and `m_WsPeakPendingBroadcasts` counters.

- `void BroadcastPythonStatus(bool pythonRunning)` — Enqueues `{"type":"python-status","running":...}`.

- `void BroadcastAiCallStarted(probName, interfaceName)` — Enqueues `{"type":"ai-call-started",...}`.

- `void BroadcastAiCallCompleted(probName, inputTokens, outputTokens, totalTokens, finishReason)` — Enqueues `{"type":"ai-call-completed",...}`.

- `void BroadcastAiCallFailed(probName, errorKind, httpStatus, errorMessage)` — Enqueues `{"type":"ai-call-failed",...}`.

- `void EnqueueLogLine(std::string const& line)` — Appends a log line to `m_PendingLogLines` under `m_LogMutex`. Drops the oldest entry when `kMaxPendingLogLines` (500) is exceeded. Skips entirely when `m_ClientCount` is zero. Called from `TerminalLogStreamBuf` on whatever thread writes logs.

- `bool IsMcpConnected()` — Returns true when the last `POST /api/mcp/heartbeat` arrived within 35 seconds. Uses `m_Mutex`.

- `void DrainPendingBroadcasts()` — Must be called from `JarvisAgent::OnUpdate` on the main thread. Drains `m_PendingLogLines` (under `m_LogMutex`), packs accumulated log lines into a single `{"type":"log","lines":[...]}` message, swaps `m_PendingBroadcasts` into a local vector (under `m_Mutex`), wraps all pending messages in a `{"type":"batch","messages":[...]}` envelope, sanitises the envelope for valid UTF-8, then calls `send_text()` once per connected WebSocket client. Updates drain-timing diagnostics (`m_WsLastDrainDurationUs`, `m_WsPeakDrainBytes`, etc.).

- `void ShutdownAssistantController()` (Studio only) — Calls `m_AssistantController.Shutdown()`. Intended to be called before `WRM` and `AiRequestPool` are reset during orderly shutdown.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **`crow::SimpleApp` (`m_Server`)** — The embedded HTTP/WebSocket server. All route registration happens in `RegisterCommonRoutes()`, `RegisterStudioRoutes()`, and `RegisterWebSocket()` / `RegisterAssistantWebSocket()` during construction.
- **`McpKeyManager` (`m_McpKeyManager`)** — Manages the encrypted `mcp_keys.json.enc` key store. Initialised lazily on first `HandleKeysUnlockPost` or eagerly in `Start()` if a master password is already cached. Used by `Authenticate()`, `HandleMcpKeys*`, `HandleLoginPost`, `HandleAdhocRunPost`.
- **`WebSessionManager` (`m_WebSessionManager`)** — Issues and validates short-lived dashboard session cookies. Session timeout is configured from `config.m_SessionTimeoutHours`.
- **`AdhocWorkflowManager` (`m_AdhocManager`)** — Created inside `SetWorkflowRegistry()` once the registry is available. Owns the `_adhoc/` base folder, quota enforcement, and the TTL reaper thread.
- **`ScriptCatalog` (`m_ScriptCatalog`)** — Scanned at `SetWorkflowRegistry()` time and on `GET /api/scripts?refresh=1`. Provides the script inventory surfaced to MCP agents.
- **`WorkflowRegistry*` (`m_WorkflowRegistry`)** — Non-owning; set by `JarvisAgent` after construction. Used by workflow list/get/CRUD/validation routes. Guarded by `m_Mutex`.
- **`WorkflowRuntimeManager*` (`m_WorkflowRuntimeManager`)** — Non-owning; set by `JarvisAgent`. Used by run-monitoring and run-control routes. Guarded by `m_Mutex`.
- **`TriggerEngine*` (`m_TriggerEngine`)** — Non-owning; set by `JarvisAgent`. Used by `HandleWebhookPost` to look up registered webhook triggers, and by `HandleWorkflowsReloadPost` to rebind triggers after a registry reload. Guarded by `m_Mutex`.
- **`Core::g_Core`** — Accessed for config, key manager, cloud connection manager, OAuth token manager, circuit breaker, launch CWD, and config file path. Global singleton assumed to outlive `WebServer`.
- **`App::g_App` (JarvisAgent)** — Accessed in `HandleStatusGet` and debug handlers for `AiRequestPool`, `PythonEnginePool`, `CurlMultiDispatcher`, and `ScriptRegistry`.
- **`AiJcwfService` / `AssistantController`** (Studio only) — Members of `WebServer`; `AiJcwfService` handles AI JCWF generation/explanation WebSocket messages and wires its broadcast callback into `m_PendingBroadcasts`; `AssistantController` manages the `/ws/assistant` channel.
- **`webServer_helpers.h`** — Header-only helpers shared between `webServer.cpp` and `webServer_studio.cpp`: `MakeJsonResponse`, `MakeAuthErrorResponse`, `MakeWorkflowJsonError`, `SetSecurityHeaders`, `VerifyHmacSignature`, `IsValidWorkflowId`, `GetWorkflowsDirectoryAbsolute`, `WriteTextFileAtomic`, `ToStringWorkflowRunState`, `ToStringTaskInstanceStateKind`, and JCWF validation wrappers.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `WebServer` is constructed and destroyed on the main thread. `Start()` launches a Crow I/O thread (`m_ServerThread`); all Crow callbacks (route lambdas, `onopen`, `onclose`, `onmessage`) execute on Crow's internal thread pool.
- **`m_Mutex`** protects: `m_Clients`, `m_ClientCount` mirror, all WS diagnostic counters, `m_PendingBroadcasts`, `m_McpLastHeartbeat`, `m_McpVersion`, `m_WorkflowRegistry`, `m_WorkflowRuntimeManager`, `m_TriggerEngine`.
- **`m_LogMutex`** is separate from `m_Mutex` specifically to avoid deadlock when spdlog (which calls `EnqueueLogLine`) fires while `m_Mutex` is held. Never hold both simultaneously.
- **`m_RateLimitMutex`** protects `m_PreAuthBuckets`, `m_AuthenticatedBuckets`, `m_AuthFailures`, `m_LastRateLimitCleanup`.
- **`m_OAuthStateMutex`** protects `m_OAuthCodeVerifiers` and `m_OAuthStateTokens`.
- **`m_McpKeysLoaded`** and **`m_ClientCount`** are `std::atomic<bool>` / `std::atomic<size_t>` and are read lock-free as optimistic fast-exit guards before acquiring heavier locks.
- `DrainPendingBroadcasts()` **must be called from the main thread** (documented as `JarvisAgent::OnUpdate`). It performs a dangling-pointer re-check by looking up each client in `m_Clients` under `m_Mutex` before calling `send_text`, because Crow's `onclose` may fire between the snapshot and the send.
- `SetWorkflowRegistry`, `SetWorkflowRuntimeManager`, `SetTriggerEngine` acquire `m_Mutex` and are expected to be called once from `JarvisAgent` startup before any concurrent HTTP traffic.
- `m_WorkflowRegistry` and `m_WorkflowRuntimeManager` are raw non-owning pointers. Lifetime must be guaranteed by `JarvisAgent`; `WebServer` does not participate in their destruction.
- `m_AdhocManager` is a `unique_ptr` owned by `WebServer`; it is constructed only after `SetWorkflowRegistry` provides the registry. Its reaper thread is started inside `SetWorkflowRegistry` and must finish before `~WebServer` or explicit `Stop()`.
- `m_McpKeyManager` and `m_WebSessionManager` are value members (not pointers); they are accessible from both Crow I/O threads (via route handlers) and the main thread (via `DrainPendingBroadcasts` diagnostics). Both are guarded by either `m_Mutex` or their own internal locking.

---

**Notes:**

- **Auth funnel is edition-agnostic.** `Authenticate()` is identical in Engine and Studio. Edition controls which routes are *registered*, not how requests are authenticated. The order inside `Authenticate()` is: lockout check → MCP bearer token OR session cookie → pre-auth rate limit on failure paths → authenticated rate limit on success → optional gateway cross-check (identity match + role downgrade). Never inline a role check; always call `CheckAuth(req, minRole)` or `CheckAdminAuth(req)` so the `forbidden reason=insufficient_role` security log line is emitted consistently.
- **Two-tier rate limiting.** Pre-auth (per-IP): burst 20, refill 100 req/min. Authenticated (per-user): burst 200, refill 1200 req/min. Buckets are seeded to full burst on first touch. Idle buckets (>10 min) and expired lockout records are evicted every 5 minutes inside `IsRateLimited()`. Lockout triggers at 10 failures in 5 minutes and lasts 15 minutes.
- **MCP key store is lazily initialised.** If the master password is not cached at `Start()` time (first run or restart without an unlock), `m_McpKeysLoaded` remains `false` and all `/api/auth/mcp-keys/*` endpoints return 503. The store is unlocked via `POST /api/settings/keys/unlock`, which also triggers `OAuthTokenManager::HydrateFromKeyManager()` to restore persisted OAuth refresh tokens.
- **Bootstrap admin key.** After `HandleKeysUnlockPost` initialises an empty MCP store, `McpKeyManager::CreateBootstrapAdminKey()` is called. If it returns a key (store was empty), the raw key is embedded directly in the JSON response body. It is never logged. `SaveMcpKeyStore()` persists immediately.
- **WebSocket broadcast batching.** All messages enqueued between `DrainPendingBroadcasts` calls are wrapped in a single `{"type":"batch","messages":[...]}` envelope and sent as one `send_text()` call per client. This prevents Crow async-write overlap that previously caused client disconnects. `DrainPendingBroadcasts` is also called at the end of each `onmessage` handler.
- **UTF-8 sanitisation.** `SanitizeUtf8()` replaces invalid byte sequences with U+FFFD before any WebSocket text frame is sent. RFC 6455 requires valid UTF-8 in text frames; without this, certain log lines or captured task output would cause client disconnects (code 1002). The whole batch envelope is sanitised once in `DrainPendingBroadcasts`.
- **Webhook auth is HMAC-SHA256 mandatory.** Webhooks without a configured secret are rejected at the handler level (403 `secret_required`). The signature header must be `sha256=<hex>`; comparison is constant-time.
- **Adhoc security boundary.** `HandleAdhocRunPost` rejects JCWF payloads that reference scripts not already present under `scripts/` on disk. The check walks every `shell.params.command` and `python.params.module` in the top-level `tasks` object. Cleanup policy is validated against the MCP key's ceiling rank (shortest-lived to longest: `on_completion`, `ttl_1h`, `ttl_24h`, `ttl_48h`, `ttl_72h`, `retain`). A submitted policy more permissive than the ceiling is rejected with 403.
- **Run artifact path safety.** `HandleRunFileGet
