### 71_doc_application_web_webSessionManager_h

<a id="71-doc-application-web-websessionmanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/71_doc_application_web_webSessionManager_h/docu.output.md`



## WebSessionManager

**Role:** `WebSessionManager` is the server-side, in-process session store that backs the j9t dashboard login flow. After a caller authenticates via MCP-key or gateway-header auth, it creates a cookie session here; every subsequent dashboard HTTP request validates its `HttpOnly`/`Secure`/`SameSite=Strict` cookie against this store. The store is purely in-memory — no disk persistence, no replication — so a j9t process restart silently invalidates all active sessions. This class is deliberately separate from `application/session/sessionManager.{h,cpp}`, which owns AI-query workflow state and has nothing to do with web logins.

**Public API:**

- `Create(user, role) → LoginResult` — Allocates a new `Session`, generates a 256-bit cryptographically random session ID via OpenSSL `RAND_bytes`, records `m_CreatedAt` and `m_LastActivity` as the current `steady_clock` time, inserts the session into `m_Sessions` under the mutex, and returns a `LoginResult` struct carrying the session ID, user identity, and role string. If `RAND_bytes` fails it logs `LOG_CORE_ERROR` and returns a `LoginResult` with an empty session ID; callers must check for this. Valid role strings observed in the struct definition are `"admin"`, `"operator"`, and `"viewer"`, but the method accepts any string — no validation is performed here.

- `Validate(sessionId) → std::optional<Session>` — Looks up `sessionId` in `m_Sessions` under the mutex. Returns `std::nullopt` immediately for an empty string, an unknown ID, or a session whose `m_LastActivity` is more than `m_TimeoutHours` hours ago (idle timeout, not absolute creation timeout). On timeout the session is erased in-place before returning `nullopt`. On success, slides `m_LastActivity` forward to `now` (rolling window) and returns a copy of the updated `Session`.

- `Destroy(sessionId) → void` — Removes the named session from `m_Sessions` under the mutex. Silently no-ops if the ID is unknown. This is the explicit logout path.

- `PurgeExpired() → void` — Iterates all sessions and erases any whose `m_LastActivity` is beyond the idle timeout. Acquires the mutex for the full iteration. Designed to be called from a background timer tick; does not itself schedule periodic work.

- `SetTimeoutHours(hours) → void` — Updates `m_TimeoutHours` under the mutex. If `hours` is zero or negative the value is clamped to 8. Takes effect for all subsequent `Validate` and `PurgeExpired` calls; sessions already in the map are not retroactively expired at the moment of the call.

- `GetTimeoutHours() const → int` — Returns the current idle timeout under the mutex.

**Collaborators:**

- **OpenSSL (`openssl/rand.h`)** — `RAND_bytes` is the sole source of session ID entropy. A failed call is the only error path in `Create`.
- **`engine.h` / `LOG_CORE_ERROR`** — Used to report `RAND_bytes` failures. This macro is the j9t core logging facility; no other engine state is accessed.
- **Upstream HTTP layer** — Not referenced in this file, but the contract is that the HTTP handler extracts the session cookie string and passes it directly to `Validate`; it is also responsible for setting the `HttpOnly`/`Secure`/`SameSite=Strict` cookie attributes on `Set-Cookie` responses.
- **Background scheduler** — `PurgeExpired` is a free function designed to be wired into a periodic tick by whoever owns the `WebSessionManager` instance; this file contains no scheduling logic.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`WebSessionManager` owns its mutex (`m_Mutex`, declared `mutable`) and all session data. Every public method acquires a `std::lock_guard` on `m_Mutex` for its full duration, so the class is fully thread-safe for concurrent callers. There is no internal thread; `PurgeExpired` must be driven externally. `Validate` both reads and writes the map under a single lock, so there is no TOCTOU gap between expiry check and `m_LastActivity` update. The class has no virtual destructor and is not designed for inheritance. Lifetime is determined entirely by the owner; because sessions are in-memory only, the instance must outlive all HTTP handler threads that call into it.

**Notes:**

- The idle timeout is a **sliding window** from `m_LastActivity`, not a fixed window from `m_CreatedAt`. A user who keeps the dashboard active will never be expired regardless of how long the session has existed.
- `RAND_bytes` failure produces an empty session ID string. Nothing in this class prevents that empty string from being inserted into `m_Sessions` if the caller passes the `LoginResult` back through without checking — callers must treat an empty `m_SessionId` in the returned `LoginResult` as a fatal error and not issue the cookie.
- `steady_clock` is used throughout, which is correct for interval arithmetic but means session timestamps are not wall-clock times and are not meaningful across process restarts or for logging absolute times.
- `PurgeExpired` acquires the mutex for a potentially O(n) linear scan over all live sessions. Under high session volume, long-held locks during purge could contend with concurrent `Validate` calls; callers should tune purge frequency accordingly.
- The comment in the header explicitly flags the naming hazard: **do not confuse this class with `application/session/sessionManager`**. The two are unrelated; one manages dashboard cookie auth, the other manages AI workflow query state.


---

### 72_doc_application_workflow_adhocWorkflowManager_h

<a id="72-doc-application-workflow-adhocworkflowmanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/72_doc_application_workflow_adhocWorkflowManager_h/docu.output.md`



## AdhocWorkflowManager

**Role:** `AdhocWorkflowManager` is the subsystem responsible for the full lifecycle of one-shot ("adhoc") workflow runs submitted via `POST /api/workflows/run-adhoc`. It owns folder creation and layout under `_adhoc/<user_slug>/<ts>_<counter>_del-<ts>/`, JCWF staging through `WorkflowRegistry`, per-user disk-quota enforcement, restart-safe attribution via `meta.json`, output inventory via `manifest.json`, and TTL-based garbage collection through a background reaper thread. The delete-at deadline is encoded directly in the folder name so the reaper requires no external state to determine what to remove.

---

**Public API:**

- `AdhocWorkflowManager(McpKeyManager& keyManager, WorkflowRegistry& registry)` — Constructor. Stores references to both collaborators. Does not touch the filesystem; callers must call `Init()` before any other method.

- `~AdhocWorkflowManager()` — Calls `StopReaperThread()`, which joins the background reaper thread. Blocks until the thread exits.

- `void Init(std::filesystem::path const& adhocBaseFolder)` — Initialises the manager against a filesystem root (typically `<cwd>/_adhoc`). Creates the base directory if absent. Walks existing user-slug subdirectories to reconstruct `m_RunIdToMeta`, `m_DiskUsageByUser`, and seeds `m_Counter` at `max_seen_counter + 1`. Legacy top-level run folders (pre-namespace builds, identified by having `_del-` directly at `l1` level) are skipped for attribution but remain eligible for reaping. Must be called once before `Stage()` or quota queries.

- `std::variant<StageResult, std::string> Stage(StageRequest const& req)` — Allocates and populates a run folder for a single submitted canvas. Performs a pre-check quota gate using `2 × jcwfJson.size()` as a rough proxy for final disk cost. Calls `SanitizeUserSlug()` on `req.m_User` to build the first path segment. Rewrites the top-level `"id"` field in the submitted JSON to the generated adhoc workflow ID, then calls `WorkflowRegistry::SaveOrUpdateWorkflowFromJson()` to produce the `.jcwf` zip and extracted directory. On success, writes `meta.json`, updates `m_RunIdToMeta` and `m_DiskUsageByUser`, calls `McpKeyManager::RecordDiskUsage()`, and returns a `StageResult` containing `m_RunId` (e.g. `adhoc_20260416T143022_0001`), `m_WorkflowId` (e.g. `_adhoc_20260416T143022_0001`), and `m_FolderPath`. Returns an error `std::string` on any failure; the partially created folder is removed before returning.

- `void OnRunCompleted(std::string const& runId)` — Called by the workflow runtime when a run reaches a terminal state. Recomputes actual disk usage by re-walking the folder, updates per-user accounting, and calls `McpKeyManager::RecordDiskUsage()`. For `on_completion` policy, immediately removes the run folder and its parent user-slug directory if the parent becomes empty. For all other policies, writes `manifest.json` and retains the folder until the reaper fires.

- `void Reap()` — Single-pass TTL sweep. Walks both legacy top-level run folders and namespaced `_adhoc/<user_slug>/<run>/` folders. Removes any folder whose `_del-<ts>` suffix encodes a UTC instant at or before `now`. Reads `meta.json` before deletion to decrement per-user disk accounting and erase the run from `m_RunIdToMeta`. Removes empty user-slug parent directories. Safe to call directly from tests or external tick loops.

- `void StartReaperThread()` — Spawns the `ReaperLoop` background thread, which calls `Reap()` every 60 seconds (implemented as 60 × 1 s sleeps to make `StopReaperThread()` responsive). Idempotent; a second call while the thread is running returns immediately.

- `void StopReaperThread()` — Sets `m_ReaperRunning` to `false` and joins the reaper thread. Blocks for up to ~1 s (the sleep granularity). Called automatically by the destructor.

- `size_t GetUserDiskUsageBytes(std::string const& user) const` — Returns the in-memory accumulated byte count for the given user. Acquires `m_Mutex`. Returns 0 for unknown users.

- `bool WouldExceedQuota(std::string const& user, int diskQuotaMb, size_t additionalBytes) const` — Returns `true` if `current_usage + additionalBytes > diskQuotaMb * 1024 * 1024`. Returns `false` unconditionally when `diskQuotaMb <= 0` (unlimited). Calls `GetUserDiskUsageBytes()` internally, which acquires `m_Mutex`.

- `size_t GetActiveRunCount() const` — Returns `m_RunIdToMeta.size()` under lock. Includes runs that have completed but are retained under a TTL policy.

- `size_t GetTotalDiskUsageBytes() const` — Sums all values in `m_DiskUsageByUser` under lock.

- `std::optional<RunInfo> GetRunInfo(std::string const& runId) const` — Returns a snapshot of the `RunMeta` for the given run ID, projected into `RunInfo` (user, role, owner_slug, cleanup_policy, folder_path). Returns `std::nullopt` if the run is unknown or has already been reaped. Used by artifact-retrieval route handlers to enforce ownership.

- `static std::string SanitizeUserSlug(std::string const& user)` — Pure function. Maps an arbitrary user string (e.g. `alice@company.com`) to a filesystem-safe slug. Allows `[A-Za-z0-9._@-]`; replaces all other characters with `_`. Truncates at 64 bytes. Exposed publicly so unit tests can validate slug generation without constructing a full manager instance.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `WorkflowRegistry` — Called during `Stage()` via `SaveOrUpdateWorkflowFromJson()` to persist the rewritten canvas JSON as a `.jcwf` zip file and extract it into the run's `workflows/` subdirectory. The registry owns the canonical workflow index; `AdhocWorkflowManager` drives it only at stage time.

- `McpKeyManager` — Called via `RecordDiskUsage(user, bytes)` after every disk-accounting mutation (`Stage()`, `OnRunCompleted()`, `Reap()`). The manager does not read quota limits back from `McpKeyManager`; limits are passed into `Stage()` and `WouldExceedQuota()` as `int diskQuotaMb` from the MCP key record held by the caller.

- `engine.h` — Provides `LOG_APP_INFO` and `LOG_APP_ERROR` macros used throughout for structured logging.

- `file/fileWatcher.h` — Included but not actively used. The class comment explicitly notes that direct envelope dispatch eliminated the need for `FileWatcher` registration/unregistration.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`AdhocWorkflowManager` is not copyable. It is intended to be constructed once at application startup, passed by reference to route handlers, and destroyed at shutdown.

All mutable state (`m_RunIdToMeta`, `m_DiskUsageByUser`, `m_BasePath`, `m_Counter`) is protected by `m_Mutex` (a `std::mutex`). `m_Counter` is additionally `std::atomic<uint32_t>` because `GenerateFolderName()` calls `fetch_add` outside the lock before the lock is taken inside `Stage()`. `m_ReaperRunning` is `std::atomic<bool>`.

`Reap()` acquires `m_Mutex` to snapshot the directory list, then releases it before calling `RemoveFolder()` and `ReadMeta()`, then re-acquires it to update accounting. This means a concurrent `Stage()` call can insert a new run between the directory scan and the accounting update; that run will simply be picked up on the next reaper tick.

`StopReaperThread()` must be called (or the destructor must run) before the `WorkflowRegistry` or `McpKeyManager` references are destroyed, since `ReaperLoop` calls `Reap()` which touches both. The destructor provides this guarantee for normal destruction order.

`Init()` acquires `m_Mutex` for its entire execution and must complete before any other call. It is not safe to call concurrently with `Stage()` or `Reap()`.

---

**Notes:**

- **Folder-name encodes TTL:** The reaper is entirely stateless with respect to policy — it parses the `_del-<ISO8601>` suffix from the directory name. No database or config file is consulted. `del-retain` maps to `time_point::max()` and is never removed. `del-on_completion` is a placeholder value; `OnRunCompleted()` removes the folder inline without waiting for the reaper.

- **Quota pre-check is approximate:** `Stage()` gates on `2 × jcwfJson.size()`, which is a rough estimate. The true disk cost (extracted directory, queue outputs) is not known until after staging completes and task outputs accumulate. Disk accounting is not transactional; a burst of concurrent submissions from one user could each pass the pre-check and collectively exceed quota before any of them updates `m_DiskUsageByUser`.

- **`RewriteWorkflowId` uses regex with `format_first_only`:** Only the first `"id": "..."` field in the canvas JSON is rewritten. Sub-workflow nested IDs are intentionally left unchanged. The regex pattern is `"id"\s*:\s*"[^"]*"`.

- **`ReadMeta` uses minimal string parsing, not a JSON library:** `WriteMeta` produces a known fixed structure; `ReadMeta` uses `find`-based extraction. Fields containing embedded `"` characters in their values would be misread. In practice, user and role values come from MCP key records and are validated upstream, but this is a latent fragility.

- **`owner_slug` backfill:** `ReadMeta()` re-derives `owner_slug` via `SanitizeUserSlug(user)` for `meta.json` files written by builds predating the field. This allows in-place upgrade without a migration step.

- **Empty user-slug directory pruning:** Both `OnRunCompleted()` (for `on_completion` policy) and `Reap()` attempt to `remove()` the user-slug parent directory if it becomes empty after a run is deleted. This is guarded against accidentally removing `m_BasePath` itself by checking `parentDir != m_BasePath`.

- **Legacy top-level run folders:** Folders at `_adhoc/<folder>` level (not inside a user-slug subdirectory) are identified in `Init()` and `Reap()` by the presence of `_del-` in the `l1` directory name. `Init()` skips their attribution; `Reap()` will still remove them when their TTL expires. No migration to the namespaced layout is performed.

- **`manifest.json` mtime conversion:** The file-time to `system_clock` conversion uses `ftime - file_time_type::clock::now() + system_clock::now()`, which is a portable but imprecise approximation. Clock skew between the two clocks can produce slightly inaccurate `modified_at` values in the manifest.

- **`WouldExceedQuota` acquires `m_Mutex` via `GetUserDiskUsageBytes()`; `Stage()` then acquires it again** under `std::lock_guard`. The quota check and the subsequent staging are not atomic — a TOCTOU window exists for concurrent submissions from the same user.


---

### 73_doc_application_workflow_aiCallEvents_h

<a id="73-doc-application-workflow-aicallevents-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/73_doc_application_workflow_aiCallEvents_h/docu.output.md`



## aiEvent.h

**Role:** Defines three lightweight, immutable event types — `AiCallStartedEvent`, `AiCallCompletedEvent`, and `AiCallFailedEvent` — that represent the lifecycle of a single AI provider call within the j9t workflow engine. All three derive from `Event` and are tagged with `EventCategoryAi` via the engine's macro system, allowing the event bus to route or filter them by category. They carry only the data produced at the moment of construction; no state changes after creation.

**Public API:**

- `AiCallStartedEvent(std::string probName, std::string interfaceName)` — Constructs a "call started" event. `probName` identifies the workflow probe (task unit) initiating the call; `interfaceName` names the AI provider interface being invoked (e.g. a model endpoint identifier). Both strings are move-constructed into the event, so callers can pass temporaries or `std::move` owned strings at no extra cost.

- `AiCallStartedEvent::GetProbName() const → std::string const&` — Returns the probe name supplied at construction.

- `AiCallStartedEvent::GetInterfaceName() const → std::string const&` — Returns the interface/provider name supplied at construction.

- `AiCallCompletedEvent(std::string probName, AiUsage usage, std::string finishReason)` — Constructs a "call completed" event. `usage` is an `AiUsage` value (token counts or equivalent, defined in `workflow/aiReply.h`) copied by value. `finishReason` is the provider-reported completion reason (e.g. `"stop"`, `"length"`).

- `AiCallCompletedEvent::GetProbName() / GetUsage() / GetFinishReason()` — Return the corresponding fields by const reference.

- `AiCallFailedEvent(std::string probName, AiError error)` — Constructs a "call failed" event. `error` is an `AiError` value (defined in `workflow/aiReply.h`) moved into the event, carrying failure details.

- `AiCallFailedEvent::GetProbName() / GetError()` — Return the corresponding fields by const reference.

- All three classes expose `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE` and `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY` macro expansions, which inject the static type identification and category bitmask methods required by the `Event` base class dispatch mechanism.

**Collaborators:**

- `event/event.h` — Provides the `Event` base class and the `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE` / `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY` macros. The category constant `EventCategoryAi` must be declared there or in a companion categories header included transitively.
- `workflow/aiReply.h` — Defines `AiUsage` (token/usage accounting) and `AiError` (structured error information) that are embedded by value in `AiCallCompletedEvent` and `AiCallFailedEvent` respectively. Any changes to those types directly affect the size and semantics of these events.
- The engine's event bus / dispatcher — consumes these events after they are constructed and dispatched by whatever component manages AI provider calls (not visible in this file).

**Threading / lifetime:**

These are value-type events intended to be constructed on the stack or heap at the call site, dispatched immediately, and not retained beyond the dispatch scope. Because `Event` subclasses in this pattern are typically passed by const reference to handlers and are not shared across threads by design, no internal synchronisation is provided. If the event bus posts events across thread boundaries (e.g. queued dispatch), the caller is responsible for ensuring the event object outlives the dispatch. `AiUsage` is stored by value (copied at construction), so there is no aliasing concern; `AiError` is moved in, transferring ownership cleanly.

**Notes:**

- `AiUsage` is copied, not moved, in `AiCallCompletedEvent` — if `AiUsage` ever becomes expensive to copy, the constructor signature should be updated to take it by value with an explicit move, or by move-only reference.
- The `probName` field appears in all three event types and is the primary correlation key for tracing a single AI call across start, completion, and failure events. Consumers that need to correlate events should index on this string.
- There is no event for partial/streaming progress; the lifecycle modelled here is strictly started → completed xor failed. Streaming intermediates, if needed, would require additional event types.
- `AiCallFailedEvent` and `AiCallCompletedEvent` are mutually exclusive outcomes for any given `probName`; the system does not enforce this structurally, so consumers must not assume both can fire for the same call.


---

### 74_doc_application_workflow_aiCallTaskExecutor_h

<a id="74-doc-application-workflow-aicalltaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/74_doc_application_workflow_aiCallTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## AiCallTaskExecutor

**Role:** `AiCallTaskExecutor` is the concrete `ITaskExecutor` implementation that handles `ai_call` workflow task nodes. When the workflow engine dispatches an `ai_call` task, this class resolves working directories, writes all queue artifact files (STNG, TASK, CNTX, PROB) to disk, performs synchronous pre-processing (glob expansion, office-format conversion via `markitdown`, template substitution), builds a structured `AiInvocation` envelope, and submits it to `AiRequestPool`. It supports three dispatch modes: single-envelope (common case), chunked fan-out with reduce pass (when the estimated token count exceeds `max_context_tokens`), and a concat-fallback when the reduce pass itself cannot be submitted. On successful submission the task transitions to `WaitingExternal`; the workflow runtime is unblocked asynchronously when `AiRequestPool::OnOutputFileCreated` fires after the AI reply is written to disk.

---

**Public API:**

- `Execute(WorkflowDefinition const&, WorkflowRun&, TaskDef const&, TaskInstanceState&) → bool` — Entry point called by the workflow engine for each `ai_call` task instance. Resolves the workflow base directory and task working directory via `TaskPathResolver`, localizes and template-substitutes all inline `QueueFileRef` paths and content, writes the PROV sidecar JSON (`PROV_provider.json`) if a `provider` param is declared, writes inline queue artifact files to disk, expands glob patterns in `cntx_files`, materializes non-inline CNTX files (including `markitdown` conversion for `.pdf`/`.docx`/`.xlsx`/`.pptx`/`.odt`), materializes non-inline PROB files, registers a pending entry with `AiRequestPool` via `RegisterPendingWorkflowTask`, then submits one or more `AiInvocation` envelopes. Sets `taskState.m_State` to `WaitingExternal` on success or `Failed` on any error, and populates `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`. Returns `true` if the task was successfully dispatched (i.e. is now waiting), `false` if it failed synchronously. Side effects: creates directories, writes multiple files to disk, registers a pending request in `AiRequestPool`, and may spawn a chunked aggregator holding shared state across asynchronous callbacks.

- `WriteTextFile(std::string const& filePath, std::string const& fileContent, std::string& outErrorMessage) → bool` — Static utility that creates all parent directories (via `std::filesystem::create_directories`) and writes `fileContent` to `filePath` in binary/truncate mode. Populates `outErrorMessage` on failure. Called internally by materialization helpers and exposed `public` so anonymous-namespace helpers (e.g. `WriteInlineQueueFileRefs`, `MaterializeCntxFilesFromQueueBinding`) can reach it without friendship.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ITaskExecutor` (`workflow/taskExecutor.h`) — base interface; `Execute` is the sole virtual method overridden.
- `AiRequestPool` (`workflow/aiRequestPool.h`) — receives `AllocateRequestId`, `RegisterPendingWorkflowTask`, `KickFileActivityWatchdog`, `Submit`, `Forget`, and `OnOutputFileCreated` calls. Owns the lifecycle of pending AI requests and delivers results via file-system events.
- `AiInvocation` (`workflow/aiInvocation.h`) — plain data envelope carrying messages, interface name, queue folder, PROB name, output schema, retry settings, chunk index/count, and optional timeout. Built here and handed to `AiRequestPool::Submit`.
- `JarvisAgent` / `App::g_App` (`jarvisAgent.h`) — global singleton; used only to obtain the `AiRequestPool*` pointer.
- `Core::g_Core` (`engine.h`) — global singleton; provides `GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()` and `GetConfig()` (which exposes `m_ApiInterfaces` and `m_ApiIndex` for interface resolution and `max_context_tokens` lookup).
- `TaskPathResolver` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — used for `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory`, `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath`, and `ResolvePath` (relative→absolute path normalization).
- `TemplateEngine` / `ExpandTemplate` (`workflow/templateEngine.h`) — used by `ApplySimpleTemplate` and `ExpandWithDefaults` to substitute `{{...}}` placeholders in inline file paths, content, and provider/model strings.
- `ChunkPlanner` (`content/chunkPlanner.h`) — provides `EstimateTokens` (whitespace-based approximation) and `Plan` (splits a string into markdown-boundary-aware chunks given a token budget).
- `FileWriter::Get().WriteWithHeader` (`session/fileWriter.h`) — used inside the chunked concat fallback to write the aggregated output file before signalling `OnOutputFileCreated`.
- `simdjson` — used in `TryExtractStringParam` (params JSON field lookup) and `BuildDefaultsMap` (defaults JSON flattening). SIMD-accelerated; requires padded input strings.
- `markitdown` CLI — invoked synchronously via `popen`/`_popen` to convert office-format files to Markdown. Must be installed and on `PATH`; not a C++ library dependency.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`AiCallTaskExecutor` itself is stateless (no instance data beyond the vtable pointer) and can be called from any thread, though in practice the workflow engine calls `Execute` from a single dispatch thread. The interesting threading concern is the chunked fan-out path: a `ChunkAggregator` struct is heap-allocated and owned by a `std::shared_ptr` that is captured by value in each per-chunk `onReply` lambda. The lambdas are invoked on whatever thread `AiRequestPool` uses for reply callbacks. The `ChunkAggregator::m_Mutex` guards `m_Parts`, `m_Remaining`, `m_AnyFailed`, and `m_FirstErrorMessage`. The last chunk to decrement `m_Remaining` to zero is the one that either submits the reduce envelope or writes the concat fallback — no explicit join exists; the aggregator lives as long as any lambda holds a reference. Callers must not free `AiRequestPool` while chunk callbacks may still fire. The `requestPool->Forget(requestHandle)` path is only safe to call before any chunk callback has started, i.e. before or immediately after a failed `Submit` on the first chunk that breaks the submission loop.

---

**Notes:**

- **Registration timing is intentionally deferred.** `AllocateRequestId` is called before slow I/O (markitdown can take seconds for large PDFs), but `RegisterPendingWorkflowTask` and `KickFileActivityWatchdog` are called only after all file materialization is complete. This prevents the 5-second file-activity watchdog from expiring during conversion. If the engine shuts down between `AllocateRequestId` and `RegisterPendingWorkflowTask`, the ID is silently orphaned — there is no rollback path for just the ID.

- **Office-format CNTX files are converted and promoted to inline.** After `MaterializeCntxFilesFromQueueBinding` runs, the `QueueFileRef` entries for office files have `m_HasInlineContent = true` and `m_Content` set to the markdown text. The original binary file is not sent to the AI.

- **Glob expansion applies only to `cntx_files`, not to `prob_files` or `stng_files`.** Matched files are sorted lexicographically for deterministic ordering. Zero matches produce a warning, not a failure.

- **`{{...}}` template substitution (lenient mode) runs before path localization.** Per-item workflow tasks (those with non-empty `taskState.m_InputValues`) get their inline paths and content substituted first; then absolute paths are computed. This ordering matters: a template that resolves to an absolute path must not have the working-directory prefix applied again.

- **Chunking and `output_schema` are mutually exclusive.** If both are set, schema wins, chunking is skipped, and a warning is logged. The provider may reject an oversized structured-output request.

- **The PROV sidecar is write-once.** If `PROV_provider.json` already exists in the task working directory, it is not overwritten. The file stores the `key_name` from the matching `ApiInterface` config entry (not the interface name), because replay tooling uses it to resolve API credentials.

- **`BuildDefaultsMap` is limited to two levels of JSON nesting.** Keys at depth 1 map as `defaults.<key>`, and keys at depth 2 map as `defaults.<outer>.<inner>`. Deeper nesting is silently ignored. Only `string` and `number` (integer via `get_int64`) leaf types are captured; booleans, arrays, and nulls are dropped without error.

- **`markitdown` path injection is guarded against shell metacharacters.** Paths containing `"`, `'`, `` ` ``, or `$` are rejected with a clear error rather than being passed to the shell command. This is the only injection guard; callers must ensure paths do not contain other shell-significant characters if `markitdown` is used.

- **The reduce-pass fallback inserts HTML comment separators** (`<!-- chunk N/M -->`) between partial replies so the concatenated output is distinguishable from a genuine single-pass response in downstream processing.

### 75_doc_application_workflow_aiInvocation_h

<a id="75-doc-application-workflow-aiinvocation-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/75_doc_application_workflow_aiInvocation_h/docu.output.md`



## AIAssistant — Core Data Types (`AIAssistant` namespace)

**Role:** This header defines the shared vocabulary types for all AI request dispatch in j9t. It declares the `Message`, `AiSettings`, `AiRetryPolicy`, and `AiInvocation` structs, plus the `MessageRole` and `StructuredMode` enumerations. Every component that constructs, submits, inspects, or retries an AI call passes these types across its boundary. The file contains no logic — it is purely a data-contract header consumed by `AiRequestPool`, `AiCallTaskExecutor`, and any other site that needs to describe or route a single AI request.

**Public API:**

This file exposes no methods. Its surface area is entirely composed of plain data types:

- `MessageRole` — Scoped enum with values `System`, `User`, `Assistant`. Controls how a message is presented to the model during prompt assembly.

- `Message` — Pairs a `MessageRole` with a `std::string` content body. Default role is `User`. Used as elements in `AiInvocation::m_Messages`.

- `AiSettings` — Carries per-request generation parameters: `m_Temperature` (default 0.0), optional seed (`m_Seed`), and optional token cap (`m_MaxTokens`). All fields are optional-or-defaulted so callers only fill what they need.

- `AiRetryPolicy` — Encodes the three independent retry ceilings (`m_HttpMaxAttempts = 3`, `m_OutputSchemaMaxAttempts = 3`, `m_TotalMaxAttempts = 10`) and the base backoff delay (`m_BackoffMs = 500 ms`). `AiRequestPool::Submit` consults this when deciding whether to re-dispatch a failed request.

- `StructuredMode` — Scoped enum controlling how structured/JSON output is requested from the model: `None` (no schema enforcement), `NativeJsonSchema` (provider's own JSON-schema feature), `ForcedToolShim` (emulates structured output via a tool-call shim for providers that lack native support).

- `AiInvocation` — The central dispatch record. Key fields:
  - `m_InterfaceName` — Identifies the logical AI interface configuration to resolve provider and model.
  - `m_ModelOverride` — Optional per-call model name that overrides the interface default.
  - `m_Settings` / `m_Retry` — Embedded generation parameters and retry policy.
  - `m_Messages` — Ordered prompt messages.
  - `m_OutputSchemaJson` / `m_StructuredMode` — Together specify whether and how JSON schema validation is applied to the response.
  - `m_Timeout` — Optional explicit curl timeout in milliseconds. When absent, `AiRequestPool::Submit` derives the timeout from per-interface `rate_limit.request_budget` knobs. When present (set by `AiCallTaskExecutor` from `taskDefinition.m_TimeoutMs`), it overrides that budget.
  - `m_QueueFolder` — Filesystem path used by `AiRequestPool` to persist/queue the request.
  - `m_ProbName` / `m_ProvName` — Problem and provider name tags, used for logging and routing.
  - `m_ChunkIndex` / `m_ChunkCount` — Optional chunk coordinates for split/chunked requests.
  - `m_SchemaAttemptNumber` — **Transient field, managed exclusively by `AiRequestPool::Submit`.** Callers must leave this at its default of 0; the pool increments it on each schema-validation retry. Reading it outside `AiRequestPool` is meaningful only for diagnostics.

**Collaborators:**

- `AiRequestPool::Submit` — Primary consumer. Reads all fields of `AiInvocation` to configure the HTTP call, apply retry logic, and enforce timeouts.
- `AiCallTaskExecutor` — Constructs `AiInvocation` instances from workflow `taskDefinition` records, including setting `m_Timeout` and populating `m_Messages`.
- Any component performing multi-provider auth signing or model routing that needs to interpret `m_InterfaceName`, `m_ProvName`, or `m_ModelOverride`.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`AiInvocation` is a value type with no internal synchronization. Callers construct one on the stack or heap, populate it, and pass it (typically by value or move) into `AiRequestPool::Submit`. Once submitted, the pool owns the instance and may mutate `m_SchemaAttemptNumber` during retries — callers must not retain a reference to a submitted invocation. All other types (`Message`, `AiSettings`, `AiRetryPolicy`) are likewise plain value types with no threading concerns of their own.

**Notes:**

- `m_SchemaAttemptNumber` is the only field with a write-after-submit contract. Every other field is read-only from the pool's perspective. Initialising this field to anything other than 0 before submission will corrupt the pool's retry accounting.
- The three retry ceiling fields in `AiRetryPolicy` are independent counters; exhausting any one of them can halt retries even if the others have headroom. `m_TotalMaxAttempts` acts as an absolute cap across both HTTP and schema failure modes.
- `m_Timeout` absent vs. present has a meaningful semantic difference (budget-derived vs. explicit), not just a default-value difference. Code that copies or serialises `AiInvocation` must preserve the `std::optional` distinction, not substitute a numeric zero.
- `StructuredMode::ForcedToolShim` implies the caller or pool must synthesise a tool-call wrapper around the schema; providers that natively support JSON schema should use `NativeJsonSchema` to avoid the shim overhead.


---

### 76_doc_application_workflow_aiReply_h

<a id="76-doc-application-workflow-aireply-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/76_doc_application_workflow_aiReply_h/docu.output.md`



## AiTypes (AIAssistant core value types)

**Role:** This header defines the three fundamental Plain Old Data (POD) structs that flow through the entire `AIAssistant` subsystem as the canonical representation of an AI provider interaction: `AiUsage` describes token-consumption accounting, `AiError` captures every category of failure that can occur between request dispatch and reply parsing, and `AiReply` is the top-level result object returned to callers after a completed (or failed) AI request. The file has no logic of its own — it is a shared contract header that every layer of the workflow engine (request dispatch, response parsing, schema validation, error propagation) depends on.

**Public API:**

- `AiUsage` — Value struct with three `int32_t` fields: `m_InputTokens`, `m_OutputTokens`, `m_TotalTokens`. Populated by the response parser from provider-returned token counts. No invariant is enforced between the three fields by this header; callers should not assume `m_InputTokens + m_OutputTokens == m_TotalTokens` without confirming the parser maintains it.

- `AiError` — Value struct carrying an error classification and optional detail. `m_Kind` is a `Kind` enum distinguishing `None`, `Http`, `Parse`, `SchemaValidation`, `Timeout`, `Transport`, and `Provider`. `m_HttpStatus` is meaningful only when `m_Kind == Kind::Http`; it holds the raw HTTP status code. `m_Message` is a human-readable description regardless of kind. Default-constructed state is `Kind::None` with status `0` and an empty message, representing "no error".

- `AiReply` — Top-level result struct. `m_Kind` is `Kind::Text` for plain-text completions, `Kind::Structured` for JSON-schema-constrained responses, or `Kind::Error` when the request failed at any layer. `m_Text` holds the completion string for `Kind::Text` replies. `m_StructuredJson` holds the raw JSON string for `Kind::Structured` replies. `m_Error` is populated (with `m_Kind != AiError::Kind::None`) when `AiReply::m_Kind == Kind::Error`. `m_Usage` carries token accounting and may be zero-filled if the provider did not return usage data or the request errored before a response was received. `m_FinishReason` reflects the provider's stop reason (e.g., `"stop"`, `"length"`, `"content_filter"`). `m_SystemFingerprint` carries the provider's system fingerprint field where applicable (currently an OpenAI-specific field).

**Collaborators:** This header is the shared data contract for the `AIAssistant` namespace. Response parsers construct `AiReply` values and fill `AiError` and `AiUsage` fields. Request dispatch layers read `AiReply::m_Kind` to determine success or failure. Schema validation code sets `AiError::Kind::SchemaValidation` on mismatch. Callers outside the namespace (workflow engine, Python bindings, webhook handlers) consume `AiReply` as an opaque result object.

**Threading / lifetime:** All three structs are value types with no internal synchronisation and no heap-allocated members beyond `std::string`. They are safe to copy, move, and pass across thread boundaries as long as the caller does not share a single instance between threads without external locking. No dynamic allocation beyond `std::string` internals occurs. Ownership follows normal C++ value semantics; no special destruction order is required.

**Notes:** `AiReply` default-constructs with `m_Kind = Kind::Error`, meaning an uninitialised or partially-constructed `AiReply` is always treated as an error by any code that checks `m_Kind` first. This is an intentional fail-safe: callers that forget to assign a reply will not silently process empty success data. When `m_Kind` is `Kind::Text`, `m_StructuredJson` is expected to be empty, and vice versa for `Kind::Structured`; however this header does not enforce that constraint — the parser is responsible for it. The `AiError::Kind::Provider` variant is distinct from `Kind::Http` and is intended for provider-level semantic errors returned with a 200 HTTP status (e.g., a well-formed error payload in the response body).


---

### 77_doc_application_workflow_aiRequestPool_h

<a id="77-doc-application-workflow-airequestpool-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/77_doc_application_workflow_aiRequestPool_h/docu.output.md`



## AiRequestPool

**Role:** `AiRequestPool` is the central tracking and dispatch hub for all AI inference requests inside the j9t workflow engine. It manages the full lifecycle of an `ai_call`: allocating a request identity, registering a pending entry (optionally bound to a specific workflow run/task), dispatching the HTTP request to `CurlMultiDispatcher` via `Submit`, receiving the response on the I/O thread, writing `.output.txt` or `.output.json` artifacts to the queue folder, validating structured output against a JSON schema (with bounded retry), and finally delivering a typed `AiRequestCompletion` to the tick-based workflow runtime through a non-blocking FIFO queue. It also runs a lightweight file-activity watchdog in `Update()` and supports cascade cancellation of all in-flight HTTP requests for a terminated workflow run.

---

**Public API:**

- `AllocateRequestId() → int64_t` — Atomically increments and returns the next request ID from `m_NextRequestId`. Wraps back to 1 on overflow. Callers must pair this with a nanosecond timestamp to form an `AiRequestHandle`.

- `RegisterPending(requestHandle) → AiRequestHandle` — Inserts a new `PendingEntry` into `m_PendingRequests`, keyed by `{requestId, requestTimestampNs}`. Starts the file-activity watchdog with a 5-second deadline. Returns the same handle on success, a zeroed handle if the input is invalid. Does not attach any workflow binding.

- `RegisterPendingWorkflowTask(requestHandle, workflowId, runId, taskId, outputFilePaths, outputSlotNames, expectedOutputPath) → AiRequestHandle` — Calls `RegisterPending` then enriches the entry with workflow identity and output routing metadata. If `expectedOutputPath` is non-empty, also inserts the entry into `m_PendingByOutputPath` under its canonicalized absolute path so that `OnOutputFileCreated` and `OnRequestFailed` can locate it by file path. Sorts `outputSlotNames` deterministically. Calls `QueueCompletionIfNeeded` in case the entry already completed before this call returned. Output slot-to-path mapping rules: if slot count equals file count, they are zipped 1:1; if there is one file and multiple slots, all slots map to that file; if there is one slot, it maps to the first file; otherwise `"file"` is used as the default key.

- `OnOutputFileCreated(fullFilePath) → bool` — Called from `Submit`'s curl callback after writing a `.output.txt` or `.output.json` file, and from the Test interface short-circuit. Canonicalises the path, looks up the entry in `m_PendingByOutputPath`, removes it from that map, reads the file content, marks the entry completed, notifies any `WaitForCompletion` waiters, and calls `QueueCompletionIfNeeded`. Returns `true` if the path matched a registered entry. Idempotent if the entry was already completed.

- `OnRequestFailed(expectedOutputPath, errorMessage) → bool` — Symmetric failure path to `OnOutputFileCreated`. Looks up by canonical path, marks the entry failed with the provided message, notifies waiters, queues a failed `AiRequestCompletion`. Returns `true` if the path matched. Called from the curl callback on HTTP error, parse error, or transport exception; also called by `Submit`'s catch block.

- `KickFileActivityWatchdog(requestHandle)` — Resets `m_FileActivityDeadline` on the named entry by another 5 seconds from now. No-op if the entry is already curl-dispatched or the watchdog is inactive. Should be called after each queue-file write by the executor so transient file I/O pauses do not falsely fire the watchdog.

- `TryConsumeResult(requestHandle, outWasFailed, outResponseText, outErrorMessage) → bool` — Non-blocking poll. Returns `false` if the entry exists but is not yet complete. Returns `true` and populates out-params if complete; also calls `Forget` to remove the entry. Returns `true` with `outWasFailed = true` if the handle is invalid. Not compatible with concurrent use of `TryPopCompletion` on the same entry.

- `TryPopCompletion(outCompletion) → bool` — Pops the front of the `m_Completed` queue under `m_CompletedMutex`. Intended for the workflow runtime tick loop. Completions appear here only for entries that had a workflow binding (`m_HasWorkflowBinding == true`). Never blocks.

- `Update()` — Periodic maintenance; must be called from `JarvisAgent::OnUpdate()`. Snapshots all pending entries, then for each non-completed entry checks whether the file-activity watchdog has expired (`m_FileActivityWatchdogActive && !m_CurlDispatched && now >= m_FileActivityDeadline`). If so, marks the entry failed with a descriptive message and calls `QueueCompletionIfNeeded`. Does not enforce any other timeout — per-attempt HTTP timeouts are owned by curl via `CURLOPT_TIMEOUT_MS`.

- `WaitForCompletion(requestHandle, timeoutMs, outResponseText, outErrorMessage) → bool` — Blocking wait on `PendingEntry::conditionVariable`. Returns `false` on timeout or failure. Prefer `TryPopCompletion` + `Update` for the workflow runtime. Safe for one-shot synchronous callers (e.g. assistant mode).

- `Forget(requestHandle)` — Removes the entry from `m_PendingRequests`. Safe to call even if the entry does not exist. Does not touch `m_PendingByOutputPath` (that map is cleared at completion time by `OnOutputFileCreated` / `OnRequestFailed`).

- `CancelRequestsForRun(runId)` — Collects all `m_PendingByOutputPath` entries whose `m_Context.m_RunId` matches, then calls `CurlMultiDispatcher::CancelByCancelKey` for each. The cancel key is the expected output path, set as `queryData.m_CancelKey` in `Submit`. Lock ordering is deliberate: snapshots paths under `m_OutputPathMutex` first, releases the lock, then calls into the dispatcher. Idempotent and thread-safe.

- `Submit(envelope, onReply) → bool` — The primary dispatch method. Performs in order: applies determinism defaults (temperature, seed) from `Core::g_Core->GetConfig()` when the envelope did not set them; resolves the `ApiInterface` via `ResolveInterface`; rejects empty prompts; handles the `Test` interface short-circuit (reads optional fixture file, writes `.output.txt`, calls `OnOutputFileCreated`, fires events, invokes `onReply` synchronously, returns); builds the HTTP request body via `IRequestBuilder::Create(interfaceType)->BuildBody`; resolves the API key and optional SigV4 params via `KeyManager`; computes a quota key as `"<host>|<modelFamily>"`; computes a size-aware `CURLOPT_TIMEOUT_MS` from the rate-limit budget in config (or uses `envelope.m_Timeout` if set); populates `CurlWrapper::QueryData` including `m_CancelKey = expectedOutputPath`; calls `OnSubmitHandoff` to disarm the file-activity watchdog; appends a request record to the transcript; pushes an `AiCallStartedEvent`; and calls `CurlMultiDispatcher::Submit` with a lambda callback. The lambda: parses the response via `ReplyParser`; strips a whole-reply triple-backtick fence via `StripWholeReplyFence` (incrementing `m_FenceStrips`); if the envelope declares `m_OutputSchemaJson` and the reply is not chunked, runs `SchemaValidator::Validate` and retries up to `m_Retry.m_OutputSchemaMaxAttempts` times by re-calling `Submit` with an appended correction message; writes `.output.txt` (text) or `.output.json` (structured) via `FileWriter::Get().WriteWithHeader`; calls `OnOutputFileCreated` or `OnRequestFailed`; appends a response record to the transcript; pushes `AiCallCompletedEvent` or `AiCallFailedEvent`; invokes `onReply`. Returns `false` without invoking `onReply` if dispatch could not be submitted (no interface, empty prompt, no API key, no dispatcher).

- `GetDirectDispatchInflight() → size_t` — Atomic load of `m_DirectDispatchInflight`. Incremented at `Submit` entry (after guard checks pass), decremented in the curl callback (including retry paths).

- `GetStructuredSubmissions() → uint64_t`, `GetSchemaValidationRetries() → uint64_t`, `GetSchemaValidationFailures() → uint64_t`, `GetChunkedDispatches() → uint64_t`, `GetFenceStrips() → uint64_t` — Lifetime-monotonic observability counters. Exposed to `/api/debug/signals` and the TUI dashboard.

- `Shutdown()` — Sets `m_ShuttingDown = true`, marks all pending entries as failed with `"shutdown"`, notifies all waiters, then clears both `m_PendingRequests` and `m_PendingByOutputPath`. Safe to call once from any thread; should be called before destroying the pool.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `CurlMultiDispatcher` (via `JarvisAgent::GetCurlMultiDispatcher()`) — Receives `QueryData` from `Submit` and invokes the curl callback on its I/O thread. Also used by `CancelRequestsForRun` via `CancelByCancelKey`.
- `IRequestBuilder` — Created per-call via `IRequestBuilder::Create(interfaceType)`; builds the provider-specific HTTP body (`BuildBody`) and resolves the endpoint URL (`ResolveUrl`).
- `ReplyParser` — Created per-response via `ReplyParser::Create(interfaceType, responseBody)`; extracts text content, usage, finish reason, system fingerprint, and any provider error.
- `SchemaValidator` — Instantiated in the curl callback when `m_OutputSchemaJson` is present; validates extracted JSON against the declared schema and formats errors for the model retry message.
- `KeyManager` (`Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()`) — Provides the API key and provider-specific params (e.g. AWS SigV4 region/secret) for the resolved interface.
- `ConfigParser::EngineConfig` (`Core::g_Core->GetConfig()`) — Source of `ApiInterface` definitions, rate-limit budgets, default output token counts, determinism settings, and the active API index.
- `FileWriter` (singleton `FileWriter::Get()`) — Writes `.output.txt`, `.output.json`, and chunk files with a model header.
- `AiTranscript` — Appends request and response records to `<prob>.transcript.json` for replay and debugging.
- `AiCallStartedEvent`, `AiCallCompletedEvent`, `AiCallFailedEvent` — Pushed to `Core::g_Core->PushEvent()` for the event bus.
- `IRateLimitStrategy` — Used to derive the quota key model-family component (`DeriveQuotaKey`) and estimate input token count (`EstimateInputTokens`).
- `JarvisAgent` / `App::g_App` — Accessed via `dynamic_cast<JarvisAgent*>(App::g_App)` to reach `GetCurlMultiDispatcher()`.
- `AiInvocation`, `AiReply` — The envelope and reply types defined in `workflow/aiInvocation.h` and `workflow/aiReply.h`.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `AiRequestPool` has no thread of its own. It is called from at least three threads: the workflow runtime tick thread (`RegisterPendingWorkflowTask`, `TryPopCompletion`, `Update`, `CancelRequestsForRun`), the curl I/O thread (the `Submit` lambda callback, which calls `OnOutputFileCreated`, `OnRequestFailed`, `QueueCompletionIfNeeded`), and any thread that calls `Submit` directly.
- Three separate mutexes guard three separate data structures: `m_MapMutex` protects `m_PendingRequests`; `m_OutputPathMutex` protects `m_PendingByOutputPath`; `m_CompletedMutex` protects `m_Completed`. Each `PendingEntry` has its own `mutex` for per-entry state and a `condition_variable` for `WaitForCompletion`.
- Lock ordering in `CancelRequestsForRun`: `m_OutputPathMutex` → `entry->mutex` (snapshot), then release both before calling into the dispatcher. This prevents a lock-ordering trap with the dispatcher's internal mutexes.
- `m_DirectDispatchInflight`, `m_StructuredSubmissions`, `m_SchemaValidationRetries`, `m_SchemaValidationFailures`, `m_ChunkedDispatches`, and `m_FenceStrips` are all `std::atomic` and require no additional locking.
- `AiRequestPool` is non-copyable. It should outlive all in-flight curl callbacks; `Shutdown()` must be called before destruction to unblock any threads waiting in `WaitForCompletion` and to drain internal state cleanly.
- The pool does not own `CurlMultiDispatcher`, `Core::g_Core`, or `App::g_App` — it borrows raw pointers each call and guards against null.

---

**Notes:**

- **Dual completion paths must not be mixed per entry.** `TryConsumeResult` calls `Forget` internally; if the caller also polls `TryPopCompletion` for the same entry, the `Forget` removes the entry from `m_PendingRequests` but the completion is still in `m_Completed`. This is safe only if the caller commits to one path per entry.
- **`QueueCompletionIfNeeded` is idempotent** via the `m_HasQueuedCompletion` flag. It is called from multiple sites (completion paths and `RegisterPendingWorkflowTask`) to handle the race where a completion arrives before the workflow binding is attached.
- **File-activity watchdog scope is narrow.** It fires only if curl dispatch never happens within 5 seconds of the last queue-file write. Once `OnSubmitHandoff` runs, `m_FileActivityWatchdogActive` is set to `false` and `Update()` will never fire the watchdog for that entry. Per-attempt HTTP timeouts are entirely owned by curl via `CURLOPT_TIMEOUT_MS`.
- **Schema retry re-enters `Submit` recursively from the curl I/O thread.** Each retry decrements `m_DirectDispatchInflight` before calling `Submit` (which will increment it again), preserving the inflight count invariant. Retry appends a correction `User` message to `m_Messages` and increments `m_SchemaAttemptNumber`; the cap is `m_Retry.m_OutputSchemaMaxAttempts`.
- **`StripWholeReplyFence` is conservative.** It only strips when the entire reply is a single fenced block with no intermediate ```` ``` ```` sequences. It preserves fences whose language tag is one of: `mermaid`, `dot`, `plantuml`, `graphviz`, `latex`, `tex`, `markdown`, `md`, because those formats are authored to live inside a fence for downstream renderers.
- **Chunked dispatches do not signal `OnOutputFileCreated` from the curl callback.** Each chunk writes a `.output.chunkN-of-M.txt` file; the executor's aggregator is responsible for writing the final `.output.txt` and must call `OnOutputFileCreated` itself. Schema validation is also skipped for chunked envelopes.
- **Cancel key equals expected output path.** `queryData.m_CancelKey` is set to the absolute normalised path of the expected `.output.*` file, making it unique per workflow task per run and allowing `CancelRequestsForRun` to match the dispatcher's in-flight handles without a separate ID namespace.
- **`WriteTextFile` logs a warning when given a relative path** and resolves it via `fs::absolute` before writing. All internal canonical-path lookups use `fs::absolute(...).lexically_normal().generic_string()`, so callers must register paths in the same form to avoid lookup misses.
- **The `Test` interface short-circuit** reuses `ApiInterface::m_Url` as a fixture file path. An absent or non-existent path produces the string `"test reply (no fixture configured)"`. It completes synchronously on the calling thread, not on any I/O thread.


---

### 78_doc_application_workflow_aiTranscript_h

<a id="78-doc-application-workflow-aitranscript-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/78_doc_application_workflow_aiTranscript_h/docu.output.md`



## AiTranscript

**Role:** `AiTranscript` is a stateless utility class responsible for persisting a human- and machine-readable audit trail of every AI round-trip to a `.transcript.json` file on disk. It maintains a JSON array by appending individual turn objects — one `"request"` entry per `AppendRequest` call and one `"response"` entry per `AppendResponse` call — to the same file, building up a chronological conversation log. The file path is supplied by the caller; no path is computed internally. The class carries no instance state; both methods are static.

**Public API:**

- `AppendRequest(transcriptPath, envelope, resolvedModel) → bool` — Serialises an outbound AI invocation into a `"request"` JSON object and appends it to the array at `transcriptPath`. `envelope` is an `AiInvocation` whose fields are read directly: `m_InterfaceName`, `m_Messages` (role + content for each), and `m_Settings` (`m_Temperature`, and optionally `m_Seed` and `m_MaxTokens`). `resolvedModel` is the concrete model string after any alias resolution the caller has already performed — it is written as-is. If `envelope.m_ChunkIndex` and `envelope.m_ChunkCount` are both populated, a `"chunk"` sub-object is emitted, enabling Phase 6 chunked-dispatch tracking. Side effects: reads the existing file (if any), rewrites it in full with the new entry appended. Returns `false` if the write fails; does not throw.

- `AppendResponse(transcriptPath, reply) → bool` — Serialises an `AiReply` into a `"response"` JSON object and appends it. The object shape varies by `reply.m_Kind`: `Kind::Text` emits a `"text"` string field; `Kind::Structured` emits a `"structured"` field containing the raw JSON from `reply.m_StructuredJson` (or `null` if empty, inserted verbatim without sanitisation); `Kind::Error` emits an `"error"` sub-object with `"kind"`, `"http_status"`, and `"message"` derived from `reply.m_Error`. All three branches also emit `"usage"` (`input`, `output`, `total` token counts), and conditionally emit `"finish_reason"` and `"system_fingerprint"` when those strings are non-empty. Returns `false` on write failure; does not throw.

**Collaborators:**

- `AiInvocation` (`workflow/aiInvocation.h`) — source of request metadata: interface name, model settings, message list, and optional chunk coordinates.
- `AiReply` (`workflow/aiReply.h`) — source of response data including the `Kind` discriminator, text/structured/error payloads, token usage, finish reason, and system fingerprint.
- `JsonHelper` (`json/jsonHelper.h`) — used exclusively for `SanitizeForJson()`, which escapes string values before embedding them in the hand-built JSON. Raw JSON payloads (e.g., `m_StructuredJson`) bypass sanitisation and are spliced in directly.
- `engine.h` — included in the translation unit; likely pulls in shared type definitions such as `MessageRole`.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`AiTranscript` has no instance state and no internal synchronisation. Both methods perform a read-then-write sequence (read existing file, build new content, truncate-write) that is not atomic at the OS level. Concurrent calls targeting the **same `transcriptPath`** from multiple threads or processes will race and corrupt the file. Callers are responsible for serialising access — either by ensuring a single writer per path or by supplying an external mutex. Callers own both the `AiInvocation` and `AiReply` objects; this class only reads them during the call and retains no references afterward. `std::filesystem::create_directories` is called on the parent path during every write, which is safe but redundant if the directory already exists.

**Notes:**

- The JSON array is maintained through a text-manipulation strategy: the implementation finds the last `']'` in the existing file content, strips trailing whitespace from everything before it, then appends `,\n  <newEntry>\n]\n`. This means any pre-existing file that lacks a `']'` character (truncated, empty after a partial write, or otherwise malformed) is silently discarded and a fresh single-element array is written in its place.
- `m_StructuredJson` is inserted into the output without passing through `SanitizeForJson`. If it contains characters that are invalid in a JSON value context the resulting transcript file will be malformed.
- Timestamps are generated with millisecond precision in UTC ISO 8601 format (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.mmmZ`) via platform-appropriate `gmtime_s` / `gmtime_r`. The timestamp is captured at the moment of the `Append*` call, not at the moment the request was dispatched or the response was received.
- The chunking convention described in the header comment (`<prob>_chunk<N>.transcript.json` plus an aggregate file) is a stated future design intent; the current implementation performs no special path manipulation for chunks — chunk metadata is only emitted as a `"chunk"` JSON field when the optional members are set.
- Floating-point temperature is serialised with `std::to_string`, which on most platforms produces a fixed six-decimal-place representation (e.g., `0.700000`). This is not configurable.

### 79_doc_application_workflow_dataflowResolver_h

<a id="79-doc-application-workflow-dataflowresolver-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/79_doc_application_workflow_dataflowResolver_h/docu.output.md`



## DataflowResolver

**Role:** `DataflowResolver` is the input-wiring layer of the j9t workflow engine. Before any task executes, this class walks the task's declared input fields and populates them with concrete string values by consulting three sources in priority order: explicit dataflow edges defined in the `WorkflowDefinition`, live run context stored in `WorkflowRun::m_Context`, and static default values declared on each `TaskIOField`. After all values are collected it performs a second pass that expands `{{inputs.x}}` template placeholders inside those values. The class deliberately does not execute tasks, mutate workflow state, or schedule work — it is a pure read-only resolver whose only output is a `TaskResolvedInputs` bag.

**Public API:**

- `ResolveInputsForTask(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskId) → std::optional<TaskResolvedInputs>` — Primary entry point. Iterates every input declared in `taskDefinition.m_Inputs`, attempts resolution via the three-source priority chain (dataflow edges → run context → default), then injects any additional key/value pairs from matching `DataflowDef::m_Mapping` entries on edges whose `m_ToTask` equals `taskId`. Finally expands templates across all resolved values. Returns `std::nullopt` only when template expansion fails. Returns a `TaskResolvedInputs` with a non-empty `m_ErrorMessage` (and an early return, but *not* `nullopt`) when a required input cannot be resolved — callers must check both the optional and `m_ErrorMessage` to detect all failure modes. Optional inputs that remain unresolved are silently omitted from `m_StringValues`.

- `TryResolveFromDataflowEdges(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, targetTaskId, targetInputName, resolvedValueOut) → bool` — Scans `workflowDefinition.m_Dataflows` for the first edge where `m_ToTask == targetTaskId` and `m_ToInput == targetInputName`. On a match it looks up the source task's `TaskInstanceState` in `workflowRun.m_TaskStates` and reads `m_OutputValues[m_FromOutput]` into `resolvedValueOut`. Returns `false` — without setting `resolvedValueOut` — if no edge matches, if the source task is absent from `m_TaskStates`, or if the named output key does not exist in that state. All three failure cases log an error via `LOG_APP_ERROR`; only the no-edge-found case is silent.

- `ExpandTemplates(rawValue, inputValues, expandedOut) → bool` — Thin wrapper around the freestanding `ExpandTemplate` function from `workflow/templateEngine.h`. Builds a `TemplateContext` pointing to `inputValues`, calls `ExpandTemplate` in `TemplateMode::Strict`, and writes the result to `expandedOut`. Returns `false` and logs on any expansion error. The strict mode means unrecognised `{{inputs.x}}` tokens cause failure rather than pass-through.

**Collaborators:**

- `workflowTypes.h` — Provides `WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, `TaskDef`, `TaskIOField`, `TaskInstanceState`, and `DataflowDef` (aliased as `DataflowEdge` in comments). These are the only data structures the resolver reads; it never writes back to them.
- `engine.h` — Pulled in for the `LOG_APP_ERROR` macro used throughout error paths.
- `workflow/templateEngine.h` — Supplies the `ExpandTemplate` free function, `TemplateContext`, and `TemplateMode::Strict`. The resolver delegates all template parsing and substitution to this module.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`DataflowResolver` holds no mutable state — both data members and all methods are effectively const (the methods are declared `const`). The default constructor and destructor are trivially generated. Instances may be stack-allocated, embedded in larger objects, or shared across threads without synchronisation as long as the `WorkflowDefinition` and `WorkflowRun` objects passed in are not concurrently mutated by another thread during the call. The class owns nothing and manages no heap resources of its own.

**Notes:**

- **Dual failure modes in `ResolveInputsForTask`:** a missing required input returns a *valid* `optional` containing a `TaskResolvedInputs` whose `m_ErrorMessage` is non-empty, while a template-expansion failure returns `std::nullopt`. Callers must handle both cases; testing only `has_value()` will silently swallow required-input errors.
- **Mapping injection happens after input iteration:** the `m_Mapping` entries from matching dataflow edges (Step 1b) are injected *after* the per-input loop, so they can add keys that are not declared in `taskDefinition.m_Inputs`. This means `m_StringValues` may contain keys not present in `taskDefinition.m_Inputs` and those injected values are still subject to template expansion in Step 2.
- **First-edge-wins semantics:** `TryResolveFromDataflowEdges` stops at the first matching edge. If two `DataflowDef` entries share the same `(m_ToTask, m_ToInput)` pair, only the one that appears first in `m_Dataflows` takes effect; the second is silently ignored.
- **Template expansion is self-referential:** `ExpandTemplates` is given the already-partially-resolved `m_StringValues` map as its context, so a template in one value can reference a sibling input resolved earlier in the same pass. There is no cycle detection; a value that references itself would be handled (or fail) according to `ExpandTemplate`'s own logic in `templateEngine`.
- **Source task must already be complete:** `TryResolveFromDataflowEdges` reads from `workflowRun.m_TaskStates[fromTask].m_OutputValues` with no check that the source task has actually finished. Callers (typically the scheduler/engine) are responsible for ensuring upstream tasks have populated their outputs before this resolver is invoked.
