### 80_doc_application_workflow_filter_filterEngine_h

<a id="80-doc-application-workflow-filter-filterengine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/80_doc_application_workflow_filter_filterEngine_h/docu.output.md`



## FilterEngine

**Role:** `FilterEngine` is the per-item expansion engine inside the j9t workflow runtime. Given a `FilterDef` that describes a data source, it reads that source, applies optional range and query constraints, and returns a flat list of `FilterItem` values that the runtime then uses to fan out a `per_item` task into one task instance per item. Four source kinds are supported natively: CSV files, plain-text line files, a tab-delimited local index file evaluated against a parsed query AST, and Polarion ALM work-item queries that go out over a live HTTP connection via `PolarionClient`. All source-kind dispatch happens inside the single public entry point `Evaluate`; the private per-kind helpers are implementation detail.

---

**Public API:**

- `Evaluate(FilterDef const& filter, std::string const& workflowBaseDir, std::string& errorMessage) const → std::vector<FilterItem>` — Main entry point. Inspects `filter.m_Source.m_Kind` and dispatches to the appropriate private evaluator (`csv`, `text_lines`, `query`, `polarion_query`). Relative paths in `FilterSource::m_Path` / `m_IndexPath` are resolved against `workflowBaseDir` before opening files. After the per-kind evaluator returns, `AddPaddedIndices` is always called on the result before returning. On any failure (bad kind, unreadable file, parse error, unknown connection) the return value is an empty vector and `errorMessage` is set to a human-readable string that includes `filter.m_Id`. Callers must check `errorMessage` rather than the vector's size alone, because a legitimately empty source also returns an empty vector.

---

**Private methods of note:**

- `EvaluateCsv` — Opens the resolved path with `std::ifstream`, reads an optional header row to build column-name mappings, then iterates data rows. Each `FilterItem` receives `index`, `row_number`, `line`, `col_N` (positional), and per-header-name fields. The range filter (`FilterSource::m_Range`, 1-based inclusive) is applied row-by-row: rows before `rangeStart` are skipped; reading stops when `dataRowNumber > rangeEnd`. `filter.m_MaxItems > 0` caps the output and emits a `LOG_CORE_WARN`. The stable key (`m_Key`) is the first column's value, or the row number if the row is empty.

- `EvaluateTextLines` — Opens the file, optionally skips blank lines when `FilterSource::m_SkipEmpty` is true (uses `Trim` for the blank check). Each item receives `index`, `row_number` (0-based line counter including skipped lines), and `text`. The stable key is the raw line content. `m_MaxItems` is enforced the same way as in `EvaluateCsv`.

- `EvaluateQuery` — Delegates expression parsing to `QueryParser::Parse`, which returns a query AST. The index file is a plain line-delimited text file where each line is tab-separated key-value pairs (`field\tvalue\tfield\tvalue…`). Each line is decoded into a `QueryDocument` (a string-to-string map) and then tested against the AST via `QueryParser::Matches`. Only matching documents become `FilterItem` entries. If `FilterSource::m_Fields` is non-empty, only those named fields are copied into `m_Values`; otherwise all fields are included. If the document contains a `doc_path` field it overrides `m_SourcePath` (used downstream for freshness checks). Stable key is the `id` field if present, otherwise the raw line number.

- `EvaluatePolarionQuery` — If `FilterSource::m_Connection` is non-empty it looks up the connection by name via `Core::g_Core->GetCloudConnectionManager().GetConnection(...)`. The connection must have type `"polarion"` or an error is returned. `m_Endpoint`, `m_KeyName`, and the `project_id` param from the connection are written into a copied `FilterDef` (`resolvedFilter`) before passing to `PolarionClient::FetchAll`. This is the only evaluator that does network I/O.

- `AddPaddedIndices(std::vector<FilterItem>&)` — Scans the item list to find the largest `m_Index` and largest `m_SourceIndex`, computes zero-pad widths (minimum 3 digits), and injects `index_padded` and, when a `row_number` key is already present, `row_number_padded` into every item's `m_Values`. Called unconditionally by `Evaluate` after each per-kind evaluator.

- `ParseRange(std::string const& range, size_t& outStart, size_t& outEnd) → bool` — Parses `"N"`, `"N-M"`, `"N-"`, or `"-M"` into 1-based inclusive bounds. A lone `"-"` is invalid. Zero values are invalid. Returns `false` on any malformed input and leaves `outStart`/`outEnd` in an undefined state. All values are `size_t`; open-ended upper bounds are set to `SIZE_MAX`.

- `SplitCsvLine(std::string const& line, char delimiter) → std::vector<std::string>` — RFC-4180-style parser: respects `"..."` quoting, treats `""` inside quotes as a literal double-quote. Does not strip surrounding whitespace from field values; callers that need trimming must do it themselves.

- `Trim(std::string const& s) → std::string` — Strips space, tab, CR, and LF from both ends.

---

**Collaborators:**

| Collaborator | Role |
|---|---|
| `FilterDef` / `FilterSource` (workflow/workflowTypes.h) | Carries all configuration: kind, path, delimiter, header flag, range, query string, field list, connection name, max_items. |
| `QueryParser` (workflow/filter/queryParser.h) | Parses the query expression string into an AST and evaluates it against `QueryDocument` records in `EvaluateQuery`. |
| `PolarionClient` (workflow/filter/polarionClient.h) | Performs Polarion REST calls and returns pre-built `FilterItem` lists in `EvaluatePolarionQuery`. |
| `CloudConnectionManager` (cloud/cloudConnectionManager.h) | Resolves named connections (`m_Connection`) to endpoint URL, key name, and extra params. Accessed via the global `Core::g_Core` singleton. |
| `Core` / `Engine` (core.h / engine.h) | Provide the global `g_Core` pointer and the `LOG_CORE_WARN` / `LOG_APP_INFO` macros used for operational logging. |

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`FilterEngine` holds no mutable state and all methods are `const`. It is safe to call `Evaluate` concurrently from multiple threads on the same `FilterEngine` instance, provided the `FilterDef` arguments are not concurrently mutated. The `PolarionClient` instance is constructed on the stack inside `EvaluatePolarionQuery` and is not shared. Access to `Core::g_Core` is done without any lock visible in this file; callers must ensure the core and its `CloudConnectionManager` are fully initialized and remain alive for the duration of the call. `FilterEngine` itself has no heap-allocated members and requires no special destruction order.

---

**Notes:**

- `m_Index` in `FilterItem` is the 0-based position in the returned result set, not in the source. `m_SourceIndex` is the original row or line number in the source (1-based for CSV data rows, 0-based for text lines). This asymmetry is intentional but easy to misread.
- Binding prefix stripping: keys in `FilterItem::m_Values` deliberately omit any variable binding prefix. The runtime is responsible for prepending the binding when injecting values into task instances. Do not add prefixes inside the evaluators.
- `row_number_padded` is only injected when a `row_number` key already exists in `m_Values`. For the `query` kind, where `row_number` is not written by `EvaluateQuery`, `row_number_padded` will be absent even after `AddPaddedIndices` runs.
- `ParseRange` treats a bare single number `"N"` as the range `N–N`, selecting exactly one row.
- The `EvaluateQuery` index format is not a binary index or SQLite file; it is a hand-rolled tab-delimited text format. Lucene/Whoosh support is noted in a comment as a future path via the Python bridge.
- `filter.m_MaxItems == 0` means unlimited across all four evaluators; any positive value is a hard cap that also emits a warning log.
- `EvaluatePolarionQuery` makes a deep copy of `FilterDef` (`resolvedFilter`) before patching in connection-derived values, so the original `FilterDef` passed by the caller is never modified.
- Unknown `m_Kind` values cause an error return rather than a panic, making it safe to add new kinds incrementally without crashing the engine.


---

### 81_doc_application_workflow_filter_filterManifest_h

<a id="81-doc-application-workflow-filter-filtermanifest-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/81_doc_application_workflow_filter_filterManifest_h/docu.output.md`



## FilterManifestManager (`workflow/filter/filterManifest.h` / `.cpp`)

**Role:** Provides persistent, file-backed change-detection bookkeeping for j9t's filter evaluation pipeline. After the `filterEngine` produces a list of `FilterItem` results for a given `FilterDef`, this class serialises a snapshot of those results (per-item source paths, file modification times, and a hash of the filter expression) to a JSON manifest file on disk. On subsequent runs it reads the saved manifest back, diffs it against the newly computed evaluation, and classifies every item as new, changed, removed, or unchanged — giving the workflow engine the information it needs to skip redundant reprocessing.

---

**Public API:**

- `BuildManifest(filterId, items, filterDef) → FilterManifest` — Constructs an in-memory `FilterManifest` from a completed filter evaluation. Calls `NowIso8601()` for the `evaluated_at` timestamp, `ComputeQueryHash(filterDef)` for the `query_hash`, and `FileMtimeString(item.m_SourcePath)` for each item's `source_mtime`. No I/O is performed. Inputs must be the live results from the filter engine for the named `filterId`.

- `WriteManifest(manifest, workflowBaseDir, errorMessage) → bool` — Serialises a `FilterManifest` to `<workflowBaseDir>/<filterID>/<filterID>.manifest.json`. Creates all intermediate directories with `std::filesystem::create_directories`. Builds JSON manually (no external JSON library for output). Opens the file with `std::ios::trunc`, overwriting any prior manifest. On failure, populates `errorMessage` with a human-readable reason and returns `false`. Emits an `INFO` log line via `LOG_APP_INFO` on success.

- `ReadManifest(filterId, workflowBaseDir, manifestOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Reads and deserialises a previously written manifest. Returns `false` (with `errorMessage` set) if the file does not exist, cannot be opened, or fails `simdjson` parsing. Partial parse results are not guaranteed to be cleared on failure; callers should treat `manifestOut` as undefined on a `false` return. Field extraction uses simdjson's on-demand API; missing JSON fields are silently skipped, leaving the corresponding struct member at its zero-initialised default.

- `CompareManifests(previous, current) → FilterManifestDiff` — Performs a key-based diff between two in-memory manifests. If `query_hash` differs, `m_ExpressionChanged` is set to `true`, all current indices are pushed into `m_NewItemIndices`, and the function returns early — no per-item comparison is done. Otherwise, it builds an `unordered_map` keyed on `m_Key` from the previous manifest and classifies each current item as new (`m_NewItemIndices`), mtime-changed (`m_ChangedIndices`), or unchanged (`m_UnchangedIndices`). Keys present in previous but absent from current go into `m_RemovedKeys`. No I/O.

- `static ComputeQueryHash(filterDef) → std::string` — Produces a SHA-256 hex digest (lowercase, 64 chars) of a semicolon-delimited canonical string built from `filterDef.m_Source` fields: `m_Kind`, `m_Path`, `m_Delimiter`, `m_HasHeader`, `m_Range`, `m_SkipEmpty`, `m_IndexPath`, `m_Query`, `m_Connection`, `m_BaseUrl`, `m_ProjectId`, and all `m_Fields` entries in order. Requires OpenSSL at compile time (`openssl/sha.h`); falls back to `std::hash<std::string>` if OpenSSL is absent, producing a decimal string instead of a hex digest. **Callers comparing hashes across builds compiled with different OpenSSL availability will get incompatible values.**

- `static ManifestPath(filterId, workflowBaseDir) → std::string` — Returns the canonical on-disk path `<workflowBaseDir>/<filterId>/<filterId>.manifest.json` using `std::filesystem::path` composition. Pure function; no I/O.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `workflow/filter/filterEngine.h` — Supplies `FilterItem` (fields: `m_Index`, `m_Key`, `m_SourcePath`) and `FilterDef` (nested `m_Source` struct with all the canonical-string fields). This file is a direct consumer of the engine's output, not a replacement for it.
- `engine.h` — Provides the `LOG_APP_INFO` macro used in `WriteManifest`.
- `simdjson` — Used exclusively for JSON reading in `ReadManifest`. JSON writing is done manually via `std::ostringstream` with a hand-rolled `JsonEscape` helper.
- `openssl/sha.h` — Optional; detected at compile time via `__has_include`. Controls whether `ComputeQueryHash` produces a proper SHA-256 hex digest or a `std::hash` fallback.
- `std::filesystem` — Used for path construction, directory creation, existence checks, and `last_write_time` retrieval.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`FilterManifestManager` is a stateless class — it holds no member data and all meaningful state lives in the `FilterManifest` value types returned or passed by the caller. Instances can be constructed on the stack or as members without concern for shared ownership. All public methods are `const` (except the static helpers). The class itself imposes no locking; **concurrent calls to `WriteManifest` for the same `filterId` and `workflowBaseDir` are not safe** — the caller must serialise access to a given manifest file. `ReadManifest` and `CompareManifests` are read-only with respect to the file system and in-memory state respectively, so concurrent reads of the same file are safe at the OS level provided no concurrent write is in progress.

---

**Notes:**

- `FileMtimeString` uses a portable clock-offset trick (`file_clock::now() - ftime + system_clock::now()`) to avoid `file_clock::to_sys()`, which is C++20 but unimplemented on MSVC at the time this was written. This introduces a small race-window skew (sub-millisecond in practice) between the two `now()` calls; it is not a reliable sub-second mtime comparison mechanism. For change detection purposes this is acceptable since filesystem mtimes are typically second-granularity.
- If `item.m_SourcePath` is empty or the file does not exist at `BuildManifest` time, `FileMtimeString` returns an empty string. On a subsequent `CompareManifests`, an item whose `source_mtime` was `""` in both manifests will be classified as **unchanged**, even if the file was later created or modified.
- `JsonEscape` handles `"`, `\`, `\n`, `\r`, and `\t` only. It does not escape control characters below 0x20 (other than the three listed) nor does it produce `\uXXXX` sequences. Manifests containing such characters in paths or keys will produce technically non-conformant JSON, though simdjson's on-demand reader will likely tolerate them on round-trip.
- The `m_ExpressionChanged` short-circuit in `CompareManifests` means that when the filter expression changes, **nothing is placed in `m_ChangedIndices` or `m_UnchangedIndices`** — all current items land in `m_NewItemIndices`. Callers must not assume `m_ChangedIndices` is populated when `m_ExpressionChanged` is `true`.
- `item_count` in the JSON is written from `manifest.m_ItemCount` (set to `items.size()` in `BuildManifest`) but is not validated against the actual length of the `items` array on read. If the JSON is hand-edited so the two diverge, `m_ItemCount` will reflect the literal field value while `m_Items.size()` will reflect actual parsed entries.
- The canonical string for `ComputeQueryHash` includes `m_Fields` entries in iteration order. If the filter engine ever presents fields in a non-deterministic order, the hash will be unstable across runs even when the logical filter is unchanged.


---

### 82_doc_application_workflow_filter_polarionClient_h

<a id="82-doc-application-workflow-filter-polarionclient-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/82_doc_application_workflow_filter_polarionClient_h/docu.output.md`



## PolarionClient

**Role:** `PolarionClient` is the Polarion ALM REST API adapter inside the j9t workflow engine's filter subsystem. It handles the full lifecycle of interacting with Polarion's JSON:API v1 interface: paginated bulk retrieval of work items (with per-page disk spilling to bound memory), single-item creation and field updates, attachment download and upload, and linked-item traversal for traceability graphs. All HTTP is done with raw libcurl handles (not via the engine's `CurlWrapper` except for CA-bundle discovery). Credentials are resolved through `KeyManager` using a `key_name` that maps to a Polarion Personal Access Token stored in the provider's `m_ApiKey` field.

---

**Public API:**

- `FetchAll(filter, workflowBaseDir, errorMessage) → std::vector<FilterItem>` — The primary entry point. Resolves the Bearer token from `KeyManager` using `filter.m_Source.m_KeyName`, creates the output directory `<workflowBaseDir>/<filter.m_Id>/` if absent, then drives a page loop starting at page 1. Each page is fetched with up to `MAX_RETRIES` (3) attempts with a 1-second sleep between retries. Each retrieved `FilterItem` is immediately written to disk as `<filterId>-<k>.json` before being appended to the result vector; this bounds peak heap use to one page of parsed items. Stops when a page returns fewer items than `page_size`, when `filter.m_MaxItems` is reached, or on any unrecoverable error. On error returns an empty vector and populates `errorMessage`.

- `HttpGet(url, bearerToken, responseBody, errorMessage) → bool` — Synchronous HTTP GET using a fresh `curl_easy_*` handle per call. Sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>` and `Accept: application/vnd.api+json`. Applies `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (30 s). Probes `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` for a system CA bundle. Returns `false` for curl transport errors or any HTTP ≥ 400 status; error body is appended to `errorMessage` if under 500 bytes.

- `HttpRequest(method, url, bearerToken, requestBody, responseBody, errorMessage) → bool` — Generic HTTP method dispatch via `CURLOPT_CUSTOMREQUEST`. Sends `Content-Type: application/vnd.api+json` and `Accept: application/vnd.api+json`. Used internally by `UpdateWorkItem` and `CreateWorkItem` for PATCH and POST respectively. `requestBody` may be empty; if non-empty it is passed as `CURLOPT_POSTFIELDS` with an explicit size, so binary-safe. Same error handling and 30 s timeout as `HttpGet`.

- `HttpDownloadFile(url, bearerToken, outputPath, errorMessage) → bool` — Streams a binary response body directly to a file opened with `std::fopen(..., "wb")`. Uses `TIMEOUT_SECONDS * 3` (90 s) to accommodate large attachments. File is opened before the curl handle performs the transfer; on curl or HTTP error the file is left on disk in an incomplete state — callers must check the return value and delete the output if needed.

- `HttpUploadFile(url, bearerToken, filePath, fileName, responseBody, errorMessage) → bool` — Builds a `curl_mime` multipart form with a single part named `"file"` using `curl_mime_filedata` (libcurl reads the source file internally). Uses 90 s timeout. Response body is captured and returned in `responseBody`.

- `UpdateWorkItem(baseUrl, projectId, workItemId, bearerToken, jsonApiPatchBody, responseBody, errorMessage) → bool` — Constructs `PATCH /rest/v1/projects/{project_id}/workitems/{work_item_id}` and delegates to `HttpRequest`. The caller is responsible for forming a valid JSON:API PATCH document in `jsonApiPatchBody`.

- `CreateWorkItem(baseUrl, projectId, bearerToken, jsonApiPostBody, responseBody, errorMessage) → bool` — Constructs `POST /rest/v1/projects/{project_id}/workitems` and delegates to `HttpRequest`. Caller supplies the JSON:API resource creation document.

- `DownloadAttachment(baseUrl, projectId, workItemId, attachmentId, bearerToken, outputPath, errorMessage) → bool` — Constructs `GET .../attachments/{attachmentId}/content` and delegates to `HttpDownloadFile`.

- `UploadAttachment(baseUrl, projectId, workItemId, bearerToken, filePath, fileName, responseBody, errorMessage) → bool` — Constructs `POST .../attachments` and delegates to `HttpUploadFile`.

- `FetchLinkedWorkItems(baseUrl, projectId, workItemId, bearerToken, responseBody, errorMessage) → bool` — `GET .../linkedworkitems`; raw JSON:API response is returned unparsed in `responseBody` for the caller to interpret. No pagination is applied.

- `UrlEncode(value) → std::string` (static) — Percent-encodes a string using `curl_easy_escape` on a throw-away curl handle. Falls back to a manual loop if `curl_easy_init()` fails; the fallback only guarantees unreserved RFC 3986 characters pass through, everything else gets `%XX`-encoded.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `workflow/filter/filterEngine.h` — Provides `FilterDef` (holds `m_Id`, `m_MaxItems`, `m_Source`), `FilterSource` (holds `m_BaseUrl`, `m_ProjectId`, `m_Query`, `m_Fields`, `m_PageSize`, `m_KeyName`), and `FilterItem` (holds `m_Index`, `m_SourceIndex`, `m_Key`, `m_Values`, `m_SourcePath`). These are the primary data carriers throughout the class.

- `keys/keyManager.h` / `core.h` — `FetchAll` calls `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager().GetProvider(source.m_KeyName)` to obtain the credential record. The `m_ApiKey` field of the returned provider is used as the Bearer token. `Core::g_Core` is a global singleton; no local reference is held past the method call.

- `curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h` — Only `CurlWrapper::GetCaBundlePath()` is used, to find a distribution-appropriate CA bundle path for `CURLOPT_CAINFO`. All actual HTTP operations use raw libcurl, not the wrapper's higher-level interface.

- `simdjson` — `ParseJsonApiPage` uses `simdjson::ondemand::parser` for streaming JSON:API response parsing. The padded string and parser are stack-local per call.

- `libcurl` — Each HTTP method allocates and destroys its own `CURL*` handle per call. There is no shared curl multi handle or connection pool.

- `engine.h` — Provides `LOG_APP_INFO` and `LOG_APP_WARN` macros used for per-page progress and retry warnings.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`PolarionClient` is a stateless value type — it holds no member variables, no curl handles, no cached credentials, and no mutable state. It may be constructed on the stack, heap, or as a member of another object with no special considerations. All methods are `const` (except the static helpers). Because each HTTP call creates and destroys its own `CURL*` handle, concurrent calls from different threads are safe as long as `curl_global_init` has already been called once elsewhere in the process (which the engine's startup is expected to guarantee). `FetchAll` calls `Core::g_Core->GetKeyManager()` — access to that global must be safe from the calling thread; the engine's existing convention is that `KeyManager` is read-only after startup and requires no lock for reads. `WriteItemFile` performs synchronous filesystem I/O on the calling thread; there is no async dispatch inside the class.

---

**Notes:**

- **Incomplete file on download failure.** `HttpDownloadFile` opens the output file before performing the transfer and does not delete it on failure. Callers must check the return value and clean up the file themselves if `false` is returned.

- **Pagination termination heuristic.** The page loop exits when `pageItems.size() < pageSize`. This is standard practice but means a page that lands exactly on the boundary causes one additional request that returns an empty `data` array. That empty-array response is valid and results in zero items, terminating the loop on the next iteration.

- **ID stripping in `ParseJsonApiPage`.** Polarion's JSON:API `id` field uses the compound form `"ProjectKey/WI-NNN"`. The parser stores the full string in `m_Values["id"]` and `m_Key`, and additionally stores the substring after the last `/` in `m_Values["work_item_id"]`. Any code consuming `FilterItem` values should prefer `work_item_id` when constructing write-back URLs and prefer `id` for equality comparisons.

- **Rich-text field extraction.** When parsing attributes, fields that are Polarion rich-text objects (`{"type":"text/plain","value":"..."}`) are unwrapped and only their `"value"` sub-string is stored. Fields that are neither plain strings nor this specific object shape are silently dropped.

- **`fields` selection in `ParseJsonApiPage`.** If `filter.m_Source.m_Fields` is empty, all attributes returned by the server are extracted. If the vector is non-empty, only the listed field names are extracted. This selective extraction is applied only to the `attributes` object; `id` and the derived `work_item_id` are always populated regardless.

- **`WriteItemFile` JSON escaping.** The file writer applies only a six-character escape table (`"`, `\`, `\n`, `\r`, `\t`, and the default passthrough). Unicode characters and control characters outside that set are written verbatim. JSON consumers that are strict about encoding may reject files containing bare control characters from Polarion rich-text content.

- **`UrlEncode` allocates a curl handle per call.** `BuildPageUrl` calls `UrlEncode` multiple times per page. If `curl_easy_init` has measurable overhead in the deployment environment, this is worth profiling for high-frequency filter refresh scenarios.

- **Trailing-slash normalisation.** `BuildPageUrl`, `UpdateWorkItem`, `CreateWorkItem`, `DownloadAttachment`, `UploadAttachment`, and `FetchLinkedWorkItems` all strip a trailing `/` from `base_url` before appending path segments. The `FilterSource::m_BaseUrl` value may therefore be supplied with or without a trailing slash.

- **`goto done`** is used in `FetchAll` to break from the inner item loop when `max_items` is reached, exiting both the item iteration and the page `while` loop cleanly. The label is at the end of the function body, just before the final log and return.

### 83_doc_application_workflow_filter_queryParser_h

<a id="83-doc-application-workflow-filter-queryparser-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/83_doc_application_workflow_filter_queryParser_h/docu.output.md`



## QueryParser (`workflow/filter/queryParser.h` + `.cpp`)

**Role:** Implements a self-contained Lucene-style query language for filtering workflow documents inside the `AIAssistant` namespace. It provides a recursive-descent tokenizer and parser that converts a query string into an owned AST (`QueryNode` tree), plus a static evaluator that tests a flat key-value document (`QueryDocument`) against that tree. The grammar supports `AND`, `OR`, `NOT`, prefix negation (`-field:value`), parenthesised grouping, exact field matching, wildcard/prefix matching (`field:prefix*`), and inclusive/exclusive range expressions (`field:[lo TO hi]`, `field:{lo TO hi}`). Range comparisons attempt numeric (`double`) interpretation first and fall back to lexicographic ordering.

---

**Public API:**

- `QueryParser::Parse(queryString, errorMessage) → std::unique_ptr<QueryNode>` — Tokenizes then parses the query string via recursive descent. Returns a fully-formed AST on success. On any tokenization or parse error, returns `nullptr` and writes a human-readable description into `errorMessage`. Callers must check for `nullptr` before using the result; `errorMessage` is the only diagnostic. Input may not be empty; an empty string is an immediate error. Tokens not recognized as punctuation, quoted strings, keywords (`AND`, `OR`, `NOT`, `TO`), or word characters are silently skipped by the tokenizer rather than propagated as errors.

- `static QueryParser::Matches(node, doc) → bool` — Evaluates a previously parsed AST against a `QueryDocument` (a `std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>`). Walks the tree recursively. `And` nodes short-circuit on first false child; `Or` nodes short-circuit on first true child. A `Not` node with a null `m_Child` evaluates to `false`. `FieldMatch` does exact or prefix comparison; `FieldRange` applies inclusive/exclusive bounds. Missing fields always produce `false`. This is a pure function with no side effects.

- `ParserState::Current() → Token const&`, `Peek(offset) → Token const&`, `Advance()`, `AtEnd() → bool` — Cursor helpers on the token vector. `Current()` and `Peek()` return a static EOF token when the position is out of range rather than undefined behaviour. `ParserState` is declared `public` so that the `.cpp` translation unit can use it in the static recursive-descent helpers; callers outside the class should not construct or mutate it directly.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `QueryNode` / `QueryNodeType` — The AST types defined in the same header. `QueryNode` is a recursive struct owned entirely via `std::unique_ptr`; `QueryParser` produces and consumes it but does not retain it.
- `QueryDocument` (`std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>`) — The document model that callers must supply to `Matches`. Constructed and owned entirely by callers; `QueryParser` never mutates it.
- No external libraries beyond the C++ standard library. No dependency on other j9t subsystems (secrets manager, webhook dispatcher, etc.); this is a pure filtering utility.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`QueryParser` holds no mutable state — all parsing state is carried in a local `ParserState` on the stack inside `Parse`. The object itself is therefore stateless and can be shared freely across threads without synchronisation. `Parse` is `const`-qualified; `Matches` is `static`.

The returned `std::unique_ptr<QueryNode>` tree is owned exclusively by the caller. Once `Parse` returns, `QueryParser` retains no references into the tree or the original query string. `Matches` takes the tree by `const&`, so the caller may evaluate the same AST concurrently from multiple threads without any locking.

`ParserState` stores a raw pointer to the `std::vector<Token>` local to `Parse`; it must not outlive that call frame. This is already guaranteed by the implementation, but any future refactor that moves `ParserState` out of scope should be aware of this raw-pointer lifetime.

---

**Notes:**

- **Wildcard stripping:** When a `FieldMatch` value ends with `*`, the trailing `*` is removed from `m_Value` before the node is stored. `m_IsWildcard` is the flag to check; do not re-inspect `m_Value` for the asterisk.
- **Minus tokenization rule:** A `-` is only emitted as `TokenType::Minus` when the very next character is non-whitespace. A standalone `-` surrounded by spaces is silently dropped (falls through to the unknown-character skip path).
- **Silent unknown-character skip:** The tokenizer increments `i` and continues on any character that does not match any recognised class. No error is set. This means malformed input may partially parse into a valid-looking but semantically incorrect AST rather than returning an error.
- **Range comparison uses `double`:** Both bounds and the document value are passed through `std::stod`. Values that parse as doubles are compared numerically; all others fall back to `std::string::compare` (byte-lexicographic). Mixed types (one numeric, one non-numeric) will throw in one path and succeed in the other, ultimately using lexicographic comparison for the whole expression.
- **`Not` with null child:** `Matches` guards `node.m_Child &&` before recursing in the `Not` case and returns `false` if the child is null. The parser itself never produces a `Not` node with a null child, but the guard prevents undefined behaviour if a caller constructs nodes manually.
- **AND/OR are not implicit:** Adjacent field expressions without an explicit `AND` or `OR` keyword are not joined automatically. The parser will report an "unexpected token" error rather than inferring conjunction.
- **Keywords are case-sensitive:** `AND`, `OR`, `NOT`, `TO` must be uppercase; `and`, `or`, etc. are treated as `Word` tokens (field names or values).


---

### 84_doc_application_workflow_internalTaskExecutor_h

<a id="84-doc-application-workflow-internaltaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/84_doc_application_workflow_internalTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## InternalTaskExecutor

**Role:** `InternalTaskExecutor` is the `ITaskExecutor` specialisation responsible for dispatching *internal* tasks — named, compiled-in operations registered under string action keys — within the j9t workflow engine. When the workflow runner encounters a task whose type resolves to internal, this class takes over: it resolves all file paths relative to the workflow and task working directories, validates that declared input files physically exist, creates any missing parent directories for declared outputs, extracts the `"action"` key from the task's JSON params blob, looks up the corresponding `ITask` factory in an `IInternalTaskRegistry`, instantiates a fresh `ITask`, and drives it to completion, writing success or failure state back into `TaskInstanceState`.

---

**Public API:**

- `InternalTaskExecutor(std::shared_ptr<IInternalTaskRegistry> const& internalTaskRegistryPtr)` — constructs the executor and stores a shared pointer to the registry. Passing a null `shared_ptr` is allowed; the executor will then fall back to `App::g_App->GetInternalTaskRegistry()` at execution time, so callers must ensure either the injected registry or the global `App` singleton is valid before `Execute` is called.

- `Execute(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun& workflowRun, TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState& taskState) → bool` — executes a single internal task. Reads `workflowDefinition` for base-directory and file-path metadata; reads `workflowRun` for logging context (workflow ID and run ID only, not mutated); reads `taskDefinition` for `m_WorkingDirectory`, `m_FileInputs`, `m_FileOutputs`, `m_ParamsJson`, and `m_Id`; and writes the result into `taskState.m_State` (`TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded` or `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed`) and `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`. Returns `true` on success, `false` on any failure. Side effects include: creating the task working directory via `std::filesystem::create_directories`, creating parent directories for all declared output paths, and invoking `ITask::Execute` on the resolved input/output path vectors.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `ITaskExecutor` / `taskExecutor.h` — abstract base whose `Execute` contract this class implements.
- `IInternalTaskRegistry` / `ITask` (`task/internalTaskRegistry.h`) — the registry is queried via `CreateTask(action)` to obtain a freshly-owned `std::unique_ptr<ITask>`; `ITask::Execute(inputs, outputs, errorOut)` does the actual work.
- `TaskPathResolver` (`workflow/taskPathResolver.h`) — provides `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory`, `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath`, and `ResolveTaskScopedPath`; all path resolution is delegated entirely to this utility rather than performed inline.
- `WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, `TaskDef`, `TaskInstanceState` — plain data structures passed in by the workflow runner; this file does not own them.
- `App::g_App` (`jarvisAgent.h`) — global application singleton used as a fallback source for the registry when the injected `shared_ptr` is null. This creates an implicit dependency on the global singleton being initialised before `Execute` runs.
- `simdjson` — used in the file-local helper `TryGetActionFromParamsJson` to parse `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` and extract the mandatory `"action"` string field. The parser and padded string are stack-allocated per call.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`InternalTaskExecutor` itself holds no mutable state beyond the `shared_ptr` to the registry, which is set at construction and never reassigned. The registry pointer is therefore safe to read from `Execute` without additional locking, provided the `shared_ptr` is not concurrently replaced from another thread (there is no mechanism to do so). The `ITask` instance created per call is owned by a local `std::unique_ptr` and is destroyed at the end of `Execute`; callers must not cache or share it. If `Execute` is called concurrently on the same `InternalTaskExecutor` instance, safety depends entirely on the thread-safety of the underlying `IInternalTaskRegistry::CreateTask` implementation, which this file does not control. The fallback path through `App::g_App` is inherently unsafe under concurrent first-use scenarios if the global singleton itself is not yet fully initialised.

---

**Notes:**

- The `"action"` field is the only field the executor itself reads from `m_ParamsJson`; the full params JSON blob is passed through unchanged to `ITask::Execute` indirectly (via the resolved path vectors). If `"action"` is absent, empty, or the JSON is malformed, `Execute` returns `false` immediately with a descriptive error in `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`.
- Input path validation (`ValidateExistingInputs`) checks existence synchronously at execution time, before the task runs. There is no retry or tolerance for files that may appear later; a missing input is an immediate hard failure.
- Output parent directories are created unconditionally before the task runs, even if the task ultimately fails or does not produce those files. Callers should not assume the presence of an output parent directory implies the task succeeded.
- The two-path registry lookup (injected `shared_ptr` → `App::g_App` fallback) means the behaviour of a null-injected executor is silently dependent on global state. Prefer always injecting a non-null registry in production setup to avoid the fallback path.
- Extensive `LOG_APP_INFO` / `LOG_APP_ERROR` calls with `[paths debug]` and `[folder creation debug]` tags are present throughout `Execute`; these are diagnostic instrumentation left in production code and will produce verbose output at INFO level during every task execution.
- `ITask::Execute` is expected to return `false` and populate its `errorOut` string on failure; an empty `errorOut` on failure is tolerated and replaced with the generic string `"Internal task failed."`.


---

### 85_doc_application_workflow_jcwfContainer_h

<a id="85-doc-application-workflow-jcwfcontainer-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/85_doc_application_workflow_jcwfContainer_h/docu.output.md`



## JcwfContainer

**Role:** `JcwfContainer` is a stateless utility class responsible for all I/O operations on `.jcwf` workflow package files, which are standard ZIP archives following a specific internal layout. It handles the full lifecycle of these packages: creation (Pack), deployment (Extract), inspection (ListEntries, ReadFile, IsValidContainer), and staleness detection (IsExtractedStale). Within the j9t engine, `.jcwf` files are the distribution and storage format for workflow definitions — each archive holds a `global.json` metadata file, a root canvas task DAG JSON, and an arbitrarily nested tree of sub-workflow subdirectories, each containing its own DAG JSON. All ZIP operations are delegated to miniz; the class itself holds no state.

**Public API:**

- `Extract(jcwfPath, targetDir, errorMessage) → bool` — Opens the ZIP at `jcwfPath` with miniz and iterates every entry, recreating the directory tree under `targetDir`. Creates `targetDir` (and any missing parent directories) if absent. For directory entries it calls `create_directories`; for file entries it calls `mz_zip_reader_extract_to_file`. On any extraction failure the archive is closed and `errorMessage` is populated with the failing entry name. On success, logs the path pair and entry count via `LOG_APP_INFO`. Returns `false` and stops at the first failing file entry.

- `Pack(sourceDir, jcwfPath, errorMessage) → bool` — Validates that `sourceDir` is a directory, then uses `mz_zip_writer_init_file` to create (or overwrite) the ZIP at `jcwfPath`. Walks `sourceDir` with `recursive_directory_iterator`; relative paths are converted to forward-slash archive names via `generic_string()`. Directory entries are written as zero-byte entries with a trailing `/`. Regular files are compressed with `MZ_BEST_COMPRESSION`. Calls `mz_zip_writer_finalize_archive` before closing; logs file count on success. `fileCount` counts only regular files, not directory entries. Silently skips entries for which `relative()` sets an error code.

- `ReadFile(jcwfPath, internalPath, outContent, errorMessage) → bool` — Extracts a single named entry from the ZIP into a heap buffer via `mz_zip_reader_extract_file_to_heap`, assigns the raw bytes to `outContent`, then frees the buffer with `mz_free`. `internalPath` must use forward slashes and match the exact entry name stored in the archive (case-sensitivity follows the underlying filesystem/miniz behaviour). Does not log on success or failure; sets `errorMessage` on failure only.

- `ListEntries(jcwfPath) → std::vector<std::string>` — Returns every entry name (files and directory markers) as stored in the ZIP central directory, in archive order. Returns an empty vector on any error, including a missing or malformed file; no `errorMessage` out-parameter is provided so callers cannot distinguish error from a genuinely empty archive.

- `IsValidContainer(jcwfPath) → bool` — Attempts to open the file as a ZIP via `mz_zip_reader_init_file` and immediately closes it. Returns `true` if and only if miniz accepts the file signature; does not validate internal structure (presence of `global.json`, etc.).

- `IsExtractedStale(jcwfPath, extractedDir) → bool` — Compares `last_write_time` of `jcwfPath` against `last_write_time` of `extractedDir` (the directory itself, not its contents). Returns `true` if the directory does not exist, if either `last_write_time` call fails, or if the ZIP's mtime is strictly greater than the directory's mtime. Returns `false` if the ZIP does not exist, on the assumption that a missing source cannot make the extraction stale.

**Collaborators:**

- `miniz` (`miniz.h`) — sole ZIP backend; all `mz_zip_*` and `mz_free` calls come from this single-header library. Callers must ensure miniz is compiled into the translation unit.
- `engine.h` — provides the `LOG_APP_INFO` macro used in `Extract` and `Pack` for structured logging. No other engine functionality is used.
- `std::filesystem` — used throughout for path manipulation, directory creation, iteration, and mtime comparison.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`JcwfContainer` has no instance state; every method is `static`. There are no mutexes or shared resources inside this class. However, all operations perform direct filesystem I/O and must not be called concurrently on the same `jcwfPath` or `targetDir` from multiple threads without external synchronisation — particularly `Pack` (which overwrites the output file) and `Extract` (which writes into a directory). `ReadFile` and `ListEntries` are read-only with respect to the archive and are safe to call concurrently on different `jcwfPath` values.

**Notes:**

- `IsExtractedStale` uses only the directory's own mtime, not the mtimes of files inside it. On most filesystems, extracting into an existing directory does not update the directory's mtime, so a partial or interrupted previous extraction may not be detected as stale. Callers requiring robust change detection should delete the extracted directory before re-extracting rather than relying solely on this check.
- `Pack` silently ignores entries for which `std::filesystem::relative` sets an error code. There is no warning or log for skipped entries.
- `ListEntries` provides no way for callers to distinguish an empty archive from a failed open. If distinguishing these cases matters, call `IsValidContainer` first.
- Entry name length in `Extract` and `ListEntries` is hard-capped at 1023 characters by the fixed `filename[1024]` buffer passed to `mz_zip_reader_get_filename`. Archive entries with longer names will be silently truncated.
- `Pack` uses `mz_zip_writer_init_file`, which creates or truncates the output file immediately. A failure mid-pack (e.g. disk full) will leave a partial or empty `.jcwf` at `jcwfPath`; there is no rollback or temp-file-then-rename pattern.
- Internal path matching in `ReadFile` is performed by miniz's own lookup, which is typically case-sensitive; callers must supply exact entry names as returned by `ListEntries`.


---

### 86_doc_application_workflow_pythonTaskExecutor_h

<a id="86-doc-application-workflow-pythontaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/86_doc_application_workflow_pythonTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## PythonTaskExecutor

**Role:** `PythonTaskExecutor` is the concrete `ITaskExecutor` implementation responsible for running a single workflow task whose logic is written in Python. It resolves file-system paths, validates declared input files, assembles a context dictionary and a call-arguments map, delegates actual Python execution to a pooled interpreter via `PythonEnginePool`, captures stdout/stderr to both in-memory state and on-disk log files, and writes the task's output values back into `TaskInstanceState` before marking the task as succeeded or failed.

---

**Public API:**

- `Execute(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState) → bool` — Entry point called by the workflow engine for every Python task. Takes the immutable workflow definition and task definition by `const&`, and mutates `workflowRun` (reads its context map) and `taskState` (writes `m_State`, `m_LastErrorMessage`, `m_OutputValues`, `m_CapturedStdout`, `m_CapturedStderr`). Side effects in order:
  1. Resolves the workflow base directory and task working directory through `TaskPathResolver`.
  2. Creates the task working directory via `fs::create_directories`; fails the task if creation fails.
  3. Validates that every path listed in `taskDef.m_FileInputs` exists on disk; fails the task with a descriptive message if any are missing.
  4. Builds a `contextValues` map from `workflowRun.m_Context` and injects four synthetic keys: `_workflow_id`, `_run_id`, `_workflow_base_directory`, `_task_working_directory`. Also injects `_file_input_N` (zero-based) for each resolved file input.
  5. Builds `callArguments` from `taskState.m_InputValues`; if the task declares exactly one file output and at least one output slot whose name contains the substring `"Path"`, and that argument is not already present, injects the resolved output path for that argument.
  6. Calls `PythonEnginePool::ExecuteWorkflowTask` with the fully resolved arguments.
  7. Stores up to 1 024 UTF-8-safe characters of captured stdout/stderr in `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` / `taskState.m_CapturedStderr`. Writes the full streams to `stdout.txt` / `stderr.txt` inside the task working directory (removes the file if the stream is empty).
  8. Merges Python-returned outputs into `taskState.m_OutputValues`, then fills any still-missing output keys using `TaskPathResolver::BuildOutputSlotMap` as a fallback.
  9. Sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded` on success or `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed` on any error, and returns the corresponding `bool`.

---

**Collaborators:**

| Collaborator | Role |
|---|---|
| `ITaskExecutor` (`taskExecutor.h`) | Abstract base class; `Execute` is the sole virtual override. |
| `PythonEnginePool` (`python/pythonEnginePool.h`) | Acquired from `App::g_App->GetPythonEnginePool()`; performs the actual embedded-Python call. If the pointer is null the task fails immediately. |
| `TaskPathResolver` (`taskPathResolver.h`) | Provides `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory`, `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath`, `ResolvePath`, `ResolveTaskScopedPath`, and `BuildOutputSlotMap` — all path normalisation and output-slot derivation logic lives here. |
| `WorkflowDefinition` | Supplies workflow-level path fields (`m_WorkflowFilePath`, `m_WorkflowBaseDirectory`, their absolute counterparts, etc.) and is read-only inside `Execute`. |
| `WorkflowRun` | Provides `m_WorkflowId`, `m_RunId`, and the `m_Context` map (key/value pairs with `.m_Value`). |
| `TaskDef` | Supplies `m_Id`, `m_WorkingDirectory`, `m_FileInputs`, `m_FileOutputs`, `m_Outputs`, `m_ParamsJson`, and is read-only. |
| `TaskInstanceState` | The primary output surface; `Execute` writes state, error messages, output values, and captured I/O streams into it. |
| `App::g_App` | Global application singleton; used to obtain the `PythonEnginePool` pointer. |
| `Core::g_Core` | Optional global core singleton; queried for `GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()` only for diagnostic logging. |
| `TruncateUtf8Safe` | Free function used to safely cap stdout/stderr to 1 024 characters without splitting a UTF-8 code point. |

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`PythonTaskExecutor` holds no member state — every field it touches is either a parameter or a local variable. Instances can in principle be created on the stack or heap and discarded after a single `Execute` call. The class does not manage the lifetime of `PythonEnginePool`; that pool is owned by the `App` singleton and must outlive any call to `Execute`. Because `PythonEnginePool` is a pool, it is expected to handle its own internal synchronisation across concurrent task executions, but `PythonTaskExecutor` itself imposes no additional locking. Callers must ensure `App::g_App` is non-null and fully initialised before invoking `Execute`.

---

**Notes:**

- The output-path injection heuristic is keyed on the substring `"Path"` (case-sensitive) appearing in an output field name. Any output slot whose name does not contain that substring will not receive an auto-injected path value, even when the task declares a single file output.
- The auto-injection of output paths into `callArguments` is guarded by a `contains` check; an explicitly provided value in `taskState.m_InputValues` always wins over the derived path.
- `TaskPathResolver::BuildOutputSlotMap` is a non-destructive fallback: it only writes keys that are absent from `taskState.m_OutputValues` after the Python call returns, preventing the fallback from overwriting actual Python-produced results.
- If `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson` is empty the code logs an error but does not fail the task before calling `ExecuteWorkflowTask`; the pool itself will be the first layer to reject the malformed task.
- `stdout.txt` and `stderr.txt` are written in binary mode with `std::ios::trunc`. If either stream is empty the corresponding file is actively removed from the working directory, keeping the directory clean of zero-byte artefacts.
- Extensive `[paths debug]` and `[folder creation debug]` log lines are emitted at `INFO` and `ERROR` level throughout; these are diagnostic-grade and are always active regardless of build type.
- The `_file_input_N` keys injected into `contextValues` give Python code access to resolved absolute paths without polluting the function's keyword-argument signature — useful when the Python function's signature is fixed and cannot accept arbitrary `**kwargs`.


---

### 87_doc_application_workflow_scriptCatalog_h

<a id="87-doc-application-workflow-scriptcatalog-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/87_doc_application_workflow_scriptCatalog_h/docu.output.md`



## ScriptCatalog

**Role:** `ScriptCatalog` (in `AIAssistant`) is a thread-safe, in-process registry of all shell, Python, and PowerShell scripts residing under the `scripts/` directory. It builds its catalog by recursively scanning that directory and parsing a lightweight `@jarvis-script` metadata block from the first 60 lines of each file. The resulting entries are served to MCP agents via `GET /api/scripts` so they can discover and reference pre-deployed scripts when composing ad-hoc JCWF workflows. Python scripts additionally receive a dotted module name (e.g. `scripts.extractChapters`) that JCWFs use in `params.module` to invoke them through the embedded Python runtime.

---

**Public API:**

- `Refresh(scriptsBaseFolder: std::filesystem::path) → void` — Discards the current catalog, walks `scriptsBaseFolder` recursively via `std::filesystem::recursive_directory_iterator`, and calls the private `ParseFile` on every `.sh`, `.py`, or `.ps1` regular file (skipping `__init__.py`). After parsing, entries are sorted ascending by `m_Path`. Logs the final count via `LOG_APP_INFO`. Acquires `m_Mutex` for the entire operation, so callers block until the scan is complete. Safe to call from any thread, including on hot-reload triggered by a file watcher.

- `List(typeFilter: std::string = {}) → std::vector<Entry>` — Returns a snapshot copy of all catalog entries. Pass `"shell"` or `"python"` to restrict to that type; pass an empty string to get everything. Acquires `m_Mutex` and returns by value, so the snapshot is stable after the call returns regardless of concurrent `Refresh` calls.

- `GetByPath(relativePath: std::string) → std::optional<Entry>` — Linear scan of `m_Entries` looking for an entry whose `m_Path` exactly matches the given CWD-relative path string (e.g. `"scripts/parseOpenSshLog.sh"`). Returns `std::nullopt` on miss. Primarily used for shell scripts; Python lookup should prefer `GetByModule`.

- `GetByModule(moduleName: std::string) → std::optional<Entry>` — Linear scan looking for an entry whose `m_Module` exactly matches the given dotted module name (e.g. `"scripts.extractChapters"`). Returns `std::nullopt` on miss. Only meaningful for Python entries; `m_Module` is empty for shell entries.

- `GetRootDirectory() → std::filesystem::path` — Returns the path passed to the most recent `Refresh` call, or a default-constructed path if `Refresh` has never been called.

- `Size() → size_t` — Returns the count of entries currently in the catalog.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` — Pulled in by the `.cpp` for the `LOG_APP_INFO` macro used in `Refresh`. No other coupling to engine internals is visible in this file.
- JCWF workflow runner — consumes `Entry::m_Path` (shell invocation) and `Entry::m_Module` (Python invocation via the embedded runtime). The path strings stored here are expected to pass the `scripts/` security boundary check enforced elsewhere in the engine.
- MCP HTTP layer — calls `List()` to populate the `GET /api/scripts` response; callers are expected to serialize the returned `Entry` structs themselves.
- File watcher — expected to call `Refresh` when the `scripts/` directory changes; `ScriptCatalog` has no internal watching mechanism.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `ScriptCatalog` owns its data exclusively (`m_Entries`, `m_RootDirectory`) and protects them with `m_Mutex` (declared `mutable` to allow locking in `const` methods). All six public methods acquire a `std::lock_guard` before touching shared state.
- `Refresh` holds the lock for the full directory scan, including file I/O in `ParseFile`. On a large `scripts/` tree this can be a non-trivial hold time; callers should not call `Refresh` on a latency-sensitive thread.
- `List`, `GetByPath`, and `GetByModule` return copies, so callers hold no reference into the internal `m_Entries` vector after the call returns. There is no iterator invalidation risk for callers.
- `ScriptCatalog` has no special construction or destruction requirements. It is not copyable or movable in any thread-significant way; treat it as a singleton or a long-lived object owned by the engine or HTTP server layer.

---

**Notes:**

- `ParseFile` reads at most `kMaxMetadataLines` (60) lines per file, keeping catalog refresh cost proportional to file count rather than file size.
- The `@description:` and `@outputs:` tags support multi-line continuation: any subsequent comment line that does not start a new `@tag:` is appended to the previous tag's value with a single space separator. `@short:` and `@params:` are deliberately treated as single-line only.
- `@params:` values are split on commas, spaces, and tabs by `SplitParams`, so both `arg1 arg2` and `arg1,arg2` are valid.
- Python module names are derived from the file's path relative to `rootDir.parent_path()` (one level above `scripts/`), with `/` replaced by `.` and `.py` stripped. A file at `scripts/foo/bar.py` becomes module `scripts.foo.bar`. `__init__.py` files are skipped entirely at the scan stage.
- `m_Path` is stored relative to `rootDir.parent_path()` (i.e. the launch CWD), not relative to `scriptsBaseFolder` itself. This is intentional so that the string can be passed directly to the security boundary check without transformation.
- `m_Executable` reflects the OS-level execute permission bits at scan time; it is not re-checked on lookup. A `Refresh` is required to pick up permission changes.
- `m_HasJarvisMarker` records whether `@jarvis-script` was found but the catalog does not filter on it — scripts without the marker are still cataloged. Callers that want to restrict to properly annotated scripts must filter on this field themselves.
- The `Refresh` scan silently stops iterating if the `recursive_directory_iterator` sets an error code mid-traversal (`if (ec) break`), which can result in a partial catalog with no error surfaced to the caller beyond the `LOG_APP_INFO` count being lower than expected.


---

### 88_doc_application_workflow_shellTaskExecutor_h

<a id="88-doc-application-workflow-shelltaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/88_doc_application_workflow_shellTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## ShellTaskExecutor

**Role:** `ShellTaskExecutor` is the `ITaskExecutor` implementation responsible for running external shell scripts as workflow task nodes inside the j9t engine. Given a `TaskDef` whose `params` JSON carries a `command` field pointing to a script under `scripts/` and an optional `args` array, it validates the script path and each argument against an injection-safety allowlist, expands JCWF `{{...}}` template macros (including `{{input[N]}}` / `{{output[N]}}`) against the task's current input values and file lists, assembles an argv-style command line, and dispatches execution via either a `popen`/`_popen` pipe (all platforms, no timeout) or a `fork`/`exec`/`poll` watchdog path (POSIX only, when `TaskDef::m_TimeoutMs > 0` and a `TaskWatchdog` is attached). After execution it persists full stdout and stderr to `stdout.txt` / `stderr.txt` in the task working directory, stores a 1024-character truncated copy in `TaskInstanceState` for the UI tooltip, maps `file_outputs` back to named output slots in `taskState.m_OutputValues` for downstream dataflow, and marks the task `Succeeded` or `Failed` accordingly.

---

**Public API:**

- `Execute(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun& workflowRun, TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState& taskState) → bool` — Main entry point, called by the engine dispatch loop for tasks of type `shell`. Performs the full pipeline: path resolution → directory creation → input existence verification → output parent directory creation → JSON params parsing (`simdjson::ondemand`) → command path validation and `.sh`→`.ps1` remapping on Windows → template expansion → argument safety checks → subprocess execution → output file writing → `taskState.m_OutputValues` population. Sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed` and `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` on any error before returning `false`. Sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Succeeded` on clean zero exit. Exit code 124 is treated as an inactivity timeout failure.

- `static void ProbeWindowsShell(bool useBashConfig)` *(Windows only)* — Must be called once at application startup. If `useBashConfig` is `false`, locks `s_WindowsShell` to `WindowsShell::PowerShell`. If `true`, runs `bash --version >NUL 2>&1` via `std::system`; on success selects `WindowsShell::Bash`, otherwise falls back to PowerShell and logs a warning. Result is cached in the process-wide static `s_WindowsShell`.

- `static WindowsShell GetWindowsShell()` *(Windows only)* — Returns the cached `WindowsShell` enum value (`PowerShell` or `Bash`). Callers outside this class can inspect the selection; internally used to choose the subprocess invocation strategy.

---

**Private helpers (internal, documented for contributors):**

- `ValidateScriptPath(std::string const& path) → bool` — Enforces that the raw `command` field starts with `scripts/` and that its `std::filesystem::path::lexically_normal()` form still starts with `scripts/`, preventing `scripts/../etc/passwd`-style escapes. Implements JCWF spec §3.1.2 path confinement.

- `IsSafeArgument(std::string const& argument) → bool` — Rejects any argument containing control characters or the shell-injection characters `;`, `&`, `|`, `>`, `<`, `'`, `"`, `` ` ``. Applied to every expanded argument before it is added to `argumentList`. Not a full sandbox — it is a belt-and-suspenders check layered on top of `ValidateScriptPath`.

---

**Collaborators:**

| Collaborator | Role |
|---|---|
| `ITaskExecutor` (`taskExecutor.h`) | Abstract base; `Execute` is the overridden virtual method |
| `TaskDef` | Source of `m_ParamsJson`, `m_FileInputs`, `m_FileOutputs`, `m_Outputs`, `m_WorkingDirectory`, `m_TimeoutMs`, `m_Environment.m_Variables`, `m_Id` |
| `TaskInstanceState` | Read for `m_InputValues`, `m_Watchdog`; written for `m_State`, `m_OutputValues`, `m_LastErrorMessage`, `m_CapturedStdout`, `m_CapturedStderr` |
| `WorkflowDefinition` | Passed to `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` to anchor relative paths |
| `TemplateEngine` (`workflow/templateEngine.h`) | `ExpandTemplate()` in `Strict` mode expands `{{...}}` macros in each raw argument |
| `TaskPathResolver` | `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` and `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath` supply absolute filesystem paths |
| `TaskWatchdog` | POSIX watchdog path: `Kick()` resets the inactivity timer; `ElapsedSinceLastKickMs()` is polled every 500 ms |
| `Core::g_Core` | `GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()` is used to resolve relative `command` paths and locate `.ps1` siblings on Windows |
| `simdjson::ondemand` | Zero-copy JSON parsing of `TaskDef::m_ParamsJson` to extract `command` and `args` |
| `TruncateUtf8Safe` | Used to cap `m_CapturedStdout` / `m_CapturedStderr` to 1024 characters without splitting a UTF-8 codepoint |

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `Execute` is intended to be called from whichever thread the engine's task-dispatch loop runs on. It is **not** re-entrant with respect to `g_ShellTaskExecutorCurrentPathMutex`, which serialises all calls that go through `ExecuteCommandWithCapturedOutput`. This mutex exists because `popen` on some platforms touches the process-wide working directory state; the comment explicitly warns that `std::filesystem::current_path` must **not** be changed inside `Execute` because the file watcher runs concurrently and depends on it.

- `ExecuteCommandWithWatchdog` (POSIX only) does **not** acquire `g_ShellTaskExecutorCurrentPathMutex`; it uses `fork`/`chdir` in the child, so the parent's CWD is untouched and concurrent tasks are safe on that path.

- `s_WindowsShell` is a process-wide static written once by `ProbeWindowsShell` (call before any tasks run) and thereafter read-only. No lock guards it; callers must ensure `ProbeWindowsShell` completes before any `Execute` calls begin.

- `ShellTaskExecutor` itself has no member state beyond the vtable; all per-execution state is local to `Execute` or in the passed `TaskInstanceState`. Instances may be stack-allocated or heap-allocated and shared freely after `ProbeWindowsShell` has run.

---

**Notes:**

- **"Option B" auto-injection:** If the raw `args` array from JCWF contains no `{{input[...}}` or `{{inputs}}` macro, `EnsureDefaultInputOutputArgs` prepends individual `{{input[0]}}`, `{{input[1]}}` … tokens (one per entry in `m_FileInputs`). Similarly, if no output macro is present, it appends `{{output[0]}}` … . This is intentional ergonomic sugar for file-pipeline tasks that do not want to spell out every argument explicitly. Workflows that already include any input or output macro get exactly what they specify.

- **Output-slot mapping (`BuildOutputSlotMap`):** When `m_FileOutputs.size() == m_Outputs.size()`, output slot names are zipped with `m_FileOutputs` by index. Any remaining output slot with no matching file output falls back to the input value of the same name in `taskState.m_InputValues`. This two-phase fallback is the contract relied on by the `make-example.jcwf` integration test.

- **Windows `.sh` → `.ps1` auto-remapping:** When `s_WindowsShell == PowerShell` and the `command` field ends in `.sh`, the executor looks for a `.ps1` sibling at the same relative path. If found it silently substitutes it; if not found it hard-fails with an instructive error. This lets `.jcwf` files remain portable without per-platform conditionals in the workflow YAML.

- **Stderr capture via temp file:** `ExecuteCommandWithCapturedOutput` cannot capture both stdout (through the `popen` pipe) and stderr simultaneously without a second pipe. It therefore redirects stderr to `.ja_stderr_tmp` inside the task working directory, reads it after the pipe closes, then deletes it. If the process is killed mid-run the temp file may be left on disk.

- **Exit code 124 convention:** Both execution paths set `exitCode = 124` to signal an inactivity timeout (matching the `timeout(1)` Unix convention). `Execute` checks this value explicitly and emits a distinct error message; callers should not need to distinguish it from other non-zero codes at the engine level.

- **Argument quoting on POSIX:** `JoinArgumentsForSystem` applies POSIX single-quote wrapping (`QuoteForPosixShell`) only to arguments that contain whitespace. Arguments without whitespace are emitted verbatim, relying on `IsSafeArgument` to have already excluded metacharacters. This is a deliberate performance trade-off; fully quoting every argument would be safer but was not chosen.

- **No support for inline shell commands:** `ValidateScriptPath` enforces the `scripts/` prefix, meaning `command` must always be a file path — not a bare executable name like `make` or `python3`. Workflows requiring generic tool invocations must wrap them in a script under `scripts/`.


---

### 89_doc_application_workflow_subWorkflowTaskExecutor_h

<a id="89-doc-application-workflow-subworkflowtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/89_doc_application_workflow_subWorkflowTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## SubWorkflowTaskExecutor

**Role:** `SubWorkflowTaskExecutor` is a concrete `ITaskExecutor` implementation that handles tasks whose job is to launch a child workflow as a sub-process of the currently running parent workflow. When the engine encounters a task with a `workflow_file` field set, this executor resolves that file path relative to the parent workflow's directory, looks the child workflow up in the `WorkflowRegistry` by its canonical absolute path, enqueues a new run of it via `WorkflowRuntimeManager`, and then transitions the parent task to `WaitingExternal` state rather than completing it immediately. Completion is deferred: the runtime manager owns the responsibility of resuming the parent task once the child run finishes, using the parent-child link registered during `Execute`.

**Public API:**

- `SubWorkflowTaskExecutor(WorkflowRegistry const* workflowRegistry) → void` — Constructor. Takes a non-owning pointer to the workflow registry. The registry pointer must remain valid for the lifetime of the executor. The runtime manager is intentionally not injected here because it is constructed after executors are registered; callers must follow up with `SetRuntimeManager`.

- `SetRuntimeManager(WorkflowRuntimeManager* runtimeManager) → void` — Late-binding injection of the runtime manager. Must be called before any `Execute` call is made. If this is not called, `Execute` will immediately fail the task with a `Failed` state and log an error. There is no thread-safety guard around this setter; callers must ensure it is called before the executor is exposed to any concurrent execution path.

- `Execute(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun& workflowRun, TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState& taskState) → bool` — Core execution entry point called by the engine for each sub-workflow task. Performs the following sequence: validates that both `m_RuntimeManager` and `m_WorkflowRegistry` are non-null and that `taskDefinition.m_WorkflowFile` is non-empty; resolves the workflow file path (relative paths are resolved against `workflowDefinition.m_WorkflowFileDirectoryAbsolute`, absolute paths are used as-is, both are canonicalised via `std::filesystem::weakly_canonical`); looks up the child workflow ID via `WorkflowRegistry::TryGetWorkflowIdByFilePath`; calls `WorkflowRuntimeManager::EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId` to create and queue the child run; calls `WorkflowRuntimeManager::RegisterSubWorkflowLink` to record the `(childRunId → parentRunId, parentTaskInstanceId)` association; then sets `taskState.m_State` to `TaskInstanceStateKind::WaitingExternal` and stores `"child_run_id=<id>"` in `taskState.m_CapturedStdout`. Returns `true` on success, `false` on any failure. On failure, `taskState.m_State` is set to `TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed` and `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` is populated with a human-readable message.

**Collaborators:**

- `ITaskExecutor` (`taskExecutor.h`) — abstract base class this executor implements; defines the `Execute` virtual interface.
- `WorkflowRegistry` — queried read-only via `TryGetWorkflowIdByFilePath` to translate a file system path into a registered workflow ID. The executor holds a `const*` to it, meaning it never mutates registry state.
- `WorkflowRuntimeManager` — called to enqueue the child run (`EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId`) and to register the parent-child completion link (`RegisterSubWorkflowLink`). This is the principal side-effecting collaborator.
- `WorkflowDefinition` / `WorkflowRun` / `TaskDef` / `TaskInstanceState` — data types passed in from the engine; the executor reads from the first three and writes exclusively to `TaskInstanceState`.
- `engine.h` — pulled in for `LOG_APP_ERROR` / `LOG_APP_INFO` logging macros.

**Threading / lifetime:**

The executor does not own either pointer it holds (`m_WorkflowRegistry`, `m_RuntimeManager`); both must outlive the executor. There is no internal synchronisation. `SetRuntimeManager` is a write with no lock, so it must complete before `Execute` is called from any thread. Once both pointers are set and the executor is registered with the engine, the executor is effectively read-only with respect to its own fields, so concurrent `Execute` calls are safe only if `WorkflowRuntimeManager`'s `EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId` and `RegisterSubWorkflowLink` methods are themselves thread-safe — this file does not enforce that. The two-phase initialisation pattern (constructor + `SetRuntimeManager`) is explicitly required by the design comment in the header ("Late binding: the runtime manager is created after executor registration").

**Notes:**

- The task does **not** complete synchronously. A return value of `true` from `Execute` means the child workflow was successfully enqueued and the parent task is now suspended in `WaitingExternal`, not that the child has finished. Callers must not interpret `true` as task completion.
- `std::filesystem::weakly_canonical` is used rather than `canonical`, so the path normalisation succeeds even if the file does not currently exist on disk at call time; existence is validated implicitly by whether the registry has a matching entry.
- If `EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId` returns an empty string, the task is failed. There is no retry logic.
- `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` is repurposed as a side-channel to propagate the child run ID string (`"child_run_id=<id>"`) to whatever reads task output downstream; this is not stdout in the traditional sense.
- Relative paths in `taskDefinition.m_WorkflowFile` are always resolved relative to the **parent** workflow's directory (`workflowDefinition.m_WorkflowFileDirectoryAbsolute`), not the process working directory, so sub-workflow references are portable regardless of where the engine process is launched from.


---

### 90_doc_application_workflow_taskExecutor_h

<a id="90-doc-application-workflow-taskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/90_doc_application_workflow_taskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



## ITaskExecutor

**Role:** `ITaskExecutor` is the central polymorphic interface through which j9t dispatches every unit of work inside a running workflow. Any task type in the system — whether it invokes an AI provider, runs embedded Python, fires a webhook, reads a secret, or integrates with an external service — must be implemented as a concrete subclass of this interface. The interface deliberately exposes a single method so that the workflow engine can hold a uniform registry of executors and call them without knowing their concrete type.

**Public API:**

- `Execute(workflowDefinition, workflowRun, taskDefinition, taskState) → bool` — The sole entry point for executing one task within a workflow run. `workflowDefinition` supplies the static, immutable description of the entire workflow (graph, task catalogue, configuration). `workflowRun` carries mutable runtime state for the in-progress execution (outputs, variables, run-level metadata); implementations are expected to read from and write to it. `taskDefinition` is the static descriptor for the specific task being executed (type, configuration, input bindings). `taskState` is the mutable per-task-instance state that the executor must update to reflect progress, outputs, and completion status. Returns `true` on success, `false` on failure; callers in the engine use this return value to decide branching and error handling. Implementations are responsible for leaving `taskState` in a consistent state regardless of the return value.

**Collaborators:**

- `WorkflowDefinition` (`workflowTypes.h`) — read-only source of the workflow's structural and configuration data; passed by const reference so executors may not modify it.
- `WorkflowRun` (`workflowTypes.h`) — shared mutable run context; multiple executors operating in the same run must treat writes to this structure carefully to avoid races.
- `TaskDef` (`workflowTypes.h`) — read-only task descriptor; provides the executor with its configuration and input bindings.
- `TaskInstanceState` (`workflowTypes.h`) — per-task mutable state; the executor writes outputs and status here.
- `WorkflowRegistry` (`workflowRegistry.h`) — included alongside this interface, indicating that the registry is responsible for mapping task types to concrete `ITaskExecutor` instances; callers should look there to understand how a given executor subclass is registered and resolved at runtime.

**Threading / lifetime:**

Concrete executors are managed through the registry, which likely holds them as `std::shared_ptr` or equivalent shared-ownership handles given the `#include <memory>` dependency. Callers must not assume an executor instance is exclusive to a single thread; if the engine dispatches tasks concurrently, the same executor instance may be called from multiple threads simultaneously unless the registry guarantees per-call instantiation. Implementations must therefore be either stateless or internally synchronized. The `WorkflowRun` argument is shared across tasks in the same run and is the most likely site of concurrent mutation — implementations must coordinate writes to it, or the engine must serialize access before calling `Execute`. The interface itself has a virtual destructor, so concrete subclasses are safe to delete through an `ITaskExecutor*`.

**Notes:**

- The interface imposes no timeout or cancellation mechanism; any cancellation contract must be established between the engine and concrete implementations out-of-band (e.g., via fields in `TaskInstanceState` or `WorkflowRun`).
- Returning `false` is the only in-band error signal; there is no exception contract or error-detail return path defined at this layer — concrete implementations must encode error information into `taskState` before returning `false` if callers need diagnostics.
- Because both `workflowRun` and `taskState` are passed by non-const reference, executors have full write access to run-level state, not just task-level state; this is a significant trust boundary and implementations should mutate `workflowRun` only as explicitly required by their task semantics.
- The `workflowRegistry.h` include at the interface level creates a compilation dependency between every translation unit that includes this header and the registry; be mindful of this when restructuring includes in large builds.


---

### 91_doc_application_workflow_taskExecutorRegistry_h

<a id="91-doc-application-workflow-taskexecutorregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/91_doc_application_workflow_taskExecutorRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



## TaskExecutorRegistry

**Role:** `TaskExecutorRegistry` is a Meyer's-singleton dispatch table that maps `TaskType` enum values to `ITaskExecutor` implementations and drives per-task execution within the j9t workflow engine. Before delegating to a registered executor it runs a pre-execution file-materialization pass (`MaterializeFiles`) that copies source files — whose paths may contain template expressions such as `{{input[0]}}` — into the task's working directory under caller-specified target names. It is the single choke-point through which all task types are launched; if no executor is registered for a given `TaskType`, it marks the task failed immediately and returns without invoking any executor.

**Public API:**

- `Get() → TaskExecutorRegistry&` — returns the process-wide singleton. Constructed on first call via a function-local static; safe under C++11 and later. No arguments; no heap allocation by the caller.

- `RegisterExecutor(TaskType type, std::shared_ptr<ITaskExecutor> const& executorPtr) → void` — inserts or overwrites the executor associated with `type` in `m_Executors`. Calling this with the same `type` twice silently replaces the previous entry. Must be called before any workflow run that exercises that task type; there is no deferred-registration mechanism.

- `Execute(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun& workflowRun, TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState& taskState) → bool` — dispatches one task. It first resolves the workflow base directory via `TaskPathResolver::ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory`, then the task working directory via `TaskPathResolver::ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath`, creates that directory if absent, expands any template expressions in the source paths of `TaskDef::m_Materialize` using `ExpandTemplate` with `TemplateMode::Strict`, copies each source file to its target name with `copy_options::overwrite_existing`, and finally calls `ITaskExecutor::Execute` on the matched executor. Returns `false` and sets `taskState.m_State = TaskInstanceStateKind::Failed` plus a human-readable `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` on any failure — missing executor, directory creation error, missing source file, copy error, or template expansion error. Returns whatever the concrete executor returns on success.

**Collaborators:**

- `ITaskExecutor` / `taskExecutor.h` — the interface all concrete executors implement; the registry holds `shared_ptr<ITaskExecutor>` per `TaskType`.
- `WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, `TaskDef`, `TaskInstanceState`, `TaskType` — core data types from `workflowTypes.h` that describe the workflow graph, a live run, and per-task configuration and mutable state.
- `TaskPathResolver` (`taskPathResolver.h`) — used for three path operations: `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory`, `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath`, and `ResolvePath` (for making relative source paths absolute within the working directory).
