- `TemplateEngine` / `ExpandTemplate` (`templateEngine.h`) — expands `{{input[i]}}`, `{{output[i]}}`, and environment variable references in the source-path templates declared in `TaskDef::m_Materialize`. A `TemplateContext` is built from `taskDefinition.m_FileInputs`, `taskDefinition.m_FileOutputs`, `taskState.m_InputValues`, and `taskDefinition.m_Environment.m_Variables`.
- `engine.h` — included for `LOG_APP_ERROR` / `LOG_APP_INFO` logging macros.

**Threading / lifetime:**

The singleton is constructed lazily and never destroyed. `m_Executors` is an unordered map with no internal locking. The expectation is that `RegisterExecutor` calls happen at program startup (before any workflow runs begin) and `Execute` calls happen afterward; concurrent calls to either method without external synchronization are a data race. The registry does not own `WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, `TaskDef`, or `TaskInstanceState` — it borrows const references to the first and third, and a mutable reference to `workflowRun` and `taskState` which it passes straight through to the concrete executor. Lifetime of those objects is entirely the caller's responsibility.

**Notes:**

- `MaterializeFiles` is a file-system operation with `overwrite_existing` semantics; it will silently replace a pre-existing file at the target path. This is intentional for idempotent re-runs but can mask stale-data bugs if the source has not changed.
- Template expansion uses `TemplateMode::Strict`, so any unrecognised template token in a source path causes the entire `Execute` call to fail with a logged error and no executor invocation.
- Absolute source paths bypass the working-directory anchor and are used as-is after `lexically_normal()`; only relative paths are resolved under the task working directory.
- If `TaskDef::m_Materialize` is empty, `MaterializeFiles` exits immediately with `true`, adding zero overhead.
- Registering a `nullptr` executor is not guarded; calling `Execute` would dereference it and crash. Callers must ensure the `shared_ptr` is non-null before registering.
- The comment inside `MaterializeFiles` explicitly cross-references `AiCallTaskExecutor` as the historical origin of the file-materialization pattern, indicating this is a generalization extracted from that executor.


---

### 92_doc_application_workflow_taskFreshnessChecker_h

<a id="92-doc-application-workflow-taskfreshnesschecker-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/92_doc_application_workflow_taskFreshnessChecker_h/docu.output.md`



## TaskFreshnessChecker

**Role:** `TaskFreshnessChecker` implements Makefile-style incremental build logic for the j9t workflow engine. Given a task's resolved input and output file paths plus a callback to retrieve any upstream dependency's output paths, it decides whether a task is already up to date and therefore safe to skip. The rule it enforces is: every declared output must exist on disk, and the oldest output's modification time must be greater than or equal to the newest modification time across both the task's direct inputs and all transitive upstream dependency outputs. Any missing file, unreadable timestamp, empty output list, or empty combined-input list causes the task to be considered stale.

---

**Public API:**

- `IsTaskUpToDate(workflowDefinition, taskId, resolvedPaths, resolveOutputPaths, comparedInputPath, comparedOutputPath) → bool`

  The single entry point. Returns `true` only when the task is demonstrably current.

  - `workflowDefinition` — the full `WorkflowDefinition` graph; used to look up the `TaskDef` for `taskId` and to walk `m_DependsOn` chains transitively.
  - `taskId` — string key identifying the task to check.
  - `resolvedPaths` — a `ResolvedPaths` struct carrying pre-resolved `m_InputPaths` and `m_OutputPaths` for this task. All paths should already be absolute or unambiguously resolvable; the method applies `lexically_normal()` internally before calling `fs::exists` / `fs::last_write_time`.
  - `resolveOutputPaths` — a `std::function<bool(taskId, outPaths)>` callback that the caller supplies to translate any upstream task ID into its concrete output paths. Returning `false` from this callback propagates as a stale result.
  - `comparedInputPath` (optional out-param) — if non-null, receives the path of the single newest input or upstream-output file that was used in the comparison. Populated on both stale and fresh returns except when the method returns early due to a missing output.
  - `comparedOutputPath` (optional out-param) — if non-null, receives the path of the single oldest output file. Same population rules as above.

  Side effects: emits `LOG_APP_INFO` structured log lines tagged `[paths debug]` at every decision point (missing file, stat failure, final comparison result). No filesystem mutations.

- `ResolvedPaths` (nested aggregate struct)

  Plain data container. Callers populate `m_InputPaths` and `m_OutputPaths` before passing to `IsTaskUpToDate`. No invariants beyond being value-copyable.

- `ResolveOutputPathsFn` (type alias)

  `std::function<bool(std::string const& taskId, std::vector<std::filesystem::path>& outPaths)>`. Callers provide this; the class never stores it beyond the duration of a single `IsTaskUpToDate` call.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `workflowTypes.h` / `WorkflowDefinition`, `TaskDef` — `WorkflowDefinition::m_Tasks` is an associative container keyed by task ID. `TaskDef::m_DependsOn` is a collection of upstream task IDs. `TaskDef::m_WorkingDirectory` is referenced by the anonymous-namespace helpers `ResolveWorkingDirectory` and `HasPathPrefix`, which are defined but currently marked `[[maybe_unused]]` — they exist in the `.cpp` but are not called by the present implementation.
- `TaskPathResolver` — provides `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory` and `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath`, used only inside the `[[maybe_unused]]` helpers; not invoked on any live code path today.
- `engine.h` — pulled in for the `LOG_APP_INFO` macro used throughout both methods.
- `std::filesystem` — all timestamp and existence queries go through the standard library; `std::error_code` overloads are used exclusively so no exceptions are thrown on stat failure.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`TaskFreshnessChecker` is a stateless class with no member variables. It can be constructed on the stack, stored by value, or shared across threads without synchronisation. All state lives in call-stack locals within `IsTaskUpToDate` and `CollectUpstreamOutputTimes`.

The `ResolveOutputPathsFn` callback is invoked synchronously and from the same thread that called `IsTaskUpToDate`. The callback must not itself call `IsTaskUpToDate` on the same `TaskFreshnessChecker` instance with a dependency that re-enters the call chain, because cycle detection (`visitedTasks`) is local to one top-level `IsTaskUpToDate` call and is not shared across nested external re-entry.

The `WorkflowDefinition` passed by const reference must remain alive and unmodified for the duration of the call. No reference to it is retained afterwards.

---

**Notes:**

- **Empty-input early-out:** If, after collecting direct inputs and all transitive upstream outputs, `inputTimes` is still empty, the method returns `false`. A task with outputs but zero inputs of any kind is never considered fresh. This is intentional: without a known baseline, staleness cannot be disproved.
- **No-output early-out:** A task with an empty `m_OutputPaths` always returns `false`, regardless of inputs. Callers that want such tasks treated as always-up-to-date must handle that case before calling this class.
- **Transitive upstream walk:** `CollectUpstreamOutputTimes` performs a depth-first traversal seeded from each direct dependency in `m_DependsOn`. The `visitedTasks` set prevents revisiting the same node but is only cycle-safe within one traversal; it is not reset between dependency seeds, so a diamond dependency (A→B, A→C, B→D, C→D) will stat D's outputs only once.
- **Any upstream missing file = stale:** If any output file of any transitively reachable upstream task is absent or unreadable, `CollectUpstreamOutputTimes` returns `false` immediately, which propagates as a stale result for the current task. There is no partial-freshness handling.
- **`latestInputTime` recomputation:** The code computes `latestInputIterator` over the merged `inputTimes` vector (direct + upstream), then separately re-scans `upstreamTimes` to potentially replace it. The second scan is redundant because `upstreamTimes` entries were already appended into `inputTimes` before the first `max_element` call. The result is always correct, but the second scan does extra work.
- **`[[maybe_unused]]` helpers:** `ResolveWorkingDirectory` and `HasPathPrefix` in the anonymous namespace are compiled but never called. They appear to be scaffolding for a planned per-task working-directory-relative path normalisation that has not yet been wired in.
- **Log verbosity:** Every `IsTaskUpToDate` call emits at minimum one `LOG_APP_INFO` line even when the task is trivially stale (e.g., no outputs). In high-frequency scheduling loops this can be noisy; callers should be aware before enabling verbose log levels in production.


---

### 93_doc_application_workflow_taskPathResolver_h

<a id="93-doc-application-workflow-taskpathresolver-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/93_doc_application_workflow_taskPathResolver_h/docu.output.md`



## TaskPathResolver

**Role:** `TaskPathResolver` is a stateless utility class (all methods are `static`) that centralises every filesystem path resolution concern for the j9t workflow engine. It translates the loose, possibly-relative, possibly-partially-specified path fields stored in `WorkflowDefinition` and `TaskDef` into canonical absolute `std::filesystem::path` values. It also builds output-slot value maps and resolves the concrete input/output file paths that the orchestrator uses to decide whether a task's outputs are fresh relative to its inputs.

---

**Public API:**

- `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition) → std::filesystem::path` — Walks a six-level fallback chain across `WorkflowDefinition` fields (`m_WorkflowBaseDirectoryAbsolute`, `m_WorkflowBaseDirectory`, `m_WorkflowFileDirectoryAbsolute`, `m_WorkflowFileDirectory`, `m_WorkflowFilePathAbsolute.parent_path()`, `m_WorkflowFilePath.parent_path()`) and returns the first non-empty result as an absolute, lexically-normalised path. If the chosen candidate is relative, it is resolved against `Core::g_Core->GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()`. Returns an empty path only when every field in the definition is empty.

- `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath(std::filesystem::path const& workflowBaseDirectoryPath, std::string const& taskWorkingDirectoryText) → std::filesystem::path` — Converts `taskWorkingDirectoryText` to an absolute path relative to `workflowBaseDirectoryPath` by delegating to `ResolveTaskScopedPath`. Per Spec §3.3, if `taskWorkingDirectoryText` is empty the method returns `workflowBaseDirectoryPath` unchanged, making the workflow base directory the implicit task working directory.

- `ResolveTaskScopedPath(std::filesystem::path const& taskWorkingDirectoryPath, std::string const& pathText) → std::filesystem::path` — Thin wrapper around `ResolvePath` that constructs a `std::filesystem::path` from `pathText` and resolves it against `taskWorkingDirectoryPath`. Emits `[paths debug]` log lines at INFO level via `LOG_APP_INFO`.

- `ResolvePath(std::filesystem::path const& baseDirectoryPath, std::filesystem::path const& path) → std::filesystem::path` — Core resolution primitive. Returns empty if `path` is empty. Returns `std::filesystem::absolute(path).lexically_normal()` unchanged if `path` is already absolute. For relative paths it checks whether `path` starts with the *last component* of `baseDirectoryPath`; if so it roots the join against `baseDirectoryPath.parent_path()` to avoid doubling the directory name (e.g. `…/workflows` + `workflows/foo.jcwf` → `…/workflows/foo.jcwf`, not `…/workflows/workflows/foo.jcwf`). Otherwise joins `baseDirectoryPath / path` and normalises. Falls back to `Core::g_Core->GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()` logging only; the actual CWD fallback is provided by `std::filesystem::absolute`.

- `BuildOutputSlotMap(TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState const& taskState, std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& outputSlotMapOut) → void` — Clears `outputSlotMapOut` then populates it for every key declared in `taskDefinition.m_Outputs`. Values are sourced first from `taskState.m_OutputValues`, then as a backward-compatibility fallback from `taskState.m_InputValues` (some older tasks wrote produced outputs into the input map). Keys already present in `outputSlotMapOut` are not overwritten by the fallback path.

- `ResolveFreshnessPathsForTask(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun const& workflowRun, TaskDef const& taskDefinition, std::string const& taskId, std::vector<std::filesystem::path>& inputPathsOut, std::vector<std::filesystem::path>& outputPathsOut) → bool` — The highest-level method. Always clears both output vectors first. Resolves the workflow base directory and task working directory, then calls `DataflowResolver::ResolveInputsForTask` to obtain template-expansion values. Merges in the task's current output-slot values via `BuildOutputSlotMap`. If any `QueueFileRef` in the task's `m_QueueBinding` carries `m_HasInlineContent == true`, prepends the resolved JCWF workflow file path to `inputPathsOut` so that changes to inline content (which live inside the JCWF file) invalidate freshness. Then iterates `taskDefinition.m_FileInputs` and `m_FileOutputs`, expanding each template string via `DataflowResolver::ExpandTemplates` before resolving to absolute paths. Returns `false` if `ResolveInputsForTask` fails or if any template expansion fails; returns `true` on full success.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, `TaskDef`, `TaskInstanceState`, `QueueFileRef`, `TaskResolvedInputs` — data types from `workflowTypes.h` that carry all input fields. `TaskPathResolver` reads these but never mutates them.
- `DataflowResolver` (`dataflowResolver.h`) — instantiated locally inside `ResolveFreshnessPathsForTask` as a const value. Used for `ResolveInputsForTask` (returns `std::optional<TaskResolvedInputs>`) and `ExpandTemplates` (expands `${variable}` placeholders in raw path strings using the merged value map).
- `Core::g_Core` (`core.h`) — a global singleton pointer. Queried via `Core::g_Core->GetLaunchCWDAbsolute()` to anchor relative paths. All call sites guard against `Core::g_Core == nullptr` before dereferencing, making the class usable in unit-test contexts where no `Core` exists, though relative-path resolution will then fall back to whatever `std::filesystem::absolute` uses as its implicit base.
- `LOG_APP_INFO` (`log/log.h`) — every resolution step emits a structured `[paths debug]` INFO log line. These are present in production builds and will appear in log output under normal operation.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`TaskPathResolver` has no instance state and no mutable static state; all methods are `static`. The class itself carries no ownership. Callers own all input objects (`WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, `TaskDef`, `TaskInstanceState`) and must ensure they remain live for the duration of each call. `DataflowResolver` is constructed on the stack inside `ResolveFreshnessPathsForTask` and is destroyed on return. The sole shared global dependency is `Core::g_Core`; callers must guarantee that if `Core::g_Core` is non-null it remains valid (not destroyed) during the call. No locks are acquired; concurrent calls with distinct arguments are safe provided `Core::g_Core` itself is thread-safe for read access.

---

**Notes:**

- The base-directory-deduplication logic in `ResolvePath` is path-string–based: it compares `path.begin()` (the first path component) against `baseDirectoryPath.filename()`. This is a textual comparison; symlinks and case-sensitivity differences on case-insensitive filesystems are not handled.
- `BuildOutputSlotMap` reads `m_InputValues` as a fallback for backward compatibility only after checking `m_OutputValues`. The guard `if (outputSlotMapOut.contains(outputName))` before the fallback means that if the same key was already resolved from `m_OutputValues`, the `m_InputValues` lookup is skipped — but the check is against the *output map*, not `m_OutputValues` directly, so the first branch's `continue` already prevents reaching the fallback in that case. The `contains` guard therefore only matters if the same output name appears twice in `taskDefinition.m_Outputs`, which would be unusual.
- `ResolveFreshnessPathsForTask` merges output-slot values into the template expansion map so that file path templates which reference a task's own produced output names (e.g. `${outputFile}`) can be expanded even at freshness-check time, before the task has fully run.
- Inline queue content freshness tracking: the check iterates all four `QueueBinding` file-ref vectors (`m_StngFiles`, `m_TaskFiles`, `m_CntxFiles`, `m_ProbFiles`). If *any* ref has `m_HasInlineContent == true`, the entire JCWF file is added to `inputPathsOut` exactly once, regardless of how many inline refs exist.
- `ResolveFreshnessPathsForTask` returns `false` and leaves both output vectors empty on any failure, including `DataflowResolver` returning `std::nullopt` or a template expansion failure. Callers must check the return value; partial population of the output vectors does not occur because they are cleared at entry and only populated after all preconditions pass (inputs) or in a loop that returns false on first failure (outputs).


---

### 94_doc_application_workflow_templateEngine_h

<a id="94-doc-application-workflow-templateengine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/94_doc_application_workflow_templateEngine_h/docu.output.md`



## templateEngine (workflow/templateEngine.h + templateEngine.cpp)

**Role:** This file implements the unified `{{...}}` placeholder expansion engine used across j9t's workflow tasks. It converts a raw template string — containing any combination of file-list references, indexed file references, environment variable lookups, named slot lookups, dataflow cross-references, and bare key lookups — into a fully resolved output string. Two modes govern error handling: `Strict` (used by shell tasks, where an unresolvable or malformed placeholder is a hard error) and `Lenient` (used by AI call and per-item templates, where unknown keys silently expand to an empty string).

**Public API:**

- `ExpandTemplate(templateText, context, mode, expandedOut, errorMessage) → bool` — The sole public entry point. Scans `templateText` left-to-right for `{{...}}` tokens. For each token it applies the resolution priority described below and appends the result to `expandedOut`. Literal text between tokens is copied verbatim. Returns `true` on success; returns `false` only in `Strict` mode when a placeholder is malformed, an index is out of range, or a key cannot be resolved. On failure, `expandedOut` contains whatever was expanded before the error, and `errorMessage` contains a human-readable description including the offending key or index. Both `expandedOut` and `errorMessage` are cleared at entry, so callers do not need to pre-clear them.

**Collaborators:**

- `TemplateContext` — A plain aggregating struct that carries four non-owning `const` pointers: `m_FileInputs` (`vector<string>`), `m_FileOutputs` (`vector<string>`), `m_InputValues` (`unordered_map<string,string>`), and `m_EnvVariables` (`unordered_map<string,string>`). Any pointer may be `nullptr`; the engine treats a null pointer as an empty collection and never dereferences it unsafely. Callers (shell task executor, AI call dispatcher, per-item template renderer) are responsible for populating and keeping these structures alive for the duration of the call.
- `TemplateMode` — A scoped enum (`Strict` / `Lenient`) supplied by the caller; the shell task layer passes `Strict`, the AI call and per-item layers pass `Lenient`.
- `JoinFileList` (anonymous namespace helper) — Joins a `vector<string>` into a single space-separated string; used for `{{inputs}}` and `{{outputs}}` expansions. Not part of the public API.

**Threading / lifetime:**

`ExpandTemplate` is a pure free function with no internal state, no static mutable data, and no synchronisation primitives. It is safe to call concurrently from multiple threads provided that the `TemplateContext` pointers remain valid and unmodified for the duration of each call. The `TemplateContext` struct itself is passed by const reference; ownership of the underlying collections stays entirely with the caller. The engine does not cache, store, or extend the lifetime of any data it reads.

**Notes:**

- **Resolution order for a bare key** (no recognised prefix): the engine checks `m_InputValues` only. If the key is absent and mode is `Strict`, expansion fails. There is no fallback to `m_EnvVariables` for bare keys; environment variables require the explicit `env.` prefix.
- **`env.NAME` is always lenient on missing keys**, even in `Strict` mode. The comment in the source explicitly labels this "same as old shell behaviour." A missing environment variable expands silently to `""` regardless of the mode flag. This is the one case where `Strict` mode does not propagate an error for an unresolvable name.
- **`{{slot.NAME}}` vs `{{inputs.NAME}}`** both resolve from `m_InputValues`; they are semantically equivalent at the expansion level and differ only in the prefix syntax exposed to template authors.
- **Malformed `{{` without a matching `}}`**: in `Strict` mode this is an error that includes the byte offset of the opening `{{`; in `Lenient` mode the remainder of the template from the opening `{{` onward is emitted as literal text and the loop exits.
- **Index parsing** uses `std::stoul` inside a bare `catch(...)` block, so any exception (including `std::invalid_argument` and `std::out_of_range`) collapses to the same error path. Out-of-range numeric indices that do parse successfully (e.g., `input[9999]` with a 2-element vector) are caught by the bounds check, not the exception path.
- **`{{inputs}}` and `{{outputs}}` always resolve** (to `""` if the corresponding pointer is null) and never fail even in `Strict` mode.
- **`{{input[N]}}` pattern matching** requires the key to be longer than 6 characters, start with the literal prefix `input[`, and end with `]`. The same structural check applies to `{{output[N]}}` with a length threshold of 7. Keys like `{{input[]}}` will pass the prefix/suffix check and then fail at `stoul`, reaching the exception handler.


---

### 95_doc_application_workflow_triggerEngine_h

<a id="95-doc-application-workflow-triggerengine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/95_doc_application_workflow_triggerEngine_h/docu.output.md`



## TriggerEngine

**Role:** `TriggerEngine` is the sole authority for deciding when a workflow run should be launched. It holds all registered trigger instances (auto, cron, file-watch, manual, webhook, S3, OneDrive, email/IMAP, Azure Blob, and GCS), evaluates them on every `Tick()` call or on incoming file/manual signals, and notifies the rest of the system by invoking a caller-supplied `TriggerCallback` with a `TriggerFiredEvent` identifying the workflow and trigger. It owns a dedicated `FileWatcher` for file-watch triggers and performs live IMAP polling inline during `Tick()`. It does **not** parse JCWF JSON — `WorkflowJsonParser` produces trigger definitions and `Orchestrator` pushes them into `TriggerEngine` via the `Add*` registration API.

---

**Public API:**

- `TriggerEngine(TriggerCallback const& triggerCallback)` — Constructs the engine, stores the callback, constructs a `FileWatcher` with an empty root path, and calls `FileWatcher::Start()`. The callback must remain valid for the lifetime of the engine.

- `AddAutoTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, isEnabled, fireImmediately = true)` — Registers an auto trigger. If both `isEnabled` and `fireImmediately` are true, fires the trigger synchronously before returning (outside `m_Mutex` to avoid deadlock). Pass `fireImmediately = false` during workflow reload to suppress re-execution of already-running workflows.

- `AddCronTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, expression, timezone, isEnabled)` — Parses a 5-field cron expression (`m h dom mon dow`; only `*` or a single integer per field). On parse failure, the trigger is stored but forcibly disabled. On success, computes `m_NextFireTime` immediately from `system_clock::now()`. `timezone` is an IANA name (e.g. `"America/Los_Angeles"`); empty means system local time.

- `AddFileWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, path, events, debounceMilliseconds, isEnabled)` — Registers a file or directory watch. Normalizes `path` via `NormalizePath`, appends the instance to `m_FileWatchTriggers`, updates `m_FileWatchIndex`, and calls `m_TriggerFileWatcher->AddPath()`. `AddPath` is idempotent — multiple triggers on the same path result in a single watcher entry.

- `AddManualTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, isEnabled)` — Stores a manual trigger record; no side effects at registration time.

- `AddWebhookTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, secret, isEnabled)` — Stores a `WebhookTriggerInstance` and updates `m_WebhookIndex` for O(1) lookup. `secret` is the HMAC-SHA256 shared secret; the comment documents that an empty secret is rejected by the validator and that the HTTP request handler rejects requests with missing or invalid signatures. The `TriggerEngine` itself does not perform HMAC validation — it only stores the secret for retrieval via `GetWebhookTrigger`.

- `AddS3WatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, bucket, prefix, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)` — Registers an S3 polling trigger. `pollIntervalSeconds` is clamped to a minimum of 60. `m_NextPollTime` is set to `steady_clock::now() + pollInterval` so the first poll is not immediate. The trigger fires on every poll interval unconditionally; the launched workflow is expected to perform actual S3 list operations to detect new objects.

- `AddOneDriveWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, folder, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)` — Same polling pattern as S3. Stores a `m_DeltaToken` field for Graph API delta queries, populated by the workflow itself.

- `AddEmailWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, folder, subjectFilter, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)` — Registers an IMAP-backed email watch. Defaults `folder` to `"INBOX"` if empty. Unlike S3/OneDrive, `Tick()` actually performs IMAP I/O (via `EmailConnector::CheckForNewMail`) and only fires the trigger when new mail is found. The `m_LastSeenUid` watermark is updated after each successful poll.

- `AddAzureBlobWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, container, prefix, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)` — Same unconditional-fire polling pattern as S3.

- `AddGcsWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, bucket, prefix, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)` — Same unconditional-fire polling pattern as S3.

- `ClearWorkflowTriggers(workflowId)` — Removes all triggers for the specified workflow from every vector, rebuilds `m_FileWatchIndex` and `m_WebhookIndex` from scratch (because `std::remove_if` shifts indices), and calls `m_TriggerFileWatcher->RemovePath()` for any file-watch paths that are no longer referenced by any remaining trigger.

- `ClearAll()` — Clears all trigger vectors and both index maps, then removes every previously watched path from `m_TriggerFileWatcher`. Used for full reload.

- `Tick(now)` — Main evaluation entry point, intended to be called from the main loop on a regular cadence (e.g. every second). Under `m_Mutex`: evaluates all enabled cron triggers against `m_NextFireTime` and advances the schedule; evaluates S3, OneDrive, Azure Blob, and GCS poll timers and queues fires; collects due email triggers into `EmailPollJob` records. After releasing the lock: performs IMAP I/O per `EmailPollJob`, re-acquires the lock to update `m_LastSeenUid` watermarks, then fires all accumulated events outside the lock.

- `NotifyFileEvent(path, fileEventType, now)` — Called by the `FileWatcher` (routed through `JarvisAgent::OnEvent` on the global event queue). Normalizes `path`, uses `m_FileWatchIndex` for fast exact-path lookup, then does a linear prefix scan for directory-match triggers not already handled. Applies debounce: a trigger will not fire again until `debounceInterval` milliseconds have elapsed since `m_LastFireTime`. The first fire is never debounced (`m_HasFiredOnce` starts false).

- `FireManualTrigger(workflowId, triggerId)` — Searches `m_ManualTriggers` for a matching enabled record and calls `FireTrigger` if found; logs a warning otherwise. Intended for invocation from CLI or Web UI.

- `GetWebhookTrigger(workflowId) → WebhookTriggerInstance const*` — O(1) lookup via `m_WebhookIndex`. Returns `nullptr` if no webhook trigger is registered for `workflowId`. The returned pointer is only valid while the caller holds no mutations; see threading notes.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `FileWatcher` (`file/fileWatcher.h`) — Owned exclusively by `TriggerEngine` (`m_TriggerFileWatcher`). `TriggerEngine` calls `Start()`, `Stop()`, `AddPath()`, and `RemovePath()` on it. File events are delivered back to `NotifyFileEvent` via the global event queue / `JarvisAgent::OnEvent` subscription (wired externally by `Orchestrator` or `JarvisAgent`).
- `EmailConnector` (`cloud/emailConnector.h`) — Used directly inside `Tick()` to resolve IMAP credentials and call `EmailConnector::CheckForNewMail()` with the stored `CloudConnection`. Network I/O occurs on whatever thread calls `Tick()`.
- `Core::g_Core->GetCloudConnectionManager()` — Accessed in `Tick()` to resolve named `CloudConnection` objects for email triggers by `m_ConnectionName`.
- `WorkflowJsonParser` / `Orchestrator` — Callers of the `Add*` registration API; they own the JCWF parsing and translation to trigger definitions.
- `core.h` / `engine.h` — Provide `LOG_APP_INFO`, `LOG_APP_WARN`, `LOG_APP_ERROR` macros used throughout.
- Howard Hinnant's `date/tz.h` — Used on non-MSVC platforms for `date::time_zone`, `date::make_zoned`, `date::current_zone`, `date::locate_zone`, and related calendar types. MSVC uses `<chrono>` C++20 equivalents directly.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

- `TriggerEngine` is non-copyable and non-movable.
- All internal state is protected by `m_Mutex` (a `std::mutex`). The mutex is acquired as a `std::scoped_lock` in every public method that reads or writes trigger vectors or index maps.
- **Deadlock avoidance:** `FireTrigger` and `FireManualTrigger` are always called **outside** `m_Mutex`. Any code path that collects events under the lock and then invokes the callback after releasing it follows this pattern. Callers of `TriggerCallback` must not re-enter `TriggerEngine` from within the callback.
- `GetWebhookTrigger` returns a raw pointer into `m_WebhookTriggers`. The pointer is valid only until the next call that modifies the vector (`ClearAll`, `ClearWorkflowTriggers`, or another `AddWebhookTrigger`). Callers must not store or dereference this pointer beyond the immediate call site without external synchronization.
- `Tick()` performs network I/O (IMAP) on the calling thread while **not** holding `m_Mutex`. It re-acquires the mutex briefly to update `m_LastSeenUid` and to read trigger identity before firing. If the email trigger vector was mutated between the lock release and re-acquisition, the `job.m_Index < m_EmailWatchTriggers.size()` guard prevents out-of-bounds access but the watermark update may be silently dropped.
- `m_TriggerFileWatcher` is created in the constructor and destroyed in the destructor; `Stop()` is called in the destructor. `AddPath`/`RemovePath` on the watcher are called **outside** `m_Mutex` in `AddFileWatchTrigger`, `ClearWorkflowTriggers`, and `ClearAll`, so callers must ensure no concurrent structural mutations of the engine during those windows.

---

**Notes:**

- **Cron expression limitations:** Only `*` (wildcard) and a single integer value are supported per field. Ranges (`1-5`), step values (`*/5`), lists (`1,3,5`), and named values (`MON`) are not parsed. A cron trigger that fails to parse is silently disabled (logged at error level) rather than rejected.
- **Cron iteration limit:** `ComputeNextFireTime` steps forward in one-minute increments for up to `60 * 24 * 366 = 527,040` iterations (one year). If no matching time is found, it returns `referenceTime`, which means the trigger will be re-evaluated but never advance — effectively disabling it with a warning log.
- **S3/OneDrive/Azure/GCS fire-always pattern:** These polling triggers fire unconditionally on every poll interval. They do **not** compare `m_LastSeenKey` / `m_LastSeenTimestamp` themselves; those fields exist on the structs but `Tick()` never reads or writes them. Change detection is delegated entirely to the workflow that runs in response.
- **Email UID watermark seeding:** On the first successful IMAP poll when `m_LastSeenUid` is empty, `CheckForNewMail` returns the highest existing UID without setting `hasNewMail`. The engine stores this as the watermark without firing the trigger, so only genuinely new messages seen on subsequent polls cause a workflow run.
- **File path normalization:** `NormalizePath` converts `\` to `/` and collapses consecutive slashes. No canonicalization (e.g. resolving `.` or `..`) is performed. `IsPathMatch` treats the watched path as a directory prefix if `eventPath` starts with `watchedPath` followed by `/` or is an exact match; partial filename prefix matches are rejected by the boundary-character check.
- **Index rebuild cost:** `ClearWorkflowTriggers` rebuilds both `m_FileWatchIndex` and `m_WebhookIndex` from scratch after erasing. For systems with large numbers of workflows being hot-reloaded frequently, this is O(N) in the total number of remaining triggers.
- **`fireImmediately = false` reload convention:** Orchestrator is expected to pass `false` when re-registering triggers after a configuration reload to prevent every auto-trigger workflow from running again. The engine itself has no reload-detection logic; the caller is responsible for this flag discipline.


---

### 96_doc_application_workflow_workflowFileIndex_h

<a id="96-doc-application-workflow-workflowfileindex-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/96_doc_application_workflow_workflowFileIndex_h/docu.output.md`



## WorkflowFileIndex

**Role:** `WorkflowFileIndex` is a thread-safe, in-memory index of every regular file under a designated workflow root directory (typically `workflows/`). It performs a single recursive scan at construction/refresh time and then answers two types of lookup queries — by basename and by relative path — without touching the filesystem again (except for the existence check inside `FindByRelativePath`). It also serialises its contents as a Markdown bullet list intended to be embedded directly into AI prompt context, giving the generation layer awareness of which workflow files are available.

---

**Public API:**

- `ScanDirectory(const std::filesystem::path& rootDir) → void` — Clears all previously indexed data, resolves `rootDir` to an absolute, lexically-normalised path, and walks it recursively via `std::filesystem::recursive_directory_iterator`. Only regular files survive; any file or directory whose name begins with `.` is silently skipped. Populates both `m_BasenameIndex` (basename → list of absolute paths, handling collisions) and `m_AllFiles` (insertion-order list of absolute paths). Emits `LOG_APP_ERROR` if the path cannot be resolved and `LOG_APP_INFO` on success with a count. This is a write operation and acquires the mutex exclusively.

- `FindByBasename(const std::string& basename) → std::vector<std::filesystem::path>` — Returns every absolute path whose filename component exactly matches `basename` (case-sensitive, platform-dependent). Returns an empty vector for unknown basenames. Does not re-check the filesystem; reflects the state at the last `ScanDirectory` call. Multiple results are possible when the same filename exists under different subdirectories.

- `FindByRelativePath(const std::string& relativePath) → std::filesystem::path` — Resolves `relativePath` against the stored root directory (`m_RootDirectory / relativePath`), normalises the result, and calls `std::filesystem::exists` to confirm the file is actually present on disk. Returns the absolute normalised path on success, or an empty `std::filesystem::path` if the root has not been set, `relativePath` is empty, or the file does not exist. This is the only read method that touches the filesystem.

- `GetRootDirectory() → std::filesystem::path` — Returns the absolute, normalised root path stored by the last successful `ScanDirectory` call. Returns an empty path if `ScanDirectory` has not been called or failed.

- `GetAllFiles() → std::vector<std::filesystem::path>` — Returns a copy of all indexed absolute paths in the order they were encountered by the recursive iterator. The copy is taken under the mutex, so it is a consistent snapshot.

- `Size() → size_t` — Returns the number of indexed files; equivalent to `GetAllFiles().size()` but avoids copying the path vector.

- `SerializeMarkdownListing() → std::string` — Produces a Markdown unordered list of all indexed files expressed as paths relative to the root directory, using generic (forward-slash) separators. Each line has the form `- <relpath>`. Returns the literal string `(no files indexed)` when the index is empty. The result is reserved at `n * 80` bytes to reduce reallocations. This output is designed to be injected verbatim into AI generation prompts.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `engine.h` — Supplies the `LOG_APP_ERROR` and `LOG_APP_INFO` macros used for diagnostic output. No other API from `engine.h` is called; the dependency is purely for logging.
- `std::filesystem` (C++17) — All directory traversal, path resolution, normalisation, and existence checking goes through the standard filesystem library. Callers must ensure the root path is accessible to the process at scan time.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

All six public methods acquire `m_Mutex` (declared `mutable`) via `std::lock_guard` before touching any member data. Reads and writes are fully serialised; there is no reader–writer distinction. `ScanDirectory` is the only method that mutates state, so concurrent calls to any combination of read methods are safe but still serialised.

`WorkflowFileIndex` has no concept of automatic refresh; if the underlying directory changes after `ScanDirectory` returns, the index is stale until `ScanDirectory` is called again. The class owns all its data by value. There is no heap-allocated child object, no callback registration, and no background thread — callers are solely responsible for deciding when to rescan.

Callers that hold a raw pointer or reference to `WorkflowFileIndex` must ensure the object outlives all concurrent users; the class itself imposes no restriction on how it is stored (stack, heap, or as a member).

---

**Notes:**

- The hidden-file filter iterates every component of `entry.path()` — not just the filename — so a file inside a directory named `.hidden/` is excluded even if the file itself has a visible name. This is by design but means files can disappear from the index unexpectedly if any ancestor directory starts with a dot.
- Basename collisions are preserved rather than discarded: `m_BasenameIndex[basename]` is a `std::vector`, so `FindByBasename` may return more than one path. Callers that assume a unique result must check the vector size.
- `FindByRelativePath` performs a live `std::filesystem::exists` call at query time, so it reflects the actual filesystem state rather than the cached index. A file that was present at scan time but has since been deleted will correctly return an empty path here, while `FindByBasename` and `GetAllFiles` would still report it until the next `ScanDirectory`.
- Path normalisation is applied with `lexically_normal()` and `lexically_relative()` throughout; no `canonical()` (which would require the path to exist) is used during scanning, keeping the scan robust if a symlink target disappears mid-walk.
- `SerializeMarkdownListing` uses `generic_string()` for relative paths, guaranteeing forward slashes on all platforms, which is important when the output is embedded in AI prompts that may be parsed cross-platform.


---

### 97_doc_application_workflow_workflowJsonParser_h

<a id="97-doc-application-workflow-workflowjsonparser-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/97_doc_application_workflow_workflowJsonParser_h/docu.output.md`



## WorkflowJsonParser

**Role:** `WorkflowJsonParser` is the sole deserialisation boundary between raw JCWF JSON text and the engine's internal `WorkflowDefinition` data structure. It supports three loading modes: a monolithic single-file parse (`ParseWorkflowJson`), and a two-part container-style split where workflow metadata lives in a `global.json` file (`ParseGlobalJson`) and the task graph lives in one or more canvas files (`ParseCanvasJson`). All parsing is driven by simdjson's `ondemand` streaming API. After structural parsing completes, the parser performs two in-place post-processing steps — propagating workflow-level `defaults` into tasks whose own values are zero, and cross-validating every task's `filter` reference against the set of declared filter IDs. The class carries no mutable state; every method is logically `const`.

---

**Public API:**

- `ParseWorkflowJson(jsonContent, workflowOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Parses a complete, self-contained JCWF document. `jsonContent` must be a non-empty UTF-8 string. On success the fully-populated `WorkflowDefinition` is written to `workflowOut`; on failure `errorMessage` receives a human-readable explanation and `workflowOut` is partially written (do not use it). Enforces that `version`, `id`, and `tasks` are present. Calls `ParseRootObject` internally, which also: validates the `major.minor` version string (hard-rejects unknown major, warns on unknown minor), injects a synthetic `auto` trigger when the `triggers` field is absent or empty, applies defaults to tasks, and validates filter references.

- `ParseGlobalJson(jsonContent, workflowOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Parses only the container-metadata subset of a JCWF document: `version`, `id`, `label`, `doc`, `base_directory`, `triggers`, `manual_start`, and `defaults`. Fields such as `tasks`, `dataflow`, `filters`, `control_nodes`, and `controlflow` are silently ignored even if present. Does **not** inject an auto-trigger and does **not** validate filter references — those responsibilities fall to the later `ParseCanvasJson` call. Writes into an existing (possibly zero-initialised) `WorkflowDefinition`; the caller is expected to subsequently call `ParseCanvasJson` on the same object.

- `ParseCanvasJson(jsonContent, workflowOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Parses the task-graph subset of a JCWF document: `tasks`, `dataflow`, `filters`, `control_nodes`, and `controlflow`. Metadata fields (`version`, `id`, `label`, etc.) are silently ignored. Requires `tasks` to be present; returns false if it is missing. After parsing, applies `workflowOut.m_Defaults` (already populated by a prior `ParseGlobalJson` call) to task-level timeout and retry fields, then cross-validates all task `filter` references. Merges into whatever `workflowOut` already contains — the caller must have called `ParseGlobalJson` first so that `m_Defaults` is valid before defaults propagation runs.

---

**Collaborators:**

- **simdjson `ondemand`** (`simdjson/simdjson.h`) — All JSON tokenisation. The `ondemand` API is single-pass and forward-only; this forces the two-pass workaround in `defaults` handling (raw JSON is extracted with `ExtractRawJson`, then re-parsed with a fresh `simdjson::ondemand::parser` to produce the typed `WorkflowDefaults`).
- **`WorkflowDefinition`, `TaskDef`, `WorkflowTrigger`, `DataflowDef`, `FilterDef`, `ControlNodeDef`, `ControlflowEdgeDef`, `RetryPolicy`, `WorkflowDefaults`, `QueueBinding`, `QueueFileRef`, `TaskIOMap`, `TaskEnvironment`** (`workflow/workflowTypes.h`) — The output domain model. All `Parse*` methods write into these types.
- **`workflowRegistry.h`** — Included in the header; the parser itself does not call registry methods, but the header is pulled in transitively for type completeness.
- **`engine.h`** — Included in the `.cpp` for access to `LOG_CORE_WARN` and `LOG_APP_INFO` logging macros.
- **`std::filesystem::path`** — Used only by the file-local `IsRelativePathString` helper to classify paths for debug logging; not exposed to callers.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`WorkflowJsonParser` has no member variables; it is stateless and trivially constructible. Instances may be created on the stack or as a `const` local. Because simdjson's `ondemand::parser` is stack-allocated inside each public method, different threads may call the same `WorkflowJsonParser` instance concurrently without synchronisation — the parser does not share any mutable state across calls. The `WorkflowDefinition` output parameter is written exclusively by the calling thread; if it is shared across threads, callers must synchronise externally. simdjson's `padded_string` makes an internal copy of `jsonContent`, so the input string need not remain alive after the method returns.

---

**Notes:**

- **Version gating:** `ParseRootObject` hard-rejects any JCWF document whose major version is not `1`. A minor version greater than `1` is accepted but logged as a warning. `ParseGlobalJson` performs no version gating at all — it reads whatever string is in `version` without validation.
- **Defaults are applied at parse time, not dispatch time.** After both `ParseWorkflowJson`/`ParseCanvasJson` return, every `TaskDef` whose `m_TimeoutMs`, `m_RetryPolicy.m_MaxAttempts`, or `m_RetryPolicy.m_BackoffMs` is zero will have been overwritten with the workflow-level default. Task-level values of zero are the sentinel meaning "no value set"; a task explicitly set to `0` in JSON is indistinguishable from an absent value, and the default will overwrite it.
- **AI defaults are explicitly skipped.** The `ai` sub-object inside `defaults` is consumed silently (`// AI defaults are resolved at dispatch time`). Do not expect `WorkflowDefaults` to carry AI provider/model information — those are applied downstream in the AI call executor.
- **`defaults` double-parse:** Because simdjson `ondemand` is strictly forward-only, the `defaults` object is serialised back to raw JSON via `ExtractRawJson` and then re-parsed with a second `simdjson::ondemand::parser`. The raw JSON is also preserved in `WorkflowDefinition::m_DefaultsJson` for later consumers.
- **Auto-trigger injection:** If the `triggers` field is absent or the triggers array is empty, a synthetic trigger `{type: Auto, id: "auto", enabled: true, params: "{}"}` is appended. This happens only in `ParseWorkflowJson` / `ParseRootObject`, not in the `ParseGlobalJson` + `ParseCanvasJson` split path.
- **`QueueFileRef` dual format:** Each entry in `stng_files`, `task_files`, `cntx_files`, and `prob_files` may be either a bare path string or an object with `path` and `content` fields. When the object form is used, `m_HasInlineContent` is set to `true` and `m_Content` carries the inline text; the file is not read from disk at execution time.
- **`onedrive_upload` and `onedrive_download` both map to `TaskType::OneDrive`**, as do `azure_blob_upload`/`azure_blob_download` → `TaskType::AzureBlob` and `gcs_upload`/`gcs_download` → `TaskType::Gcs`. The direction of transfer is expected to be encoded in `params`, not inferred from the type enum.
- **Unknown fields are not errors.** Unrecognised keys at the root, trigger, task, and retry levels produce `LOG_CORE_WARN` entries and are skipped, preserving forward compatibility. Missing required fields (`version`, `id`, `tasks`, trigger's `type`/`id`) are hard errors.
- **Filter cross-validation runs inside the parser.** A task referencing a filter ID that does not appear in the same document's `filters` array causes a hard failure with a descriptive message. This validation runs in both `ParseWorkflowJson` and `ParseCanvasJson`.
- **Debug path logging is always on.** Every call to `ParseTask` unconditionally emits `LOG_APP_INFO` entries tagged `[paths debug]` for every file input, output, and queue binding file ref. There is no compile-time or runtime flag to suppress this; callers loading many workflows will see substantial log volume.


---

### 98_doc_application_workflow_workflowJsonParserDetails_h

<a id="98-doc-application-workflow-workflowjsonparserdetails-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/98_doc_application_workflow_workflowJsonParserDetails_h/docu.output.md`



## WorkflowJsonParser (workflowJsonParser.cpp / workflowJsonParserDetails.h)

**Role:** This file implements the structural JSON deserialization layer for j9t workflow definitions. `WorkflowJsonParser` reads simdjson `ondemand` values representing a workflow document and populates typed C++ structs (`WorkflowTrigger`, `TaskDef`, `ControlNodeDef`, `ControlflowEdgeDef`, `DataflowDef`, `FilterDef`, `QueueBinding`, `TaskEnvironment`, `TaskIOMap`) used by the rest of the workflow engine. Every parse method follows a strict fail-fast contract: it validates JSON types, enforces required fields, and writes a human-readable diagnostic into an `errorMessage` output parameter on any failure, returning `false` immediately. Unknown fields are tolerated but logged as warnings via `LOG_CORE_WARN`, making the parser forward-compatible with schema extensions. A free function `AIAssistant::ParseTaskQueueBinding` declared in `workflowJsonParserDetails.h` handles `QueueBinding` deserialization and is delegated to directly by the member method of the same name.

**Public API:**

- `ParseTriggers(value, triggersOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Expects `value` to be a JSON array; iterates each element as an object and delegates to the (separately defined) `ParseTrigger` method. Appends populated `WorkflowTrigger` instances to `triggersOut`. Fails if the value is not an array, any element is not an object, or `ParseTrigger` fails for any entry.

- `ParseControlNodes(value, controlNodesOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Expects a JSON array of objects. Clears `controlNodesOut` before parsing. Each entry must contain `"id"` (string) and `"type"` (string, mapped to `ControlNodeType` via `StringToControlNodeType`); `"label"` is optional. Currently the only recognised type string is `"branch"` mapping to `ControlNodeType::Branch`; anything else maps to `ControlNodeType::Unknown`.

- `ParseControlflow(value, controlflowOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Expects a JSON array of objects. Clears `controlflowOut` before parsing. Each edge object requires `"from"`, `"to"`, and `"kind"` string fields. `"kind"` is mapped to `ControlflowKind` via `StringToControlflowKind`; recognised values are `"normal"`, `"error_signal"`, and `"on_error"`; anything else maps to `ControlflowKind::Unknown`. Optional fields `"from_port"` and `"to_port"` are captured verbatim.

- `ParseTasks(value, tasksOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Expects the JSON value to be an object (keyed map of task definitions). Each value must itself be an object delegated to the separately defined `ParseTask`. If a task's `m_Id` field is empty after parsing, the map key is used as the ID. Results are inserted into the provided `std::unordered_map<std::string, TaskDef>`.

- `ParseTaskInputs(value, inputsOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Expects a JSON object mapping port names to descriptor objects. Each descriptor may contain `"type"` (string), `"required"` (bool), and `"default"` (string). All three are optional in the descriptor object; the parser does not enforce their presence. Populates `TaskIOMap` (an `std::unordered_map<std::string, TaskIOField>`).

- `ParseTaskOutputs(value, outputsOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Same structure as `ParseTaskInputs` but without `"default"` support; only `"type"` (string) and `"required"` (bool) are parsed for each output descriptor.

- `ParseTaskEnvironment(value, environmentOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Expects a JSON object. Populates `TaskEnvironment` with optional `"name"` (string) and `"assistant_id"` (string) fields. The `"variables"` sub-object is parsed with `simdjson::to_json_string`, meaning each variable value is stored as its raw JSON serialisation (not unquoted), regardless of type.

- `ParseTaskQueueBinding(value, bindingOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Thin delegation to the free function `AIAssistant::ParseTaskQueueBinding` defined in `workflowJsonParserDetails.h`. No logic lives in this member beyond the forwarding call.

- `ParseDataflow(value, dataflowsOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Expects a JSON array. Each element is parsed by `ParseSingleDataflow`. All four fields `"from_task"`, `"from_output"`, `"to_task"`, and `"to_input"` are required; missing any causes failure after the full object has been iterated. Optional `"mapping"` sub-object entries are serialised with `simdjson::to_json_string` and then have surrounding JSON double-quotes stripped from string values before storage in `DataflowDef::m_Mapping`.

- `ParseSingleDataflow(jsonObject, dataflowOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Parses a single already-obtained `simdjson::ondemand::object`. Called internally by `ParseDataflow`. Required-field validation happens after iterating all fields, not field-by-field; an incomplete entry returns an error only at the end of the loop.

- `ParseFilters(value, filtersOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Expects a JSON array. Each element is parsed by `ParseFilter`. Emits an `LOG_APP_INFO` trace line per successfully parsed filter.

- `ParseFilter(jsonObject, filterOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Requires `"id"` (string), `"source"` (object parsed by `ParseFilterSource`), and `"binding"` (string). Optional `"max_items"` (integer, stored as `uint32_t`). Additionally validates that `filterOut.m_Source.m_Kind` is non-empty after source parsing, even though `ParseFilterSource` itself does not enforce it.

- `ParseFilterSource(jsonObject, sourceOut, errorMessage) → bool` — Parses a `FilterSource` struct from an already-obtained object. Recognised fields: `"kind"`, `"path"`, `"delimiter"`, `"has_header"` (bool), `"range"`, `"skip_empty"` (bool), `"index_path"`, `"query"`, `"fields"` (array of strings into `m_Fields`), `"connection"`, `"base_url"`, `"project_id"`, `"key_name"`, `"page_size"` (integer stored as `uint32_t`). All fields are optional at this layer; required-field semantics are enforced by the calling `ParseFilter`.

**Collaborators:**

- `simdjson` (`simdjson::ondemand`) — all JSON access goes through the simdjson on-demand API. Callers must ensure the underlying `padded_string` or document buffer remains alive for the entire parse call, because on-demand values hold raw pointers into that buffer.
- `workflow/workflowTypes.h` — defines all target structs: `WorkflowTrigger`, `TaskDef`, `TaskIOField`, `TaskIOMap`, `TaskEnvironment`, `ControlNodeDef` (`m_Id`, `m_Type`, `m_Label`), `ControlflowEdgeDef` (`m_From`, `m_To`, `m_Kind`, `m_FromPort`, `m_ToPort`), `DataflowDef` (`m_FromTask`, `m_FromOutput`, `m_ToTask`, `m_ToInput`, `m_Mapping`), `FilterDef` (`m_Id`, `m_Source`, `m_Binding`, `m_MaxItems`), `FilterSource`, `QueueBinding`.
- `workflow/workflowJsonParserDetails.h` — declares `AIAssistant::ParseTaskQueueBinding` (free function). The `.cpp` of that detail header is not shown here; callers must link against its translation unit.
- `engine.h` — pulled in for `LOG_CORE_WARN` and `LOG_APP_INFO` macros used throughout.
- `WorkflowJsonParser` (the class itself) — `ParseTrigger`, `ParseTask`, and `ParseFilter`-related helpers are declared elsewhere (presumably in the class declaration and a companion `.cpp`); this file implements only the subset of methods shown.

**Threading / lifetime:**

All methods are `const` and carry no mutable shared state themselves. The parser object can in principle be used from multiple threads simultaneously if the `simdjson::ondemand` values passed in are independent (on-demand parsers are not thread-safe and must not be shared across threads). The critical lifetime constraint is the simdjson buffer: every `std::string_view` extracted by on-demand iteration is valid only while the originating `padded_string` / document is alive. All such views are immediately converted to `std::string` copies before being stored in output structs, so callers do not need to extend buffer lifetime beyond the parse call itself. The exception is `simdjson::to_json_string` results used in `ParseTaskEnvironment` and `ParseSingleDataflow`; these are similarly copied immediately.

**Notes:**

- `ParseControlNodes` and `ParseControlflow` call `controlNodesOut.clear()` / `controlflowOut.clear()` at entry, unlike `ParseTriggers`, `ParseTasks`, and `ParseDataflow`, which do not clear their output containers. Callers should pass empty containers to all methods to avoid surprising accumulation.
- In `ParseSingleDataflow`, required-field validation fires after the entire field loop. If JSON contains `"from_task"` but not `"to_task"`, the error is reported only after all other fields are processed.
- `ParseFilterSource` accepts `"page_size"` as `int64_t` cast to `uint32_t` with no bounds checking; a negative or oversized value in JSON will silently wrap.
- Mapping values in `ParseSingleDataflow` have JSON string quotes stripped with a simple front/back character check. Non-string JSON values (numbers, booleans, objects, arrays) are stored as their raw JSON text without stripping; only string-typed values are unquoted.
- `ControlNodeType::Unknown` and `ControlflowKind::Unknown` are silently accepted without an error return; consumers downstream are responsible for rejecting unknown enum values if that is required.
- The `ParseFilter` method emits `LOG_APP_INFO` (application-level, not core) upon success, which is unusual relative to the rest of the file that only warns on unknown fields. This trace line will appear in production logs for every workflow load.


---

### 99_doc_application_workflow_workflowRegistry_h

<a id="99-doc-application-workflow-workflowregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/99_doc_application_workflow_workflowRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



## WorkflowRegistry

**Role:** `WorkflowRegistry` is the authoritative in-memory catalogue of all workflow definitions available to the j9t engine. It scans a directory for `.jcwf` files (ZIP containers), extracts them on demand, parses their `global.json` metadata and per-canvas JSON files into `WorkflowDefinition` objects, and indexes them by workflow ID. It also handles the reverse operation — serialising a JSON payload back to disk as a `.jcwf` container — and exposes lookup, validation, dependency-graph, and removal operations. It is the single source of truth for what workflows exist and where their files live.

---

**Public API:**

- `LoadDirectory(workflowsDirectoryPath) → bool` — Clears any existing state, then performs a non-recursive scan of the given directory for `.jcwf` files. Each file is handed to `LoadContainer`. Files that fail to parse are recorded in `m_BrokenWorkflows` rather than causing the whole load to abort. Returns `false` only if the path is empty, missing, or not a directory; an empty (but valid) directory returns `true`. Callers should check `GetBrokenWorkflows()` after a successful return to detect partial failures.

- `ValidateAll() → bool` — Iterates every registered `WorkflowDefinition` and checks: (a) the map key matches `m_Id`; (b) every `TaskDef` has a non-empty `m_Id` that matches its map key; (c) every `m_DependsOn` entry references a task that actually exists in the same workflow. Validation failures are logged as warnings but do not mutate state. Returns `true` only if every check passes.

- `GetWorkflowIds() → std::vector<std::string>` — Returns a sorted list of all registered workflow IDs. Safe to call at any time; returns an empty vector when the registry is empty.

- `GetWorkflow(workflowId) → std::optional<WorkflowDefinition>` — Returns a **copy** of the `WorkflowDefinition` for the given ID, or `std::nullopt` if not found. Returning by value keeps call-site lifetimes simple and means the caller's copy is not invalidated by concurrent registry mutations.

- `GetBrokenWorkflows() → std::vector<BrokenWorkflow> const&` — Returns a const reference to the list of `.jcwf` files that failed to load during the last `LoadDirectory` call. Each `BrokenWorkflow` carries the absolute container path (`m_ContainerPath`), the stem used as a display name (`m_Stem`), and the parser error string (`m_Error`). The reference is valid until the next mutating call.

- `TryGetWorkflowFilePathAbsolute(workflowId) → std::optional<std::string>` — Returns the absolute, weakly-canonical path to the canvas JSON for the given workflow, or `std::nullopt` if the ID is unknown or `m_WorkflowFilePathAbsolute` was not populated.

- `TryGetWorkflowIdByFilePath(absoluteFilePath) → std::optional<std::string>` — Reverse lookup: given an absolute file path, finds the matching workflow ID. The input and every stored path are normalised with `std::filesystem::weakly_canonical` before comparison. For sub-workflows, it additionally compares against `m_WorkflowFileDirectoryAbsolute` (the containing folder) because file-watching events may arrive at directory granularity.

- `GetSubWorkflowDependencyGraph() → std::unordered_map<std::string, std::vector<std::string>>` — Builds and returns a directed adjacency map: parent workflow ID → list of child workflow IDs referenced by `TaskType::SubWorkflow` tasks. Relative `m_WorkflowFile` paths are resolved against the parent's `m_WorkflowFileDirectoryAbsolute`. Entries for a parent are only added if at least one child resolves successfully via `TryGetWorkflowIdByFilePath`; unresolvable references are silently dropped.

- `SaveOrUpdateWorkflowFromJson(workflowJson, workflowFilePathAbsolute, errorMessage) → bool` — Parses `workflowJson` using `WorkflowJsonParser`, writes the canvas JSON to `<stem>/<workflowId>.json` inside an extracted directory adjacent to the target `.jcwf` path, merges any missing `m_Label` / `m_Doc` from an existing `global.json`, writes or creates `global.json`, packs the directory back into the `.jcwf` ZIP via `JcwfContainer::Pack`, and upserts the registry entry. On failure, `errorMessage` is populated and `false` is returned; the on-disk state may be partially written. This is the only path through which the registry accepts externally authored workflow JSON at runtime.

- `RemoveWorkflow(workflowId, deleteFile, errorMessage) → bool` — Erases the entry from `m_Workflows`. If `deleteFile` is `true` and `m_WorkflowFilePathAbsolute` is non-empty, deletes that file from disk via `std::filesystem::remove`. Note that this removes only the canvas JSON, **not** the extracted directory or the `.jcwf` container. Returns `false` (with `errorMessage` set) if the ID is unknown or file deletion fails.

- `Clear()` — Resets `m_Workflows` and `m_BrokenWorkflows` to empty. Called internally at the start of `LoadDirectory`.

---

**Collaborators:**

- `workflow/jcwfContainer.h` (`JcwfContainer`) — provides `Extract`, `Pack`, and `IsExtractedStale`. All ZIP handling is delegated here; `WorkflowRegistry` never touches ZIP bytes directly.
- `workflow/workflowJsonParser.h` (`WorkflowJsonParser`) — parses raw JSON strings into `WorkflowDefinition` objects. Three entry points are used: `ParseGlobalJson` (metadata), `ParseCanvasJson` (tasks and dataflow), and `ParseWorkflowJson` (merged format sent by the editor).
- `workflow/workflowTypes.h` — defines `WorkflowDefinition`, `TaskDef`, `TaskType`, `WorkflowTrigger`, `WorkflowTriggerType`, and related value types that this class stores and returns.
- `engine.h` / `core.h` — pulled in for logging macros (`LOG_APP_INFO`, `LOG_APP_WARN`, `LOG_APP_ERROR`). No other functionality from these headers is exercised in this file.
- File-watcher subsystem (external, not in this file) — uses `TryGetWorkflowIdByFilePath` and `TryGetWorkflowFilePathAbsolute` to correlate filesystem change events with registered workflows.

---

**Threading / lifetime:**

`WorkflowRegistry` has no internal synchronisation. All public methods read or write `m_Workflows` and `m_BrokenWorkflows` directly. Callers must serialise access externally if the registry is shared across threads. `GetWorkflow` returns a value copy specifically to avoid returning references into the map that could be invalidated by a concurrent write. `GetBrokenWorkflows` returns a `const&` and is therefore only safe to use when no mutation is in progress. The registry owns all `WorkflowDefinition` objects by value inside `m_Workflows`; there are no shared or weak pointers into those objects. Sub-workflow `WorkflowDefinition` entries are stored alongside root entries in the same flat map — there is no tree structure in memory.

---

**Notes:**

- `LoadWorkflowFile` is a deprecated stub that always returns `false` and logs a warning. It exists only to satisfy the header declaration; all loading must go through `LoadContainer`.
- Sub-workflow IDs are synthesised at load time using the pattern `<parentId>__<folderName>` (double-underscore separator). This is a naming convention that callers resolving `m_WorkflowFile` references must be aware of.
- `LoadContainer` defaults an `Auto` trigger (id `"auto"`, enabled, empty params JSON) if `global.json` provides no triggers. Sub-workflows loaded by `LoadContainerSubWorkflows` receive no triggers and have `m_ManualStart` forced to `true`.
- `SaveOrUpdateWorkflowFromJson` performs a partial metadata-preservation pass: it reads the existing `global.json` and copies `m_Label` / `m_Doc` into the parsed definition only if those fields are empty in the incoming JSON. Other global fields (triggers, `manual_start`) are not merged from existing data; they come exclusively from the incoming JSON.
- `RemoveWorkflow` with `deleteFile = true` deletes only `m_WorkflowFilePathAbsolute` (the canvas JSON). The extracted folder and the `.jcwf` container are **not** cleaned up; callers that need full removal must handle those paths separately.
- `GetSubWorkflowDependencyGraph` silently drops edges where a `sub_workflow` task's `m_WorkflowFile` cannot be resolved to a registered ID. There is no warning for unresolvable references; callers must not assume the graph is complete.
- `LoadDirectory` treats a directory iteration error mid-scan as a `break` rather than a return-false, meaning workflows enumerated before the error are retained. The return value is still `true` in that case.
- The extracted directory produced by `LoadContainer` is placed as a sibling of the `.jcwf` file, named after the file stem. This side effect on the filesystem is unconditional whenever `JcwfContainer::IsExtractedStale` returns `true`.


---
